# Engine errata audit — 2026-11-07 — details

## EA-01 · 01.02.01 Starting Strength and Half-Strength — A leader whose bodyguard is wiped out is judged on its own wounds, so it never takes the half-strength battle-shock test

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.01: 'The starting strength of an attached unit is the number of models that unit contains at the start of the first battle round ... If all of the Intercessors were destroyed, the remaining Captain would be below half-strength, despite having his full wounds remaining.'
- **Engine:** CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299, no edition gate) detaches every leader when the last bodyguard model dies. After that, CommandPhase._identify_units_needing_tests (CommandPhase.gd:383-421) tests the leader as its own unit. GameState.is_below_half_strength_combined / is_at_half_strength_combined then fall back to the single-model wound check (GameState.gd:1323-1331, 1339-1347, 1393-1399), so a leader at full wounds is never below or at half-strength. SMAbilitiesA.at_or_below_half_strength (SMAbilitiesA.gd:698-703, used by Exhortation of Rage / Executioner) inherits the same result.
- **Scenario:** A Captain (W5) leads 5 Intercessors and the opponent kills all 5. By the rule the attached unit has 1 of 6 models left, so it is below half-strength and must take a battle-shock test every Command phase. At Ld 6+ it fails about 28% of the time, which leaves it battle-shocked (OC '-', can't be targeted by its own stratagems, can't start actions). The engine never asks for the test, because the Captain is now a 1-model unit with full wounds.
- **Fix sketch:** At the start of battle round 1, store each attached unit's starting strength (bodyguard plus leader/support models) on every component (e.g. flags.attached_starting_strength + attached_group). Keep using it after detaching: is_below/at_half_strength_combined should count alive models of the original group against that stored total, with dead bodyguard models still counting. Alternatively, keep the attachment for strength purposes (see the 19.01.01 commentary).
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/CharacterAttachmentManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/GameState.gd`, `40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** In 11e (01.02.01, plus 19.02 and the 19.01.01/24.24.01 commentary) the attached unit keeps the starting strength it had at the start of battle round 1 (Captain + 5 Intercessors = 6) after its bodyguard is wiped. The engine never uses that number. There are two paths. (a) If `check_bodyguard_destroyed` runs after the deaths are applied (human-defender `AllocationGroupOverlay`), the leader is detached without an edition gate. It is then judged as a 1-model unit on its own wounds: it tests only when its own wounds are at or below half, and a full-wounds Captain never does. (b) In auto-resolved saves the check runs before the deaths are applied, so the leader can stay linked to the dead bodyguard. `CommandPhase._identify_units_needing_tests` then skips both units: the bodyguard has no living models and the leader has `attached_to` set. So a full-wounds lone leader is never asked for the below-half-strength battle-shock test the rule requires. The same wrong "not below half-strength" result reaches Exhortation of Rage (`SMAbilitiesA.at_or_below_half_strength`), Executioner (`SMAbilitiesC.gd:522-527`, which calls `GameState.is_below_half_strength_combined` directly) and Avenge the Fallen (`RulesEngine.gd:14054`).
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. I read the code only and did not run the game.

**Rule.** The launch text says the same thing as the errata commentary for 01.02.01: an attached unit's starting strength is its model count at the start of battle round 1, and "If all of the Intercessors were destroyed, the remaining Captain would be below half-strength, despite having his full wounds remaining" (core_rules.txt:3984-4023). Section 19 of the 11e text has only 19.01-19.04. Nothing in it makes a leader its own unit again. The text supports the opposite in three places:
- 19.02: destroyed-unit triggers fire only when "the last model that started the battle in an attached unit is destroyed".
- 19.01.01 commentary: only units with a special rule split, into units with "their original starting strengths".
- 24.24.01 commentary: "an attached unit below starting strength only contains models with Lone Operative" once the bodyguard is dead.

No errata or FAQ reverses this.

**Engine.**
- `CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed` (lines 275-299) and `detach_character` have no edition gate. They clear `attached_to` and the bodyguard's `attached_characters` list.
- Nothing stores a combined starting strength. I grepped for starting_strength, attached_group, original/former/was_attached and found nothing.
- `GameState.is_below/at_half_strength(_combined)` counts `models.size()` of the bodyguard plus the currently attached characters. With no attached characters it falls back to the one-model wound check (lines 1323-1331, 1339-1347, 1393-1399). So a detached full-wounds Captain is never at or below half.
- `CommandPhase._identify_units_needing_tests` (369-421) has no other handling for a leader whose bodyguard is gone.
- The comments that justify detaching cite a "19.05" that does not exist in 11e (RulesEngine:4259, StratagemManager:3736). The launch audit `.llm/audit_2026_launch/findings/03_11_leader.md:28` approved the detach against the 10e rule.
- RULINGS.md records no ruling on this. Delta-audit row 205 marks the rule done, but `test_iss065_at_half_battleshock.gd` only checks 3 and 4 Intercessors dead, never all 5.
- The Ld 6+ fail chance of 10/36 (about 28%) is correct. The army data gives no ATSKNF immunity.

**Overstatements.**
1. "Never" is too strong. In the detached path the leader does test once its own wounds fall to half (even W) or below.
2. The engine section describes only the path where the detach happens. That is the human-defender `AllocationGroupOverlay._finalize_batch`, which applies the diffs before the check. In auto-resolved saves (`ShootingPhase._auto_roll_saves_11e`:3786, plus the phase-side calls in Shooting and Fight) the check reads live GameState before the deaths are applied, so the leader may stay linked to the wiped bodyguard. Nothing else calls the check later. `CommandPhase` then skips both units: the bodyguard because it has no living models, the leader because `attached_to` is set. There is still no test in either case.
3. Executioner is not in `SMAbilitiesA`. It is `SMAbilitiesC.gd:522-527`, which calls `GameState.is_below_half_strength_combined` directly. The wrong result is the same. Avenge the Fallen (RulesEngine:14054) is affected the same way.

## EA-02 · 01.06 (outside the assigned 02.01-03.04.01 range; found while auditing the 02.02.01 Ld limits) — Attached-unit leadership rolls use the WORST Ld instead of the best

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.06: 'if the result is equal to or greater than one or more of the Ld characteristics in that unit, that roll succeeds.'
- **Engine:** CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership takes the numerically highest (worst) Ld across the bodyguard and attached characters (CommandPhase.gd:960-965, 'if char_ld > ld: ld = char_ld'), and the battle-shock test passes on 2D6 >= that value (CommandPhase.gd:1010-1011). The same value feeds the other leadership rolls (:1115, :1439).
- **Scenario:** Boyz (Ld 7) led by a Warboss (Ld 6) at half strength: the engine passes on 7+ (58%), the rules on 6+ (72%). Gretchin (Ld 8) with a Runtherd (Ld 7): the engine needs 8+, the rules 7+.
- **Fix sketch:** Use mini() across the components' Ld, after each component's 'set' rules, then apply the Ld modifiers and the 5+..8+ clamp.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership (40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd:956-964) returns the highest-numbered (worst) Ld among the bodyguard and its attached characters. Under 01.06 + 19.01 the best (lowest) Ld should be used.

Rolls that use the worst Ld:
- the Command-phase battle-shock test (:984/:1012, :1115/:1127, and the END_COMMAND auto-resolve);
- the forced battle-shock tests in FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2385) and the Lissen 'Ere Ld test (:1267);
- SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership (:1000-1007, Fearsome Assault), which has the same maxi bug.

Only the log is affected at :1439, the MOB MENTALITY auto-pass, where no dice are rolled.

The Ork codex helper (OrkAbilities11e.best_leadership) and the Custodes one (CustodesAbilities._best_ld) already use the min correctly.

The AI's estimates (AIDecisionMaker._effective_leadership_for, OrkCodexOther._effective_leadership) copy the worst-Ld rule and need the same fix.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute it.

RULES: The launch text for 01.06 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:188-192) says a leadership roll succeeds "if the result is equal to or greater than one or more of the Ld characteristics in that unit". So the lowest (best) Ld in the unit decides. Rule 19.01 (:3094-3101) says "An attached unit is a single unit for all rules purposes", so the leader's Ld counts. The errata/FAQ doc changes nothing here: its only 01.06 entry, 01.06.01, says "Leadership test" means the same as "leadership roll". The 19.x FAQs do not mention Ld.

ENGINE: CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership (40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd:946-972) starts from the bodyguard's Ld. It then runs `if char_ld > ld: ld = char_ld` (:963-964), which keeps the worst value. The battle-shock step skips attached characters (:381-385), so the bodyguard tests for the whole Attached unit. The pass check is `effective_roll >= leadership` at :1012, and again in _resolve_battle_shock_test (:1115/:1127). The END_COMMAND auto-resolve (:2530-2533) goes through the same handler.

NO RULING DEFENDS IT:
- .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md:191 calls the CommandPhase max rule "a pre-existing inconsistency, flagged, not changed".
- tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:240 (IB-01) records the same bug.
- The code's own comments say the intent is the "best" Ld: CommandPhase.gd:982 and :1114, CommandController.gd:683, OrkCodexOther.gd:298. Only the docstring (:947-952) argues for "highest", based on a misread rule.

DATA: Boyz Ld 7 / Warboss 6, and Gretchin 8 / Runtherd 7. The Runtherd is a Support unit with can_lead ["GRETCHIN"] in 40k/armies/ork11e_codex_test.json. The scenario's odds are right: 21/36 vs 26/36, and 15/36 vs 21/36.

WHERE THE CLAIM IS SLIGHTLY OFF:
- The pass check is at :1012, not :1010-1011.
- :1439 is the MOB MENTALITY auto-success. No dice are rolled there; only the logged Ld is wrong.

THE BUG IS WIDER THAN CLAIMED:
- FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2385; Big Gob, SQUIG FLINGIN', Supa-glowy Fing) uses the same helper.
- So does the Lissen 'Ere Ld test (:1267).
- SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership (40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd:1000-1007, used by Fearsome Assault) uses maxi despite its name, although RULINGS.md:450 says "best Ld".
- The AI copies the worst-Ld rule in AIDecisionMaker._effective_leadership_for (:26281) and OrkCodexOther._effective_leadership (:300).

NOT AFFECTED: The Ork codex rolls (OrkAbilities11e.best_leadership :1109) and Custodes codex rolls (CustodesAbilities._best_ld :1097) already use mini, which is correct.

Player-visible: the roll card, the Command-phase test buttons (CommandController.gd:686-697) and the roll-call queue (:2488) all show the worse Ld.

## EA-03 · 04.01.02 — [CLOSE-QUARTERS]/[PISTOL] either/or not enforced in normal/assault/indirect shooting

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 24.07 (the rule the 04.01.02 Sidearms commentary refers to): 'When using another shooting type, for each model in that unit (excluding MONSTER/VEHICLE models), you can only select one of: one or more of its [CLOSE-QUARTERS] weapons; one or more of its other ranged weapons.' [PISTOL] is identical (24.27).
- **Engine:** ShootingPhase._validate_assign_target skips the per-model check at 11e (ShootingPhase.gd:853). _validate_confirm_targets runs only shooting-type gates (898-922), and NormalShooting has no weapon_allowed. RulesEngine.validate_shoot's MA-25 check (6229-6256) uses is_pistol_weapon, which reads only the PISTOL keyword (8900-8909); 11e army data carries 'close quarters'. The phase also only ever calls it with a single assignment. ShootingController says exclusivity 'does not apply at all' at 11e (7041-7049) and auto-assigns every row (1735). Only the AI applies 24.07 (AIDecisionMaker.gd:289-310).
- **Scenario:** A Reiver Squad (each model: bolt carbine + special-issue bolt pistol [CLOSE-QUARTERS]) or a Captain on Bike (twin bolt rifle + heavy bolt pistol) uses normal shooting. With one eligible target, auto-assign aims every row at it, so each model fires both its carbine/rifle and its pistol. 11e allows only one of the two sets per model.
- **Fix sketch:** At 11e, for every shooting type except close_quarters, reject a non-MONSTER/VEHICLE model that holds both a _snap_is_close_quarters_weapon assignment and a non-CQ assignment (_validate_assign_target and _validate_confirm_targets). Have auto/quick-assign pick one set per model, reusing the AI sanitizer's either/or. Make validate_shoot use _snap_is_close_quarters_weapon instead of is_pistol_weapon.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/ShootingController.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/shootingtypes/NormalShooting.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** At 11e (the only player edition), the interactive shooting path never enforces the 24.07 per-model [CLOSE-QUARTERS]/[PISTOL] either/or in normal, assault or indirect shooting. ShootingPhase._validate_assign_target skips its MA-25 per-model check at edition>=11 (line 853) and only sends validate_shoot a single assignment. _validate_confirm_targets only runs the shooting type's weapon_allowed/target_allowed (Normal/Indirect allow all weapons; Assault only checks [ASSAULT]). No process or resolve step filters per model. ShootingController's weapon rows, single-target auto-assign (on by default) and quick-assign 'All -> X' all let every row fire; its comment wrongly says exclusivity 'does not apply at all' at 11e, following ISSUES.md ISS-048's misreading of 24.27. The atomic SHOOT path (validate_shoot MA-25, used by the AI and 'Shoot All Remaining') is edition-blind but only checks the PISTOL keyword (is_pistol_weapon), so it misses the 'close quarters' tag that almost all 11e data uses. The engine applies 24.07 only in the AI sanitizer (AIDecisionMaker._sanitize_shoot_assignments), the snap-shot builder (RulesEngine.build_snap_assignments_11e) and the Ork GUN-CRAZY forced-shoot picker (ShootingPhase._ork11e_forced_shoot_assignments). Result: e.g. every Reiver, Intercessor or Infiltrator model, or a Captain on Bike, can fire both its rifle/carbine and its pistol in one normal-shooting activation. The developers' own .llm/ork_codex_11e_design.md lists this as an open gap.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: the launch text 24.07 says: "When using another shooting type, for each model in that unit (excluding MONSTER/VEHICLE models), you can only select one of: one or more of its [CLOSE-QUARTERS] weapons; one or more of its other ranged weapons". 24.27 says [PISTOL] and [CLOSE-QUARTERS] "are identical for all rules purposes". The errata file does not change 24.07. Its only related entry, the 04.01.02 Sidearms commentary, backs the restriction: "you may not be able to select all of their weapons". Neither RULINGS.md nor docs/ records a ruling against it.

ENGINE, interactive human path (ASSIGN_TARGET -> CONFIRM_TARGETS -> RESOLVE_SHOOTING), checked line by line:
(1) ShootingPhase.gd:853 gates the MA-25 per-model check on `edition < 11`. edition is 11 by default (scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27, "the only edition players get").
(2) _validate_assign_target sends RulesEngine.validate_shoot only one assignment (870-883), so its MA-25 block can never see two weapons there.
(3) _validate_confirm_targets at 11e only runs each type's weapon_allowed/target_allowed and returns valid (906-922). NormalShooting and IndirectShooting inherit the base weapon_allowed, which allows everything. AssaultShooting only checks [ASSAULT].
(4) _process_assign_target, _process_confirm_targets, _process_shoot and resolve_shoot/resolve_shoot_until_wounds do no per-model filtering.
(5) In ShootingController, the 11e weapon rows are gated only by weapon_allowed (1648-1675). Single-target auto-assign queues every row (1735; auto_assign_single_target defaults to true, line 92). Quick-assign 'All -> X' turns exclusivity off at 11e, and its comment wrongly says it 'does not apply at all' (7036-7049).
(6) RulesEngine.validate_shoot's MA-25 check (6229-6256) is edition-blind but uses is_pistol_weapon, which only reads a PISTOL keyword (8900-8909). For close_quarters, AbilityRegistry.to_display_string gives 'close-quarters', so the derived keyword is CLOSE-QUARTERS, not PISTOL. 11e data uses close_quarters almost everywhere (sm11e 89 weapons, sw11e 44, ork11e 11).
Scenario confirmed: Reiver Squad models have no model_profiles and empty wargear, so each carries Bolt Carbine and Special-issue Bolt Pistol ('close quarters'). Captain on Bike is MOUNTED. 32 non-MONSTER/VEHICLE units in sm11e_codex_test.json carry both sets (Intercessors, Infiltrators, Scouts, Heavy Intercessors...).

Developer acknowledgement: ISSUES.md ISS-048 says 'The 10e pistol mutual-exclusivity retires at 11e (24.27)'. That misreads 24.27, and the developers' own later doc lists the gap as open: .llm/ork_codex_11e_design.md:727-729 ('ordinary shooting still does not enforce the 24.07 close-quarters either/or (GUN-CRAZY's forced picker and the AI planner do)') and 755-758 ('the human weapon tree is unchanged'). PRD.md:68-69 requires 'sidearm exclusivity per 24.07'. git log --all shows 24.07 work only for the AI (49854ab) and the Ork forced shoot (f3a1a02). The excluded Hidden items are unrelated.

OVERSTATED:
(a) 'Only the AI applies 24.07' is wrong. RulesEngine.build_snap_assignments_11e (snap shooting / Fire Overwatch, 1612-1680) and ShootingPhase._ork11e_forced_shoot_assignments (Ork GUN-CRAZY forced normal shooting, 5733-5770) also apply it.
(b) 'The phase only ever calls it with a single assignment' is only true for ASSIGN_TARGET. The atomic SHOOT action (ShootingPhase._validate_shoot:1144, used by the AI, 'Shoot All Remaining' and Mathhammer) validates the whole list. There, MA-25 still blocks a PISTOL-keyword plus other mix at 11e, but not 'close quarters' weapons.

## EA-04 · 04.01.03 — Models fire every profile of a multi-profile ranged weapon

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.01.03: 'if a selected weapon has more than one profile, then the controlling player must also select one of those profiles' (a different profile may be chosen per model).
- **Engine:** Each profile is its own weapon id with its own tree row (ShootingController.gd:1578). Single-target auto-assign queues every row (1735), and quick-assign takes every selectable row (7029-7100). _process_assign_target only dedups models within the same weapon_id (ShootingPhase.gd:1502-1516), and no validator checks sibling profiles (RulesEngine.validate_shoot 6046-6258; ShootingPhase 898-922). Models carry all profiles of a multi-profile weapon (RulesEngine.gd:7925-7931). Only the AI sanitizer (AIDecisionMaker.gd:272-287), snap shots (RulesEngine.gd:1626-1690) and GUN-CRAZY forced shooting (ShootingPhase.gd:5743-5760) pick one profile.
- **Scenario:** A Grey Hunters pack leader with a plasma pistol gets both 'Plasma Pistol – Standard' and '– Supercharge' rows. Against a lone eligible target both are auto-assigned, so he fires two plasma shots (one supercharged, with its hazard roll) instead of one. A Scout Squad missile launcher likewise fires both Frag and Krak.
- **Fix sketch:** Group weapon ids by base name (_snap_weapon_base_name). In _validate_assign_target, reject (or in _process_assign_target, replace) an assignment that gives a model a second profile of a weapon it already uses. In the UI, show sibling profiles as one row with a profile toggle and auto-assign only the best profile.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/ShootingController.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: errata/commentary 04.01.03 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:280-282) says that if a selected weapon has more than one profile, the player must select one of those profiles; a different profile may be chosen per model. The launch text 04.01 (core_rules.txt ~609-620) selects weapons per model. No RULINGS.md entry allows several profiles. The developers already list this as a known pre-existing gap in .llm/ork_codex_11e_design.md:754-757: "The SHOOT validator does not reject two profiles of one weapon for the same model (04.01.03) ... Since AIR-2 the AI planner applies [it] itself ... the human weapon tree is unchanged". Git history agrees: f3a1a02 only added the Hunter-profile target limit, and 49854ab (AIR-2) fixed the AI only.

Each part of the claim checked out in the code:
(1) Data: Grey Hunters store 'Plasma Pistol – Standard' and '– Supercharge' as separate weapons (sw11e_gw_import_2000.json; wargear says '1x Plasma Pistol'). The loadout resolver gives a model every profile of a weapon named by its base name (RulesEngine.gd:7925-7944, 7987-7990; datasheet-defaults path 8152-8161). test_sw11e_codex_data.gd:164 counts one plasma pistol model only because its _per_model helper merges the two profile ids, which shows the model holds both. Each profile becomes its own id via _generate_weapon_id.
(2) UI: _refresh_weapon_tree makes one row per weapon id (ShootingController.gd:1578). Normal shooting's weapon_allowed is the base class and allows everything (ShootingType.gd:37), so both rows can be selected. With one eligible target, both rows are queued (1735) and flushed through _auto_assign_target (6722). Quick-assign (7029-7115) also takes every selectable row. So do two paths the claim doesn't mention: Apply to All (6842-6917) and Shoot All Remaining (_build_auto_shoot_plan 4594-4615).
(3) Validators: every UI path checks only through the phase validator. _validate_assign_target (ShootingPhase.gd:813-884) passes just the new assignment to RulesEngine.validate_shoot, so pending sibling assignments are never seen. validate_shoot (6046-6258) checks ownership, Hunter targets, range/visibility, ONE SHOT and pistol either/or, but not sibling profiles. _validate_confirm_targets at 11e (898-922) checks only the shooting type. _validate_shoot (1137-1145, the atomic SHOOT) is the same.
(4) Processing: _process_assign_target removes duplicate models only within the same weapon_id (1502-1516). _process_confirm_targets merges only matching weapon+target pairs (1607-1640). resolve_shoot runs every assignment and rolls a hazard check for each [HAZARDOUS] one (867-878). So the supercharge profile fires and rolls its hazard alongside the standard profile.
(5) One-profile pickers exist only on automated paths: the AI sanitizer (AIDecisionMaker.gd:264-286), snap shots (RulesEngine.gd:1653-1670), and the GUN-CRAZY forced shot (ShootingPhase.gd:5743-5754), which HAIL OF VENGEANCE also uses. The claim misses one: Custodes RAPID REACTIONS (CustodesCodexMovement.gd:591-624). No profile picker or sibling grouping exists anywhere in ShootingController.

The claim is not overstated. Its list of one-profile paths is incomplete, and the player is exposed through more paths than it names (Apply to All, Shoot All Remaining). Severity high and player-visible are justified: with a single eligible target the extra profile fires without the player choosing it.

## EA-05 · 05.04.01 — Engine-side melee saves (AI defender / auto-allocate) never reach the attached CHARACTER

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 05.03/05.04.01: allocation groups cover all models in the target (attached) unit, with one group per CHARACTER model. 'Once all models in an allocation group are destroyed, the next group in the allocation order becomes the current one.' Excess attacks are lost only when every model in the target unit is destroyed.
- **Engine:** _resolve_melee_assignment_saves passes the bodyguard dict (RulesEngine.gd:15030) to _apply_saves_via_allocation_11e (15067). That calls Allocation.build_groups(target_unit) (3647), which has no CHARACTER group for the leader. Once the bodyguard group dies, remaining failed saves are 'lost — unit destroyed' (Allocation.gd:200-202). _precision_group_11e also finds no CHARACTER group (3978-3988). Only resolve_allocation_batch_11e builds the attached virtual unit (4126). FightPhase takes the engine path whenever the defender is AI or auto-allocate is on (FightPhase.gd:1444-1452, 1915). Ranged resolve_shoot/_resolve_assignment does the same (4412, 5687); it is used by GUN-CRAZY SHOW-OFFS forced shots (ShootingPhase.gd:5797).
- **Scenario:** Human vs AI: Custodian Guard charge an AI Boyz mob with 3 Boyz left, led by a Warboss, and inflict 8 failed saves. The 3 Boyz die and the other 5 failed saves are discarded, so the Warboss takes nothing. A [PRECISION] weapon cannot promote him either. Against a human defender (overlay path) the same attack spills onto the Warboss.
- **Fix sketch:** In _resolve_melee_assignment_saves and _resolve_assignment, allocate against _build_attached_allocation_unit_11e(target_id).unit and remap diff paths to their source units, as resolve_allocation_batch_11e does (RulesEngine.gd:4167-4175). Simplest route: call resolve_allocation_batch_11e with the CasualtyPreference group order.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/Allocation.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** At 11e, every engine auto-resolve allocation path builds its groups from the raw bodyguard unit dict, so an attached CHARACTER in a separate unit dict is never in any allocation group. The melee paths are _resolve_melee_assignment_saves, used for AI defenders, for human defenders with auto-allocate on, and for both the staged and one-shot fight flows. The ranged path is resolve_shoot/_resolve_assignment. As a result:
(1) failed saves left over after the last bodyguard model dies are discarded as "unit destroyed" instead of moving on to the CHARACTER group;
(2) [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] crits only reach bodyguard models;
(3) [PRECISION] / Epic Challenge cannot promote the leader's group.
Only the batch path (resolve_allocation_batch_11e) folds the leader in; it is used by AllocationGroupOverlay and ShootingPhase._auto_roll_saves_11e. The overlay serves a human defender with auto-allocate off, so that defender correctly takes the spill-over. Affected ranged callers include GUN-CRAZY SHOW-OFFS and the other forced-shot path (ShootingPhase.gd:5797, MovementPhase.gd:6051), Fire Overwatch auto-resolve (MovementPhase.gd:2763/6170, ChargePhase.gd:3663) and Mathhammer. Normal Shooting-phase auto saves are already correct.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute the claim and could not; the cited code is accurate.

Rule: 05.03 builds the groups from "all models in the target unit", with one group per CHARACTER model. 05.04 loses excess attacks only once "all models in the target unit are destroyed". 19.01 says an attached unit is "a single unit for all rules purposes", and the 19.01.01 commentary keeps it one unit. No RULINGS.md entry or doc accepts the engine's behaviour as a deliberate ruling. docs/RULES_AUDIT_FOLLOWUPS_2026-10-17.md lists "automatic vs interactive allocation parity" as not started. CORE_RULES_REMEDIATION P1-3 later fixed the ranged auto saves only, not melee.

Data model: CharacterAttachmentManager.attach_character keeps the leader as a separate unit dict, linked by attached_to / attachment_data.attached_characters. Its models are never merged into the bodyguard. GameState.create_snapshot is a plain deep copy. FightPhase._fight_folded_board is used only for pile-in and consolidate moves.

Melee engine path:
- FightPhase.gd:1444-1452 sets interactive_saves = defender_is_human and not auto_alloc. Otherwise it calls _process_roll_dice_auto, which calls resolve_melee_attacks.
- The staged human-attacker path calls resolve_melee_saves_auto (FightPhase.gd:1915).
- Both end in _resolve_melee_assignment_saves. There, target_unit = units.get(target_id) (RulesEngine.gd:15030) is the raw bodyguard; FightPhase validation turns attached characters into the bodyguard id. It is never reassigned before _apply_saves_via_allocation_11e (15067).
- That function calls Allocation.build_groups(target_unit) (3647), which builds no CHARACTER group for the leader. Once the bodyguard groups are dead, every remaining roll is "lost: unit destroyed, excess attacks are lost" (Allocation.gd:200-202).
- Devastating wounds also go only to the bodyguard (apply_devastating_wounds_11e(target_unit)).
- _precision_group_11e (3978-3988) builds its groups from the same raw unit, so [PRECISION] / Epic Challenge finds no CHARACTER group and promotes nothing.
- No 10e-era PRECISION or spill-over code runs at 11e (regular_wounds_to_save is set to 0 when use_allocation_11e).
- Only the batch path folds the leader in: resolve_allocation_batch_11e (4126) via _build_attached_allocation_unit_11e. That path is used by AllocationGroupOverlay (FightController picks it at 11e) and ShootingPhase._auto_roll_saves_11e. So a human defender with auto-allocate off does take the spill-over onto the Warboss, which confirms the asymmetry.
- The developers' own notes agree. RulesEngine's comment near 4294 says wounds that finish the last bodyguard "must still spill onto the CHARACTER group". CasualtyPreference.gd:647 says the "RAW target unit the engine auto-resolve paths pass — only its own models can take wounds".
- The only existing test of "character reached last" through resolve_shoot (test_iss041b) embeds an is_character model inside one unit dict, not the real separate-unit layout. No test covers the auto melee path against a real attached unit.

Ranged: _resolve_assignment does the same (4412 then 5687), and resolve_shoot itself folds nothing.

Small corrections:
(a) "Only resolve_allocation_batch_11e builds the virtual unit" is loose. precision_eligible_groups_11e (4037) and the public wrapper build_attached_allocation_unit_11e, used by the overlay and ShootingPhase auto saves, also build it. The point stands, because no engine auto-resolve path uses it.
(b) The ranged exposure is wider than GUN-CRAZY SHOW-OFFS. Every resolve_shoot caller is affected:
- the Fire Overwatch auto-resolve paths (MovementPhase.gd:6170 when the defender is not interactive, MovementPhase.gd:2763, ChargePhase.gd:3663)
- the other forced-shot path (MovementPhase.gd:6051)
- Mathhammer.gd:154
Normal Shooting-phase AI/auto saves are already correct (P1-3).
(c) In the scenario, the 5 leftover rolls should be checked against the Warboss's own Sv, so "8 failed saves" is loose wording. The result is the same: today the Warboss takes nothing.

Related, but not part of the claim: FightPhase._staged_fight_target_destroyed checks only the bodyguard's models. Later weapon assignments against an Attached unit whose bodyguard has died are therefore skipped.

## EA-06 · 05.04.04 — Attached unit treated as destroyed once its bodyguard models die: remaining weapons skipped

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 05.04.04: 'When every model in a unit has been destroyed, that unit is destroyed.' An attached unit with only leader models left is still that unit (19.02: 'If that unit only contains leader/support models, use the highest T characteristic of those models').
- **Engine:** Every destroyed-target check reads only the targeted (bodyguard) dict's models. _is_unit_destroyed (ShootingPhase.gd:4812-4823) feeds the sequential skip loop (3991) and the remaining-weapon filters (4209, 4236, 7497-7529). The same applies to the AI save path's any_alive check (ShootingPhase.gd:3761-3768) and to melee _staged_fight_target_destroyed (FightPhase.gd:1718-1741). The leader is then detached by CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed.
- **Scenario:** A Space Marine squad's bolt rifles kill the last two Boyz of a Boyz mob led by a Warboss. Its plasma gun and grenade launcher assignments at that unit are then logged 'Skipped … target destroyed', although the Warboss is still on the table and still part of the targeted unit. Likewise in melee: after choppas kill the last Boy, the Nob's power klaw assignment is skipped.
- **Fix sketch:** Replace the bodyguard-only checks with an attached-unit check: alive models across attached_unit_component_ids(attached_unit_target_id(id)). Resolve the remaining weapons against the leader models via _build_attached_allocation_unit_11e, using the leaders' T: _get_attached_unit_toughness currently falls back to the dead bodyguard's profile T. Defer detaching the leader until the attacking unit's attacks are resolved.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/CharacterAttachmentManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Under 05.04.04, 19.01 and 19.02, an attached unit whose bodyguard models are all destroyed but whose leader survives is not destroyed. The engine still treats it as destroyed for the rest of the attacking unit's activation. Targeting always resolves to the bodyguard id, and every remaining-weapon destroyed check reads only the bodyguard dict:
- ShootingPhase._is_unit_destroyed (4812), used by the _resolve_next_weapon skip loop (3989) and the miss-path filter and auto-complete (4209/4236);
- the after-saves kill count _targets_destroyed_by_diffs (7486-7529), which filters the remaining weapons and auto-completes the activation;
- the _auto_roll_saves_11e any_alive check (3761-3768) for later save batches;
- FightPhase._staged_fight_target_destroyed (1718), used by the melee skip loop (1731).

Example: bolt rifles kill the last Boyz (leftover wounds do spill onto the Warboss within that batch). The plasma gun and grenade launcher are then logged 'Skipped … — target Boyz destroyed' and the activation ends with the Warboss untouched.

In melee, the skip fires once the casualties are in live state. With an interactive defender that is always the case, because the AllocationGroupOverlay writes the deaths before APPLY_MELEE_SAVES. With auto-allocate, the next assignment's check runs before the previous weapon's diffs land, so only the assignments after it are skipped.

CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed has no edition check and also detaches the leader. It cites a '19.05' that does not exist in the 11e text: the 19.01.01 commentary, the starting-strength example and the 24.24.01 commentary all keep the leader-only remainder as the same attached unit. _get_attached_unit_toughness, given a wiped bodyguard, falls back to the bodyguard's profile T instead of the leaders' highest T.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute this claim. Both the rules and the code back it up.

RULES. Launch 19.01: "An attached unit is a single unit for all rules purposes." 19.02: "If that unit only contains leader/support models, use the highest T characteristic of those models." 19.02 also says destroyed-unit triggers fire only when "the last model that started the battle in an attached unit is destroyed". Destroyed (05.04.04): "When every model in a unit has been destroyed, that unit is destroyed." The starting-strength example says a Captain left after all his Intercessors die is still the attached unit (below half-strength). The errata back this up:
- 05.02.01 commentary falls back to the highest T of the models still on the battlefield.
- 19.01.01 commentary says leaders "remain a single unit for all rules purposes", even under the rules of units that split off.
- 24.24.01 commentary says Lone Operative turns back on once only the CHARACTER remains in the attached unit.

Chapter 19 of the 11e text ends at 19.04. There is no 19.05, so the engine's "19.05: the leader is its own unit again" comments (RulesEngine.gd:4259, ShootingPhase.gd:1004, StratagemManager.gd) have no basis. RULINGS.md, the docs and git history have no ruling or fix that covers this.

ENGINE.
- **The target is always the bodyguard.** get_eligible_targets skips units with attached_to set (RulesEngine.gd ~7167). ShootingController.gd:486 sends clicks through attached_unit_target_id. FightPhase.gd:823 refuses an attached character as a melee target. Leader models live in their own unit dict. Only _build_attached_allocation_unit_11e folds them in, so leftover wounds do spill onto the Warboss within one weapon's batch.
- **Every remaining-weapon "destroyed" check reads only the bodyguard dict:**
  - ShootingPhase._is_unit_destroyed (4812-4823) feeds the _resolve_next_weapon skip loop (3989-4016, logs "Skipped X — target Y destroyed") and the miss-path filter and auto-complete (4209, 4236). Single-player fast roll goes through the same loop (3866-3893).
  - After saves, the _targets_destroyed_by_diffs count (7486-7529) marks the bodyguard destroyed, filters out the remaining weapons, and the AUTO-COMPLETE branch ends the activation.
  - _auto_roll_saves_11e checks any_alive (3761-3768) on the bodyguard only and skips later save batches.
  - FightPhase._staged_fight_target_destroyed (1718-1725) feeds the melee skip loop (1731-1741).
- **No redirect.** Nothing in ShootingPhase re-targets the leader. tests/unit/test_skip_attacks_target_destroyed.gd has no attached-unit cases.
- **Leader is detached.** CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed (275-298) has no edition check and detaches the leader. AllocationGroupOverlay.gd:1159-1164 writes the deaths to GameState and detaches straight away.
- **Wrong T.** _get_attached_unit_toughness (6272), given a wiped bodyguard, falls back to the bodyguard's profile T and never looks at the leaders' T.

WHERE THE CLAIM IS SLIGHTLY OFF.
- Lines 7497-7529 don't call _is_unit_destroyed. They count killed models from the diffs, but that check is still bodyguard-only.
- In melee with an interactive defender, the skip happens exactly as claimed. With auto-allocate, the next assignment's check (_staged_fight_continue_to_saves → _staged_fight_assignment_complete → _staged_fight_roll_hits) runs before the previous weapon's casualty diffs reach GameState. So only the assignments after that one get skipped.
- The melee example is muddled: a Nob is a bodyguard model in the Boyz unit, not a leader. The mechanism holds for any later melee assignment against a led unit whose last bodyguard model just died.
- The suggested fix ("defer detaching the leader") doesn't go far enough. 11e has no default rule that splits the leader off at all.

Side note, not fully checked: the 11e melee auto-save path (_resolve_melee_assignment_saves → _apply_saves_via_allocation_11e) passes in the raw bodyguard dict without folding in the leader.

## EA-07 · 06.03 FAQ (allocation of [HAZARDOUS] mortal wounds) — [HAZARDOUS] mortal wounds all go to one bearer model, with no spillover

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 06.03 FAQ: failed [HAZARDOUS] hazard rolls — 'No, you allocate the mortal wounds to the unit as per Mortal Wounds (06.02).' Under 06.02 each MW is allocated one at a time (wounded non-CHARACTER, then non-CHARACTER, then wounded CHARACTER, then CHARACTER) until all are inflicted or the unit is destroyed.
- **Engine:** RulesEngine.resolve_hazardous_check (RulesEngine.gd:11456-11630) gets the 11e count right (1-2 fails, 1 MW each, 3 if all MONSTER/VEHICLE; 11484, 11536-11539). It then sends every MW to one model that holds the Hazardous weapon (_find_hazardous_allocation_target, RulesEngine.gd:11545 and 11672-11717, the Balance Dataslate priority) and applies only min(MW, that model's wounds) (11573); the excess is discarded. Not edition-gated. Every [HAZARDOUS] path uses it (RulesEngine.gd:870, 13419, 13633; ShootingPhase.gd:1964, 3127, 7348; FightPhase.gd:1962; MovementPhase.gd:6233).
- **Scenario:** 10 Hellblasters (W2) fire plasma incinerators and 3 of 10 hazard rolls fail (3 MW). Engine: all 3 go to one Hellblaster, 2 are applied, 1 is lost, so 1 model dies. 11e: 1 model dies and a second is wounded. With W1 bearers and 2 failures, the engine kills 1 model and the rules kill 2. In a unit with one plasma bearer and a wounded trooper, the engine puts the MW on the bearer; 06.02 requires it on the wounded non-CHARACTER model.
- **Fix sketch:** At edition >= 11, replace the single-model block with the 06.02 path used elsewhere: roll Feel No Pain per MW (mortal_wounds_after_fnp), then call Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e on the unit (including attached characters) with CasualtyPreference.engine_auto_preference, and emit current_wounds/alive diffs per model. Drop _find_hazardous_allocation_target and the min() cap at 11e.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** At edition 11, RulesEngine.resolve_hazardous_check (RulesEngine.gd:11609-11779) gets the fail band right (1-2, line 11637). It also gets the count right: 1 mortal wound per failed roll, or 3 if every model is MONSTER/VEHICLE (11689-11692). It then puts all of that call's mortal wounds on a single model. Feel No Pain is rolled for that model (11716), and the damage is capped with min(actual_mw, current_wounds) at 11726, so any excess is dropped. Nothing in this block checks the edition. 06.03, 06.02 and the 06.03 FAQ instead require one-at-a-time allocation across the unit: wounded non-CHARACTER, then non-CHARACTER, then wounded CHARACTER, then CHARACTER. The intended Balance Dataslate "bearer first" priority (_find_hazardous_allocation_target, 11825-11870) never matches in live games. _model_has_hazardous_weapon reads model["weapons"], which no live model has (live loadouts are model.ranged_loadout or meta.model_profiles). So every priority misses and the fallback at 11868-11870 picks the first alive model in the unit's list, whether or not it carries the weapon and whether or not it is wounded. Results: W1 models with 2 failed rolls lose 1 model instead of 2. W2 models with 3 mortal wounds lose 1 model with the third wound dropped, instead of 1 dead and 1 wounded. In a unit with a wounded trooper, the wound goes to model 0 rather than to the wounded trooper. This affects every weapon [HAZARDOUS] path, native or granted: RulesEngine.gd:870, 13604 and 13818; ShootingPhase.gd:1966, 3129 and 7375; FightPhase.gd:1962; MovementPhase.gd:6242. Codex hazard rolls already spill across the unit and are not affected: [SUPA-HAZARDOUS], Try Dat Button and Shokk-boosta. The fix in the claim's sketch still stands: at edition 11, use mortal_wounds_after_fnp plus Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e across the whole unit, including attached components.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule text: the launch 06.03 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:1067-1074) says a failed hazard roll means "that unit suffers 1 mortal wound (see above)". Under 06.02 (:1037-1061) each mortal wound is allocated one at a time, picking a wounded non-CHARACTER model, then a non-CHARACTER, then a wounded CHARACTER, then a CHARACTER, until all are inflicted or the unit is destroyed. The errata FAQ (40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:427 and :694) confirms: "No, you allocate the mortal wounds to the unit as per Mortal Wounds (06.02)". The errata doc has no dataslate-style exception. RULINGS.md and docs/ record no decision to keep the old allocation. The delta audit A2 fix (11th_edition_delta_audit.md:39, :333) changed only the fail band and the mortal-wound count.

Engine (current tree, RulesEngine.gd:11609-11779): the fail band (11637) and the 1-or-3 mortal-wound count (11689-11692) are gated on edition 11. After that, all mortal wounds go to one model picked at 11698. Feel No Pain is rolled for that model (11716). Damage is capped with min(actual_mw, current_wounds) at 11726, and the excess is dropped. Nothing in that block checks the edition. GameConstants.edition defaults to 11 (scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27).

Every weapon [HAZARDOUS] path, native or granted, uses this function and only appends its diffs: RulesEngine.gd:870, 13604 and 13818; ShootingPhase.gd:1966, 3129 and 7375; FightPhase.gd:1962; MovementPhase.gd:6242. Unit tests pin the old Balance Dataslate priority with no edition gate (tests/unit/test_hazardous_keyword.gd:243-273).

The correct per-wound path already exists elsewhere: Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e (scripts/rules/Allocation.gd:348) and mortal_wounds_after_fnp (RulesEngine.gd:~16975). Codex hazard rolls use AttackSequence.hazard_rolls plus unit-wide allocation that spills over: [SUPA-HAZARDOUS], Try Dat Button and Shokk-boosta. RULINGS.md:156 even describes [SUPA-HAZARDOUS] as "mortal wounds to the bearer's unit (as [HAZARDOUS])". Only the weapon [HAZARDOUS] path skips this.

The scenario numbers hold:
- W2 models, 3 mortal wounds: the engine applies 2 and loses 1, so one model dies. 06.02 gives one dead model and a second wounded.
- W1 models, 2 failures: the engine kills one model. 06.02 kills two.

Overstated part: the claim says the mortal wounds go to "one model that holds the Hazardous weapon". _model_has_hazardous_weapon (RulesEngine.gd:11873-11883) reads model["weapons"], but live models never have that key. None of the 1032 models in 40k/armies/*.json have it, and nothing writes it. Live loadouts are meta.weapons plus model.ranged_loadout or meta.model_profiles, read through _get_model_weapon_ids (RulesEngine.gd:7835). So in real games all three bearer priorities fail, and _find_hazardous_allocation_target takes its "shouldn't happen" fallback (11868-11870): the first alive model in the list. The bearer priority only works on test boards that put weapons on the models. The claim's third example ("the engine puts the MW on the bearer") is wrong for the same reason: the engine picks the first alive model, ignoring both the bearer and the wounded trooper unless one is first in the list. The claim's line numbers are also out of date by about 150 lines.

## EA-08 · 09.02.02 Commentary (conditions labelled with a mode) / 09.07 WHILE MOVING — Ordered Retreat fall-backs can move through enemy models

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 09.07 WHILE MOVING: 'Desperate Escape: Each model that is moved can be moved through enemy models.' 09.02.02: conditions 'labelled with a mode name only apply if you selected that mode'; ordered retreat is not mandatory, so the player may choose desperate escape. 03: a model's 'base cannot be moved through enemy models' unless a rule says otherwise.
- **Engine:** The MovementPhase move validators apply _path_crosses_enemy_bases only to NORMAL/ADVANCE moves (MovementPhase.gd:759, 893, 8145, 8232). For FALL_BACK, _check_engagement_range_at_position checks only the end point (7080-7084). So every fall-back, Ordered Retreat included, can pass through enemy bases. FallBackMove.before_moving returns can_move_through_enemies only for DE (FallBackMove.gd:112), but nothing reads it. The Fall Back button never sends payload.fall_back_mode (MovementController.gd:1498-1509), so a unit that is not battle-shocked cannot choose DE.
- **Scenario:** Intercessors that are not battle-shocked are engaged and wrapped by Boyz, so the only way out is through Boyz bases. Engine: Fall Back defaults to Ordered Retreat and the models are dragged straight through the Boyz with no hazard rolls and no battle-shock roll. 11e: Ordered Retreat cannot cross enemy bases; to cross them the unit must use Desperate Escape (one hazard roll per model, then a battle-shock roll).
- **Fix sketch:** At edition >= 11, apply the enemy-crossing check to FALL_BACK unless active_moves[unit].fall_back_mode == 'desperate_escape' (or a move-through grant such as Calistus or Shokk-boosta applies). Add an Ordered Retreat / Desperate Escape choice for unshocked units to the fall-back UI and the AI (payload fall_back_mode).
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/FallBackMove.gd`, `40k/scripts/MovementController.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** At 11e (the default edition), an Ordered Retreat fall-back can move through enemy models other than MONSTER/VEHICLE models, with no hazard rolls and no battle-shock roll. The MovementPhase path validators apply _path_crosses_enemy_bases only to NORMAL/ADVANCE moves (MovementPhase.gd:761, 896, 8176, 8264). For FALL_BACK only the destination is checked (_check_engagement_range_at_position 7111-7115; CONFIRM_UNIT_MOVE also checks only the end position and coherency). The MovementController drag code has no enemy-path check. This is a 10e carry-over: the 10e per-model crossing test (_process_desperate_escape) is disabled at 11e (MovementPhase.gd:3542, ISS-064) and no Ordered Retreat crossing ban replaced it. FallBackMove.before_moving's can_move_through_enemies (FallBackMove.gd:112) is never read. Enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE bases still block non-FLY fall-backs in every mode (_path_crosses_monster_vehicle_bases), and FLY units skip both checks regardless of taking to the skies (a separate issue). The phase accepts payload.fall_back_mode (MovementPhase.gd:2921), but the human Fall Back button (MovementController.gd:1488-1511) never sends it and no UI offers the choice, so an unshocked human unit cannot select Desperate Escape. The AI's Desperate Escape retry (AIPlayer.gd:2823-2852) never fires for crossing reasons, because Ordered Retreat staging never fails on crossing. Fix: at edition >= 11, apply the enemy-base crossing check to FALL_BACK unless fall_back_mode == 'desperate_escape' or a fall-back move-through grant applies: Calistus or SUPER-HEAVY WALKER (already inside _path_crosses_enemy_bases), Grapnel Launchers (_o6_moves_through_models), or taking to the skies. Shokk-boosta is advance-only and not relevant. Then add an Ordered Retreat / Desperate Escape choice to the fall-back UI, and have the AI choose deliberately.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule text confirmed. docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:389-395 (03.01): "Its base cannot be moved through enemy models" unless otherwise stated. 09.07 (:1417-1441): WHILE MOVING "Desperate Escape: Each model that is moved can be moved through enemy models." The 09.02.02 commentary in docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:460-462, also in the launch sidebar and repeated at 09.07.01: mode-labelled conditions apply only if that mode was selected, and ordered retreat is not mandatory, so a unit may select desperate escape. So Ordered Retreat cannot cross enemy bases.

No refutation found:
(1) MovementPhase.gd only calls _path_crosses_enemy_bases when move_data.mode is NORMAL or ADVANCE: SET_MODEL_DEST at 761, STAGE_MODEL_MOVE at 896, group validation at 8176, group helper at 8264. Line numbers have drifted a few from the claim.
(2) _check_engagement_range_at_position (7111-7115) tests only the FALL_BACK destination. _validate_confirm_unit_move (1003) checks only ending engagement range and coherency. _validate_begin_fall_back has no path check.
(3) The client does no enemy-path check in any mode: MovementController._compute_move_rejection (2724), _update_model_drag_world and _group_move_rejection_for check only cap, board edge and overlaps.
(4) FallBackMove.before_moving's can_move_through_enemies (FallBackMove.gd:112) is read by nothing except test_iss040_move_types.gd:115.
(5) In ordered_retreat mode there are no hazard rolls (before_moving returns {}), and the battle-shock roll only fires for desperate_escape (MovementPhase.gd:3612). The legacy 10e per-model crossing test _process_desperate_escape is gated to edition < 11 (MovementPhase.gd:3542, ISS-064), so at 11e an Ordered Retreat crosses for free. Root cause: the validators keep the 10e rule (fall back may cross, with per-model tests) and nothing replaced it at 11e.
(6) _on_fall_back_pressed (MovementController.gd:1488-1511) never sends fall_back_mode, and no .tscn or UI script offers a Desperate Escape choice, though the phase would read payload.fall_back_mode (MovementPhase.gd:2921). The AI's Desperate Escape retry (AIPlayer.gd:2823-2852) only runs when Ordered Retreat staging fails, which never happens for crossing reasons. AIDecisionMaker._compute_fall_back_destinations checks end points only.

No ruling in .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md or docs/ allows it. The delta audits mark 09.07 green only for hazard and battle-shock. No commit addresses it. Edition 11 is the default (40k/scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd).

Minor overstatements:
- Non-FLY fall-backs, in any mode, are still blocked by enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE bases (_path_crosses_monster_vehicle_bases runs regardless of mode). So the leak is through non-MONSTER/VEHICLE enemy bases, which is exactly the Intercessors/Boyz scenario.
- FLY units skip both checks regardless of taking to the skies, which is a separate issue.
- The fix sketch's Shokk-boosta grant is advance-only (_o6_moves_through_models), so it is irrelevant to fall-back.

## EA-09 · 15.01 FAQ (optional +1CP section) — Heroic Intervention's Into the Fray costs 1CP instead of 2CP

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** FAQ 15.01: an optional +1CP section is not a modifier. 'A stratagem that costs 1CP and is making use of an optional +1CP section has an original cost of 2CP.' Into the Fray (15.11) is such a +1CP section.
- **Engine:** heroic_intervention_11e has a flat cp_cost of 1 (StratagemManager.gd:5110-5119), and _base_cp_cost returns strat.cp_cost for core stratagems (:1623-1630). ChargePhase._process_use_heroic_intervention calls use_stratagem(player, 'heroic_intervention', unit_id) without the chosen mode (ChargePhase.gd:3855-3876). The dialog prices both buttons with the same cost (HeroicInterventionDialog.gd:159-174). Codex -1CP discounts (Countercharge, Laurels of Vigilance, Honour or Death, Celeritous Sentries) are therefore taken off 1CP instead of 2CP.
- **Scenario:** At the end of the opponent's Charge phase, the defender has 1CP and an unengaged squad 5″ from an enemy unit that did not charge. The dialog shows 'Into the Fray (1 CP)'; the squad counter-charges and the defender is left with 0CP. By the rules Into the Fray needs 2CP, so with 1CP only Leap to Defend (chargers only) is legal. Every Into the Fray use is 1CP too cheap, and with a -1CP discount it is free instead of 1CP.
- **Fix sketch:** Pass the chosen mode as context.variant_id to can_use_stratagem, use_stratagem and preview_cp_cost. Have _base_cp_cost add 1 for the into_the_fray variant of heroic_intervention_11e before discounts are applied (original cost 2CP). Price and enable the Fray button from that preview, and make the AI's mode choice budget 2CP.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/StratagemManager.gd`, `40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd`, `40k/dialogs/HeroicInterventionDialog.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Not refuted; the claim is accurate. RULE: the launch text (core_rules.txt:2734-2766) prints HEROIC INTERVENTION at 1CP with a '+1CP' tag on the Into the Fray mode. The 15.01 FAQ (errata_faq_commentary.md:576-577) says this extra cost is not a modifier and the original cost is 2CP, so Into the Fray costs 2CP and a -1CP modifier makes it 1CP. ENGINE: heroic_intervention_11e has a flat cp_cost 1 with no per-mode pricing (StratagemManager.gd:5113-5122). _base_cp_cost (:1626-1633) prices variants only for codex stratagems (codex_for) and returns the flat cp_cost for core ones. preview_cp_cost (:107-117) passes an empty context. ChargePhase._process_use_heroic_intervention reads hi_mode, then calls use_stratagem(player, 'heroic_intervention', unit_id) with no mode or context (ChargePhase.gd:3853-3876). The validation at :3753 also ignores the mode. Every entry point (ChargeController._on_heroic_intervention_used, GameManager, NetworkManager, AI) routes through that call. HeroicInterventionDialog hardcodes the header cost (hi_cost := 1 at 11e, :88-90) and gives Leap and Fray buttons the same row_cost (:159-174). The AI gates on the flat cost of 1 for both modes (AIDecisionMaker.gd:28065). The codex -1CP rules never look at the mode: Countercharge (SWDetachments11e.gd:133-147), Laurels of Vigilance (SMDetachmentsB.gd:489-507), Honour or Death (SMAbilitiesC.gd:847-855) and Celeritous Sentries (CustodesEnhancements.gd:645-653, applied through _cus11e_stratagem_override). So Into the Fray goes from 1CP to 0CP instead of 2CP to 1CP. SCENARIO VERIFIED: with 1CP, can_use_stratagem passes (cost 1). get_heroic_intervention_eligible_units_11e (:4341-4427) offers any unengaged unit within 6" of a non-charging enemy. Using Into the Fray leaves 0CP. NO REFUTATION FOUND: RULINGS.md and docs/ contain no decision to keep Into the Fray at 1CP. Heroic Intervention is not in CORE_WINDOW_STRATAGEMS and no variant pricing exists for it. The developers' own audits list it as open: docs/RULES_AUDIT_FOLLOWUPS_2026-10-17.md:36 ('Into the Fray +1CP not charged'), tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:47,220, and research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md:4370. test_core_stratagems_11e.gd:151 ('HI costs 1 CP at 11e' on into_the_fray) and scenario iss15_heroic_intervention_fray_11e.json (expect_cp delta -1) lock in the wrong 1CP cost and will need updating. The line numbers cited in the claim are within a few lines of the actual code. Only edge case: 'every use is 1CP too cheap' does not hold when two -1CP discounts stack, because both the rules and the engine then reach the 0CP floor. This is too small to call the claim overstated.

## EA-10 · 17.01 (launch text, context for the 17.01 FAQ; not an update item) — MONSTER/VEHICLE normal/advance moves cannot pass through enemy non-M/V models

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 17.01: 'Each time you make a normal or advance move with a unit, MONSTER/VEHICLE models in that unit can be moved through friendly and enemy models (excluding other MONSTER/VEHICLE models).'
- **Engine:** On NORMAL/ADVANCE every non-FLY unit runs _path_crosses_enemy_bases (MovementPhase.gd:759-762, 893-896, 8145-8149, 8232-8236). The helper (7422-7475) exempts only Custodes ka'tah and SUPER-HEAVY WALKER, never MONSTER/VEHICLE movers, so the move fails with 'Cannot move through enemy models during Normal Move or Advance'.
- **Scenario:** A Leman Russ or Battlewagon making a normal move cannot drive through a line of enemy Gretchin or Guardsmen even though it would end clear of them. 11e allows the move.
- **Fix sketch:** In _path_crosses_enemy_bases, when the mode is NORMAL/ADVANCE and the moving unit is MONSTER/VEHICLE, ignore enemy models whose unit is not MONSTER/VEHICLE. Endpoint, overlap and engagement checks stay.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Accurate, with current line numbers. 17.01 is not implemented: in 40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd, every normal/advance path check (_validate_set_model_dest :762-765, _validate_stage_model_move :896-899 [the action the drag UI sends], _process_group_movement :8177-8180, _validate_individual_move_internal :8264-8267, _ork11e_translation_legal :6584) runs _path_crosses_enemy_bases for any non-FLY unit not covered by Shokk-Boosta or Grapnel Launchers. The helper (:7454-7507) exempts only Calistus ka'tah and SUPER-HEAVY WALKER, so plain MONSTER/VEHICLE movers are blocked by enemy non-M/V bases. docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md:150 wrongly marks 17.01 as done.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: launch 03.01 says a base cannot be moved through enemy models "unless otherwise stated". 17.01 (core_rules.txt:2867-2874) is that exception: on normal/advance moves, MONSTER/VEHICLE models can move through friendly and enemy models, excluding other MONSTER/VEHICLE. The errata doc's 17.01 entry (lines 596-599) is only an FAQ about friendly units on other move types, so the launch text stands. RULINGS.md has no 17.01 decision. Engine: GameConstants.edition defaults to 11 (scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27). Every normal/advance path validator in MovementPhase.gd calls _path_crosses_enemy_bases for any non-FLY unit unless _o6_moves_through_models (Shokk-Boosta / Grapnel Launchers only, :7136) applies. Call sites: :762-765 _validate_set_model_dest; :896-899 _validate_stage_model_move, the action the drag UI sends (MovementController.gd:2844); :8177-8180 _process_group_movement; :8264-8267 _validate_individual_move_internal; :6584 _ork11e_translation_legal. The helper (:7454-7507) exempts only Custodes Calistus ka'tah (CustodesKatah.moves_through_all_models needs Calistus active) and SUPER-HEAVY WALKER (UnitAbilities.is_super_heavy_walker needs the keyword or the datasheet ability). It never checks whether the mover is MONSTER/VEHICLE. SettingsService.unit_skips_collision is off by default and only affects end-position overlap, not paths. No branch in git history has 17.01 engine code. No test pins vehicle-vs-enemy-infantry either way; test_monster_vehicle_movement_blocking.gd is 10e-era. So a Leman Russ or Battlewagon (no FLY, not SHW) normal-moving through enemy infantry is refused with 'Cannot move through enemy models during Normal Move or Advance', which 11e allows. docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md:150 wrongly marks 17.01 as done ('Pre-existing; preserved'); 10e had no such exemption. The substance of the claim is accurate; only its line numbers are stale.

## EA-11 · 19.01.01 Commentary (and the 19.01 'single unit' rule) — Wiping the bodyguard dissolves the Attached unit into separate units

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'When the bodyguard unit in an attached unit ... is destroyed, all of those leader/support units remain a single unit for all rules purposes.' Starting strength is fixed at the start of battle round 1, so a lone Captain left from a 6-model attached unit is below half-strength (01.02.01 example).
- **Engine:** check_bodyguard_destroyed (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299, called from the Shooting, Fight and Movement phases) runs detach_character() on every attached character once the bodyguard has no models. A leader+support pair becomes two independent units, and each is judged on its own starting strength: is_below_half_strength_combined falls back to the single-unit check (GameState.gd:1308-1370, CommandPhase.gd:411). The bodyguard dict's wipe is also credited as a destroyed unit (ShootingPhase.gd:313-342) even though the attached unit survives.
- **Scenario:** Captain + 5 Intercessors; all 5 Intercessors die. Engine: the Captain becomes a 1-model unit at full wounds, takes no battle-shock test next Command phase, and the opponent gets kill/secondary credit for the squad. 11e: the Captain is still the attached unit (1 of 6), is below half-strength and must test; no destroyed-trigger fires until he dies. A Leader + Support pair is split by the engine into two separately activated and targeted units.
- **Fix sketch:** At edition 11 do not detach when the bodyguard is wiped. Keep the link, re-heading the Attached unit on the first surviving component in attached_unit_target_id and attached_unit_component_ids. Keep the battle-round-1 starting strength across all components. Fire unit-destroyed hooks (kill credit, secondaries, Deadly Demise) only when the last model of the whole Attached unit dies.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/CharacterAttachmentManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/GameState.gd`, `40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with small corrections. At every edition, CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299) detaches every attached character once the bodyguard dict has no alive model. It is called from ShootingPhase, FightPhase, MovementPhase (Fire Overwatch saves) and the wound/allocation overlays. The engine justifies this with a '19.05: the leader is its own unit again' rule that does not exist in 11e. Effects:
(1) The remnant leader is judged on its own starting strength. GameState.is_below_half_strength_combined / is_at_half_strength_combined (GameState.gd:1361/1426) fall back to the single-unit check, so a full-wound Captain left from Captain + 5 Intercessors takes no battle-shock test (CommandPhase.gd:411/421). Under the pg-86 starting strength rule he is 1 of 6, below half-strength, and must test.
(2) The bodyguard dict's wipe is reported as a destroyed unit by ShootingPhase._check_kill_diffs (297-350), FightPhase._check_kill_diffs (393-440) and SecondaryMissionManager.check_and_report_unit_destroyed / MissionManager.record_unit_destroyed. The leader's death is counted again later, so one Attached unit gives two kills. 19.02 says unit-destroyed triggers fire once, when the last model that started the battle in the Attached unit dies.
(3) Leader + Support pairs, and the two leaders of a 'Bodyguard'-ability unit (Ork Boyz / Kroot Carnivores — the exact 19.01.01 case), are split into separate units, while 19.01.01 keeps them one unit.
Likely (not fully traced): 'while leading' leader abilities stop after the detach, contrary to the 19.04 footnote.
Fix-sketch correction: Deadly Demise (24.08) triggers each time a model is destroyed, so it should not be deferred to the Attached unit's last model. Only unit-destroyed rules (kill/secondary credit and similar) should wait.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULES. Launch text: 19.01 'An attached unit is a single unit for all rules purposes'. 19.02 'Rules that are triggered when a unit is destroyed are only triggered when the last model that started the battle in an attached unit is destroyed'. Appendix pg 86 (core_rules.txt ~3986-3988, 4018-4024): an attached unit's starting strength is its model count at the start of battle round 1, and a Captain left from Captain + 5 Intercessors 'would be below half-strength, despite having his full wounds remaining'. 19.04 footnote: leaders keep 'while this model is leading a unit' abilities after the bodyguard is destroyed. Errata 19.01.01 Commentary: even under a datasheet rule that splits leaders off, 'all of those leader/support units remain a single unit for all rules purposes'. The 11e text has only 19.01-19.04; there is no 19.05. RULINGS.md has no ruling on this.

ENGINE. CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed (275-299) has no GameConstants.edition gate and calls detach_character on every attached character once the bodyguard has no alive model. detach_character clears both attached_to and attached_characters, and nothing connects to detach_completed. It is called from the 11e allocation paths too: ShootingPhase 3459/3695/3788/7148/7224, FightPhase 2276/2334, MovementPhase 6336 (snap-shot saves), WoundAllocationOverlay 1117 and AllocationGroupOverlay 1164. RulesEngine.attached_unit_target_id / attached_unit_models / attached_unit_component_ids (4264-4360) treat a wiped bodyguard as no longer heading the unit. Their comments, ShootingPhase:1006 and the tests (test_attached_char_fight_gate_11e, test_hidden_attached_target_11e, test_counter_offensive) all justify this with '19.05: the leader is its own unit again', a section that does not exist in 11e (it looks like the 10e Leader rule carried over).

Battle-shock: after the detach, GameState.is_below_half_strength_combined (1361) and is_at_half_strength_combined (1426) get no attached characters from get_attached_characters and fall back to the single-unit check with starting strength = models.size() = 1. A full-wound Captain is therefore neither below nor at half, and CommandPhase._identify_units_needing_tests (411/421) does not queue him for a test.

Kill credit: ShootingPhase._check_kill_diffs (297-350) and FightPhase._check_kill_diffs (393-440) treat the all-dead bodyguard dict as a destroyed unit. They call SecondaryMissionManager.check_and_report_unit_destroyed ('enemy_unit_destroyed' VP) and MissionManager.record_unit_destroyed (kill count), and neither groups attached units together. The leader's later death is counted again.

Two leaders: CharacterAttachmentManager:121 allows two leaders for units with the 'Bodyguard' datasheet ability (Ork Boyz; Kroot Carnivores' data names the exact 'become separate units at their original Starting Strengths' clause). That is the commentary's case, and the engine splits the two leaders into separate units.

Docs: ISS-059 only relies on 'an all-characters remnant detaches to its own profile' for Toughness, which 19.02 already covers without a detach. docs/rules/11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:165 says 'Attached unit stays a unit', which the code contradicts.

Minor inaccuracies in the claim: the GameState line range should be 1330-1394, and kill credit also comes from FightPhase, SecondaryMissionManager and the allocation overlay. The fix sketch wrongly includes Deadly Demise: 11e 24.08 triggers it per destroyed model.

## EA-12 · 21.03 FAQ (take to the skies effects only when declared) — FLY units get take-to-the-skies benefits on normal/advance/fall-back moves without declaring it

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** The take-to-the-skies effects (move through all models incl. enemy and MONSTER/VEHICLE, ignore vertical distance, cross all terrain) apply only to a FLYING unit that declared it when selected to move. The cost is -2" (0 with HOVER).
- **Engine:** Only the 13.06 dense-terrain gate reads move_data.took_to_skies (MovementPhase.gd:7165). The enemy-base and MONSTER/VEHICLE-base crossing checks skip any unit with the FLY keyword (MovementPhase.gd:759, 766, 893, 900, 8145, 8152, 8232, 8239, 6540-6556). _get_movement_terrain_penalty returns 0 for every FLY unit (7361-7362), so it pays no difficult-ground or climb cost and accrues no vertical distance toward [HEAVY]'s 3".
- **Scenario:** Deffkoptas make a normal move straight through an enemy Guard screen without ticking 'Take to the skies'. The engine accepts it at full M; 11e only allows moving through enemy models if they take to the skies (-2"). A FLY unit with [HEAVY] weapons climbing a ruin floor also books 0" vertical, so it keeps [HEAVY].
- **Fix sketch:** Replace the _unit_has_fly_keyword(unit_id) exemptions in the crossing checks and in _get_movement_terrain_penalty with the declared move_data.took_to_skies.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** In 11e, a FLY unit that has not declared take to the skies is still exempt from the model-crossing checks. MovementPhase._unit_has_fly_keyword (7127) is a plain keyword test, and it skips the enemy-base check on normal/advance moves (762, 896, 6584, 8177, 8264) and the MONSTER/VEHICLE-base check on every move, Fall Back included (769, 903, 6586, 8184, 8271). In the phase, move_data.took_to_skies is read only by the 13.06 dense gate (7196). So Deffkoptas (MOUNTED + FLY, no VEHICLE) can make a normal move through an enemy infantry screen at full M without the 21.03 -2". _get_movement_terrain_penalty also returns 0 for every FLY unit (7392), so non-INFANTRY FLY units that have not taken to the skies skip the vertical climb cost when they end on a light or low (2" or less) feature. That understates moved_max_inches for [HEAVY] in that narrow case. The difficult-ground half of that skip is not a deviation, because 11e has no difficult ground. Severity is medium, not high: ticking the existing checkbox gives the legal version at -2" (0 with HOVER). Fix: make the crossing exemptions and the climb-cost skip read move_data.took_to_skies (at edition 11) instead of the FLY keyword.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the core of the claim. Static reading only; I did not run the game.

Rules:
- Launch 03.01 (core_rules.txt:385-393): a model's base cannot be moved through enemy models unless a rule says otherwise.
- 21.03 (3363-3375): the fly effects apply only to a FLYING unit whose controller declares take to the skies ("If it does, while resolving that move..."). Those effects are -2", ignore vertical distance, and move through all models (enemy and MONSTER/VEHICLE included) and all terrain.
- FAQ 21.03 (errata_faq.md:679-682) is about when to declare: when the unit is selected to move, and before the advance/charge roll. So the "only when declared" rule comes from the 21.03 launch text, not from the FAQ.
- 17.01 does not rescue the scenario. Deffkoptas are MOUNTED + FLY, not VEHICLE (Datasheets_keywords.csv:96-100; 40k/armies/recon_stomps.json and ork11e_codex_test.json).
- The default edition is 11 (scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27).

Engine:
- `_unit_has_fly_keyword` (MovementPhase.gd:7127-7132) is a plain keyword test. It does not check took_to_skies or the edition.
- That test exempts the unit from the enemy-base and MONSTER/VEHICLE crossing checks in SET_MODEL_DEST (762, 769), STAGE_MODEL_MOVE (896, 903; this is the action the drag UI sends), `_ork11e_translation_legal` (6571, 6584-6586), group validation (8177, 8184) and `_validate_individual_move_internal` (8264, 8271).
- In the phase, only the 13.06 dense gate (7196) reads move_data.took_to_skies. The comment at 7221 says the model-crossing checks also read it; they do not.
- `_get_movement_terrain_penalty` returns 0 for any FLY unit (7392-7393), which also skips the vertical climb cost. Since model_distances includes the penalty (3121-3123) and moved_max_inches is built from it (3456-3462), [HEAVY]'s 3" test can leak too.
- The UI checkbox only sends SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES. Drops still go through STAGE_MODEL_MOVE, so an unticked FLY unit can be dragged through an enemy screen at full M.

No ruling keeps this behaviour. RULINGS.md has nothing on it. ISSUES.md ISS-061 says the took_to_skies pathing leniency was "deferred". tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:230 lists it as report-only. research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md:2871 calls it "a 10e carry-over that is more permissive than 21.03". The tests that pin it are 10e-era: unit/test_monster_vehicle_movement_blocking.gd:165-187 and test_t103_vertical_climb_cost.gd:54.

Where the claim is overstated:
1. Difficult ground. 11e 13.06 has no difficult ground, so FLY paying no difficult-ground cost is correct. The real problem there is the 10e 2" penalty still charged to non-FLY units (TerrainManager.gd:627-660, a separate issue, TER-02). Only the skipped climb cost breaks 21.03.
2. [HEAVY] "climbing a ruin floor" is mostly not reachable:
   - INFANTRY FLY units never pay a climb in this engine (7436-7441).
   - Non-INFANTRY FLY units that did not take to the skies are refused entry to dense ruins over 2" by the 13.06 gate.
   - Units that took to the skies are correctly charged 0, which matches the [HEAVY] FAQ.
   - So the leak is limited to non-INFANTRY FLY units that end inside a feature they cannot move through but the gate allows: light features, or dense features of 2" or less.
3. Fall Back. The enemy-base check runs only for NORMAL/ADVANCE, for every unit, so on fall-back the FLY exemption only matters for the MONSTER/VEHICLE check. Desperate Escape legitimately allows moving through enemy models anyway.
4. Severity. The legal route (ticking the box, which the UI offers) gives the same crossing at -2". So the net gain is 2" of move for non-HOVER FLY units, nothing for HOVER units on crossing, plus skipped climb costs. Medium is more accurate than high.

Aside, not part of this claim: the issue the FAQ actually covers, declaring before the advance roll, also looks open. The ADVANCE active_move is created with mode_locked false (around line 1567), so the checkbox stays visible. `_validate_set_take_to_skies` (7240-7244) accepts ADVANCE after the roll has been made.

## EA-13 · 21.03 FAQ (take to the skies on charge moves) — Charges have no take-to-the-skies declaration or -2"; FLY chargers get 10e fly benefits automatically

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Taking to the skies applies 'when that unit is selected to make a normal / advance / fall-back / charge move ... you must declare that a unit will take to the skies before rolling for that advance/charge move'. While it does: -2" to the maximum distance (0 with HOVER), ignore vertical distance, move through all models and terrain.
- **Engine:** There is no take-to-the-skies state in ChargePhase.gd or ChargeMove11e.gd; MoveType.take_to_skies_modifier is only called from MovementPhase. The full 2D6 roll is the maximum distance and the target ceiling, min(12, roll) (ChargeMove11e.gd:74-107). Meanwhile any FLY charger skips the solid-terrain path sweep (ChargePhase.gd:2241, 2314) and pays the 10e diagonal climb instead of ignoring vertical distance (TerrainManager.gd:540-551; no difficult ground, 566).
- **Scenario:** Jump-pack Assault Intercessors roll a 7 against a target 7" away behind a dense ruin wall. 11e: they must take to the skies, so the maximum is 5" and the target is not selectable, or they must path around the wall. The engine lets them select it and pass through the wall. Over a 6"-tall ruin the engine adds a ~5-6" diagonal climb where 11e costs only the flat 2".
- **Fix sketch:** Add a take_to_skies flag to DECLARE_CHARGE, with a UI tick before CHARGE_ROLL. When set and the unit lacks HOVER (check the ability name as well as the keyword), use roll-2 for selectable_distance_ceiling and the move cap, zero the vertical and difficult-ground penalties, and allow crossing terrain and models. When not set, treat FLY chargers like any other unit (solid sweep and normal climb costs).
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/ChargeMove11e.gd`, `40k/autoloads/TerrainManager.gd`, `40k/scripts/ChargeController.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Charges have no 21.03 take-to-the-skies declaration. ChargePhase.gd, ChargeController.gd and ChargeMove11e.gd never offer or read it, and MoveType.take_to_skies_modifier is called only from MovementPhase. So the -2" never applies to a charge (0" with HOVER), and per FAQ 02.02 that reduction should also limit target selection (FAQ 21.03: declared before the roll). Madcap Artillery "grounded" is also ignored for charges. Instead, every FLY charger silently gets 10e-style flying: it skips the 13.06 solid-terrain sweep (ChargePhase.gd:2241/2314) and pays a diagonal climb, sqrt(h^2 + c^2) - c, where h is the terrain height and c the horizontal crossing distance (TerrainManager.gd:577-582). Meanwhile the full roll stays the move cap and the min(12, roll) target ceiling. The Movement phase is different: there, FLY without the declaration is blocked by the 13.06 gate (MovementPhase.gd:7193-7213). This changes outcomes only for FLY units without INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE, such as Land Speeder, Storm Speeders, Caladius Grav-tank, Deffkoptas and Dawneagle Jetbike. Example: a Land Speeder rolls 8 against a target 8" away behind a 5"-tall dense ruin wall.
