In 11e it must either take to the skies (maximum 6", so the target cannot be selected) or climb over or go around without the -2". In the engine it selects the target and crosses the wall for the diagonal cost, with no declaration and no -2". INFANTRY FLY units such as jump-pack Assault Intercessors are handled correctly: 13.06 lets them go through dense walls without taking to the skies, and the engine charges them no climb there. The difficult-ground exemption matches 11e, which has no difficult ground. Model pass-through is not FLY-specific, because charge paths are checked only at their end positions for every unit. Severity: medium.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the core of the claim. The scenario and the severity are wrong, though.

What the rules say:
- 21.03 (launch text lines 3363-3375): taking to the skies is an optional declaration on a normal, advance, fall-back or charge move. It costs -2" of maximum distance and grants: ignore vertical distance, move through all models and all terrain.
- FAQ 21.03 (errata lines 681-682): for a charge, it must be declared before the charge roll.
- FAQ 02.02 (errata lines 160-161): charge targets must be within the reduced distance (a roll of 9 lets you select targets within 7").
- 24.17 HOVER (launch lines 3771-3780): HOVER units do not take the -2".
- No RULINGS.md entry covers 21.03 for charges.

What the engine does (confirmed):
1. No charge-phase declaration exists. Nothing in ChargePhase.gd, ChargeController.gd or ChargeMove11e.gd reads or offers take to the skies. MoveType.take_to_skies_modifier is called only from MovementPhase (lines 1196, 1486, 2872, 7297).
2. The full roll, after modifiers, is both the move cap and the target ceiling: min(12, roll) (ChargePhase.gd 663-687 and 846-874; ChargeMove11e.gd 74-107). The -2" never applies to a charge.
3. Every FLY charger skips the 13.06 solid-terrain path sweep (ChargePhase.gd lines 2241 and 2314).
4. FLY chargers that cannot traverse a terrain piece pay a 10e-style diagonal climb (TerrainManager.gd 577-582, inside calculate_charge_terrain_penalty at 546).
5. This contradicts the engine's own Movement phase. There, a FLY unit without took_to_skies is refused by the 13.06 dense-terrain gate and told to tick "Take to the skies" (MovementPhase.gd 7193-7213).
6. The gap is already on record and has no fix:
   - ISS-061's DONE status covers only BEGIN_NORMAL_MOVE, BEGIN_ADVANCE and BEGIN_FALL_BACK.
   - tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:230 (CH-12) lists it as an unfixed engine-vs-rules defect.
   - No later commit addresses it.
7. Extra consequence: Madcap Artillery "grounded" lasts until the start of the Ork player's next turn, so it covers the grounded unit's own turn. OrkAbilities11e.cannot_take_to_skies is checked only in MovementPhase, so a grounded FLY unit still gets the FLY charge exemption.

What is overstated:
- The scenario is wrong. Assault Intercessors with Jump Packs carry FLY, INFANTRY and JUMP PACK (sm11e_codex_test.json). Under 13.06 (launch lines 2163-2181), INFANTRY move horizontally through dense terrain without taking to the skies. So selecting a target 7" away on a roll of 7 and charging through a ruin wall is legal in 11e.
- The engine gets that case right. can_move_through_11e passes INFANTRY (TerrainManager.gd 1380-1385). In the charge penalty, the unit_can_traverse branch (558/567, via _passes_dense_feature_11e at 1433) means INFANTRY pay no climb on dense pieces, so the "~5-6" diagonal" does not happen for them.
- The "no difficult ground" point is not an 11e deviation, because 11e has no difficult ground at all.
- Charge-path model pass-through is not checked for any charger: only end positions are checked for overlaps (ChargePhase.gd 2970-3034). FLY therefore gains nothing special there.
- The TerrainManager line numbers have drifted: the diagonal is now at 577-582 and the difficult-ground exemption at 603.
- The real impact is limited to FLY units without INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE crossing dense features taller than 2", plus the missing optional declaration. Medium severity, matching CH-12's earlier rating.

## EA-14 · 23.04 FAQ ([HAZARDOUS] mortal wounds; same as 06.03 FAQ) — Hazardous mortal wounds forced onto the hazardous-weapon bearer, no spill-over

- **Severity:** high · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Failed hazard rolls from [HAZARDOUS] weapons: 'No, you allocate the mortal wounds to the unit as per Mortal Wounds (06.02).' Each mortal wound goes to a wounded non-CHARACTER model first, else any non-CHARACTER, else CHARACTERs, and removes 1 wound, spilling to the next model.
- **Engine:** resolve_hazardous_check picks one model via _find_hazardous_allocation_target (RulesEngine.gd:11672-11712). Priority is wounded hazardous bearer, then non-CHARACTER bearer, then CHARACTER bearer, applied at 11545. All mortal wounds land on that model via min(actual_mw, current_wounds) (11573) and the excess is discarded ('no spillover per Balance Dataslate'). Every shooting and fight path calls it (ShootingPhase.gd:1964/3127/7348, FightPhase.gd:1962).
- **Scenario:** A 10-model Guard squad with one plasma gun fails a hazard roll. The engine kills the plasma gunner; under 06.02 the owner removes a lasgun trooper and keeps the plasma gun. Hellblasters (2W) failing 3 hazard rolls: the engine puts all 3 MW on one model, kills it and loses the third; 11e kills one and wounds a second.
- **Fix sketch:** At edition 11, allocate the hazard mortal wounds with Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e over the (attached) unit, built via _build_attached_allocation_unit_11e with the defender/CasualtyPreference order. Roll FNP per wound with mortal_wounds_after_fnp. Keep the bearer-first priority for 10e only.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/Allocation.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with updated references. At edition 11, RulesEngine.resolve_hazardous_check (RulesEngine.gd:11609) uses the 11e band (1-2 fails, line 11637) and 1 MW per failure (3 if every model is MONSTER/VEHICLE, lines 11689-11691). It then still assigns the wounds the 10e/Balance Dataslate way, with no edition check. _find_hazardous_allocation_target (11825-11870) picks the wounded bearer, then a non-CHARACTER bearer, then a CHARACTER bearer, then the first alive model. All wounds go on that one model via min(actual_mw, current_wounds) (11726) and the excess is discarded. Only the firing unit's own models are considered (11684-11686), not the whole Attached unit. Every [HAZARDOUS] path calls it: RulesEngine.gd 870/13604/13818, ShootingPhase.gd 1966/3129/7375, FightPhase.gd 1962, MovementPhase.gd 6242 (snap shooting / Fire Overwatch). Under the 06.03/23.04 FAQ and 06.02, each hazard mortal wound should go through the unit's 06.02 order with carry-over, as the engine already does for desperate escape, combat and emergency disembark, and codex hazard rolls via Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e. Effects: a single-special-weapon squad always loses the special-weapon bearer, and multi-wound units lose overflow mortal wounds that should wound a second model.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: the launch text 06.03 says a failed hazard roll means 'that unit suffers 1 mortal wound (see above)', pointing to 06.02. Both FAQ entries (06.03 and 23.04 in docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md) say the wounds are NOT forced onto the [HAZARDOUS] bearers and are allocated to the unit per 06.02. 06.02 picks a model for each mortal wound separately (wounded non-CHARACTER, then any non-CHARACTER, then CHARACTERs), so damage carries over to the next model. In 11e only [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] 24.10 and Deadly Demise say leftover mortal wounds are lost; hazards have no such exception.

Engine: RulesEngine.resolve_hazardous_check (RulesEngine.gd 11609-11777) only checks the edition for the fail band (line 11637) and the wounds per failure (11689-11691); that is the delta-audit A2 fix. Allocation has no 11e branch. _find_hazardous_allocation_target (11825-11870) picks the wounded bearer, then a non-CHARACTER bearer, then a CHARACTER bearer, then the first alive model. All wounds land on that one model via damage_to_apply = min(actual_mw, current_wounds) (11726) and the rest are dropped (comment at 11710: 'no spillover per Balance Dataslate v3.3'). Only the firing unit's own models are considered (11684-11686).

Callers: every [HAZARDOUS] path goes through this function with no 11e alternative: RulesEngine.gd 870/13604/13818, ShootingPhase.gd 1966/3129/7375, FightPhase.gd 1962, and MovementPhase.gd 6242 (snap shooting / Fire Overwatch). No override exists in EffectPrimitives, AbilityRegistry, CasualtyPreference or the codex scripts. No RULINGS entry backs bearer-first; RULINGS:156 sends Supa-hazardous wounds 'to the bearer's unit (as [HAZARDOUS])'. tests/unit/test_hazardous_keyword.gd pins bearer-first only in fixtures where every model carries the weapon.

The engine already does this correctly elsewhere: desperate escape, combat disembark and emergency disembark (MovementPhase 2950/9079, TransportManager 628, SMCodexAMovement 645) and the Ork codex hazard rolls (Supa-hazardous, Try Dat Button!, Shokk-boosta) all use Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e or a unit-wide allocation with carry-over. So the [HAZARDOUS] weapon path was only half moved to 11e.

Both scenarios hold. Guard squad: the plasma gunner is the only non-CHARACTER bearer, takes the 1 MW and dies. Hellblasters: 3 MW, min(3, 2) = 2, one dies and the third is lost; under 06.02 one dies and a second is wounded. The claim's line numbers are stale and it misses the MovementPhase snap-shot caller.

## EA-15 · 01.02.02 Persisting Rules Effects — MORTIS SNARES snare stays active until something triggers it instead of ending with its trigger period

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.02: 'If you have a rule that specifies when it is triggered ... and it does not specify how long it is active for, it is only active for the duration of that specified period ... After that period, that effect is no longer applicable/active.' MORTIS SNARES has WHEN: 'End of your Movement phase' and gives the snare no duration.
- **Engine:** SMCodexACommand.apply stores the objective in state.factions[p].sm11e_snared_objectives (SMCodexACommand.gd:172-185). Nothing ever expires it: clear() deliberately keeps it ('The snare persists until an enemy unit triggers it', SMCodexACommand.gd:217-220). snare_detect (SMCodexACommand.gd:383-427, hooked in BasePhase.gd:247-255) fires on any later enemy move, in any phase and any turn, until it triggers. This is recorded as a ruling in RULINGS.md:402.
- **Scenario:** Phobos Shadow Force: at the end of the Space Marine Movement phase, Infiltrators snare the objective they control. In the opponent's next Movement phase a Boyz unit ends a normal move within range of it. The engine rolls a D6 and on a 2+ the Boyz take D6 mortal wounds. Under 01.02.02 the snare stopped being active at the end of the Space Marine Movement phase, so no roll happens.
- **Fix sketch:** Clear factions[p].sm11e_snared_objectives (and the unit marker) when the Movement phase it was used in ends (e.g. SMCodex11e on_phase_transition with old_phase == MOVEMENT). Update RULINGS.md:402 and the AI heuristic that uses it whenever an objective qualifies. If 'until triggered' is a deliberate house ruling, record it explicitly as a deviation from Commentary 01.02.02 instead.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexACommand.gd`, `40k/phases/BasePhase.gd`, `.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md`
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: Commentary 01.02.02 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:28) says: "If you have a rule that specifies when it is triggered ... and it does not specify how long it is active for, it is only active for the duration of that specified period ... After that period, that effect is no longer applicable/active." The launch text (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:3024, :3253) only has "► Persisting Rules Effects" sidebar pointers, and 15.01 (:2485-2512) sets no default duration for stratagems. The MORTIS SNARES text (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/space-marines/wahapedia/Stratagems.csv:92; curated scripts/40kdc/curated/space_marines_codex_11e.json:473-490, where "duration_phrase" is null) is "WHEN: End of your Movement phase" with no "until ...". The Space Marines codex source has no errata date or FAQ (Source.csv). When GW wants an objective state to last, it says so, e.g. Vowed Target: "your vowed objective until your next Command phase".

ENGINE (all confirmed):
- SMCodexACommand.gd:171-185 apply() writes state.factions[p].sm11e_snared_objectives.
- clear() at :219-221 (the claim says :217-220, a small offset) removes only the unit marker. Its comment reads "The snare persists until an enemy unit triggers it".
- The only other writer is snare_detect (:383-427), which removes the objective once it triggers. snare_detect is called from BasePhase.gd:247-255 after every outermost action, in any phase.
- No SMCodex11e phase-transition hook clears it. state.factions is only reset at army load or new game (ArmyListManager.gd:390, StateSerializer.gd:180).
- Tests pin this behaviour on purpose: test_sm11e_stratagems_a.gd:886 checks "the snare outlasts the phase". Windowed scenario tests/scenarios/sp/sm11e_mortis_snares.json snares at the end of P1's Movement phase, then triggers it during the Ork player's Movement phase, which is the claim's scenario exactly.
- The AI (ai_score :269-273) spends the CP whenever it controls an objective with an enemy within 24".

REFUTATION ATTEMPTS FAILED:
- RULINGS.md:402 (commit 968d38f, 2026-10-23, before the errata extract) says "the snare has no printed duration — it lasts until it triggers". That concedes the exact case 01.02.02 covers, then draws the opposite conclusion. It never cites 01.02.02 and doesn't record a deliberate deviation.
- The engine's own convention for effects with no printed duration is end of phase (RULINGS.md:390, "no duration printed — the engine default"; also :271). The snare is the outlier.
- No RULINGS, docs or git entry mentions 01.02.02.
- An independent same-day research note (research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07.json:4305, :8912) reached the same conclusion.
- The only counter-argument is designer intent: reading "That objective is no longer snared" as an implied until-triggered duration, since otherwise the stratagem is nearly useless. That clause is a one-shot end condition, not a stated duration, and the developers never recorded such a reading.

NUANCE FOR THE FIX: under 01.02.02 the snare stays live for the whole end-of-Movement-phase step. Rapid Ingress (15.07, "End of your opponent's Movement phase", core rules :2714-2722) could still trigger it there, though the >8" set-up restriction makes that rare. So it should expire when that Movement phase ends, after the movement_phase_end window, not when the stratagem resolves. The claim's fix sketch already says this. The claim is accurate and not overstated.

## EA-16 · 01.02.02 Persisting Rules Effects — Da Jump's granted Deep Strike lasts across turns until the unit arrives

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.02: 'If a rule or effect is given to a unit and it does not specify how long it is active for, it is only active for the phase in which it was given.' Da Jump: 'In your Movement phase ... Place this unit in strategic reserves. This unit has Deep Strike.' (no duration).
- **Engine:** OrkAbilities11e sets ork11e_ab_da_jump_ds on every component (OrkAbilities11e.gd:2110-2123). It clears the flag only once the unit is no longer IN_RESERVES (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1335-1337), and GameState.unit_has_deep_strike honours it until then (GameState.gd:550-553). Da Jump is also offered in the movement_phase_end window (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1398-1399), when the unit can no longer arrive that phase. This is recorded as a ruling in RULINGS.md:183.
- **Scenario:** A Weirdboy with Boyz uses Da Jump in the end-of-Movement-phase window of round 2. In round 3 the engine lets the unit Deep Strike anywhere more than 9" from enemies. Under 01.02.02 Deep Strike lapsed at the end of round 2's Movement phase, so the unit must make a normal ingress move: wholly within 6" of a battlefield edge and more than 8" from enemies (20.04).
- **Fix sketch:** Expire ork11e_ab_da_jump_ds at the end of the Movement phase it was granted in (OrkAbilities11e.on_phase_transition with old_phase == PH_MOVEMENT). The start-of-phase use still lets the unit Deep Strike back in the same Move Units step. Update RULINGS.md:183 and the AI's Da Jump timing.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/orks/OrkAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/autoloads/GameState.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real. Under Commentary 01.02.02, the Deep Strike that Da Jump grants ("In your Movement phase ... This unit has Deep Strike", no duration) lasts only until the end of the Movement phase it was used in. The engine follows RULINGS.md:183, a 2026-10-18 ruling that predates the errata doc. It keeps ork11e_ab_da_jump_ds until the unit leaves reserves (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1335-1337; GameState.unit_has_deep_strike at GameState.gd:552-553). So a Da Jumped unit that does not arrive in that same phase keeps Deep Strike. That covers three cases: Da Jump used in the movement_phase_end window (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1399, opened just before phase_completed in MovementPhase._complete_movement_phase), Da Jump used in round 1 (20.03 bars arrival before round 2), or a unit simply left in reserves. Such a unit can later use Deep Strike placement in a later own Movement phase or via RAPID INGRESS (MovementPhase.gd:2389). What it should get is a normal ingress (20.04): wholly within 6" of a battlefield edge, more than 8" from enemies, and outside the opponent's deployment zone before round 3. The leftover Deep Strike wrongly removes the edge requirement (and the deployment-zone ban before round 3); the enemy distance is 8" either way at 11e (24.09; IngressMove.gd:53), not 9". Only human players are affected, because the AI never uses Da Jump (ai_score 0.0). Fix: clear the flag when leaving PH_MOVEMENT in OrkAbilities11e.on_phase_transition and update RULINGS.md:183. A start-of-phase Da Jump in round 2+ can still Deep Strike back in the same Move Units step.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute the claim. Rule: the codex Da Jump text (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/orks/wahapedia/Datasheets_abilities.csv:23) is "In your Movement phase ... Place this unit in strategic reserves. This unit has Deep Strike." It states no duration. Commentary 01.02.02 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:28) says a rule that names its trigger ("In the Shooting phase") but no duration is active only for that period, and an effect given to a unit with no duration is active only for the phase it was given in. Nothing extends it: the persisting-effects carry-over into reserves covers only effects that have a duration, and 20.02 Repositioned Units (core_rules.txt:3227-3230) keeps only effects with "a specified duration or under specified circumstances". When the codex wants a granted Deep Strike to last, it says so (Tellyporta: "Until the end of the battle"; orks/RULES.md:174). Engine: the claim's citations are accurate. OrkAbilities11e.gd:2121-2122 sets ork11e_ab_da_jump_ds on every component. Its only clear is :1335-1337, when the unit is no longer IN_RESERVES (called from PhaseManager.gd:159); no other code clears it. GameState.gd:552-553 honours the flag, and the DS placement is then offered by MovementPhase.gd:7840 / :4262, Rapid Ingress at :2389, and MovementController.gd:1263. Da Jump is offered in movement_phase_end (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1399), and MovementPhase._complete_movement_phase (:5826-5858) opens that window right before phase_completed, so a unit Da Jumped there cannot arrive that phase. Other ways the flag outlives the phase: a round-1 use (20.03 bars arrival before round 2), a unit left in reserves, and Rapid Ingress in the opponent's turn. Ruling: RULINGS.md:183 ("lasts until the unit arrives ... the rule exists to bring the unit back") was committed 2026-10-18, before the errata doc landed (d97882c, 2026-11-07), and does not cite 01.02.02. Its premise is also wrong: from round 2 a start-of-phase Da Jump can Deep Strike back in the same Move Units step (09.02 lets a reserves unit not yet selected be selected; the engine has no same-turn arrival block). No test checks persistence past the phase: test_ork_abilities_11e.gd _section_da_jump only checks that the flag clears after arrival. Overstated details: 11e Deep Strike is "more than 8"" (24.09; IngressMove.gd:53; GameConstants.gd:50), not 9". The AI never uses Da Jump (ai_score 0.0 at OrkAbilities11e.gd:2325), so there is no AI timing to update. Caveat: the Orks faction pack v1.2 PDF named in Source.csv is not in the repo, so a faction FAQ could not be ruled out; nothing in the repo suggests one exists.

## EA-17 · 01.02.03 Revived and Adding Models to a Unit — Models revived by healing reappear where they died, ignoring the 01.02.03 set-up rules

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.03: 'Models returned to a unit on the battlefield must be set up as follows: They must be set up in coherency with models in that unit that started that phase on the battlefield. They can be set up engaged with one or more enemy units, but only if those enemy units were already engaged with the unit...'
- **Engine:** The generic heal_diffs helpers revive a destroyed model by setting alive=true and current_wounds=1, leaving it at its stored death position (positions are kept on death, e.g. RulesEngine.gd:2061-2066). There is no coherency, overlap, board-edge or engagement check: OrkAbilities11e.gd:1196-1242 (Crude Surgery, Clever Know-wotz), SWAbilities11e.gd:1699-1745 (Healing Balms), SMAbilitiesA.gd:2052-2098 (Helix Gauntlet), SMAbilitiesC.gd:1638-1688, SMDetachmentsB.gd:682-725 (Narthecis Gauntlet). Only DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS uses the compliant placement (OrkCodexMovement.gd:953-1131). These loops also don't skip CHARACTER models (02.02.04).
- **Scenario:** A Painboy leads 10 Boyz (W1). Fire Overwatch kills 3 Boyz standing on an objective, then the mob charges 9" away and consolidates. In the next Ork Command phase, Crude Surgery heals 3 with every surviving model at full wounds. The engine revives the 3 Boyz back on the old objective: out of coherency with the mob, adding OC there, or overlapping a model that now stands on that spot. Under 01.02.03 they must be set up in coherency with the mob's current models and may not newly engage an enemy unit.
- **Fix sketch:** Route the revive branch of every heal_diffs through the existing OrkCodexMovement.heal_unit_diffs/_revive_position logic (or a shared helper). It finds a spot in coherency with models that started the phase on the battlefield, with no overlap, walls or off-board placement and no new engagement; if there is no legal spot the wound is lost. Also skip CHARACTER models (02.02.04).
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/orks/OrkAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesA.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMDetachmentsB.gd`, `40k/scripts/stratagems/ork_codex/OrkCodexMovement.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with three corrections. Every heal helper except DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS revives a destroyed model where it died: OrkAbilities11e.heal_diffs (Crude Surgery, Fix Dat Armour Up, Push Dat Bit Back In, Clever Know-wotz), SWAbilities11e (Healing Balms, Gift of the Iron Wolf), SMAbilitiesA (Helix Gauntlet, Master of the Forge, Blessings of the Omnissiah), SMAbilitiesC (Narthecium) and SMDetachmentsB (Narthecis Gauntlet). Each only sets alive=true and current_wounds=1 and leaves the stored death position. That breaks 01.02.03 in three ways: the model need not be in coherency with the unit's current models, it can overlap a model that has since moved onto that spot, and it can newly engage an enemy unit. Until then it also counts for OC at every phase end (MissionManager recomputes control each phase at 11e), and it can shoot or be targeted from that spot. Corrections: (1) The missing board-edge and wall checks don't matter, because the death spot was legal when the model died. (2) The engine's end-of-turn coherency removal (PhaseManager._enforce_coherency_11e) later destroys out-of-coherency models, but only at the end of the turn, and it never catches overlap or new engagement. (3) The CHARACTER problem (02.02.04) is real but narrower than stated: the loops visit the bodyguard first, so a dead CHARACTER is only revived once no dead non-CHARACTER model is left. For example, Push Dat Bit Back In on a Beast Snagga unit whose only casualty is its Beastboss brings him back. Only OrkCodexMovement.heal_unit_diffs/_revive_position, used by DES alone, does the placement correctly and skips CHARACTER models.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. The rule and the code both check out.

Rule: 01.02.03 is in the launch text (core_rules.txt about lines 4105-4129, "REVIVED") and in the errata commentary file (line 32). Returned models must be set up in coherency with models that started the phase on the battlefield, and may only be engaged with enemies already engaged with the unit. 02.02.04 (errata file line 177) says a heal revives a destroyed model "(excluding CHARACTER models)" with 1 wound. The heal texts all trigger this: Crude Surgery, Helix Gauntlet, Narthecium, Healing Balms and Narthecis Gauntlet each say "this unit heals N wounds" in the 11e codex CSVs.

Engine:
- Death keeps the position. RulesEngine.gd:2061-2066 (also 5962 and 9753) only sets alive=false.
- The five helpers sit at exactly the cited lines: OrkAbilities11e.gd:1196, SWAbilities11e.gd:1699, SMAbilitiesA.gd:2052, SMAbilitiesC.gd:1638, SMDetachmentsB.gd:682. Each revive only writes current_wounds=1 and alive=true. None writes a position or checks coherency, overlap or engagement, and none skips CHARACTER. The Ork helper's only skip, the ork11e_never_revive flag, is never set anywhere.
- Nothing fixes the position later. GameState.apply_state_changes has no hook for a model coming back, and nothing reads the "revived" counts except log text.
- The compliant OrkCodexMovement.heal_unit_diffs / _revive_position is only called by DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS (lines 916 and 935). Commit c91e22e fixed DES alone.
- RULINGS.md:179/320/375 describe only which models heal first, nothing about placement. The Ork heal test checks only alive counts. RulesEngine.apply_heal_11e (revives in place) is called only from tests. StratagemManager's COME ON LADZ! revives at the death spot as a stated simplification, but it is a 10e stratagem that is not in the 11e Ork CSVs.
- The OC impact is real: MissionManager._on_phase_completed_11e recomputes objective control at the end of every phase at 11e.

Overstatements in the claim:
1. The missing board-edge and wall checks don't matter in practice. The stored death spot was a legal place on the board when the model died.
2. There is a partial backstop the claim doesn't mention. PhaseManager._enforce_coherency_11e destroys out-of-coherency models at the end of the turn. Until then the revived models count for OC at each phase end and can shoot or be shot from the old spot. Overlap and new engagement are never caught.
3. The CHARACTER problem is narrower than stated. attached_group_ids puts the bodyguard first, so a dead CHARACTER only comes back once no dead non-CHARACTER model is left. Example: Push Dat Bit Back In on a Beast Snagga unit whose only casualty is its Beastboss brings him back at 1 W, then heals him to 3 W.

## EA-18 · 01.02.06 Splitting Units — Splitting Kommandos with an attached leader (Taktikal Know-wotz) copies the leader into both halves

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.06 errata: 'A unit must be split into two units, each containing as equal a number of models as possible (when splitting a unit in this way, make a note of which models form each of the two new units). If a rule states a specific starting strength of the split units, but this is not possible ... (e.g. due to attached Leader/Support models), split the unit as stated above.'
- **Engine:** GameState.split_unit_at_deployment (GameState.gd:1122-1199) only accepts exactly 10 alive bodyguard models (GameState.gd:1154). It builds the second half as a deep copy of the source (GameState.gd:1164), which copies attachment_data.attached_characters, so both halves list the leader while the leader's attached_to still points at the first half. DeploymentController offers the split with no attachment check (DeploymentController.gd:511-547), and setup_placement_models reads the copied list for the second half (DeploymentController.gd:399-415). The Space Wolves splits correctly refuse while a leader is attached (SWAbilities11e.gd:1057).
- **Scenario:** Kommandos (always 10 models) are led by Boss Snikrot, attached in Declare Battle Formations. At deployment the player accepts the Taktikal Know-wotz split. The engine makes two 5-model halves and both carry Snikrot in attachment_data. GameState.get_attached_characters (GameState.gd:510-514) then counts him in both halves' combined models and half-strength checks, and he is offered for placement again with the second half. By the errata the 11 models split 6 (with Snikrot) and 5.
- **Fix sketch:** In split_unit_at_deployment, reset the copy's attachment_data to {attached_characters: []} and keep the leader with exactly one half (let the player choose which, as the errata's 'make a note' implies). Count leader models when splitting as evenly as possible. Alternatively, refuse the split while a leader is attached, as SWAbilities11e.split_block_reason does.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/GameState.gd`, `40k/scripts/DeploymentController.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** The claim is correct, apart from where Snikrot must go and two line ranges.

What the errata requires: when Kommandos (10 models) with Boss Snikrot or a Kill Kommanda bearer attached are split by Taktikal Know-wotz, 01.02.06 splits the whole 11-model Attached unit 6/5. The player notes which models form each half, so the leader is in exactly one half, either the 6 or the 5.

What the engine does:
- DeploymentController._maybe_offer_combat_squad_split (DeploymentController.gd:511-554) offers the split with no attachment check.
- GameState.split_unit_at_deployment (GameState.gd:1122-1209) splits only the 10 bodyguard models 5/5. It deep-copies the source (line 1164), so both halves keep attachment_data.attached_characters=[leader], while the leader's attached_to still names the first half.
- As a result, both halves count the leader in get_combined_models and in the half-strength checks (via get_attached_characters, GameState.gd:510-514).
- The second half's set-up folds the leader into placement again (setup_placement_models, DeploymentController.gd:399-415, does not filter deployed characters at deployment). COMPOSITE_DEPLOY then moves him with no validation, while he stays attached_to the first half.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute this and couldn't. Every step of the chain checks out in the code.

1) Rule. The 01.02.06 errata (errata_faq_commentary.md:44) adds the case "(e.g. due to attached Leader/Support models)" to stated-starting-strength splits, so an Attached unit can be split and the split is "as equal as possible". Taktikal Know-wotz (Orks_2000.json:1950) says "split this unit into two units, each with a starting strength of 5". The launch core text has no 01.02.06. RULINGS.md:187 only fixes the split timing at deployment; nothing in it covers attached leaders. research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07.json:2856 is just an AI note that already expects the 6/5 split with Snikrot.

2) Reachable. Boss Snikrot has leader_data.can_lead=['KOMMANDOS']. The Kill Kommanda enhancement also lets its bearer lead KOMMANDOS (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:16). Lone Operative does not block attachment (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:77, FormationsPhase.gd:211). FormationsPhase._build_formation_changes (1144-1163) writes attached_to and attachment_data.attached_characters before Deployment. Kommandos are always 9 + 1 Nob.

3) Split offered with no attachment check. Main.gd:9797/9933 calls begin_deploy, and begin_deploy calls _maybe_offer_combat_squad_split (DeploymentController.gd:276, 511-554). That function only counts the bodyguard's own 10 alive models (line 526) and the ability. It never looks at attachment_data.

4) Leader copied into both halves. split_unit_at_deployment (GameState.gd:1122-1209) requires exactly 10 alive models (line 1154). It builds the sibling with _deep_copy_dict(source) (line 1164), and _deep_copy_dict (1598) recurses into attachment_data. Only models, ids, flags and names are rewritten afterwards. So both halves list Snikrot, while his attached_to still names the first half. No code anywhere reconciles attached_to against attached_characters.

5) What follows:
- get_attached_characters (510-514) returns the list as stored. It feeds get_combined_models (1215), is_below_half_strength_combined (1339) and is_at_half_strength_combined (1404), so both halves count Snikrot.
- For the second half, setup_placement_models (DeploymentController.gd:399-415) only filters by status when only_reserves_characters is set, and that is false at deployment. So Snikrot is folded into that placement again.
- COMPOSITE_DEPLOY (DeploymentPhase.gd:864-887) writes his new position with no validation. He physically moves to whichever half deploys last, while still attached_to the first.
- No test covers this (test_ork_abilities_11e.gd:1266-1273 and the T-026 tests have no leader).

6) The Space Wolves split does refuse when a leader is attached (SWAbilities11e.gd:1057), so the Kommandos path is the outlier.

Minor overstatements:
- The errata does not put Snikrot in the 6-model half. The player notes which models form each half, so he can be in either: Snikrot + 5 Kommandos / 5 Kommandos, or Snikrot + 4 / 6.
- The cited line ranges are a little short.

## EA-19 · 01.04.05 Table Quarters — Table-quarter scoring tests model centres against shared centre lines, ignoring base size and the 1 mm dividing line

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.04.05: 'divide the battlefield with imaginary lines 1mm thick drawn lengthwise and widthwise through the centre of the battlefield to create four equal rectangles. These rectangles are the table quarters.' 01.04.01: 'A model is wholly within a specified distance if every part of its base is within that distance. A unit is wholly within if every model in that unit is wholly within.'
- **Engine:** SecondaryMissionManager._check_table_quarter_presence builds four rectangles that share the centre lines (SecondaryMissionManager.gd:1083-1088). Its 'wholly within this quarter' test, _is_unit_wholly_in_rect (SecondaryMissionManager.gd:2937-2949), checks only each model's centre point with inclusive bounds. Leader and bodyguard are judged as separate units. This feeds 11e Engage on All Fronts (SecondaryMissionData.gd:82-89) and the Reconnaissance Sweep primary (MissionManager.gd:2719-2724, PrimaryMissionData11e.gd:212-221).
- **Scenario:** End of turn with Engage on All Fronts: a Rhino holds an objective on the long midline, more than 6" from the centre, with its centre 1" left of the line and its hull crossing it. The engine counts it as wholly within the left quarter, which can make 3 quarters and score VP. By 01.04.01/01.04.05 its base crosses the 1 mm line, so it is wholly within no quarter. A one-model unit centred exactly on the line counts for both quarters.
- **Fix sketch:** Test every model's full base with Measurement.shape_wholly_in_polygon (rotation-aware, as the deployment and ingress checks do) against each quarter rectangle shrunk by 0.5 mm (about 0.02") on its centre-line sides. Evaluate an attached unit as one unit, requiring every component's models to be inside.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/SecondaryMissionManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/MissionManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Table-quarter presence (SecondaryMissionManager._check_table_quarter_presence plus _is_unit_wholly_in_rect, SecondaryMissionManager.gd:1083-1088 and 2937-2949) tests only model centres against closed rectangles that share the x=22 / y=30 centre lines. It ignores base size and the 1mm dividing line from 01.04.05, and it scores an attached Leader and its Bodyguard as separate units. Effects:
(a) Any model whose centre lies on one side of a centre line but whose base crosses it (or the 1mm band) counts as wholly within that quarter.
(b) A centre exactly on a line passes both neighbouring quarters.
(c) A Bodyguard wholly in one quarter counts even when its Leader straddles the line or stands in another quarter, and the Leader can add a second quarter.
The error only ever over-awards. It feeds 11e Engage on All Fronts (SecondaryMissionData.gd:82-89) and the Reconnaissance Sweep primary (MissionManager.gd:2719-2724).

Accurate examples: a single-model unit holding an 11e home objective set 0.25 inch off a quarter line, more than 6 inches from the centre (take_and_hold_vs_disruption_3.json obj_home_1 at (21.75, 13.5); take_and_hold_mirror_2.json obj_home_1 at (36.5, 29.75)), whose base overhangs the line. Or a Caladius Grav-Tank (95x150mm oval, at least 1.87 inch half-width) centred 1 inch from the line. The engine scores either as wholly within a quarter; by the rules it is in none.

The Rhino example does not reproduce with the engine's data: the shipped Rhino is a 32mm round base with no hull, so its centre 1 inch off the line does not cross it. The "both quarters" case needs an exact on-line float position, so it is rare. The AI mirror AIDecisionMaker._get_covered_quarters (line 24435) has the same centre-only flaw and should be fixed alongside.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule confirmed: errata 01.04.05 (40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:95-97) defines the quarters as four rectangles split by 1mm centre lines. 01.04.01 says a model is wholly within only if every part of its base is. The developers' own RULINGS.md:398 reads "wholly within" as "every point of every base". No ruling or doc exempts table quarters, and no test pins centre-only behaviour.

Engine confirmed at the cited lines. SecondaryMissionManager._check_table_quarter_presence (1083-1088) builds closed rectangles sharing x=22/y=30 on the 44x60 board (GameState.gd:56). _is_unit_wholly_in_rect (2937-2949) compares only each model's centre, with inclusive bounds. So a model whose centre is on one side but whose base crosses the line counts as wholly within that quarter, and a centre exactly on a line passes both neighbouring quarters. The base-aware helper Measurement.shape_wholly_in_polygon exists and is used by DeploymentPhase, ScoutPhase, IngressMove and DeploymentController, but not here.

Attached characters are separate unit entries (CharacterAttachmentManager: attached_to / attachment_data). The loop iterates GameState units one by one, with no merging (SecondaryMissionManager only mentions "attached" in its destroyed-unit checks). So a Leader and its Bodyguard are judged separately.

Both 11e paths go through this one function: Engage on All Fronts scoring_11e (SecondaryMissionData.gd:82-89, via _check_condition at :926) and Reconnaissance Sweep (MissionManager.gd:2719-2724, PrimaryMissionData11e.gd:217-218). No branch (hidden-followups, origin/main) differs on these files. The AI mirror _get_covered_quarters (AIDecisionMaker.gd:24435) is also centre-only.

Where the claim is overstated: the shipped Rhino (sm11e_codex_test.json U_RHINO_A) is a 32mm round base with no hull data, so "centre 1 inch off the line, hull crossing it" does not reproduce. In the 11e layouts the only objective exactly on a centre line is obj_center (22,30), which the 6 inch rule excludes, though several home objectives sit 0.25 inch off a line. The "counts for both quarters" case needs an exact float position on the line, so it is rare. The direction of error is over-awarding only.

## EA-20 · 01.05.02 — Wound re-rolls (Twin-linked, 'failed'/'all' grants) choose dice by raw roll vs the S/T threshold, ignoring wound modifiers and Anti-X crits

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.05.02: 're-rolls happen before modifiers (if any) are applied'. The die is re-rolled and then modified, but whether a wound roll failed is still decided by its modified result and the critical-wound check (05.02).
- **Engine:** apply_wound_modifiers re-rolls when the raw roll is below wound_threshold (RulesEngine.gd:776; REROLL_ONES_TWOS at :782 works the same way). It ignores the ±1 wound modifier and is never given the critical-wound threshold: AttackSequence.evaluate_wound_roll calls it without crit_threshold (AttackSequence.gd:66-67). The hit path was already fixed to use the post-modifier fail test and never re-roll a crit (RulesEngine.gd:683-701, r5:F6/F9). Twin-linked feeds the wound path in all three resolvers (RulesEngine.gd:3232-3234, 5333-5334, 14747-14748).
- **Scenario:** (1) A Brutalis Dreadnought fires its Twin Icarus Ironhail Heavy Stubber (S4, anti-fly 3+, twin-linked; armies/sm11e_codex_test.json) at a T5 FLY unit, so it wounds on 5+. Raw 3s and 4s are critical wounds, but the engine re-rolls them (raw < 5), so each certain crit becomes a 4-in-6 chance. (2) Twin-linked fire at a target with -1 to wound (e.g. Praesidium Shield, Hardy Bioniks): a raw 4 against 4+ fails after the modifier but is not re-rolled. (3) With +1 to wound (e.g. Lance on the charge), a raw 3 against 4+ already wounds, but the engine re-rolls it.
- **Fix sketch:** Pass crit_threshold from AttackSequence.evaluate_wound_roll into apply_wound_modifiers. Copy apply_hit_modifiers: pre_net = clamp(net modifier, -1, 1); would_fail = roll == 1 or (roll < crit_threshold and roll + pre_net < wound_threshold). Use would_fail for REROLL_FAILED, and `roll <= 2 and would_fail` for REROLL_ONES_TWOS. Add attack-goldens cases for +1, -1 and Anti.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with one correction. apply_wound_modifiers (RulesEngine.gd:776; REROLL_ONES_TWOS at :782) picks the dice for Twin-linked and the "failed"/"all" wound re-roll grants with a raw-roll test (raw < S-vs-T threshold). It ignores the capped ±1 wound modifier and the Anti-X critical-wound threshold, which AttackSequence.evaluate_wound_roll never passes in (AttackSequence.gd:66-67). As a result it re-rolls certain critical wounds: with the Brutalis Twin Icarus stubber (anti-fly 3+) against a T5 FLY unit, raw 3-4 are re-rolled; against T9+ FLY, raw 3-5. It also re-rolls raw rolls that a +1 already turns into wounds, and it keeps raw rolls that a -1 turns into failures. The -1 example needs fixing: Praesidium Shield and Hardy Bioniks only apply when S > T, so they produce the case at 3+ or 2+ (e.g. a raw 3 against 3+ with -1 fails and is not re-rolled). A "raw 4 vs 4+" case needs a -1 that ignores S and T, such as 'Ard as Nails or an attacker -1 to wound effect. The same flaw reaches all three resolvers and the preview/AI forecast (_preview_wound_probs). The hit-side equivalent was already fixed (r5:F6/F9, RulesEngine.gd:683-701).
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. Every code reference checks out.

Rules: Errata 01.05.02 and the 02.02.01 commentary only fix the order: the die is re-rolled first and modifiers are applied to the new result. Nothing says the decision to re-roll ignores modifiers. Launch 05.02 decides whether a wound roll fails or wounds from the MODIFIED result, after the unmodified-1 fail and the unmodified critical-wound check. 24.03 makes an unmodified Y+ a critical wound. Twin-linked (24.38) says "you can re-roll the wound roll", so the engine's automatic re-roll-failed policy has to be judged against what actually fails.

Engine:
- RulesEngine.gd:760 `apply_wound_modifiers(roll, modifiers, wound_threshold, rng)` takes no crit threshold.
- :776 re-rolls on `roll < wound_threshold`, with no `pre_net` term.
- :782 does the same for REROLL_ONES_TWOS (`roll <= 2 and roll < wound_threshold`).
- AttackSequence.gd:66-67 passes only `wound_threshold`, although `crit_threshold` is in scope. It is used only after the re-roll (:81).
- The `wound_threshold` passed is the raw S-vs-T chart value in all three resolvers (RulesEngine.gd:3166, 5270, 14717). It is never reassigned or lowered to the Anti threshold.
- Twin-linked and the "failed"/"all" grants set REROLL_FAILED at :3232-3234, :3255-3262, :5333-5335 and :14747-14749.
- No code removes the bit when Anti applies.
- The hit side was fixed in exactly this way: `would_fail` at :687, comment "The old test was raw < BS", pinned by test_ork_codex_shooting_fixes.gd section M. The wound side was not fixed.
- Other wound re-roll helpers in the engine (PRIME TARGET `auto_single_reroll_wound`, CustodesCodex11e.gd:324) already pick dice by the modifier-aware `is_wound`. So `apply_wound_modifiers` is the one place still using the raw test.
- No uncommitted change and no other branch fixes it: git diff touches only AIDecisionMaker.gd, and `git log --all -G` finds no crit-aware signature.
- RULINGS.md:275 (EYE OF THE PACK) says REROLL_ONES_TWOS uses "the same raw-roll test as the engine's re-roll-failed". That describes the current convention. It is not an adjudication of 01.05.02, modifiers or Anti, and no ruling cites 01.05.02.
- No unit test pins the edge cases: the tests use raw 2 with +1 and raw 3 with -1, which fail either way.

Scenario 1 holds:
- The Brutalis's Twin Icarus Ironhail Heavy Stubber (sm11e_codex_test.json:3551) is S4 with structured `anti FLY 3` and `twin_linked` abilities.
- get_weapon_profile builds special_rules from those structured abilities, giving "twin-linked", so `has_twin_linked` is true. `get_critical_wound_threshold` returns 3 against FLY.
- Against T5 it wounds on 5+, so raw 3s and 4s, which are certain critical wounds, are re-rolled. P(wound) drops from about 0.89 to about 0.78 per die. Against a T9+ FLY target (6+) raw 3-5 are all re-rolled, giving about 0.72.

Scenarios 2 and 3 work as described:
- -1 at 4+ with Twin-linked: about 0.50 instead of 0.56.
- +1 at 4+: about 0.83 instead of 0.89.
- The Game Log shows "Twin-linked: 3→x" (ShootingPhase.gd:8056-8062), so players can see it.
- The preview/AI forecast (`_preview_wound_probs`, :18386) goes through the same evaluator, so it carries the same error.

One overstatement: the -1 sources named in scenario 2 cannot produce the "raw 4 vs 4+" case. Praesidium Shield (CustodesAbilities.gd:558) and Hardy Bioniks (OrkAbilities11e.gd:884) apply only when S > T, which means the attack wounds on 3+ or 2+.

## EA-21 · 01.06.01 — Leadership / battle-shock roll of an Attached unit uses the worst Ld instead of passing against any model's Ld

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.06.01: a 'Leadership test' is the leadership roll. 01.06: roll 2D6; 'if the result is equal to or greater than one or more of the Ld characteristics in that unit, that roll succeeds'.
- **Engine:** CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership keeps the HIGHEST Ld number, i.e. the worst, across the bodyguard and attached characters (CommandPhase.gd:946-972, `if char_ld > ld` at :963). Its docstring cites a 10e rule. The helper serves every Command-phase battle-shock roll, every force_battle_shock_test (FactionAbilityManager.gd:2385) and the Taktik test (:1267). SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership, used by Fearsome Assault, also takes the max (SMAbilitiesC.gd:1006). OrkAbilities11e.best_leadership and CustodesAbilities._best_ld take the lowest (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1093-1099, CustodesAbilities.gd:1097-1103). RULINGS.md:191 records this as a flagged, unfixed inconsistency (also AI-lab IB-01).
- **Scenario:** A Warboss (Ld 6+) leads Boyz (Ld 7+) (armies/orks.json). The Attached unit is at half strength and rolls a 6 for its Command-phase battle-shock roll. Under 01.06 that passes, because 6 meets the Warboss's 6+. The engine compares against 7 and battle-shocks the unit: OC '-', no stratagems, no actions. This happens on 5/36 of such rolls, and also on forced tests and Desperate Escape rolls. A Chaplain on Bike (5+) leading Outriders (6+) behaves the same way.
- **Fix sketch:** In _get_effective_leadership, take the lowest Ld among the Attached unit's living components (`if char_ld < ld`), keeping the existing Seeker of the Unfound, Show-offs and Madcap/Runt-maw adjustments. Change SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership to use mini. Fix the 10e docstring and update RULINGS.md:191. The AI's mirror (AIDecisionMaker.gd:26141-26148) calls the same helper, so it follows.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd`, `40k/autoloads/FactionAbilityManager.gd`, `.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md`
- **Verifier correction:** Under 01.06, which no errata changes (01.06.01 only says "Leadership test" = leadership roll), a leadership or battle-shock roll succeeds if 2D6 >= any Ld in the unit, i.e. the best (lowest) Ld.

CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership (CommandPhase.gd:946-972, `if char_ld > ld` at :963) instead uses the highest (worst) Ld across the bodyguard and its attached characters. It also counts destroyed characters, because attached_characters is never pruned. This helper decides:
- every Command-phase battle-shock roll: queue :2488, _handle_battle_shock_test :984, _resolve_battle_shock_test :1115;
- every FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2385): Desperate Escape (MovementPhase.gd:3612), Squig Flingin', Impending Crunch, Howlmaw, Kaptaris, Huntress' Eye, Roar of Mork, SM codex actions, IMPENDING KRUNCH;
- the CommandController and OrkCodexOther Ld displays and fail-probability estimates.

SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership (maxi at :1006, over all components including dead ones) makes Fearsome Assault roll against the worst Ld, contradicting RULINGS.md:450. OrkAbilities11e.best_leadership and CustodesAbilities._best_ld correctly use the lowest Ld of living components. RULINGS.md:191 and ai_lab IB-01 record this as a known, unfixed inconsistency.

Effect: a Warboss (6+) leading Boyz (7+) fails a roll of exactly 6 (5/36), and a Chaplain on Bike (5+) leading Outriders (6+) fails a 5 (4/36).

Not affected: the Taktik test at FactionAbilityManager.gd:1267 is 10e-only (refused at edition >= 11) and is issued by a character id.

Fix:
- Use the lowest Ld among living components in both helpers.
- The AI mirror is AIDecisionMaker._effective_leadership_for (:26177-26199). Its snapshot fallback repeats the max loop at :26197 and its comment at :26313-26321 misstates 01.06, so both need their own fix.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute it. The core claim holds; a few details are overstated.

Rule: the launch text 01.06 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:188-192) says the leadership roll succeeds "if the result is equal to or greater than one or more of the Ld characteristics in that unit". 01.07 says a battle-shock roll is a leadership roll. The errata/FAQ file changes nothing here. 01.06.01 only says "Leadership test" means the same as leadership roll, 01.07.02 says the same for battle-shock test, and the 19.x Attached-unit entries say nothing about Ld. So an Attached unit tests against its best (lowest) Ld.

Engine: CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership (40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd:946-972) starts from the bodyguard's Ld and keeps `if char_ld > ld` (:963), i.e. the worst number. The result is compared as `effective_roll >= leadership` (:1011), so a higher number is harder to pass. The docstring cites "10e" and gives the example "Bodyguard Ld 5 + Character Ld 8 -> use 8". That is a misreading: 10e also used the "best" Ld, which is the lowest number.
- In the Command phase, attached characters are skipped (:383) and the bodyguard id is tested, so this max rule decides every Command-phase battle-shock roll: the roll-call queue (:2488), _handle_battle_shock_test (:984) and _resolve_battle_shock_test (:1115).
- FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2385) uses the same helper. That covers Desperate Escape (MovementPhase.gd:3612), Squig Flingin', Impending Crunch, Howlmaw, Kaptaris, Huntress' Eye, Roar of Mork, SMCodexBAction and IMPENDING KRUNCH.
- SMAbilitiesC._best_leadership uses `maxi` (:1006) over group(), which includes dead components. So Fearsome Assault rolls against the worst Ld, which contradicts RULINGS.md:450's own "vs the unit's best Ld".
- OrkAbilities11e.best_leadership (:1093-1099) and CustodesAbilities._best_ld (:1097-1103) correctly take the lowest Ld among living components.
- RULINGS.md:191 records this as "a pre-existing inconsistency, flagged, not changed". It is not a ruling that the max is correct. The ai_lab V2-4 verifiers confirmed IB-01's engine part (tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:240).
- No branch contains a fix (`git log --all -S"if char_ld < ld"` finds nothing), and no test pins the max behaviour.

Scenario: the data matches. In orks.json, Warboss Ld 6 can_lead BOYZ, and Boyz are Ld 7. In the SM rosters, Chaplain on Bike Ld 5 can_lead OUTRIDER SQUAD, and Outriders are Ld 6. None of these units has FEARLESS/ATSKNF or an auto-pass ability. A roll of exactly 6 (5/36) passes under 01.06 but fails in the engine.

Overstatements:
(1) The Taktik test (FactionAbilityManager.gd:1267) is the 10e Lissen 'Ere flow. can_issue_taktik refuses it at edition >= 11 (the default is 11), and the issuer is a character id with no attached list, so the max loop is never reached there.
(2) The AI mirror is AIDecisionMaker._effective_leadership_for at :26177-26199, not :26141-26148. Its snapshot-only fallback repeats the max loop (`if char_ld > ld` at :26197), so fixing the helper does not fix that path. The comment at :26313-26321 also misstates 01.06.
(3) Adding to the fix sketch: attached_characters is never pruned when a character dies, and the CommandPhase helper does not check alive status. A min rule must filter to living models, as the Custodes and Ork helpers already do.

## EA-22 · 01.07 — Battle-shocked CONSERVAI / Tactical Mainstay units always fail the action they started

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.07 FAQ (repeated under 16.01): a unit with a rule allowing it to start an action while battle-shocked can also continue that action if it becomes battle-shocked after starting it. 'A: Yes'.
- **Engine:** complete_actions clears performing_action and sets action_failed for any unit with flags.battle_shocked (ActionsManager.gd:272-276). It does this even though can_start_action exempts CONSERVAI and Tactical Mainstay units from the battle-shock gate (ActionsManager.gd:138-146). RULINGS.md:16 explicitly says 'an action already started still fails if the unit later becomes battle-shocked'. All 11e mission actions (Sabotage 3VP, Vanguard Operation 4VP, Extract Intelligence 7VP) complete through this path at end of turn (MissionManager.gd:1989-2025, PhaseManager.gd:557).
- **Scenario:** (1) Literal FAQ case: a Custodian Guard unit under CONSERVAI is engaged with SM Reivers, which CONSERVAI allows. It starts Sabotage in its Shooting phase. At the start of its own Fight phase, Fearsome Assault makes it take a -1 battle-shock roll and it fails. At end of turn the engine fails Sabotage and scores no VP; per the FAQ it completes. (2) An Intercessor Squad (Tactical Mainstay) failed its Command-phase battle-shock roll and then starts Extract Intelligence, as its ability allows. The engine always fails the action at end of turn, losing 7 VP, so the ability is useless for completing actions.
- **Fix sketch:** In ActionsManager.start_action, stamp flags.action_ignores_shock=true when _cus11e_conservai(unit) or _sm11e_abilities_b().action_ignores_engaged_and_shocked(unit). In complete_actions, skip the battle-shock failure when that stamp is set, and clear the stamp with performing_action. Correct RULINGS.md:16 and add a positive case next to test_iss057_actions_11e.gd:122-126.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/ActionsManager.gd`, `.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md`, `40k/autoloads/MissionManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/PhaseManager.gd`, `40k/tests/test_iss057_actions_11e.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I couldn't refute the claim. Every part of it checks out in the code.

RULE: docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:139-142 (01.07) and :591-594 (16.01) contain the same FAQ: "If a unit has a rule that allows it to start an action when it is battle-shocked can it also continue doing that action if it becomes battle-shocked after starting an action? A: Yes". The launch text 01.07 (core_rules.txt ~212-218) says a battle-shocked unit "is not eligible to start an action (16), and any action it has started cannot be completed". The FAQ overrides that second clause for units with such a rule. Only two rules in the engine qualify. CONSERVAI (CustodesKatah.gd:95-101, "being engaged / battle-shocked does not prevent your unit from being eligible to start an action") and Tactical Mainstay (SMAbilitiesB.gd:816-819, plus the codex CSV text).

ENGINE:
- ActionsManager.can_start_action (:138-146) lets CONSERVAI and Tactical Mainstay units past the battle-shock gate.
- ActionsManager.complete_actions (:272-276) clears performing_action and sets action_failed for any unit with flags.battle_shocked, with no exemption.
- No other path handles this. The only caller is PhaseManager._complete_actions_11e (:557). Grepping performing_action and action_failed finds nothing else, nothing in the codex scripts and nothing in BasePhase. No branch and no worktree (hidden-followups included) changes this code.
- Mission actions confirm the impact. Sabotage, Vanguard Operation and Extract Intelligence are registered with completes:"end_of_turn" (MissionManager.gd:1980-2025) and score only through this hook. Starting one makes the action path authoritative (ShootingPhase.gd:2754-2757, MissionManager.gd:2752-2811), so a failed action scores 0 with no positional backstop.

RULINGS: .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md:16 rules the opposite: "an action already started still fails if the unit later becomes battle-shocked (core rule unchanged)". That entry dates from 2026-10-22 and came from the launch text. The FAQ doc was only added on 2026-11-07, so the ruling is out of date, not a deliberate reading of the FAQ.

BOTH SCENARIOS ARE REACHABLE:
- Scenario 1: Fearsome Assault is live. FightPhase.gd:224 calls SMAbilitiesC.on_fight_phase_start (:966-1018) at the start of either player's Fight phase, and a failed roll writes flags.battle_shocked on every component of the unit.
- Scenario 2: in 11e, battle-shock is not cleared during the turn. CommandPhase._clear_battle_shocked_flags returns early for edition >= 11. So a unit shocked in the Command phase is still shocked when end-of-turn completion runs. That makes the "battle-shocked" half of the two start permissions useless: the unit can start the action but can never complete it.
- Tests: test_iss057_actions_11e.gd:122-126 only covers a plain unit, and no test covers completion under CONSERVAI or Tactical Mainstay.

NUANCE (not an overstatement): the FAQ literally covers a unit that "becomes battle-shocked after starting". Scenario 2, a unit already shocked when it starts, follows by clear implication, because the unit is in the same state at completion. The FAQ's "also" treats the start-while-shocked case as the obvious one.

FIX NOTE: the CONSERVAI flag lasts until the next Command phase start (CustodesKatah.gd:249-253), and Tactical Mainstay is permanent. So a live check in complete_actions would work as well as the proposed stamp. Keep the plain-unit failure test.

## EA-23 · 02.02 (FAQ: charge-roll / max-distance modifiers vs charge targets) — Charges cannot take to the skies; FLY chargers get its benefits without the -2in

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'A charging unit may only select charge targets that are within the maximum distance of their charge roll, including any modifiers ... A FLYING unit chooses to take to the skies and rolls a charge roll of 9in; that charge's maximum distance is 7in' (declared before rolling, 21.03).
- **Engine:** Roll modifiers are applied before the post-roll filter (ChargePhase.gd:795-850, 873), which is correct. But the Charge phase has no take-to-the-skies declaration (MoveType.take_to_skies_modifier is consumed only in MovementPhase.gd:1188-1196, 1478-1484, 2864-2872, 7262-7266). Instead, every FLY charger skips the solid-terrain path sweep and gets the reduced FLY terrain penalty (ChargePhase.gd:2238-2266, 2311-2319) at its full roll.
- **Scenario:** Deffkoptas (FLY MOUNTED) roll 9in and charge a unit 8.5in away behind a dense ruin taller than 2in. The engine lets them cross the ruin and keeps the target selectable at 9in. Under the rules they must take to the skies to cross it, which cuts the maximum to 7in so the target is not selectable, or else path around it without TTS.
- **Fix sketch:** Add a take_to_skies flag to DECLARE_CHARGE / CHARGE_ROLL, set before rolling. If set, subtract 2in (0 with HOVER) from charge_data.distance before selectable_distance_ceiling and the move cap. Gate the FLY terrain and model-crossing exemptions on that flag rather than on the FLY keyword.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/ChargeMove11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/MoveType.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, but slightly overstated. The Charge phase has no take-to-the-skies declaration. ChargePhase.gd, ChargeController.gd and ChargeMove11e.gd never read take_to_skies, and SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES only accepts NORMAL, ADVANCE or FALL_BACK moves (MovementPhase.gd:7212-7213). As a result, FAQ 21.03 (declare before the roll) and FAQ 02.02 (taking to the skies cuts the maximum distance used to pick targets) are not implemented. A FLY charger's target ceiling (ChargePhase.gd:873) and move cap (2272) are always the full modified roll, never roll -2". Instead, the FLY keyword alone (2241) exempts the unit from the 13.06 solid-wall path check (2314) and from difficult ground, even when Madcap Artillery has made it "grounded". In place of the take-to-the-skies rule the engine applies the 10e diagonal climb penalty, sqrt(h²+c²)−c (TerrainManager.gd:539-547), so vertical distance is not ignored. The error cuts both ways. The engine is too generous on target selection and on crossing walls without declaring. It is too strict over thin tall walls (about 5.5" of penalty over a 5" wall, versus the 2" for taking to the skies) and always too strict for HOVER units, which should pay nothing. The charge code has no FLY model-crossing exemption to put behind a flag (it only checks where models end up, for every unit). Whether the Deffkopta charge in the scenario succeeds depends on the path, but the 8.5" target does stay selectable on a 9" roll.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute this claim.

Rules: Launch 21.03 says a FLYING unit selected to make a normal, advance, fall-back or charge move may declare that it takes to the skies. It then gets -2" maximum distance (0 with HOVER), ignores vertical distance and moves through all models and terrain. FAQ 21.03 (errata_faq:679-682) says the declaration for a charge comes before the charge roll. FAQ 02.02 (errata_faq:160-161) says charge targets must be within the modified maximum distance: on a 9" roll, a unit taking to the skies can only select targets within 7". Launch 13.06 lets FLY MOUNTED/VEHICLE models (not INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE) cross dense sections over 2" only by climbing, unless they take to the skies.

Engine, searched across the repo:
- Take to the skies exists only in MovementPhase (lines 1188-1196, 1478-1484, 2864-2872, 7262-7266) and MovementController. The SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES validator rejects anything except NORMAL, ADVANCE or FALL_BACK (MovementPhase.gd:7212-7213).
- ChargePhase, ChargeController and ChargeMove11e never read it. No DECLARE_CHARGE or CHARGE_ROLL payload carries it, and nothing on main or hidden-followups has it either.
- _resolve_charge_roll applies the roll modifiers, then filters targets at selectable_distance_ceiling(total) = min(12, roll), at line 873. The move cap is the full rolled_distance (line 2272). So -2" is never applied.
- `has_fly` comes from the keyword alone (line 2241). It skips the 13.06 solid-wall sweep (line 2314) and difficult ground (TerrainManager.gd:566), even for a unit made "grounded" by Madcap Artillery (cannot_take_to_skies is only checked in MovementPhase).
- No RULINGS.md entry or doc approves this. The FLY diagonal is the 10e T2-8 carry-over (docs/history/MASTER_AUDIT.md:466-470).
- The project's own 2026-10-31 audit already lists it as an unfixed engine defect (tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:62 and 230). It is not on the already-being-fixed list.

Where the claim goes too far:
1. FLY chargers do not get all the take-to-the-skies benefits. TerrainManager.gd:539-547 still charges the 10e diagonal climb penalty, sqrt(h²+c²)−c, using category heights of 3.5"/6". Over a thin 5" ("tall", 6") wall that is about 5.5", more than the 2" the rules charge. HOVER units, for whom taking to the skies costs nothing, always pay it. So the error cuts both ways.
2. The charge code has no FLY model-crossing exemption to put behind a flag. Charge validation only checks where models end up for overlaps, for every unit.
3. Whether the Deffkopta charge actually succeeds depends on the path. Over a thin wall it would fail on a 9" roll. Two things are certain: the 8.5" target stays selectable at 9", and the path over the ruin is allowed with no declaration.

## EA-24 · 02.02.01 — Hit/wound modifiers are netted by sign presence, not by their cumulative total

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'All modifiers are cumulative ... Hit rolls and wound rolls can never be modified by more than -1 or +1. After all the cumulative modifiers ... have been calculated, if the total modifier would be -2 or worse, it is changed to -1; +2 or better, +1.'
- **Engine:** Each source ORs a PLUS_ONE or MINUS_ONE bit: the ModifierStack's own capped net becomes a bit (RulesEngine.gd:2796-2806, 4983-4993), Ork/SM/SW/Custodes datasheet hit_plus/hit_minus are booleans (:10520-10523), and codex apply_hit_bits adds more (SMCodex11e.gd:302-311). apply_hit_modifiers and apply_wound_modifiers then net the bits to +1, 0 or -1 (RulesEngine.gd:676-718, 790-798). So +1,+1,-1 nets 0 instead of +1, and -1,-1,+1 nets 0 instead of -1.
- **Scenario:** Ork Killa Kans with Spiteful Power Trip (+1 to hit, the target has no T7+ model) and a Mek's Clever Know-wotz (+1 to hit) shoot a Space Marine unit under UMBRAL EVASION (-1 to hit). The rules give net +1; the engine applies no modifier. Likewise, an engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE shooter (-1, 10.06) firing a non-CQ weapon at an engaged VEHICLE (-1, 17.03) with one +1 source should be at -1, but the engine gives 0.
- **Fix sketch:** Carry signed integer modifiers per roll: add every source as a ModifierStack 'hit_roll'/'wound_roll' entry instead of OR-ing bits, apply the ignore filters per entry, and clamp the sum to [-1, +1] once just before evaluate_hit_roll / evaluate_wound_roll.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/ModifierStack.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: the official 02.02.01 commentary (errata doc line 165) says 'All modifiers are cumulative ... After all the cumulative modifiers to such a roll have been calculated, if the total modifier would be -2 or worse, it is changed to -1; +2 or better, +1.' Rule 24.02 only stops the same core or weapon ability from stacking, so it doesn't merge different datasheet abilities. Engine: RulesEngine.apply_hit_modifiers (around 666-718) and apply_wound_modifiers (around 760-798) build the net as +1 if the PLUS_ONE bit is set and -1 if the MINUS_ONE bit is set, then clamp. Any mix of signs comes out as 0, whatever the counts. Nearly every source ORs one of those bits: the attack assignment, effect flags, Tank Hunters, Wall of Dakka and the other shooting sources (2556-2700); the shared datasheet aggregator, whose keys are booleans (OrkAbilities11e._empty_mods hit_plus/hit_minus/wound_plus, folded by ork11e_ab_hit_bits 10516-10523; Custodes/SW/SM merge into the same dict at 10494-10506); and the codex apply_hit_bits/apply_wound_bits hooks (SMCodex11e 304-313, Custodes). ModifierStack only sums three hit_roll entries ([HEAVY] +1, 10.06 -1, 17.03 -1). Its clamped net is then turned back into one PLUS_ONE or MINUS_ONE bit (2795-2805 and 4982-4992). AttackSequence.evaluate_hit_roll/evaluate_wound_roll pass the bitfield through unchanged. The wound side has no stack at all (shooting wound net at 3340-3345). Scenario 1 checks out: OrkAbilities11e.attack_mods sets the same m.hit_plus=true for Clever Know-wotz (717-719) and Spiteful Power Trip (778-780; Killa Kans datasheet, 'no T7+ model'), giving one PLUS_ONE bit. UMBRAL EVASION (SMCodexACombat.hit_bits 344-356) ORs MINUS_ONE, so the engine applies 0 where the rules give +1. Scenario 2 also holds: 10.06 and 17.03 are both -1 to hit (core rules 1530-1536, 2898-2908). They sum to -2 in the stack, clamp to -1 and become MINUS_ONE. [HEAVY] needs an unengaged shooter, so any +1 (for example Tank Hunters against the VEHICLE) arrives as PLUS_ONE, and the engine applies 0 instead of -1. Nothing makes this intended. RULINGS.md has no ruling on it; line 369 (Walking Fortress) relies on the bit design but says nothing about mixed signs. docs/history/STRATAGEMS_AND_ABILITIES_PLAN.md:455 assumed 'the +1/-1 cap naturally handles overlapping sources', which only holds when all sources share a sign. doc_change_audit.md:65 describes hit modifiers as 'a net-sum ModifierStack', which is wrong for almost every source. No test pins +1,+1,-1 to 0, no commit on any branch addresses it, and it isn't one of the excluded Hidden items. Nuances: the three in-stack hit entries net correctly among themselves. The engine is wrong whenever both signs are present with unequal counts. The claim undersells the scope: the melee loops OR the same bits (melee_hit_modifiers around 14472-14526, melee_wound_modifiers around 14741-14821), and the damage preview around 17975-17996 uses the same hit_plus/hit_minus booleans.

## EA-25 · 02.02.01 — Modified BS (and Ld) are not clamped to 2+..6+ (Ld 5+..8+)

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'After all modifiers have been applied: ... Ld cannot be 4+ (or better) or 9+ (or worse) ... BS cannot be 1+ (or better) or 7+ (or worse).'
- **Engine:** BS worsening and improvement (cover, plunging fire, stack 'bs') are added straight onto the per-attack BS (RulesEngine.gd:2761, 2790; auto-resolve :4948, :4977) and compared unclamped (AttackSequence.gd:54); only the display floors at 2 (RulesEngine.gd:2143). Ld: CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership adds improvements and penalties with no clamp (CommandPhase.gd:967-972).
- **Scenario:** Lootas (Deffgun BS 6+ [HEAVY], armies/orks.json) remain stationary (+1 to hit) and shoot a unit with the benefit of cover. The engine uses BS 7+, so a 5 (+1 = 6) misses and only 6s hit; the rules clamp BS to 6+, so 5s hit too (2/6 instead of 1/6). The low end is also affected: a BS 2+ shooter with plunging fire (BS 1+) against a -1-to-hit target hits on a 2 in the engine but misses under the 2+ clamp. For Ld, Madcap Artillery 'zapped' on Fenrisian Wolves (Ld 8) makes the engine test on 9+ where the rules use 8+.
- **Fix sketch:** After all characteristic modifiers, clamp each per-attack BS/WS to [2, 6]. Exempt the snap-shot BS-7 marker, which is an unmodified-6 rule rather than a characteristic. Clamp the final Ld to [5, 8] in _get_effective_leadership, and keep roll modifiers separate from the characteristic.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`, `40k/phases/CommandPhase.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Refutation failed; claim holds and line refs are accurate. RULE: errata Commentary 02.02.01 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:165): 'After all modifiers have been applied: ... Ld cannot be 4+ (or better) or 9+ (or worse) ... WS/BS cannot be 1+ (or better) or 7+ (or worse)'. Launch text: Benefit of Cover 13.08 'worsen the BS characteristic of that attack by 1', Plunging Fire 22.05 'improve the BS characteristic ... by 1' (characteristic modifiers, so the clamp applies); [HEAVY] 24.16 'add 1 to the hit roll' (roll modifier). Madcap Artillery data text: 'While a unit is zapped, that unit has -1 Ld' (characteristic modifier). No RULINGS.md / docs decision exempts this; no test pins unclamped BS. ENGINE (GameConstants.edition defaults to 11): RulesEngine.gd:2761-2763 adds ModifierStack net('bs') to bs/bs_per_attack, :2790 adds +1 per covered attack; auto-resolve repeats it at :4948-4950/:4977. ModifierStack.net() caps only DICE_ROLL_TYPES at ±1 and its header says characteristic modifiers 'accumulate'. apply_hit_modifiers (RulesEngine.gd:666) caps the roll modifier but never clamps the threshold; AttackSequence.evaluate_hit_roll:54 tests 'final_roll >= threshold' on the raw BS. Only display clamps exist: _hit_threshold_display maxi(shown,2) at :2143 and the preview label clampi(...,2,6) at :18297. The preview's p_hit still uses unclamped BS (:18079-18100). The only other floor is the Ork Super Runts WS improvement (maxi(2,...) at :14015). Ld: CommandPhase._get_effective_leadership (CommandPhase.gd:967-972) does ld -= effect_improve_leadership and ld += OrkAbilities11e.leadership_penalty with no clamp; battle-shock pass is 'effective_roll >= leadership' (:1011); FactionAbilityManager, CommandController, the AI and the Ork stratagems all call this function. SCENARIOS CHECK OUT: orks.json Deffgun BS 6 [heavy] -> BS 7 in cover, +1 Heavy: a 5 (6<7) misses, only 6s hit (1/6) vs 2/6 clamped. BS 2+ with plunging fire (BS 1) and -1 to hit: a raw 2 -> 1 >= 1 hits in the engine, misses at the 2+ clamp. sw11e_codex_test.json Fenrisian Wolves Ld 8 zapped -> engine 9+ (10/36) vs rules 8+ (15/36). NUANCES (not overstatements): the high-end clamp changes outcomes only with a net +1 hit modifier and the low end only with a net -1 (unmodified 6 always hits, 1 always fails). WS is effectively unaffected: there is no WS-worsening path and the one WS improvement is already floored at 2. Related unclamped sites: SMDetachmentsA.gd:377 floors a '+1 Ld' improvement at 2, not 5. Enhanced Runt-maw's '-1 to leadership rolls' (a roll modifier) is encoded as +1 Ld (OrkEnhancements11e.gd:1062-1068), which a naive Ld clamp would mis-handle; this supports the fix sketch's point to keep roll modifiers separate from the characteristic.

## EA-26 · 02.02.04 — Unit heals can revive destroyed CHARACTER models; model heals act on the whole unit

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'If all models in that unit have their starting number of wounds ... revive one of those destroyed models (excluding CHARACTER models) ... If a rule states that a model heals ..., only that model can regain wounds ... excess ... do not cause a destroyed model from that unit to revive.'
- **Engine:** Every live heal_diffs (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1196-1240, SMAbilitiesA.gd:2052-2095, SWAbilities11e.gd:1699-1740, SMAbilitiesC.gd:1638-1680, SMDetachmentsB.gd:682-720) walks Common.attached_group_ids. That list still contains dead leaders (CustodesCommon.gd:163-173), and dead models keep their positions, so the heal revives the first dead model with no CHARACTER test. It also picks the wounded model bodyguard-first instead of letting the player choose. Gift of the Iron Wolf ('That VEHICLE model heals D3') calls the unit heal (SWAbilities11e.gd:1799).
- **Scenario:** A Captain in Gravis Armour (leader) and an Apothecary Biologis (support) are attached to Heavy Intercessors. A Precision attack kills the Captain. Next Command phase, with every Heavy Intercessor alive and unwounded, Helix Gauntlet heals D3: the engine brings the Captain back with 1W, but under the rules no CHARACTER revives and the heal is wasted.
- **Fix sketch:** In the revive pass, skip models whose component unit has the CHARACTER keyword. Add a model-only variant for 'this/that model heals' that never revives. Let the controlling player choose which wounded model heals, with the AI using a sensible default.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/orks/OrkAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesA.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMDetachmentsB.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Five codex heal helpers can revive a destroyed CHARACTER (leader or support) model, which 02.02.04 forbids:
- OrkAbilities11e.heal_diffs: Crude Surgery (including its automatic end-of-Command-phase heal), Fix Dat Armour Up, Push Dat Bit Back In, Clever Know-wotz.
- SMAbilitiesA.heal_diffs: Helix Gauntlet, Master of the Forge, Blessings of the Omnissiah.
- SWAbilities11e.heal_diffs: Healing Balms, Gift of the Iron Wolf.
- SMAbilitiesC.heal_diffs in its default mode: Narthecium.
- SMDetachmentsB.heal_diffs: Narthecis Gauntlet.

All five run their revive pass over OrkCodexCommon/SWCommon.attached_group_ids. That list still includes a dead leader, because attached_characters is only pruned when the whole bodyguard is wiped. The pass then revives the first destroyed model with no CHARACTER check. The SM/SW versions only need a non-null position, and dead models keep theirs. The Ork version checks an ork11e_never_revive flag that nothing sets.

So once every bodyguard model is alive and at full wounds, a dead leader comes back, and the rest of the heal goes into him. The availability checks (_healable / unit_wounds_lost) even offer the heal, or auto-apply it for Crude Surgery, just because the leader is dead.

Example: a Captain in Gravis Armour and an Apothecary Biologis are attached to Heavy Intercessors, and a Precision attack kills the Captain. Narthecis Gauntlet (D3+1), not Helix Gauntlet (Infiltrator Squad wargear), brings the Captain back with 2-4 W. Under 02.02.04 the heal is wasted. OrkCodexMovement.heal_unit_diffs (DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS) already excludes CHARACTER models and should be the model for the fix.

Secondary issue: the 'that VEHICLE model heals' abilities (Gift of the Iron Wolf, Master of the Forge, Blessings of the Omnissiah, Clever Know-wotz) call the unit-wide heal. This only changes the outcome for VEHICLE units with more than one model, such as Killa Kans or a 2-model Invader ATVs unit. There the engine can heal a different model or revive a destroyed one.
