The fix can reuse the existing SMAbilitiesC own_model_only mode or the Custodes Caducatrix pattern.

Auto-picking the first wounded model instead of letting the player choose is the engine's documented heal convention and is minor.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the main claim. Some details are wrong, so a corrected claim is attached.

Rule: the 02.02.04 commentary (rules-appendix, errata doc line 177) says that once every model is at full wounds, a heal revives a destroyed model "(excluding CHARACTER models)". It also says a "model heals" rule only heals that model, and any excess never revives a model. The launch text only says this section is "continued in the app", so the commentary is the governing text. The 01.02.03 sentence about revived leaders covers rules that explicitly revive models. It does not override the 02.02.04 exclusion.

Engine, verified:
(1) OrkCodexCommon.attached_group_ids (line 121) and SWCommon.attached_group_ids (line 167) list every id in `attached_characters` that is still in `units`. Destroyed units are never erased from `units`. `detach_character` is only called from `check_bodyguard_destroyed`, which only runs when every bodyguard model is dead. So a dead leader stays in the group.
(2) A killed model only gets alive=false (RulesEngine 2064 and 4106); its position is kept.
(3) The revive pass in each of the five helpers picks the first dead model with no CHARACTER check:
- SMAbilitiesA 2052, SWAbilities11e 1699, SMAbilitiesC 1638 (default mode) and SMDetachmentsB 682 only require a non-null position.
- The Ork version at OrkAbilities11e 1212 checks an `ork11e_never_revive` flag that nothing in the codebase sets.
After the revive, the remaining heals go into the revived model.
(4) The availability checks count the dead leader as healable: SMDetachmentsB._healable, the `_healable` checks in SMAbilitiesA, SMAbilitiesC and SWAbilities11e, and Ork `unit_wounds_lost`. Crude Surgery also auto-heals at the end of the Command phase (`on_command_phase_exit`), so no player input is needed.
(5) The codebase already does this correctly in one place. OrkCodexMovement.heal_unit_diffs (DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS) skips CHARACTER models, and test_ork_codex_movement.gd:1157 checks that. RULINGS.md 179/320/375 only describe "a destroyed model returns with 1 W". No ruling allows reviving CHARACTERs, and RULINGS 445 says healing never brings Titus back.

Where the claim is wrong or overstated:
- **Scenario:** Helix Gauntlet is Infiltrator Squad wargear (SPACE_MARINES_GENERIC.md:381), not something Heavy Intercessors can use. The matching rule for that unit is the Narthecis Gauntlet enhancement on the Apothecary Biologis, which heals D3+1 via SMDetachmentsB. The test fixture has exactly this trio: HINT, CAP_GRAVIS and BIOLOGIS.
- **Wounds on return:** the Captain does not come back with 1W. He comes back with the whole heal, 2-4 W.
- **"Every heal_diffs":** not quite. SMAbilitiesC has an `own_model_only` mode (used by Righteous Fury) that never revives, and the Custodes Caducatrix already heals only the bearer model. A model-only variant therefore already exists in places.
- **Gift of the Iron Wolf:** it does call the unit heal (SWAbilities11e 1799), and so do Master of the Forge and Blessings of the Omnissiah (SMAbilitiesA 2170) and Clever Know-wotz (Ork 2087). This only matters for VEHICLE units with more than one model, such as Killa Kans (3 models in ork11e_codex_test) or Invader ATVs (VEHICLE, 1-2 models). For single-model vehicles the result is the same.
- **Player choice:** auto-picking the first wounded model (bodyguard first) is the engine's recorded heal convention ("a wounded model first", RULINGS 350 "first wounded model"). It is a small simplification, not the core bug.
- **Citation:** the claim cites CustodesCommon. The five files actually use OrkCodexCommon and SWCommon, which hold identical copies of the function.

## EA-27 · 03.01.01 — Attached units move every model, leader included, on the bodyguard's M

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'if different models in the moving unit have different M characteristics, the maximum distance for that move will be different for those models'
- **Engine:** MovementPhase.gd:1183-1214 sets one move_cap_inches from get_unit_movement(bodyguard) (:203-266, unit-level stats.move). Every staged model, attached CHARACTER models included, is checked against that cap (:744-749, :870-879), and unstaged characters are moved rigidly by the bodyguard's delta (_move_attached_characters :9253-9330) without checking their own M.
- **Scenario:** Trajann Valoris (M8) leading Allarus Custodians (M7) (armies/cus11e_codex_test.json) is capped at 7in instead of 8in, and Ghazghkull (M8) leading Meganobz (M5) (armies/orks.json) at 5in. The reverse also happens: a Chaplain in Terminator Armour (M5) leading Wolf Guard Terminators (M6) is allowed to move 6in.
- **Fix sketch:** Build a per-model cap from each model's own component unit (M plus that move's advance roll, TTS and M modifiers). Validate staged and ridden models against their own cap in _validate_stage_model_move, _process_group_movement and _move_attached_characters, and clamp the rigid ride to the character's cap.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute. RULE: the launch text 'Different Move Characteristics' (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt ~4048-4056, normal/advance) and Commentary 03.01.01 (errata_faq_commentary.md:222-224, any move whose distance uses M, so fall back too) give each model its own max distance. 19.01 makes an Attached unit 'a single unit for all rules purposes'. The only bodyguard-statline exception in 19.x is 19.02 (T, attacks only). Nothing in RULINGS.md, docs/ or git history rules on or fixes 03.01.01. ENGINE (MovementPhase.gd): normal (:1183-1214), advance (:1565 total_move = bodyguard M + roll) and fall back (:2890) each set ONE move_cap_inches from get_unit_movement(actor unit). That function (:203-266) reads only that unit's meta.stats.move + flags and has no attachment logic. Attached characters cannot begin their own move (:618-619), so they never get a cap of their own. _validate_stage_model_move resolves the character's models (:828-836) and checks them against move_data.move_cap_inches (:875-880). _process_group_movement (:8111-8130) and _process_stage_model_move (:3123) use the same single cap. Confirm (:3382-3530) does no per-model check. _move_attached_characters (:9253-9330) moves unstaged characters by the first bodyguard model's delta plus an overlap nudge, with no distance check. No other path changes this: the UI uses one cap (MovementController._get_effective_move_cap :4259, reach rings :6750+); the MoveType templates' max_distance_inches is unit-level and never called; CharacterAttachmentManager.attach_character does not merge stats. DATA: cus11e_codex_test.json Trajann M8, can_lead includes ALLARUS CUSTODIANS (M7). orks.json Ghazghkull M8, can_lead includes MEGANOBZ (M5). 11e SM codex Datasheets_leader.csv lists Chaplain In Terminator Armour (M5) -> Wolf Guard Terminators (M6); Captain/Librarian/Ancient in TA (M5) can also lead them. The codex CSVs give the same M values. So a faster leader is wrongly capped and a slower leader is wrongly allowed the bodyguard's M; the player sees it in the refused drags and the reach rings. Minor imprecision only: :744-749 is the SET_MODEL_DEST check, which looks only at bodyguard-unit models; the character-inclusive checks are :875-880 and :8111-8130. Fix caution: unit-wide M modifiers stored on the bodyguard's flags (FFI, effect_plus_move, etc.) apply to every model of the Attached unit (19.04). A per-model cap must therefore be each model's own base M plus the Attached unit's modifiers, not get_unit_movement(char_unit) alone. The bodyguard shift applied when only the character is moved (:3483-3515) would also need clamping once a character's cap can exceed the bodyguard's.

## EA-28 · 03.01.02 — FLY units move through enemy (and MONSTER/VEHICLE) models without taking to the skies

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Only a model with a move-through rule 'can make its move through enemy models' (otherwise 'Its base cannot be moved through enemy models', 03.01). In 11e, FLY grants that only while taking to the skies, which also subtracts 2in (21.03).
- **Engine:** The enemy-base and MONSTER/VEHICLE-base crossing checks are skipped for any unit with the FLY keyword (MovementPhase.gd:759-769, 893-903, 8145-8152, 8232-8239 via _unit_has_fly_keyword :7096-7101), whatever move_data.took_to_skies says. The -2in applies only when the player ticks TTS (:1188-1196), and the default drag flow starts with it off.
- **Scenario:** Stormboyz (FLY, M12) make a full 12in normal move straight through a screen of enemy Intercessors without declaring TTS. The rules forbid crossing the screen unless they take to the skies, which limits the move to 10in.
- **Fix sketch:** At edition 11, replace _unit_has_fly_keyword(unit_id) in those crossing gates with bool(move_data.took_to_skies). Keep the codex move-through rules (_o6_moves_through_models / Calistus) as separate exemptions.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** At edition 11, both live movement validators let any FLY unit pass through enemy models on a Normal or Advance move without declaring take to the skies or losing 2". The validators are SET_MODEL_DEST (MovementPhase.gd:759) and STAGE_MODEL_MOVE (:893, the action the drag UI and AI send). Their enemy-base crossing gate exempts every FLY unit through _unit_has_fly_keyword (:7096-7101) and ignores move_data.took_to_skies. The drag flow starts every move with took_to_skies=false and a full-M cap. So Stormboyz (FLY, M12) can make a full 12" normal move straight through an Intercessor screen, which breaks 03.01, 21.03 and 03.01.02. Enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE models can be crossed the same way. The FLY exemption on the MONSTER/VEHICLE gate (:766, :900) is wrong only for enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE models; there it also matters on fall-back moves, which skip the enemy-base gate. For friendly MONSTER/VEHICLE models it is correct, because 11e 03.01 and FAQ 17.01 let any model move through friendly models; that gate's friendly block is itself a 10e carry-over. Fix: require bool(move_data.took_to_skies) in place of the FLY keyword for crossing enemy models in both live validators. Keep the codex move-through exemptions. Do not swap the MONSTER/VEHICLE gate wholesale: that would forbid legal moves past friendly vehicles and break test_fly_unit_can_cross_friendly_vehicle. The 8145/8232 sites are in group-move helpers with no non-test callers.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rules: launch 03.01 says "Its base cannot be moved through enemy models" (core_rules.txt:391-393). Launch 21.03 gives FLY units "move through all types of model" only "if it does [take to the skies]", together with "Subtract 2in from the maximum distance" (:3363-3375). The 21.03 FAQ (errata md:679-682) makes taking to the skies a declaration made when the unit is selected to move. Commentary 03.01.02 (errata md:226-228) only lets models with a move-through rule pass enemy models.

Engine (static read, edition 11 is the default and the only edition players get, GameConstants.gd:27):
- In SET_MODEL_DEST (MovementPhase.gd:759, 766) and STAGE_MODEL_MOVE (:893, :900), both crossing gates skip any unit for which _unit_has_fly_keyword (:7096-7101) is true. That function is a bare "FLY" in keywords check with no edition gate and no took_to_skies check. STAGE_MODEL_MOVE is what the drag UI sends (MovementController.gd:2844). The client drop check _compute_move_rejection (:2724) has no enemy-base check, so the phase gate decides alone.
- Neither _path_crosses_enemy_bases (:7422) nor _path_crosses_monster_vehicle_bases (:7477) reads took_to_skies. Its comment is the 10e rule: "Only Fall Back and FLY units may move through enemy models". The MoveType templates have no crossing logic.
- took_to_skies is opt-in and defaults to false (:1188-1196). The drag flow starts a NORMAL move at selection with took_to_skies=false (MovementController.gd:1811-1818, 1828-1831).
- Engagement range is checked only at the destination (:7080-7094), so the Stormboyz scenario works: they are FLY, M12 (Orks_2000.json; 11e datasheet M12), and Rokkit Charge is a melee buff, not move-through.
- The SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES docstring (:7190-7191) says took_to_skies is "read by ... the model-crossing checks", but they never read it, so the intended hookup was missed.
- The developers' own audits already list this as a 10e carry-over gap: research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md:2871 and tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:230.
- No test pins FLY crossing enemy models without taking to the skies.

Overstatements:
(1) The MONSTER/VEHICLE gate also blocks friendly MONSTER/VEHICLE bases. In 11e, 03.01 lets any model move through friendly models, and FAQ 17.01 (errata md:596-599) confirms this for MONSTER/VEHICLE units. So a FLY unit passing a friendly vehicle without taking to the skies is legal, and test_fly_unit_can_cross_friendly_vehicle expects exactly that. The FLY exemption on that gate is wrong only for enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE models. The fix sketch's blanket swap to took_to_skies would forbid a legal move.
(2) Lines 8145-8152 and 8232-8239 are inside _process_group_movement and _validate_individual_move_internal, which have no non-test callers. Only the 759/766 and 893/900 sites are live.

Out of scope: every FALL_BACK move (Ordered Retreat included) skips the enemy-base gate for every unit, which is a separate issue.

## EA-29 · 03.02.02 — No set-up exceptions for oversized models (disembark within 1in, touch the battlefield edge)

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'If a disembarking model is so large that it is not possible to set it up wholly within 3in ... set that model up with its base within 1in of that TRANSPORT's base ... and not engaged'. Deployment and strategic reserves: 'it must be set up so that it is touching a battlefield edge', and it cannot move or shoot that turn.
- **Engine:** Disembark requires the farthest base point within 3in or 6in (TransportManager.gd:806-815, used at MovementPhase.gd:8546). Rapid mode is mandatory after the transport moves, otherwise the fallback is combat mode (DisembarkMove.gd:84-93). Ingress requires bases wholly within 6in of an edge (IngressMove.gd:62-70, 113-126). No validator has a large-model fallback.
- **Scenario:** Ghazghkull Thraka (80 mm base, about 3.15in) leads Boyz in a Battlewagon, which the shipped TRANSPORT text allows (armies/orks.json). After the wagon makes a normal move, rapid disembark (3in) is mandatory and impossible, so the unit can never get out. If the wagon stays still, the only legal mode is combat disembark: 6in, a hazard roll per model, battle-shocked, no charge. Under the rules Ghazghkull is placed within 1in of the hull and the unit disembarks normally. Separately, a Stompa (180 mm, armies/recon_stomps.json) put in strategic reserves can never make its ingress move.
- **Fix sketch:** In the disembark validator, if a model's base cannot fit wholly within 3in of the hull, accept a base within 1in that is not engaged. In the ingress and deployment validators, accept 'touching a battlefield edge' when the base cannot fit, and set a no-move / no-ranged-attacks flag for that turn (AIRCRAFT excepted for reserves).
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/TransportManager.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/DisembarkMove.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/IngressMove.gd`, `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 03.02.02 Setting Up Large Models is not implemented. No disembark, ingress, RAPID INGRESS or deployment validator has a large-model fallback, in the engine, the UI or the AI.

(1) Disembark: TransportManager.is_position_within_disembark_range (806-815, 822-829) requires the farthest point of the base within the set-up distance. Callers: MovementPhase.gd:8578 / 8925 / 6742, DisembarkController.gd:330 and the AI ring. An 80mm base (3.15") can never pass the 3" check, and there is no "within 1in of the hull, not engaged" alternative. Ghazghkull (orks.json: INFANTRY, 80mm; the Battlewagon text explicitly carries him as 4 spaces) attached to 10 Boyz therefore cannot make a 3" disembark. Note: after a normal move the engine resolves tactical mode, not rapid, because select_mode keys rapid on flags.moved_this_phase, which only SurgeMove sets. It is still 3", plus cannot_move and cannot_charge from TransportManager.disembark_unit. The human DisembarkDialog hides the Combat Disembark option once the transport has moved, and with a stationary wagon only offers it when an enemy is near the hull. A human therefore cannot get the unit out after the wagon moves, nor from a stationary wagon with no enemy close. Otherwise the only option is combat disembark (6", a hazard roll per model, battle-shocked, no charge).

(2) Ingress: IngressMove.validate_setup (62-70, 113-126), RAPID INGRESS (MovementPhase.gd:2459-2469) and the placement UI (DeploymentController.gd:3217-3281) all require a base wholly within a 6" edge band. A Stompa (180mm, recon_stomps.json) put in strategic reserves can never arrive and is destroyed at the end of round 3. There is no "touching a battlefield edge" alternative and no no-move / no-ranged-attacks flag (AIRCRAFT excepted).

(3) Deployment: DeploymentPhase._validate_model_position (302) requires the base wholly within the zone, with no edge fallback. On the default Search and Destroy map (6"-deep L zones, largest inscribed radius about 3.515") a Stompa (radius 3.543") cannot be deployed at all.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: Commentary 03.02.02 (rules-appendix, errata_faq_commentary.md:242-244) is official. The launch text points to it: the 20.03 SEE ALSO "Setting Up Large Models" (core_rules.txt:3254). The launch text only says "wholly within the set-up distance" (18.04 and 20.04), so on its own an oversized base has no legal set-up. RULINGS.md and docs/ have no ruling on it. research/ only restates the rule. No commit on any branch touches it.

DISEMBARK: TransportManager.is_position_within_disembark_range (806-815) measures to the farthest point of the base. For a circle that is the near-edge gap plus the diameter (822-829). Ghazghkull's 80mm base is 3.15", so it can never be within 3"+0.01. Every caller uses this check with no fallback: MovementPhase._validate_confirm_disembark (8578), _auto_disembark_positions (8925), the helper at 6742, DisembarkController._validate_disembark_position (330) and the AI's 3" ring (AIDecisionMaker ~8927). The only widening is Space Marines AGGRESSIVE DISEMBARKATION (SMCodex11e.gd:426-433). The scenario is real with shipped data. In orks.json Ghazghkull is INFANTRY ORKS, base_mm 80, can_lead BOYZ. The Battlewagon text says "Each GHAZGHKULL THRAKA model takes up the space of 4 models". U_BOYZ_K is 10 models, so 10+4=14 of 22 fits.

MODE DETAIL (minor inaccuracy in the claim): DisembarkMove.select_mode chooses rapid only on the transport's flags.moved_this_phase (line 84). Only SurgeMove sets that flag (SurgeMove.gd:113). A normal move sets flags.moved, so the live engine actually picks tactical mode, still 3". TransportManager.disembark_unit then sets cannot_move and cannot_charge (281-294). The outcome is the same: an 80mm model cannot be placed.

COMBAT FALLBACK IS NARROWER THAN CLAIMED: DisembarkDialog only offers the Combat Disembark checkbox when the transport has NOT moved AND an enemy is within 3" + engagement range + base size of the hull (DisembarkDialog.gd:132-133, 151-185). So a human player has no way out after the wagon moves, and no way out at all with a stationary wagon and no enemy close. With an enemy close, the only option is combat mode: 6", a hazard roll per model, battle-shocked, no charge.

INGRESS: At edition 11, MovementPhase returns early on IngressMove.validate_setup (4290-4330). That calls _wholly_within_setup_distance (IngressMove.gd:62-70, 113-126). RAPID INGRESS (MovementPhase.gd:2459-2469) and the placement UI (DeploymentController.gd:3217-3221, 3281) use the same wholly-within-6" band test. A 180mm Stompa base has a 141.7px radius and the band is 240px wide, so it can never fit. FormationsPhase._validate_declare_reserves does not stop a Stompa going into reserves. It therefore can never arrive, and is destroyed at the end of round 3.

DEPLOYMENT: DeploymentPhase._validate_model_position (302) requires the base wholly within the zone, with no edge fallback. On the engine's Search and Destroy map (the MainMenu default, MainMenu.gd:190) the zones are 6"-deep L shapes (DeploymentZoneData.gd). The largest circle that fits has a radius of about 3.515", smaller than the Stompa's 3.543". So the Stompa cannot even be deployed on the default map, and the deployment clause matters too.

Minor: the claim's line ref MovementPhase.gd:8546 is the mode log line; the range check itself is at 8578.

## EA-30 · 03.03 (FAQ: single contiguous group) — Coherency accepts units split into several groups

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'When checking coherency for my unit can my unit be in multiple groups? No, it must be in a single contiguous group.'
- **Engine:** AttackSequence.check_unit_coherency (AttackSequence.gd:229-262) only requires one mate within 2in and every pair within 9in; it has no connectivity test. The deployment, movement, charge, fight, ingress and End-of-Turn checks all delegate to it (MovementPhase.gd:1025-1039, DeploymentPhase.gd:193-235, PhaseManager.gd:576-611).
- **Scenario:** A 10-model Boyz unit deploys or ends a move as two clumps of five, 3in apart, with every model within 9in of every other. The engine accepts this and never removes models; under the FAQ the unit is out of coherency, so the move is illegal and End of Turn removes models.
- **Fix sketch:** Inside check_unit_coherency, run a BFS/union-find over the 2in/5in adjacency graph and report every model outside the largest connected component as an offender, alongside the existing 2in and 9in tests.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute. FAQ 03.03 (errata md:254-255) says a unit cannot be in multiple groups and must be a single contiguous group; the launch text's What is Coherency sidebar (core rules :527) also says 'single group'. Neither .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md nor docs/ has any 03.03 or contiguity ruling. AttackSequence.check_unit_coherency (AttackSequence.gd:229-260) only checks, per model, one mate within 2in+0.05in tolerance and no model beyond 9in+tol (edge-to-edge, Measurement.model_to_model_distance_px). No connectivity test exists: a search of the engine for BFS, union-find, visited sets or component walks over coherency links found nothing. Every validator delegates to it: MovementPhase.gd:1033 (normal/advance/fall back) and 2509/4433/8579/8180->8277 (Rapid Ingress, reinforcements, disembark, group moves), DeploymentPhase.gd:228, the DeploymentController.gd:1897 UI gate, ScoutPhase.gd:677, ChargePhase.gd:2509, RulesEngine.gd:12672, FightPhase.gd:3871, MoveType.gd:67, ConsolidationMove.gd:162, ReactiveMove11e.gd:91/246, plus End of Turn PhaseManager.gd:591/609 and ScoringPhase.gd:634 via check_attached_unit_coherency -> attached_coherency_offenders -> check_unit_coherency. The developers' own test (tests/test_iss042_coherency_11e.gd:45-53) says 'The 11e 9in envelope is what closes this hole', which only holds for groups more than 9in apart (its fixture uses groups 10in apart). The scenario checks out geometrically: 10 Boyz on 32mm bases (1.26in) in two clumps of five (rows of 3+2, about 0.04in gaps) with a 3in edge gap have no cross-clump link within 2.05in, and the farthest pair is about 8.2in edge to edge, inside the envelope. check_unit_coherency therefore returns coherent; the move or deployment is accepted and End of Turn removes nothing. Small inaccuracy: the function ends at line 260, not 262. Two caveats for the fix, which make it bigger but do not weaken the claim. (1) Some code has its own copy of the two tests and would not be reached by a fix inside check_unit_coherency alone: DisembarkController._placements_coherent (DisembarkController.gd:577), CasualtyPreference.CoherencyModel (meant to give the same verdict as End of Turn) and AI helpers (AIDecisionMaker._cm_unit_coherent and others). (2) attached_coherency_offenders only flags a model when both readings condemn it (the merged Attached unit and each component alone). If each reading flags 'models outside the largest group', the two readings can choose different groups (on a tie, or when the leader changes which group is larger), the overlap is empty and a split Attached unit passes. Connectivity must be judged on the merged reading.

## EA-31 · 03.03.01 — Move-phase coherency ignores attached CHARACTER models; 11e ingress set-up skips the engine coherency check

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Coherency ensures units 'are set up and end every kind of move as a single group' (an attached unit is one unit, 19.01).
- **Engine:** _validate_unit_coherency_after_move builds its model set from the bodyguard dict only and maps staged moves by bare model_id (MovementPhase.gd:1041-1072; at :1061 a leader's 'm1' overwrites the bodyguard's 'm1'). End of Turn never condemns a single-model component (AttackSequence.gd:347-349). By contrast, ChargePhase includes the leader (ChargePhase.gd:2476-2500). At 11e, _validate_place_reinforcement returns valid right after the ingress template (MovementPhase.gd:4269-4316); its coherency block (:4418-4435) runs only on the legacy path, so only the deployment UI checks (DeploymentController.gd:973).
- **Scenario:** A Captain is attached to an Intercessor Squad. During a normal move the player stages only the Captain, 6in ahead onto an objective. The move confirms, nothing is removed at End of Turn, and the Captain holds the objective while still unable to be targeted separately from his far-away bodyguard.
- **Fix sketch:** At CONFIRM_UNIT_MOVE, validate coherency over attached_unit_component_ids, keyed by '<unit>:<model>', as ChargePhase._validate_unit_coherency_for_charge does. Let End of Turn treat a split-off leader as an offender. Run _check_models_coherency over every arrival group in the 11e ingress branch.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 03.03/19.01: when a player confirms a normal, advance or fall-back move (CONFIRM_UNIT_MOVE), the coherency check does not treat the attached unit as one unit. MovementPhase._validate_unit_coherency_after_move (1041-1072) measures only the bodyguard's models and keys staged moves by bare model_id (line 1061). Every leader's only model is "m1", which collides with the bodyguard's "m1", so the result depends on staging order:
- If squad m1 is staged after the leader, the leader's staged destination is dropped and he is never measured.
- If the leader is staged after squad m1, his destination replaces squad m1's in the check. That can falsely reject a legal move, or approve a stranded squad m1, which End of Turn later destroys.
- The leader is also ignored when squad m1 is dead or only one bodyguard model is left.

The claim's own scenario is wrong. Staging ONLY the Captain auto-translates the whole squad by his delta (_process_confirm_unit_move 3479-3517), and the bare-id substitution usually gets that move rejected anyway.

Working repro: attach a Captain to Intercessors. Drag the Captain 6" onto an objective, then nudge Intercessor m1. The move confirms with the Captain stranded: no auto-translate happens, and _move_attached_characters skips explicitly staged models.

End of Turn never removes him. check_attached_unit_coherency only condemns models that fail both the merged and the standalone reading, so a single-model leader is never an offender (AttackSequence 315-349, deliberately kept in line with the movement validator). ChargePhase, disembark and pile-in/consolidate already validate the whole attached group with composite keys.

Lower-severity second issue: at 11e, the server-side PLACE_REINFORCEMENT validation skips coherency. _validate_place_reinforcement returns valid at 4329 after IngressMove.validate_setup, and the 4418-4435 coherency block is legacy-only. Rapid Ingress checks the bodyguard only (2494-2511). Human players are not affected, because DeploymentController.confirm() (973) enforces coherency over the bodyguard plus leader before sending the action. The gap only affects AI, network and scripted arrivals, for example an AI leader auto-placed by _auto_place_attached_character_model. This was already logged as RS-13.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the core claim. The example scenario is wrong, though, and the ingress half is narrower than stated. All of this is from reading the code; nothing was run.

Rules: 19.01 says an attached unit is a single unit for all rules purposes. 03.03, and the 03.03.01 commentary, require a unit to be set up and to end every move in coherency. Neither the errata doc nor RULINGS.md has anything that exempts a leader.

Movement (confirmed):
- The only coherency gate at CONFIRM_UNIT_MOVE is MovementPhase._validate_unit_coherency_after_move (1041-1072). The UI Confirm button (MovementController.gd:1586) does no check of its own, and the MoveType after_moving_conditions are never called from MovementPhase.
- The check measures only the bodyguard's own models. It keys staged moves by bare model_id at line 1061 and ignores model_source_unit_id.
- Every CHARACTER in 40k/armies uses model id "m1", so it always collides with the bodyguard's "m1". The collision cuts both ways, depending on staging order (re-staging appends at the end):
  - If squad m1 is staged after the leader, the leader's destination is dropped and he is never measured.
  - If the leader is staged last, his destination stands in for squad m1's. That can falsely reject a legal move, or approve a stranded squad m1, which End of Turn then destroys.
- The leader is also ignored if bodyguard m1 is dead, and the check exits early when the bodyguard has 1 or fewer live models (line 1055).
- By contrast, ChargePhase 2476-2500, disembark (8515ff) and pile-in/consolidate all validate the whole attached group with composite keys.

The claim's scenario is wrong:
- Staging ONLY the Captain does not strand him. _process_confirm_unit_move (3479-3517) auto-translates every bodyguard model by the Captain's delta when no bodyguard model was staged.
- In typical geometry the bare-id substitution (Captain's 6" destination standing in for squad m1) makes the validator reject that move anyway.
- A real repro: drag the Captain 6" ahead, then nudge squad m1 (or have m1 dead, or only one Intercessor left). The move confirms. No auto-translate happens because a bodyguard model was staged, and _move_attached_characters skips the explicitly-moved Captain (9311).

End of Turn (confirmed): check_attached_unit_coherency (AttackSequence 315-349) only condemns a model that fails both the merged reading and the standalone reading. A lone leader never fails the standalone reading, so he is never removed. The comments say this is deliberate, to agree with the movement validator's bodyguard-only check, so it compounds the gap rather than ruling it legal.

Ingress (real in the engine, not visible to human players):
- At 11e, _validate_place_reinforcement returns valid at line 4329 right after IngressMove.validate_setup, which has no coherency test. The coherency block at 4418-4435 only runs on the legacy path.
- Human arrivals are blocked client-side: DeploymentController.confirm() (973) checks coherency over the combined bodyguard plus leader set-up.
- Only engine-only paths are exposed: AI, network and scripted actions. The AI checks its bodyguard formation (AIDecisionMaker 8101/8122), but the engine auto-places the leader in a spiral with no coherency guarantee.
- Rapid Ingress checks coherency for the bodyguard only (2494-2511).
- This gap was already logged as RS-13 in tools/ai_lab/rules_audit_11e.md:235.
- The cited line range 4269-4316 is slightly off: the 11e branch is 4286-4329.

## EA-32 · 04.02 FAQ — Unit-level target check requires one enemy model to be both in range and visible

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.02 FAQ: the enemy model that is visible to the attacking model and the enemy model in range of its weapon can be different models of the same unit. Answer: 'Yes'.
- **Engine:** RulesEngine._check_target_visibility runs the Hidden gate and LoS only on target models already within range, so it returns visible only for a same-model pair (RulesEngine.gd:6779-6790). get_eligible_targets (7277) and validate_shoot (6216) depend on it, and explain_target_ineligibility mirrors it (7422-7445). get_eligible_shooter_models already decouples range (nearest model, 11926-11951) from LoS (any model, 11965-11985).
- **Scenario:** A 10-model unit has its front models in a ruin 20" from a bolt-rifle squad: within 24" but obscured/Hidden, so not visible. Its rear models stand in the open at 25-26": visible but out of range. Per the FAQ each shooter may target the unit. The engine greys it out ('No valid targets in range and LoS'), and validate_shoot rejects it.
- **Fix sketch:** In _check_target_visibility and explain_target_ineligibility, compute any_in_range and any_visible independently over the target's models for each attacking model. Accept the unit when the same ATTACKER model satisfies both. Keep the Hidden gate on the visibility half.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real as stated. Corrections: (1) A greyed-out target shows explain_target_ineligibility's message: 'X is Hidden ... your closest model is 20.0" away', or 'No line of sight to X — terrain blocks every sight line' when obscured. Both are wrong under the FAQ. 'No valid targets in range and LoS' is the error validate_shoot returns when it rejects ASSIGN_TARGET/SHOOT. (2) The same coupled check (RulesEngine._check_target_visibility, which only runs the LoS/Hidden test on target models already in range, RulesEngine.gd:6779-6796) also drives the Fire Overwatch snap-target candidates (RulesEngine.gd:1518, via get_eligible_targets) and the AI's visibility check (AIDecisionMaker.gd:189-193). A shooter whose only possible targets look like this cannot even be selected to shoot (P3-96). (3) The hidden-followups branch applies the 04.02 FAQ only to get_eligible_shooter_models; its _check_target_visibility still requires the same target model, so this is not already being fixed.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: The errata/FAQ file (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:292-295) has this 04.02 FAQ: "can the enemy model that is visible to my model and the enemy model that is in range of my model's weapon be different enemy models in the same unit? A: Yes". The launch text already points the same way. 04.02 requires the target to be "Visible to the model that has that weapon" and "Within range of that weapon" (core_rules.txt:628-640), and 06.01 says a unit is visible if one or more of its models are (core_rules.txt:1099).

ENGINE (current branch): RulesEngine._check_target_visibility (40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd:6761-6799) loops over attacker model x target model pairs. The Hidden gate (6790-6794) and the LoS check (6796) only run inside `if distance <= range_px` (6779), for that same target model. Out-of-range models are never checked for visibility, and in-range models that are not visible are skipped. The function only returns visible when a single target model is both in range and visible; otherwise it returns "No valid targets in range and LoS". Every target gate goes through it:
- get_eligible_targets (7277) builds the target list (ShootingPhase.gd:189/205, ShootingController.gd:1199/1284/2640) and the Fire Overwatch snap-target candidates (RulesEngine.gd:1518).
- validate_shoot (6216) is the authoritative check for ASSIGN_TARGET (ShootingPhase.gd:883) and SHOOT (ShootingPhase.gd:1144).
- The AI calls it directly (AIDecisionMaker.gd:189-193).
explain_target_ineligibility (7422-7446) has the same coupling: LoS and Hidden are only tested on in-range pairs.

NOTHING HANDLES IT ELSEWHERE:
- ShootingType.target_allowed (scripts/rules/shootingtypes/ShootingType.gd:45-53) only checks 17.03 engagement. SnapShooting's unit-level LoS + 24" check is an extra check, and validate_shoot still runs after it.
- unit_sight_line / _has_los_to_target_unit do decouple, but they are only used for Indirect Fire penalties and codex helpers, not for the target gate.
- There is no developer ruling: RULINGS.md (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/) and the docs/rules audit files contain nothing on 04.02 model pairing.
- No test pins the split case. los_overlay_targetable_vs_visible covers a unit that is wholly out of range.

THE SCENARIO WORKS: TerrainManager.is_model_hidden (TerrainManager.gd:1001-1045) and the 13.09 text (core_rules.txt:2292-2303) are both per model. So front models in the ruin can be hidden while rear models in the open are not.

NOT FIXED ON ANOTHER BRANCH: on hidden-followups, commit 4c38460 adds an explicit "04.02 FAQ" comment and the Hidden gate to get_eligible_shooter_models (the per-model check) only. That branch's _check_target_visibility (~6825-6892) still requires the same target model. The developers applied the FAQ to the per-model check but not to the unit-level gate.

MINOR INACCURACY: the greyed-out reason the player sees is not the literal 'No valid targets in range and LoS'; that is validate_shoot's error. The toast (ShootingController.gd:5784) shows explain_target_ineligibility's text. In the scenario that is code "hidden" ("...visible only within 15" detection range; your closest model is 20.0" away"), or code "no_los" ("terrain blocks every sight line") in the obscured variant. Both are wrong under the FAQ.

## EA-33 · 04.03.02 — A model cannot split one melee weapon's attacks between several engaged targets

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.02 (fighting) and 04.03.02: a melee weapon may target several engaged units, no more than its A. The player declares how many of that weapon's attacks go to each target (at least one each), and only that many dice are gathered per target.
- **Engine:** AttackAssignmentDialog 'Split…' only divides a section's MODEL count between weapons/targets and requires more than one model (AttackAssignmentDialog.gd:135-139, 753). Assignments are {attacker, target, weapon, models} with no attack count (FightPhase.gd:1286-1291). _find_one_weapon_rule_conflict rejects a second assignment of the same model and weapon to another target (FightPhase.gd:1235-1257).
- **Scenario:** A lone Shield-Captain, Warboss or Dreadnought is engaged with a 1-model remnant and a full squad. It cannot put one attack into the remnant and the rest into the squad; all its attacks must go to one unit.
- **Fix sketch:** Add an optional per-assignment 'attacks' count. It is valid when one model+weapon has several targets, each engaged with that model, with sum <= A, >=1 per target, and targets <= A. Exempt same-weapon multi-target assignments from _find_one_weapon_rule_conflict. Gather only the declared dice in _resolve_melee_assignment_hits. Show per-target attack spinners in AttackAssignmentDialog, including single-model sections.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/dialogs/AttackAssignmentDialog.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: The launch text says a melee weapon may target one or more engaged enemy units, no more than its A (04.02, core_rules.txt:646-656). The SPLITTING MELEE ATTACKS sidebar (core_rules.txt:703-714) and errata commentary 04.03.02 (errata_faq_commentary.md:322-324) add: declare how many of that weapon's attacks go to each unit, at least 1 each, and gather only that many dice per target. No errata or FAQ changes this, and RULINGS.md has no entry on it (grepped split/04.02/04.03).

ENGINE, checked layer by layer:
(1) Dialog. AttackAssignmentDialog's Split mode divides a section's MODEL count. The spin boxes have max_value = g.models.size() (line 856), and _on_split_count_changed keeps the counts summing to the group size (1787-1819). The Split button only appears when g.models.size() > 1 (line 753). The 'Best Weapons' auto-plan also places whole models one at a time (1216-1300). A one-model section (Shield-Captain, Warboss, Dreadnought, or an attached leader component) can only cycle its single target.
(2) Action shape. Dialog assignments are {attacker, weapon, target, models} (1413-1418). FightController sends ASSIGN_ATTACKS with only unit_id/target_id/weapon_id/attacking_models (FightController.gd:2536-2542). FightPhase stores the same shape (FightPhase.gd:1284-1289) and reads no attack-count field anywhere.
(3) Validation. _find_one_weapon_rule_conflict (FightPhase.gd:1235-1254) returns a conflict for any second non-Extra-Attacks assignment whose models overlap, without checking whether the weapon is the same. So 'same model, same weapon, second target' is rejected by _validate_assign_attacks (859-861) and silently dropped in _process_assign_attacks (1278-1282).
(4) Resolution. _resolve_melee_assignment_hits gathers the full A of every swinging model for each assignment (RulesEngine.gd:13997-14040). Two same-model assignments would double the attacks, not split them.
(5) Developer acknowledgment. RulesEngine.gd:14119-14122 says outright: 'Melee attack-splitting isn't modelled, so a melee weapon always has a single target here.' docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md:94 likewise records 'melee gather + melee split-across-targets not implemented'.

The one contrary record, 11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:134, marks 'Melee split attacks across engaged targets' as done with only 'Melee path.' as evidence. The code shows it is not, so at most that row describes the model-level split.

The 'partial' status is accurate. Multi-model sections can send different models at different targets, which only roughly approximates the rule because the step size is a whole model's attacks. Single-model sections cannot split at all, so the scenario as described is correct.

Minor nits that don't affect the verdict:
- The cited line numbers have drifted slightly: pending_attacks.append is at 1284-1289, not 1286-1291, and the conflict function ends at 1254, not 1257.
- A fix would also need to gate [CLEAVE], which currently assumes one target (RulesEngine.gd:14119-14153), so that it applies only when all of a weapon's attacks target one unit (04.02.03 'single target').

## EA-34 · 04.03.03 — Weapons whose target stops being eligible are skipped instead of re-targeted

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.03.03: 'If a unit that was an eligible target for a rule or attack when it was selected stops being an eligible target …, the controlling player can select new targets.' Destroyed units cannot be targeted (05.04.04).
- **Engine:** Sequential shooting skips every remaining weapon whose target is destroyed (ShootingPhase.gd:3988-4013, 4209, 7497-7529), and melee does the same (FightPhase.gd:1731-1741). Models that lose range/LoS before resolution are silently dropped (RulesEngine.gd:4424-4436). No path offers a new target. Only charges re-select (ChargePhase.gd:862-895).
- **Scenario:** An Intercessor squad declares bolt rifles and its grenade launcher at a 2-model unit; the bolt rifles resolve first and wipe it. Under 04.03.03 the grenade launcher may pick another eligible target; the engine discards its attacks ('Skipped … target destroyed'). The same happens to a melee unit's second weapon. This reading treats 'destroyed' as 'no longer eligible' (05.04.04) and should be confirmed in RULINGS.
- **Fix sketch:** When the next weapon's target is destroyed or no longer eligible, recompute get_eligible_targets for that weapon and its bearers. If any target remains, pause for a new-target pick (AI: best target), re-run the 04.02 checks, then resolve. Skip only when nothing is eligible.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute this claim. Every code citation checks out, and nothing in the engine, codex scripts or rulings covers 04.03.03 for attacks.

RULE TEXT
- 04.03.03 commentary (errata_faq_commentary.md:326-328): if a unit that was an eligible target when selected stops being an eligible target, the controlling player can select new targets. Its own example is an out-of-phase move that takes the target out of range.
- 05.04.04 (errata_faq:395; launch core_rules.txt:4044): destroyed units cannot be selected or targeted by rules.
- The launch text's Resolve Attacks sidebar (core_rules.txt:676-678) links straight to 'Target No Longer Eligible or Viable', so the rule is meant to apply during resolution.
- 05.04's 'any excess attacks are lost' (core_rules.txt:810-811) only covers the save rolls in the batch being resolved. Weapons not yet gathered are not part of it.
- No RULINGS.md entry (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md), delta/doc-change audit or ISSUES.md entry decides 04.03.03 for shooting or melee.

ENGINE (all verified)
- ShootingPhase.gd:3987-4013: `_resolve_next_weapon` logs 'Skipped X — target Y destroyed' and moves on. Every human mode (sequential, sequential_staged, single-player auto-continue fast roll) goes through this loop.
- ShootingPhase.gd:4208-4213 and 7520-7527: weapons with destroyed targets are filtered out of the remaining-weapons list.
- The between-weapon UI has no target control:
  - NextWeaponDialog and WeaponOrderDialog only reorder, and they display targets read-only.
  - ShootingResolutionDock.on_next_weapon offers only 'Next Weapon'.
  - `_process_continue_sequence` (ShootingPhase.gd:7809) rebuilds the order as completed weapons plus the dialog's filtered list, so a skipped weapon is simply dropped.
- Bearers that lose range/LoS after targets are confirmed are dropped, with no re-target offer:
  - RulesEngine.gd:4424-4436 (auto-resolve path) and RulesEngine.gd:2213-2224 (staged path). The code comment there says 'deaths, reactive movement, LoS changes'.
  - A partial drop is only a print to stdout; the player sees a message only when every bearer is dropped.
  - This branch does not depend on how 'destroyed' is read: it is exactly the commentary's out-of-range example, for instance when an earlier weapon kills the closest target models.
- Melee: FightPhase.gd:1731-1741 skips with 'Skipped X — target destroyed'. The batch `resolve_melee_attacks` (RulesEngine.gd:13347) has no re-target either.
- No re-target code anywhere: grep for RETARGET, REASSIGN, 'new target', 'select new' and 04.03.03 across phases, autoloads, codex and scripts/rules finds only:
  - charge post-roll re-filtering (ChargePhase.gd:862-895);
  - Combat Embarkation's 'select new charge targets' (SMAbilitiesC.gd:1795);
  - the pre-confirm split-fire 'move' picker (ShootingController.gd:6130), which is the 04.02 select-targets step, not a mid-resolution re-target.
- Fire Overwatch also skips weapons once its target is destroyed (RulesEngine.gd:1308-1317), but that is fine because the stratagem fixes the target.

CAVEATS (not refutations)
- The headline scenario (grenade launcher after bolt rifles wipe the target) rests on reading 'destroyed' as 'no longer eligible'. 05.04.04 plus the 'or Viable' heading support that reading well. The claim itself says this should be recorded as a ruling.
- Existing behaviour is pinned by 10e-era design rather than any 11e ruling: PRPs/weapon_order_selection_prp.md:227, the unit test 40k/tests/unit/test_skip_attacks_target_destroyed.gd, and AttackAssignmentDialog.gd:2141 ('The surplus does NOT carry to another unit'). Any fix would have to update these too.
- Severity medium and 'partial' (only charges re-select) are fair.

## EA-35 · 06.01 FAQ (LoS from any part of the observing model) — Cover's 'fully visible' check and Explosives' 'visible' check use only the attacker's base centre

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 06.01 FAQ: 'Line of sight is drawn from any part of the observing model to any part of the model being observed.' 13.08: a model has cover if it is not fully visible to the attacking model because of terrain. Each part of the target therefore counts as seen if any part of the attacker can see it.
- **Engine:** TerrainManager._visibility_lines_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1193-1206) draws lines from o = the observer's centre (1194) to the target's centre plus 8 rim points. model_fully_visible_11e (1215) feeds unit_has_cover_11e (1421), which the live 11e resolver checks per attack (RulesEngine.gd:2788, 4975). model_visible_11e (1209) gates Explosives targets (StratagemManager.gd:3257). Targeting itself uses EnhancedLineOfSight, which samples points on both bases (EnhancedLineOfSight.gd:42-80), so targeting and cover can disagree.
- **Scenario:** A Heavy Intercessor stands just outside the corner of an obscuring ruin area. Lines from its base edge to a Trukk in the open are clear; lines from its centre cross the area. RAW: the Trukk is fully visible, so no cover. Engine: the Trukk is a legal target (base-sampled LoS) but counts as 'not fully visible' from the centre, so it gets the benefit of cover and every attack's BS is worsened by 1. The same geometry can also wrongly drop an Explosives target.
- **Fix sketch:** In _visibility_lines_11e, sample the observer's base too (reuse the EnhancedLineOfSight shape sample points or _sight_points(observer)). Count a target point as clear if any observer point has an unblocked line to it. Keep visible = any target point clear and fully visible = all target points clear. Add the usual distance bounds to limit the extra line tests.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/TerrainManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/autoloads/StratagemManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Not overstated; same claim with line refs updated to HEAD b48b429: TerrainManager._visibility_lines_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1316-1330) draws sight lines only from the observer's base centre (o, line 1317) to the target's centre + 8 rim points, contrary to 06.01 and its FAQ (LoS is drawn from any part of the observing model). model_fully_visible_11e (1338) -> unit_has_cover_11e (1511, check at 1543-1544) is the 13.08 not-fully-visible test, applied per firing model by RulesEngine._resolve_assignment_hits:2788 and _resolve_assignment:5028 (also preview_attack:18219, ModifierStack.gd:114, LOSLineVisual.gd:170). model_visible_11e (1332) gates Explosives targets (StratagemManager.gd:3260). Targeting samples both bases (EnhancedLineOfSight.gd:41-77), so a target can be legal yet still get cover (-1 BS on that model's attacks) when only a base-edge line clears a terrain corner, and an Explosives target within 8in can be wrongly dropped. The same centre-only observer also drives Gone to Ground's _obscured_by_dense_11e (1244-1256) and FactionAbilityManager._nearest_visible_enemy_within (2340).
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: 06.01 launch text (core_rules.txt:1025-1031) already says LoS is drawn 'from any part of that model to any part of the model being observed'; the 06.01 FAQ (errata_faq_commentary.md:405-408) confirms it. MODEL FULLY VISIBLE (core_rules.txt:1087-1093) and 13.08's second bullet (core_rules.txt:2278-2290) are therefore judged with lines from any part of the attacker. ENGINE (HEAD b48b429): TerrainManager._visibility_lines_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1316-1330) draws every line from o = _model_vec(observer), the base centre only (1317), to _sight_points(target) (1326, centre + 8 rim points); the comment at 1259-1261 says so. model_fully_visible_11e (1338) feeds unit_has_cover_11e (1511, check at 1543-1544), called per firing model by the live resolvers RulesEngine._resolve_assignment_hits:2788 and _resolve_assignment:5028, plus preview_attack:18219, ModifierStack:114 and LOSLineVisual:170. model_visible_11e (1332) gates Explosives targets (StratagemManager.gd:3260 -> the Main.gd:16271 enemy picker). Targeting (_check_target_visibility -> _check_line_of_sight -> EnhancedLineOfSight.check_enhanced_visibility) samples both bases, any shooter point x any target point (EnhancedLineOfSight.gd:41-77), so targeting and cover/Explosives can disagree. REFUTATION ATTEMPTS FAILED: no RULINGS.md entry on observer sampling (only Smoky Gubbinz at :146); the delta audit row for 06.01 (11th_edition_delta_audit.md:100) marks it partial ('9-point base sampling'), which records an approximation, not a ruling that it is correct; visibility_exclusions_for uses the observer's whole base but only when it overlaps the area, so it does not help an observer just outside; main has the identical centre-only code (TerrainManager.gd:1185-1186 on main); not on the already-being-fixed list; the test_los_line_visual (b) fixture still gets cover under any-part sampling, so it does not pin centre-only behaviour. GEOMETRY CHECK (offline Python copy of _sight_points, the segment-edge crossing test and the base-overlap exclusion; not run in Godot, as the task forbids): 40mm observer, 32mm target 20in away, obscuring rectangle 2-6in ahead starting 0.1in off the centre line, observer base not overlapping it: centre-only gives 6/9 clear lines -> not fully visible -> cover; any observer point gives 9/9 (one rim point alone sees all 9) -> RAW no cover. Explosives case with the target 7in away: centre-only model_visible_11e is false while EnhancedLineOfSight is true. MINOR INACCURACIES: line refs are stale (1193-1206/1194 -> 1316-1330/1317, 1209 -> 1332, 1215 -> 1338, 1421 -> 1511, RulesEngine 4975 -> 5028, StratagemManager 3257 -> 3260); cover is per firing model, so 'every attack' means every attack by that model. If anything the scope is slightly wider: the same centre-only observer drives Gone to Ground's _obscured_by_dense_11e (1244-1256, the -3in detection range applies too often), FactionAbilityManager._nearest_visible_enemy_within:2340 and unit_fully_visible_11e.

## EA-36 · 09.07.01 Commentary (one hazard roll per model) — Attached units — Desperate Escape by an Attached unit rolls and allocates for the bodyguard's models only

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 09.07.01: a unit making a Desperate Escape test 'must make one hazard roll for each model in that unit'. An Attached unit is one unit (19.01), and its mortal wounds follow 06.02 across all its models.
- **Engine:** FallBackMove.before_moving counts the alive models of _unit(board, unit_id) (FallBackMove.gd:79-95), which is only the bodyguard dict (MoveType._unit, MoveType.gd:87). MovementPhase applies the MW with Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e on that same dict (MovementPhase.gd:2944-2955), so MW left after the bodyguard dies are dropped as out.lost (Allocation.gd:360). WIND-SWIFT EVASION's fight-phase fall-back has the same limit (SMCodexAMovement.gd:627-650).
- **Scenario:** A battle-shocked unit of 5 Intercessors led by a Captain falls back. The engine makes 5 hazard rolls; the rules require 6. If the hazard MW destroy all 5 Intercessors, the remaining MW are lost instead of going to the Captain.
- **Fix sketch:** Count alive models across RulesEngine.attached_unit_component_ids(unit_id) for both the roll count and the all-MONSTER/VEHICLE check. Allocate the MW once over the whole Attached unit (06.02: non-CHARACTER models first) and write the diffs back to each component.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/FallBackMove.gd`, `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexAMovement.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** When an Attached unit makes a Desperate Escape, the engine makes hazard rolls only for the bodyguard's own models. FallBackMove.before_moving counts alive models of _unit(board, unit_id), which is only the bodyguard dict. Both callers pass only the bodyguard/head id: MovementPhase._process_begin_fall_back_after_windows (MovementPhase.gd:2914-2955) and SMCodexAMovement._fall_back_before_moving (WIND-SWIFT EVASION, 627-650). Combat disembark (MovementPhase.gd:9071-9086) and Shokk-boosta (OrkAbilities11e.gd:2468-2479), by contrast, loop over every component. So 5 Intercessors led by a Captain make 5 hazard rolls instead of the 6 that 09.07 and 19.01 require, one roll short for each attached leader/support model. The resulting MW are also allocated only within the bodyguard dict (Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e on the bodyguard, out.lost discarded). That allocation limit has no effect in the stated scenario: at most 5 MW against 10 Intercessor wounds, and with 1 MW per failure the MW can never exceed the bodyguard's remaining wounds. Under today's count, leftover MW can only be lost when every bodyguard model is MONSTER/VEHICLE (3 MW per failure, which is itself wrong when the leader is not M/V) or when WHERE D'YA FINK adds extra rolls; no shipped army pairs a leader with such a bodyguard. Once the roll count includes leader models, the fix must allocate across the whole Attached unit (06.02: non-CHARACTER models first, then the leader) and apply the all-MONSTER/VEHICLE test to the whole unit.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: 09.07 says "Make a hazard roll for each model in your unit (06.03)". The 09.07.01 commentary repeats "one hazard roll for each model in that unit". 19.01 (launch text line 3099) says "An attached unit is a single unit for all rules purposes", so the leader's model is in the unit and needs a roll. Under 06.02 the MW keep going until "that unit is destroyed". No RULINGS.md, ISSUES.md or docs entry records a deliberate bodyguard-only choice.

ENGINE: The roll count is confirmed wrong. FallBackMove.before_moving (FallBackMove.gd:78-95) counts alive models of _unit(board, unit_id). MoveType._unit (MoveType.gd:87) is a plain board.units lookup, and BasePhase.get_unit and GameState.get_unit do no merging either. CharacterAttachmentManager.attach_character only writes attached_to and attached_characters, so the leader's models stay in their own dict. Both callers pass only the bodyguard id:
- MovementPhase._process_begin_fall_back_after_windows (MovementPhase.gd:2914-2955). There is no loop over components; it calls Allocation.apply_mortal_wounds_11e(unit=bodyguard, ...) and ignores out.lost (Allocation.gd:360).
- SMCodexAMovement._fall_back_before_moving (627-650). WIND-SWIFT EVASION passes `head`, and only the fell_back flag is spread across the group.

Other "one hazard roll per model" paths do cover the whole Attached unit, which shows the fall-back path is an oversight: combat disembark (MovementPhase.gd:9071-9086) loops `for gid in group_positions`, and Shokk-boosta (OrkAbilities11e.gd:2468-2479) loops `for gid in group(unit_id)`. No test pins the bodyguard-only count: the "5 rolls" check in test_ork_codex_movement.gd:856 uses an Allarus unit whose decoded save shows attached_characters: [].

OVERSTATED: The claim says hazard MW that wipe the Intercessors are lost instead of reaching the Captain. That cannot happen in the stated scenario. At most 5 failures give 5 MW against 10 Intercessor wounds. More generally, with 1 MW per failure and one roll per bodyguard model, the MW can never exceed the bodyguard's remaining wounds, so out.lost is always 0 today. It is reachable only when every bodyguard model is MONSTER/VEHICLE: then a failure costs 3 MW (also wrong under 06.03 if the leader is not M/V), or WHERE D'YA FINK adds extra rolls. A scan of 40k/armies/*.json found no leader whose can_lead includes a MONSTER/VEHICLE unit. Spill-over to the leader only becomes a real requirement once the count includes leader models, which the fix sketch already covers. The player sees the defect as one roll too few per attached leader/support model in the dice log, about 1/3 MW less per leader on average. Severity is roughly low-to-medium.

## EA-37 · 13.01 FAQ (within a terrain area) / 13.08 first bullet — Benefit of cover's in-area test uses the base centre, not any part of the base

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 13.01 FAQ: models within the boundary of a terrain area are within that terrain area. 01.04.01: a model is within if any part of its base is within. 13.08: an INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM model within a terrain area qualifies for cover.
- **Engine:** unit_has_cover_11e calls area_at(p), where p is the model's centre (TerrainManager.gd:1410-1419); area_at is a point-in-polygon test (:847-852). Hidden (pieces_model_within, :927-941, 1035) and terrain objectives (Measurement.model_overlaps_polygon, MissionManager.gd:511-516) count any part of the base. Any-part is also the engine's own stated 01.04 convention (TerrainManager.gd:896-899).
- **Scenario:** A 10-model INFANTRY squad lines a ruin's edge. Every base touches the terrain area, but two models' centres are just outside it, and the shooter sees every model fully. Rules: every model is within the terrain area, so the unit has the benefit of cover (-1 BS). Engine: the two edge models fail the centre test, so the unit gets no cover. Yet those same models count as within the area for Hidden and for controlling a terrain objective.
- **Fix sketch:** In unit_has_cover_11e, replace area_at(centre) with an any-part test: pieces_model_within(m) is non-empty, or a shape-aware model_overlaps_polygon against the area pieces. Update the AI probe that copies this test (AIDecisionMaker.gd:29034-29076) to match.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/TerrainManager.gd`, `40k/scripts/AIDecisionMaker.gd`, `40k/scripts/LOSLineVisual.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute this claim. The engine tests the base centre for 13.08's in-area condition, but the rules use any part of the base.

RULES: The launch text 01.04 says to measure "to or from the closest part of that model's base". The 01.04.01 commentary says "A model is within a specified distance if any part of its base is within that distance". Its example applies this to terrain areas: a model is "wholly within a terrain area if no part of its base extends beyond the footprint". No errata or FAQ changes the first bullet of 13.08, which reads "INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM ... within a terrain area". The 14.01 diagram also counts a vehicle that only partly overlaps as "within the terrain area". The 13.01 FAQ the claim cites is really about models that are off the ground floor or overhanging, so it only loosely supports the claim. 01.04 / 01.04.01 is the real basis.

ENGINE (this branch): `TerrainManager.unit_has_cover_11e` (line 1511) builds `p` from the model's centre (1536) and tests `in_area_ok = ... not area_at(p).is_empty()` (1542). `area_at` (884-889) is a plain `Geometry2D.is_point_in_polygon` check. Every live cover decision goes through this function:
- `RulesEngine.gd` 2788 and 5028 (per-attack BS worsening in both resolvers)
- `RulesEngine.gd` 18216-18219 (preview)
- `ModifierStack.gd` 106-121
- `LOSLineVisual.gd` 170 (the on-screen cover colour)
- the SM codex helpers

Other "within a terrain area" checks use any part of the base:
- Hidden uses `pieces_model_within` (circle overlap, 964-978, called at 1070).
- Terrain objectives use `Measurement.model_overlaps_polygon` (`MissionManager.gd` 511-516). The comment there says the centre does not need to be inside ("mek-contested bug").
- The engine's own convention comment (`TerrainManager.gd` 933-936) says any part.
- `RULINGS.md` 494 and 507 rule that codex "within a terrain area" means any part of the base.

NO RULING FOUND: I searched `RULINGS.md`, `docs/`, the ISS-053 notes and the tests. Nothing chooses the centre on purpose:
- `11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md`:147 marks the in-area condition as partial.
- `.llm/ai-movement-intent-guide.md`:514 only describes the behaviour.
- The AI mirrors the engine on purpose (`AIDecisionMaker.gd` 29091-29092 says "TerrainManager.area_at tests the centre", and 29331-29336 / 29440 test attached characters' centres with `area_at`), while it uses any part for Hidden.
- `test_iss053` only places centres well inside or well outside an area.

CAN THE OTHER BULLET COVER IT? The not-fully-visible bullet does not hide the gap in practice. The Solid exemption is also centre-based (`visibility_exclusions_for`, 1005), so on old-style pieces with no `piece_class` the in-footprint sight points would be blocked and cover granted anyway. But every shipped layout is authored as "area" and "feature" pieces (734 area, 1276 feature, 0 old-style):
- "area" pieces never Solid-block.
- The target's own area is excluded from Obscuring (any-part test).

So an edge model with no wall between it and the shooter is fully visible and gets no cover. The AI checks pass an empty attacker, so that bullet never applies there. The same code is on main: the claim's TerrainManager line numbers (847, 1380, 1411) are main's, and its AI line numbers (29034-29076, `_mc_fit` / `_mc_fit_disc`) are this branch's.

SCOPE: The claim is accurate as stated. The gap shows when the shooter has clear lines to the edge models. If those lines cross a dense wall, the second bullet grants cover anyway.

## EA-38 · 13.08 FAQ (cover per attacking model) — Rapid Fire, Blast and +A bonus attacks take the first firing model's cover

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** FAQ 13.08: 'For each attacking model in your unit, determine whether the target unit has the benefit of cover.' Dice for attacks by models the target has cover against are collected separately.
- **Engine:** The per-attack -1 BS reads cover_model_per_attack and falls back to ms_firing_models[0] (RulesEngine.gd:2781-2792; 4968-4979). That array is filled only for each model's base attacks (2290-2293; 4498-4499). These bonus attacks add entries to bs_per_attack only: Rapid Fire (2368-2370; 4573), Blast (2386-2388; 4591), MA-29 +A (2410-2415; 4619), Try Dat Button! (2422-2427; 4631), Not a Shell Wasted (2433-2438; 4642) and Purgation Sweep (2449-2451; 4655). All of them are therefore judged from the first firing model's view.
- **Scenario:** Ten models with a [RAPID FIRE 1] weapon, all within half range, shoot a unit in the open. The first model in the assignment sees every target model only partly, past a ruin wall, so the target has cover against it; the other nine see the target fully. Rules: only that model's 2 attacks are at -1 BS. Engine: its base attack and all 10 Rapid Fire attacks are at -1 BS, 11 instead of 2. With the geometry reversed, the other models' bonus attacks lose cover they should have.
- **Fix sketch:** Append the bearer model to cover_model_per_attack wherever a per-model bs_per_attack entry is added (Rapid Fire, MA-29, Try Dat Button!, Not a Shell Wasted). Give Blast and Purgation bonus attacks per-model entries instead of falling back to model[0]. Apply the fix in both _resolve_assignment_hits and _resolve_assignment.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Bonus attacks from Rapid Fire, Blast, MA-29 +A, Try Dat Button! and Not a Shell Wasted take the first firing model's cover. Both live resolvers fill `cover_model_per_attack` only for base attacks:
- `_resolve_assignment_hits`: RulesEngine.gd 2291-2293.
- `_resolve_assignment`: 4550-4552 on this branch (4497-4499 on main).

The bonus attacks are added to `bs_per_attack` only (Rapid Fire 2368/4625, Blast 2386/4643, MA-29 2410/4668, Try Dat Button! 2422/4680, Not a Shell Wasted 2433/4691). The per-attack 13.08 cover loop (2781-2791 and 5021-5031) therefore judges all of them from `ms_firing_models[0]`. This breaks the FAQ's per-attacking-model rule whenever the models' views differ, i.e. a target that is not in a terrain area and is partly blocked from some shooters. Purgation Sweep is not affected: it applies only to [TORRENT] weapons, which auto-hit, so the BS worsening never matters. The fix is to append the bearer model to `cover_model_per_attack` in each of those five blocks (Blast needs per-model entries), in both resolvers, and to add a test with a Rapid Fire or Blast weapon, since E6 only uses a 1-attack weapon.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute the claim and could not, apart from one item.

Rule: FAQ 13.08 says to work out cover separately for each attacking model. The launch text for [RAPID FIRE] (24.30) and [BLAST] (24.05) adds the extra dice "for that weapon", so those dice belong to the model carrying it. RULINGS.md has no 13.08 entry.

Engine: in both resolvers, `cover_model_per_attack` is filled only in the base-attack loop:
- `_resolve_assignment_hits`: RulesEngine.gd 2291-2293.
- `_resolve_assignment`: 4550-4552 on this branch. The claim's 4498-4499 and 4968-4979 match main, which has the same code.

The bonus-attack blocks add to `bs_per_attack` only:
- Rapid Fire: 2368-2370 and 4625-4627.
- Blast: 2386-2388 and 4643-4645.
- MA-29 +A: 2410-2415 and 4668-4673.
- Try Dat Button!: 2422-2427 and 4680-4685.
- Not a Shell Wasted: 2433-2438 and 4691-4696.

The per-attack cover loop (2781-2791 and 5021-5031) sends every index past the base attacks to `ms_firing_models[0]`. Its cache is keyed by model id, so every bonus attack gets model 0's cover result.

Things that could have refuted it, and don't:
- `unit_has_cover_11e` really does depend on the attacking model, through `model_fully_visible_11e`.
- `ModifierStack` skips unit-level cover when `per_attack_cover` is true, so nothing else applies it.
- Nothing upstream splits batches by cover. `gather_identical_attacks` merges same-weapon assignments.
- The UI and AI build one assignment per weapon with every model that carries it (`ShootingController` 4604-4621, `AIDecisionMaker` `_get_alive_model_ids`). So a 10-model Rapid Fire squad is one batch and the fallback fires in normal play.
- The developers documented the fallback as a simplification, not as a rules reading: code comments at 2766-2769 and 5006-5009, and docs/rules/11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:287-288 ("bonus attacks fall back to the first firer").
- The E6 test uses a 1-attack weapon with no bonus attacks, so nothing tests this case.

The 11-instead-of-2 scenario arithmetic checks out, and so does the reversed case (9 instead of 18).

Overstated: Purgation Sweep. `CustodesCodexShooting.bonus_ranged_attacks_per_model` returns 0 unless the weapon is [TORRENT]. Torrent attacks take the auto-hit branch (2509 and 4761), and the cover loop sits inside the non-Torrent `else`. Those attacks never roll to hit, so the -1 BS cannot change anything.

## EA-39 · 14.02.01 Commentary — Objective control is settled after end-of-phase/turn rules instead of before them

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'At the end of each phase and turn, the first thing players do (before any other at the end of the phase/end of the turn rules or abilities) is determine which objectives are controlled by which player.'
- **Engine:** Movement phase: Rapid Ingress resolves before _complete_movement_phase (MovementPhase.gd:4958-4960, 2165-2196). That function's determination (:5831-5833) is followed by Thievin' Scavengers / Raise the Banner and the movement_phase_end windows (Fire Overwatch). Then a second full determination runs with secured-lock breaking on (_recheck_objectives_after_end_rules :5853, 5864, 5874-5877; check_all_objectives defaults to phase_end=true, MissionManager.gd:360). Charge phase: nothing is determined before SELFLESS SERVICE / HEROIC INTERVENTION; the only end-of-phase determination is MissionManager._on_phase_completed_11e, which runs after them (ChargePhase.gd:1812-1864; MissionManager.gd:128-130). Fight phase: the fight_phase_end window, Sweeping Advance and Acrobatic Escape all run before the determination (FightPhase.gd:5486-5534, 285-289). End of turn: after the check on entering ScoringPhase (ScoringPhase.gd:93), the engine removes out-of-coherency models, resolves Acrobatic Escape and end-of-turn redeploys, and runs the turn-ending hooks (ScoringPhase.gd:430-526; PhaseManager.gd:48-57,67-68). It then determines control again on turn_ending (MissionManager.gd:132-134) and scores primaries from that result (ScoringPhase.gd:530-532).
- **Scenario:** (1) End of Player A's turn: Player B's squad is down to two models, both on objective X but 3″ apart, so out of coherency. At OC 2 each they contest A's OC 4 on X. Rules: control is determined first (contested), then B removes one model, and A's end-of-turn primary does not score X. Engine: the model is removed first, the re-check gives A control of X, and A scores it. (2) Player B holds X secured with no models nearby. At the end of B's Movement phase, Player A Rapid Ingresses an OC 5 unit onto X, and B shoots it dead. Rules: the determination came before the ingress, and A has 0 OC at the end of the Shooting phase, so B keeps X. Engine: the ingress counts at the end of the Movement phase and breaks B's lock, so X ends the turn uncontrolled and B cannot score it. Heroic Intervention onto a secured objective at the end of the Charge phase breaks locks the same way.
- **Fix sketch:** Run one binding determination at each phase/turn end, before any end-of-phase rule. Call it before Rapid Ingress in END_MOVEMENT, at the top of ChargePhase._process_end_charge, before the Fight phase end triggers, and at the start of END_TURN before coherency removal, redeploys and hooks. The later re-checks (_recheck_objectives_after_end_rules, _on_phase_completed_11e, _on_turn_ending_11e) must not change stored control or secured locks, or score_primary_eot_11e should read a snapshot of the first result.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/ScoringPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/PhaseManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/MissionManager.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. Every engine fact in it checks out against the current code. The line numbers are off by about 1 to 3 lines, which doesn't change anything.

Rule: errata_faq_commentary.md:555-557 (14.02.01 Commentary) says control is determined first at the end of each phase and turn, before any other end-of-phase or end-of-turn rule. The launch text supports the same reading:
- 14.02 (core_rules.txt:2401-2416) determines control at the end of each phase and turn.
- 14.03 (:2427-2435) breaks a secured lock only on a greater enemy level of control at the end of a phase.
- RAPID INGRESS and FIRE OVERWATCH are WHEN 'End of your opponent's Movement phase', and HEROIC INTERVENTION is 'End of your opponent's Charge phase' (:2714-2741). The engine's own RI definition uses trigger end_of_enemy_movement (StratagemManager.gd:599-623).
- Regaining Coherency happens 'In the End of Turn step' (:532-536). That step resolves non-mission rules first, then mission scoring (:1202-1210).

There is no RULINGS.md or docs decision that adopts re-determining control after end-of-phase rules. The only rationale is a code comment (MovementPhase.gd:5870-5876), and it contradicts the commentary. The team's own research reads 14.02.01 the same way as the claim (research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md:4422: a WHILE THEIR BACKS ARE TURNED move only changes control from the end of the next phase).

Engine, phase by phase:

Movement:
- END_MOVEMENT goes to _continue_end_movement_rapid_ingress (MovementPhase.gd:4963, 2168-2201), which pauses for RI.
- _process_place_rapid_ingress_reinforcement sets the RI unit up and only then calls _complete_movement_phase (2710; the decline path at 2568, the overwatch paths at 1916/1992).
- That function's first check_all_objectives() (5833-5836) defaults to phase_end=true (MissionManager.gd:360), so the RI unit counts and the secured lock is erased (MissionManager.gd:925-937).
- After Thievin' Scavengers / Raise the Banner and the movement_phase_end window (fire_overwatch_11e, WHILE THEIR BACKS ARE TURNED, Finkin' Cap moves), _recheck_objectives_after_end_rules (5856, 5867, 5877-5880) and PhaseManager phase_completed → MissionManager._on_phase_completed_11e (128-130) determine control again, with lock-breaking still on.
- No snapshot or freeze of the first result exists anywhere.

Charge: _process_end_charge (ChargePhase.gd:1812-1865) runs the charge_phase_end window (SELFLESS SERVICE), then the HI window, then phase_completed, with no determination before them. The only end-of-phase determination is _on_phase_completed_11e. The other call, at :1497, is a mid-phase refresh with phase_end=false.

Fight: _run_end_of_fight_triggers (FightPhase.gd:5480-5535) runs the fight_phase_end window (KEEP IT RUNNIN' embark, INSTINCTIVE HUNTERS to reserves, WIND-SWIFT EVASION move), Sweeping Advance and Acrobatic Escape. Control is only determined after that, on phase_completed and in _on_phase_exit (284-289).

End of turn:
- ScoringPhase entry (≈:91) determines control at the right time, and secondaries are scored from that result.
- _handle_end_turn then makes the human coherency removals (429-446), the Acrobatic Escape vanish (448-467) and the end-turn redeploys (469-489).
- Next, run_turn_ending_hooks runs the AI coherency removal (_enforce_coherency_11e, PhaseManager.gd:576-611, which sets alive=false) before it emits turn_ending (PhaseManager.gd:48-57).
- _on_turn_ending_11e (MissionManager.gd:132-134) then determines control again with phase_end=true.
- score_primary_eot_11e (ScoringPhase ≈:528-530; MissionManager.gd:1823-1835) reads that result through objective_control_state. The EOT primaries hold_more, hold_central, hold_new, hold_min, per_new_objective and hold_enemy_home all depend on it (PrimaryMissionData11e.gd:94-168).

Both scenarios therefore follow the claim. Scenario (1): the removed model is skipped because it is no longer alive (MissionManager.gd:806-807), so A gets 4 vs 2 and scores X. Scenario (2): B's lock is erased at the end of the Movement phase, so X is 0 vs 0 after the shooting. One limit: a RI unit must be set up wholly within 6" of an edge (or Deep Strike, 20.04), and HI or other end-of-phase moves give the same result.

Nuances that don't refute the claim:
- In the Movement phase, the first determination is correctly placed for the secure abilities (14.03 FAQ). Only RI comes before it.
- At the end of the turn, the entry determination is correct. It is overridden only for primary scoring.
- When the re-check picks up Fire Overwatch casualties, it usually changes only control during the Shooting phase, not end-of-turn VP.
- The VP that actually change come from end-of-phase arrivals, moves or removals that hit secured locks, and from end-of-turn removals made before EOT primary scoring.
- The claim's 'partial' status and 'medium' severity are fair.

## EA-40 · 16.01 FAQ — Actions by units allowed to act while battle-shocked still fail on battle-shock

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
