- **Rule:** FAQ 16.01: 'If a unit has a rule that allows it to start an action when it is battle-shocked can it also continue doing that action if it becomes battle-shocked after starting an action? A: Yes'.
- **Engine:** ActionsManager.complete_actions cancels any action whose unit is battle-shocked at completion, with no exemption (ActionsManager.gd:272-276). can_start_action lets Conservai units (CustodesKatah.gd:543-547) and Tactical Mainstay units (SMAbilitiesB.gd:816-819) start actions while battle-shocked (ActionsManager.gd:137-146), so those actions always fail. RULINGS.md:16 records this as intended ('an action already started still fails if the unit later becomes battle-shocked'); the FAQ overrules that ruling.
- **Scenario:** A Space Marine Intercessor Squad (Tactical Mainstay) fails its battle-shock roll in its Command phase. In the Shooting phase it starts the Sabotage mission action on an objective; the engine allows this, and the action completes at end of turn (MissionManager.gd:1988-1999). At the end of the turn, complete_actions sees battle_shocked and cancels the action, so the Sabotage VP are lost. Under the FAQ the action continues and completes. The same happens to a Custodes unit under Conservai, and to such a unit that becomes battle-shocked after starting the action.
- **Fix sketch:** In complete_actions, skip the battle-shock cancel when the unit has a start-while-battle-shocked rule: reuse _cus11e_conservai / action_ignores_engaged_and_shocked, or set a flag in start_action that complete_actions checks. Update RULINGS.md:16.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/ActionsManager.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesB.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesKatah.gd`, `40k/autoloads/MissionManager.gd`, `.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md`
- **Verifier correction:** ActionsManager.complete_actions (ActionsManager.gd:272-276) fails every action whose unit is battle-shocked at end of turn. It has no exemption for the only two rules that let a unit start an action while battle-shocked: Intercessor Tactical Mainstay (SMAbilitiesB.gd:816-819) and the Custodes CONSERVAI ka'tah (CustodesKatah.gd:543-547). can_start_action already exempts both at start (ActionsManager.gd:137-146). This contradicts FAQ 16.01 / 01.07, and RULINGS.md:16 records the now-overruled ruling.

The player-visible loss is limited to actions whose completion does not need the unit to control an objective. Vanguard Operation (4 VP; completes if no enemy units are in the terrain area) started by a battle-shocked Tactical Mainstay or Conservai unit is failed. Because a started action makes the action path decide the score (vanguard_started_this_turn), it scores 0 instead of 4. Hold Position does nothing on completion, so only flags and the log differ.

The Sabotage example is wrong. Sabotage and Extract Intelligence complete only if the unit controls the objective, and a battle-shocked unit (OC '-') can never be controlling one (14.02). Those VP are correctly lost under the FAQ too.

The fix must do two things: exempt Tactical Mainstay and Conservai units from the battle-shock cancel, and make MissionManager.on_mission_action_completed_11e require the unit to control the objective for Sabotage and Extract Intelligence (today it checks only range). Without the second part, skipping the cancel would wrongly award those VP to battle-shocked units. Update RULINGS.md:16.
- **Verifier reasoning:** CONFIRMED CORE. The FAQ is real (errata_faq_commentary.md:593-594, also filed under 01.07 at :141-142). Launch 01.07 (core_rules.txt:220-221) says a battle-shocked unit cannot start an action, and any action it has started cannot be completed. The only two codex rules that let a unit start an action while battle-shocked are Intercessor Tactical Mainstay (SPACE_MARINES_GENERIC.md:452) and the Custodes CONSERVAI ka'tah (custodes/RULES.md:63). ActionsManager.can_start_action exempts both (ActionsManager.gd:137-146). But complete_actions fails any action whose unit is battle-shocked, with no exemption (ActionsManager.gd:272-276). It is the only live completion path, via the end-of-turn hook PhaseManager._complete_actions_11e (PhaseManager.gd:68, 554-565). The flag is still set at that point: in 11e, battle-shock is not auto-cleared (CommandPhase.gd:346-350), and Conservai lasts until the next Command phase (CustodesKatah.gd:247-253). RULINGS.md:16 records the opposite ruling ("an action already started still fails if the unit later becomes battle-shocked (core rule unchanged)"). Tests check only the start side (test_custodes_army_rules_11e.gd:651-673). No code anywhere applies the FAQ.

OVERSTATED. The Sabotage scenario is wrong. Per missionCards.json, Sabotage and Extract Intelligence complete only "if that unit controls that objective" (missions doc: a "hold-the-objective action"). Under 14.02 (core_rules.txt:2424-2427), a unit counts as controlling an objective only if it has a model with OC 1+. A battle-shocked unit has OC '-' (01.07). So those VP are lost even under the FAQ. The project's own research notes already say this (research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md:1617, :2061). The engine's completion handler for those two actions checks only range, not control (MissionManager.gd:2071-2104 using _nearest_objective_in_range_11e). So the current cancel happens to give the right 0 VP there. The claim's fix sketch, which just skips the cancel, would wrongly award VP to battle-shocked units.

REAL LOSS. Vanguard Operation has no control condition: it completes if no enemy units are in the terrain area. A battle-shocked Tactical Mainstay or Conservai unit can start it, because can_start_action reads the base OC stat and the mission gate has no battle-shock check. complete_actions then fails it. Once started, the action path decides the score (vanguard_started_this_turn), so it scores 0 instead of 4 VP (MissionManager.gd:2780-2785). The generic Hold Position action does nothing when it completes, and nothing reads the action_failed / action_completed flags, so for it only the flags and the log differ. Secondary-mission actions complete immediately and are not affected. The case where a unit becomes battle-shocked after starting is real in the engine but rarely reachable within one turn.

## EA-41 · 18.04.01 Commentary — Rapid disembark after an ingress move ignores the transport's ingress restrictions

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'When a unit uses the rapid disembark mode after its TRANSPORT makes an ingress move, the models must follow the same rules and restrictions as that TRANSPORT did', e.g. more than 8" from all enemy units and not within the opponent's deployment zone.
- **Engine:** _validate_confirm_disembark (MovementPhase.gd:8478-8608) checks set-up distance, engagement range (8558-8570), board edge, overlaps and the codex-only Drop Pod Assault 8" (8603). It never looks at the transport's ingress (flags.arrived_from_reserves from IngressMove.after_moving_effects, IngressMove.gd:138-146).
- **Scenario:** Round 2: a Trukk arrives from Strategic Reserves just over 8" from the enemy and outside their deployment zone, and its Boyz rapid-disembark 3" forward. The engine accepts models ~5" from enemy units and inside the opponent's deployment zone; 18.04.01 requires >8" and none in the opponent's DZ.
- **Fix sketch:** When the transport made an ingress move this phase, apply IngressMove.validate_setup's checks to every disembark position, in both _validate_confirm_disembark and the DisembarkController ghosts. That means >8" horizontally from enemies and no opponent DZ before battle round 3, or the Deep Strike variant if it arrived that way.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/DisembarkController.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/IngressMove.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with three corrections. At HEAD b48b429, neither _validate_confirm_disembark (MovementPhase.gd:8510-8641) nor DisembarkController._validate_disembark_position (324-384) applies the transport's ingress restrictions (core 18.04 WHILE MOVING, restated by commentary 18.04.01). The missing checks are more than 8" horizontally from all enemy units, none in the opponent's deployment zone before battle round 3, and arguably wholly within 6" of a battlefield edge (or the Deep Strike >8" variant). Only the Drop Pod Assault codex hook (8635-8640) has an 8" check, and only for its assault disembark. A loaded transport can be reserved, arrive by PLACE_REINFORCEMENT and have its cargo set up as close as 2" (engagement range) to the enemy, inside the opponent's deployment zone.
Correction 1: the engine does not label this disembark "rapid". DisembarkMove.select_mode keys rapid off flags.moved_this_phase, which only SurgeMove sets, so it resolves as "tactical" at 3". TransportManager.disembark_unit then applies cannot_move and cannot_charge from flags.moved, which matches rapid. A fix must therefore not depend on dis_mode == "rapid".
Correction 2: flags.arrived_from_reserves is never cleared, so test that the transport ingressed this turn (arrived_from_reserves_turn == the current round, with its owner active).
Correction 3: the claim's line numbers are about 32 lines stale.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute; static trace only, the game was not run. The rule is in the launch text, not only the commentary: core_rules.txt:3041-3044 (18.04 WHILE MOVING, rapid disembark after an ingress move) is restated by errata_faq_commentary.md:635. No RULINGS.md or docs/ entry exempts it.

The path is reachable end to end at HEAD b48b429:
(1) A loaded transport can be put in reserves. FormationsPhase._validate_declare_reserves (393-457) and the embarkation validator (325-391) never refuse it. The cargo keeps embarked_in with status DEPLOYED (1179-1190), and ScoringPhase.gd:976-982 already handles cargo of reserved transports.
(2) PLACE_REINFORCEMENT checks only the transport against IngressMove.validate_setup (MovementPhase.gd:4290-4330). _process_place_reinforcement (4681+) applies IngressMove.after_moving_effects (moved, arrived_from_reserves, no_moves_until_charge_phase; IngressMove.gd:138-146) and does not touch the cargo.
(3) get_available_actions (7910-7925) then offers DISEMBARK_UNIT. TransportManager.can_disembark only refuses advanced or fallen-back transports (TransportManager.gd:194-201). DisembarkMove.eligible (33-57) adds only the on-battlefield and embarked-this-phase checks. No reinforcements-step or ordering gate exists.
(4) _validate_confirm_disembark (MovementPhase.gd:8510-8641) checks the 3"/6" set-up distance, a 2" engagement range (8590-8602; 11e ER is 2", GameConstants.gd:32-33), the board edge, coherency and overlaps. Its only 8" check is the Drop Pod Assault hook (8635-8640 -> SMAbilitiesA.disembark_setup_errors 1196-1221). That hook runs only when the Drop Pod's F_DROP_POD_DISEMBARK flag is set, and it covers an 18.06 assault disembark.
(5) DisembarkController._validate_disembark_position (324-384) has the same gap, so its ghosts show green.
No reader of arrived_from_reserves (TransportManager, ModifierStack, codex made_ingress_this_turn helpers) constrains disembark positions, and the GameManager routes delegate to MovementPhase.

Inaccuracies in the claim: its line numbers are about 32 lines stale. The engine also never labels this a rapid disembark. DisembarkMove.select_mode (DisembarkMove.gd:84) keys rapid off transport flags.moved_this_phase, which only SurgeMove writes (SurgeMove.gd:113). A disembark after an ingress (or a normal move) therefore resolves as "tactical" (3"), and TransportManager.disembark_unit (281-295) applies cannot_move and cannot_charge from flags.moved. That is rapid-equivalent, but a fix gated on dis_mode == "rapid" would never run. Finally, flags.arrived_from_reserves is never cleared (it is missing from ScoringPhase flags_to_reset, 833-858), so "ingress this turn" needs arrived_from_reserves_turn == the current battle round with the transport's owner active, as SMAbilitiesA.made_ingress_this_turn (726-733) does.

## EA-42 · 18.05 Errata (filed under 17.03) — Emergency disembark destroys models instead of setting them up engaged

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Set up each model ... wholly within the set-up distance ... and as close as possible to that TRANSPORT. Or: If the above is not possible while remaining unengaged, set up that model ... as close as possible ... and engaged. Each model that still cannot be set up is destroyed.'
- **Engine:** _emergency_disembark_candidates rejects every spot within engagement range of an enemy (RulesEngine.gd:17441). _emergency_disembark_placements then destroys any model with no candidate (17397). There is no second, engaged pass.
- **Scenario:** A Rhino carrying 5 Intercessors is destroyed in the Fight phase while surrounded by Boyz, so every spot within 6" is within 2" of an enemy. The engine destroys all 5 passengers; the errata sets them up engaged, as close as possible to the hull.
- **Fix sketch:** In _emergency_disembark_placements, when a model has no unengaged candidate, build a second candidate list without the engagement filter before destroying it. Keep the other constraints: wholly within 6", no overlaps or walls, nearest first, coherency.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rules: the launch text of 18.05 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:3061-3078) says each model that cannot be set up wholly within 6" and as close as possible is destroyed. Set Up (03.02, :491-510) requires the unit to be unengaged. The official errata (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:609, filed under 17.03) adds a second option: if a model cannot be set up unengaged, set it up wholly within 6", as close as possible, and engaged. Only a model that still cannot be set up is destroyed. RULINGS.md (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md) records no decision on 18.05.

Live path: in ShootingPhase, FightPhase and WoundAllocationOverlay, `_check_kill_diffs` calls `_resolve_transport_destruction_if_applicable` (P1-60), which calls `RulesEngine.resolve_transport_destruction` (RulesEngine.gd:17252). That calls `_emergency_disembark_placements` (:17485). P1-60 runs before the older P3-32 `TransportManager.resolve_transport_destroyed` and clears `transport_data.embarked_units`, so P3-32's ring placement does not run after it. The ring placement has no legality checks anyway, so it offers no engaged fallback either.

`_emergency_disembark_candidates` (:17592) rejects every grid spot where `b.enemy and is_in_engagement_range_shape_aware` is true (:17625-17626, reason 'within engagement range of an enemy'). In `_emergency_disembark_placements`, a model with no chosen candidate goes into `out.destroyed` (:17582), and the caller kills it (alive=false, wounds 0). There is no second pass without the engagement filter. Main has the same code (:17436 / :17392), so it is not fixed there either. No codex or data override exists: abilities.json only mentions emergency disembark in the Deadly Demise text.

Existing tests and docs match the pre-errata behaviour:
- `test_core_rules_audit_e28e4f7.gd:404-426` places an enemy swarm on a 2" grid and asserts every passenger is destroyed. Yet each grid-square centre is 56.6px from its four neighbours, more than the 50.4px combined radius of two 32mm bases. Those spots are legal engaged set-ups under the errata.
- docs/CORE_RULES_REMEDIATION_2026-10.md (P1-4) describes only the 'outside engagement range' filter.
- research/ai_movement_intents_2026-11-07_details.md (ADJ-37) says outright: 'Neither implements the errata's engaged fallback.'
- The issue is not on the 'already being fixed' list.

The claim is not overstated. The gap is slightly wider than the 'surrounded' scenario: a model is also destroyed instead of set up engaged when the unengaged spots run out, or when the only unengaged spots would break coherency. The fix sketch is sound. It should also be gated to edition 11 and should update the P1-4 test.

## EA-43 · 19.04 FAQ (single-model rules such as Feel No Pain) — An attached CHARACTER's own Feel No Pain is not used while he is attached

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Rules that just apply to one model in an attached unit (for example, a rule that grants a model Feel No Pain or Lone Operative) only apply to that model' — so they still apply to that model.
- **Engine:** 11e allocation folds characters into a duplicate of the bodyguard unit (_build_attached_allocation_unit_11e, RulesEngine.gd:4367-4393); only wounds, save and invuln are copied onto character models. FNP comes from that copy: get_unit_fnp_vs(live bodyguard) at 4137 and get_model_fnp_vs(virtual unit, model) at 3744-3748 (get_model_fnp, 16808-16825). The character unit's own 'Feel No Pain X+' ability is never consulted.
- **Scenario:** Trajann Valoris (Feel No Pain 5+) leads Custodian Guard and a [PRECISION] attack selects his group. Failed saves allocated to Trajann get no FNP roll because the Guard have none; 11e rolls his 5+ for each wound. Also affects the Beastboss (6+), Ulrik (6+), Captain Titus (5+), Iron Father Feirros (5+) and Vulkan He'stan (6+) in the rosters.
- **Fix sketch:** When building the virtual allocation unit, stamp each character model with its own unit's best FNP (get_unit_fnp plus the conditional grants of the character unit). Have get_model_fnp / get_model_fnp_vs take the better of that per-model value and the unit-wide grants.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Accurate claim with current line numbers: `resolve_allocation_batch_11e` (RulesEngine.gd:4131) resolves every 11e save batch against `_build_attached_allocation_unit_11e` (4414). That is a deep copy of the BODYGUARD unit (its id, meta and flags). Each CHARACTER model copy gets only `is_character`, `alloc_source_unit_id`, wounds/save/invuln fallbacks and the Custodes `decorate_alloc_model` tag. The per-wound Feel No Pain at 3744-3748 calls `get_model_fnp_vs(bodyguard copy, character model)`, and `get_model_fnp` (16993) falls back to `get_unit_fnp(bodyguard copy)` (16877, which uses `get_named_fnp` over the bodyguard's abilities). So an attached CHARACTER's own Feel No Pain is never rolled on wounds allocated to him, whether from [PRECISION] or from a spill in the same batch. Example: Trajann 5+ with Custodian Guard.

The same root cause also drops these while attached:
- `stats.fnp` that list-build enhancements write onto the character (Artificer Armour / Standard of the Emperor Ascendant, SMDetachmentsA.gd:393-399)
- Genalchemic Warding (checked on the bearer's unit dict)
- Vulkan's Seeker of the Unfound 4+
- Kaius's Veteran Bodyguard 4+ (SMAbilitiesC.unit_fnp needs a CHARACTER unit dict, and the bodyguard copy never is one)

Devastating-wound and mortal-wound Feel No Pain is rolled once with the bodyguard's unit-level value.

The reverse also happens: a character without Feel No Pain would inherit a bodyguard's datasheet Feel No Pain through the same fallback. No current roster has this case.

Once the bodyguard is destroyed and the character is his own unit again, his Feel No Pain applies correctly.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. The rules text and the code path both support it.

RULES: Core rule 24.12 says "Each time a model with this ability would lose a wound, roll one D6", so Feel No Pain belongs to the model. The launch text of 19.04 says abilities that affect a single specified model "only ever apply to that model, even while part of an attached unit". The 19.04 FAQ says a rule granting a model Feel No Pain "only appl[ies] to that model". So Trajann keeps his own 5+ while attached, and the Custodian Guard do not get it.

ENGINE TRACE (current line numbers): Both shooting and melee 11e saves go through `resolve_allocation_batch_11e` (RulesEngine.gd:4131). That covers the AllocationGroupOverlay in ShootingPhase and FightController, and `_auto_roll_saves_11e`. It folds the characters in with `_build_attached_allocation_unit_11e` (4414). That function makes `combined = base_unit.duplicate(true)`, so the result carries the bodyguard's id, meta and flags. Each character model copy gets only: `is_character`, `alloc_source_unit_id`, wounds/save/invuln fallbacks, and the Custodes `decorate_alloc_model` tag (Praesidium Shield bearer, Moment Shackle 3+ invuln). No Feel No Pain is copied and no abilities are merged.

In `_apply_saves_via_allocation_11e` (3645), the damage_provider calls `get_model_fnp_vs(scratch_unit, models[model_index], ...)` at 3744-3748, which goes to `get_model_fnp` (16993). Trajann's model `m1` (cus11e_codex_test.json) has no `model_type`, so this returns `get_unit_fnp(scratch)` (16877). That function reads:
- the bodyguard's `stats.fnp`
- `get_named_fnp` over the bodyguard's `meta.abilities` (10091)
- the bodyguard's flags
- the codex hooks, called with the bodyguard copy

Custodian Guard Sodality has none of these, so the result is 0. The conditional Feel No Pain only applies to psychic attacks and mortal wounds. So a [PRECISION] attack, or failed saves that spill onto Trajann in the same batch that kills the last Guard, get no Feel No Pain roll.

NOTHING ELSE HANDLES IT:
- The UnitAbilityManager "Feel No Pain" row only matches the exact 10e name, and it writes to the character's own flags anyway.
- RULINGS.md:335 fixed named Feel No Pain inside `get_unit_fnp`, but that only reads the dict it is given, and here that dict is the bodyguard copy.
- The tests (test_sm11e_abilities_a/c, test_custodes_enhancements_11e) only call `get_unit_fnp` on the character's own dict. They never go through the attached allocation path.

ROSTERS: The leaders affected match the claim: Trajann 5+, Beastboss 6+, Ulrik 6+, Titus 5+, Feirros 5+, Vulkan 6+, plus Guilliman 5+ and Caanok Var 5+. In each case Feel No Pain exists only as an ability name, and none of their bodyguards has any.

Once the bodyguard is destroyed and the character detaches (19.05), he is targeted as his own unit and his Feel No Pain works. So the bug only happens while he is attached, as the title says.

The claim is not overstated. Its line numbers have drifted, and the real scope is somewhat wider than it states.

CAUTION ON THE FIX SKETCH: "the better of the per-model value and the unit-wide grants" would keep the reverse bug. A character without Feel No Pain currently inherits the bodyguard's datasheet Feel No Pain through the `get_unit_fnp` fallback. There is no roster case of this today. Only effects that target the unit (stratagems, auras, "this unit" abilities) should be shared across the attached unit.

## EA-44 · 19.04.01 Commentary — Dead leaders keep conferring abilities and enhancements (Custodes, Space Marines and Space Wolves modules)

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Once the models conferring such effects are destroyed, that attached unit does not continue to benefit from them.' Per 19.04 that is when the last model of that leader/support unit, or the bearer, is destroyed; if it died to an attack, the effect lasts until the attacking unit finishes.
- **Engine:** Common.attached_group_ids includes attached characters whose models are all dead (CustodesCommon.gd:163-174). The faction group helpers use it without an alive filter: CustodesAbilities group/group_holder (193-207), CustodesEnhancements group_units/group_bearer (238-262), SMAbilitiesA group_holder (308-323), SMDetachmentsA group_units/group_bearer (218-229, 323-329), SWAbilities11e group_holder (239-255). Characters leave attachment_data only on detach (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:205-228). Only the Ork module filters dead components (OrkAbilities11e.gd:258-267).
- **Scenario:** A Blade Champion (Swift Onslaught: re-roll advance and charge rolls) leading Custodian Guard is sniped; on later turns the Guard still get both re-rolls (charge_reroll_source, CustodesAbilities.gd:628-636). A Space Marine Captain's 'this unit' enhancement keeps buffing his squad after he dies.
- **Fix sketch:** Filter out components with no alive models in these group helpers, as OrkAbilities11e.group does. When the source died to an attack, keep it active until the attacking unit has resolved all its attacks. A revived character re-enters automatically.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesCommon.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesAbilities.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesEnhancements.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesA.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMDetachmentsA.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWAbilities11e.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 19.04 is not applied in the Custodes, Space Marines and Space Wolves modules. Attached characters stay in attachment_data.attached_characters after their own death: detach only happens when the whole bodyguard dies (check_bodyguard_destroyed), and ISS-059's "detach-on-death" is a stub in the test. As a result, CustodesCommon.attached_group_ids (163) and SWCommon.attached_group_ids (167; the SM modules use SWCommon) both return dead leaders. The helpers built on them never check for alive models: CustodesAbilities, SMAbilitiesA and SWAbilities11e group/group_holder/group_has, CustodesEnhancements group_units/group_bearer (238-262), and SMDetachmentsA group_units/group_bearer (230, 337). So the attached unit keeps a dead leader's abilities and a dead bearer's enhancements for the rest of the game. Examples: Swift Onslaught and Seeker's Instincts re-rolls, Unstoppable Valour, Bestial Fury, Oblivion Knight +1 to hit, Radiant Mantle -1 to hit, Martial Paragon, Gunnery Honours, Seal of Shrouding. Players can see this: re-roll prompts and modifier notes name the dead leader's ability. The generic UnitAbilityManager leader path and OrkAbilities11e.group already skip dead components, but none of these paths implements the "until the attacking unit has resolved all its attacks" grace.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: 19.04 (launch text, Abilities in Attached Units table) says a leader/support unit's ability applies to the attached unit until the last model of that leader/support unit is destroyed. An enhancement or wargear bearer's applies until that model is destroyed. If the death came from an attack, the effect lasts until the attacking unit has resolved all its attacks. The 19.04.01 commentary repeats this, and RULINGS.md has no ruling on it.

Engine facts, all checked in code:
1) A character dying never detaches it.
   - Model deaths only set units.<id>.models.N.alive=false. The unit stays in state.units.
   - CharacterAttachmentManager.detach_character (line 205) is only called from check_bodyguard_destroyed, i.e. when every bodyguard model is dead. There is no code that detaches a character when the character dies.
   - The only diffs that write attachment fields are set-up writes in DeploymentPhase/FormationsPhase and the Unleash the Lions split.
   - ISS-059's "19.04 expiry through the existing detach-on-death flow" exists only in the test: test_iss059_attached_units_11e.gd:93-94 sets alive=false and then calls cam.detach_character() by hand.
2) RulesEngine.attached_unit_target_id (4285) only checks that the bodyguard is alive. So for the bodyguard, head = bodyguard, and both CustodesCommon.attached_group_ids (163) and SWCommon.attached_group_ids (167) return the dead character id. Their only filter is units.has(cid).
3) None of the cited helpers checks for alive models:
   - CustodesAbilities group/group_holder/group_has (193-207)
   - SMAbilitiesA (308-323)
   - SWAbilities11e (239-255)
   - CustodesEnhancements group_units/group_bearer (238-262)
   - SMDetachmentsA group_units (230) and group_bearer (337)
   They read meta.abilities / meta.enhancements, and those are unchanged on a dead unit. Common.unit() does not filter dead units either.
4) Callers that use these with no alive check include:
   - charge_reroll_source / advance_reroll_source (Swift Onslaught, Seeker's Instincts; reached via EffectPrimitives.has_effect_reroll_charge/advance and ChargePhase:3413)
   - SMAbilitiesA Unstoppable Valour
   - SWAbilities11e Bestial Fury
   - CustodesEnhancements Radiant Mantle (-1 to hit against the unit), Oblivion Knight (+1 to hit), Auric Eagle
   - SMDetachmentsA Gunnery Honours, Venator, Martial Paragon, Seal of Shrouding, Linebreaker, Spearpoint Scouts
5) The engine does know the rule elsewhere, which shows these modules miss it rather than follow a deliberate ruling. UnitAbilityManager._apply_leader_abilities (3091) and unit_has_leader_ability (4352) skip dead characters, and OrkAbilities11e.group (260-267) filters dead components with a 19.04 comment. Some single callers also filter (CustodesKatah:228, SMAbilitiesC:757), but the shared helpers do not.

Small inaccuracies in the claim:
- The SM and SW modules preload SWCommon.gd (attached_group_ids at 167), not CustodesCommon.gd. The code is the same and so is the defect.
- The SMDetachmentsA line numbers are 230-241 and 337-343.
- player_visible should be true: the charge/advance re-roll prompt and modifier notes name the dead leader's ability.

Related but separate, not part of this claim: CharacterAttachmentManager.attached_unit_keywords (the 19.03 keyword union) also includes dead characters, so the pg-67 ANTI-PSYKER revert only happens in the test.

## EA-45 · 21.03 FAQ (declare before the advance roll) — Take to the skies can be declared or withdrawn after the advance roll

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'For an advance/charge move, this means you must declare that a unit will take to the skies before rolling for that advance/charge move.'
- **Engine:** BEGIN_ADVANCE records the payload declaration before rolling (MovementPhase.gd:1272-1277). But SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES accepts an ADVANCE whose roll is already made: it only checks that the mode is NORMAL/ADVANCE/FALL_BACK and the reduced cap (7192-7230, 7212-7213). After a re-roll pause, _resolve_advance_roll rebuilds the move with mode_locked=false (1557-1563), and MovementController shows the checkbox whenever the mode is unlocked (MovementController.gd:1798-1818, 1827-1853).
- **Scenario:** A FLY unit with CP for a Command Re-roll advances. After the D6 and the re-roll decision, the player ticks 'Take to the skies' only if the result still clears the wall. Or they un-tick a pre-roll declaration to regain 2" while nothing is staged. 11e fixes the declaration before the roll.
- **Fix sketch:** Reject SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES on ADVANCE moves once advance_roll is set (keep it for NORMAL and FALL_BACK). Keep the checkbox hidden after an advance roll, and preserve mode_locked through _resolve_advance_roll.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/MovementController.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, and wider than stated. _validate_set_take_to_skies (MovementPhase.gd:7223-7261) accepts SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES on an ADVANCE whose roll is already made, in both directions: tick on if no model has moved past (M-2)+roll, tick off if nothing is staged. It rebuilds the cap from the stored advance_roll (_take_to_skies_recomputed_cap 7293-7304). The UI exposes this after ANY advance roll, not only after a re-roll pause. The checkbox's visibility is only re-checked in _reset_mode_selection_for_new_unit, which runs on unit selection, begin_unit_movement and disembark (MovementController.gd:1961/1983). _on_movement_mode_locked (2316) and _on_unit_move_begun (2015) never hide it again, so the box shown at selection stays clickable after Confirm Movement Mode and the roll. The J hotkey works too, and _on_take_to_skies_toggled (1827-1853) sends the action without checking mode_locked or the roll. A Command Re-roll pause also leaves the final move with mode_locked=false: _resolve_advance_roll always builds it unlocked (1561-1566), and the LOCK sent during the pause lands on the stale auto-begun NORMAL entry. That also exposes the pad chip (pad_menu_options 6453, _pad_mode_resolved 6510). tests/test_take_to_skies_toggle.gd T5 (155-170) asserts that ticking and un-ticking after the roll are accepted, and no RULINGS or doc entry justifies this against the 21.03 FAQ.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute this. I only read the code; the game was not run, per the task constraints.

RULE: The FAQ for 21.03 (errata_faq_commentary.md:679-682) says the effects of taking to the skies apply when the unit is selected to move, and that for an advance or charge move "you must declare that a unit will take to the skies before rolling for that advance/charge move". The launch text (core_rules.txt:3363-3366) already says "before moving any models". I found no ruling in .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md or docs/ that allows a declaration after the roll.

ENGINE:
(1) BEGIN_ADVANCE stores the declaration before the D6 in _pending_take_to_skies (MovementPhase.gd:1275-1280). _resolve_advance_roll applies it (1481-1488). That part is correct.
(2) _validate_set_take_to_skies (7223-7261) checks only: actor, edition, FLY, Madcap 'grounded', that a move is active and not completed, and that the mode is NORMAL, ADVANCE or FALL_BACK (7243-7244). It never checks advance_roll, the advanced flag or mode_locked.
   - Ticking on is refused only if a model has already moved past the reduced cap (7247-7255).
   - Un-ticking is refused only if something is staged (7256-7260).
   - _take_to_skies_recomputed_cap (7293-7304) rebuilds the cap from the stored advance_roll, so both directions work after the roll.
(3) The repo's own headless test codifies this. tests/test_take_to_skies_toggle.gd T5 (lines 155-170) runs BEGIN_ADVANCE, reads the roll, then asserts "toggle accepted on an Advance" (cap becomes 10 + roll) and that un-ticking restores 12 + roll.

UI EXPOSURE: confirmed, and wider than the claim says.
- MovementController only re-checks the checkbox's visibility in _update_take_to_skies_visibility. That runs only from _reset_mode_selection_for_new_unit (1961/1983), which is called on unit selection (1333), begin_unit_movement (1398) and disembark (3439).
- Neither _on_movement_mode_locked (2316) nor _on_unit_move_begun (2015) hides it again. So the box shown when the unit was selected stays visible after Confirm Movement Mode and the roll, even with no re-roll pause.
- _on_take_to_skies_toggled (1827-1853) and the J hotkey (1890-1900) send SET_TAKE_TO_SKIES without checking mode_locked or the roll.
- The re-roll mechanism in the claim is also correct:
  - _resolve_advance_roll always rebuilds the move with mode_locked=false (1561-1566).
  - During a Command Re-roll pause, the LOCK_MOVEMENT_MODE sent right after BEGIN_ADVANCE (MovementController.gd:1679-1683) lands on the stale auto-begun NORMAL entry. has_pending_stratagem_window (BasePhase.gd:456) only covers stratagem windows, not re-roll pauses, so the lock goes through.
  - USE/DECLINE_COMMAND_REROLL then rebuilds the move unlocked. That also brings back the pad 'Take to the skies' chip (pad_menu_options 6453, _pad_mode_resolved 6510).

SIDE NOTE (outside this claim): ticking on is also accepted after models have been staged, as long as they fit under the reduced cap. This applies to NORMAL, ADVANCE and FALL_BACK, and goes against even the launch text's "before moving any models". So the fix sketch's "keep it for NORMAL and FALL_BACK" still leaves that gap.

The claim is accurate, not overstated. 'corrected' only sharpens the mechanism and line numbers.

## EA-46 · 24.28.01 Commentary — [PRECISION] + [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] mortal wounds skip the selected CHARACTER group

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Mortal Wounds inflicted by a [PRECISION] attack with the [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] ability are first applied to the CHARACTER group that was selected for those [PRECISION] attacks (if that group is not already destroyed).'
- **Engine:** The precision group only reorders the save allocation (RulesEngine.gd:3661-3669). The devastating-wound loop calls Allocation.apply_devastating_wounds_11e(target_unit, 1, dmg, preferred_targets) (RulesEngine.gd:3836). Its select_mortal_wound_target follows the 06.02 order, non-CHARACTER first (Allocation.gd:376-399).
- **Scenario:** Ghazghkull's Adamantine 'Eadbutt ([PRECISION][DEVASTATING WOUNDS]) attacks Intercessors led by a Captain and selects the Captain's group. A critical wound's mortal wounds kill an Intercessor instead of hitting the Captain. The same applies to the Judiciar's Executioner Relic Blade and Kor'sarro Khan's Moonfang.
- **Fix sketch:** Pass the precision group id into the devastating-wound loop. Make apply_devastating_wounds_11e pick from that group's alive models (wounded first) while it has any, then fall back to the 06.02 order.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/Allocation.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute; the static trace is unambiguous.

RULE: The official commentary (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:723-725, 24.28.01) says mortal wounds from a [PRECISION] attack with [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] go first to the CHARACTER group selected for those [PRECISION] attacks, if that group is not already destroyed. The launch text makes the selected group the current allocation group only for normal saves (core_rules.txt:3856-3876, 24.28). It also makes [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] critical wounds into mortal wounds against the target unit, applied after normal damage and capped at one model per critical wound (core_rules.txt:3664-3700, 24.10). Without the commentary, those mortal wounds would follow the 06.02 order, which takes non-CHARACTER models first.

ENGINE (40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd is unmodified in the working tree):
- `_apply_saves_via_allocation_11e` reads `precision_gid` only to move that group to the front of the save `order` (RulesEngine.gd:3660-3669).
- The [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] loop (RulesEngine.gd:3807-3855) never reads `precision_gid`. Each critical wound calls `Allocation.apply_devastating_wounds_11e(target_unit, 1, dmg, preferred_targets)` (RulesEngine.gd:3845).
- That function (Allocation.gd:376-399) picks its target with `select_mortal_wound_target` (Allocation.gd:297-337). The order there is fixed: wounded non-CHARACTER, then any non-CHARACTER, then wounded CHARACTER, then any CHARACTER. `preferred` only changes the pick within the class that already won, so it cannot lift a CHARACTER above a living bodyguard. In the folded attached unit the Captain is the only model with `is_character=true`, so an Intercessor is always picked first.

EVERY 11e ENTRY POINT REACHES THIS LOOP:
- `resolve_allocation_batch_11e` (RulesEngine.gd:4132-4208). It builds the folded attached unit and passes `precision_group` from the attacker's pick in the overlay (AllocationGroupOverlay.gd:398-414 and 740-748). Human defenders use it in both shooting and fight, and `ShootingPhase._auto_roll_saves_11e` uses it for AI or delegated defenders (ShootingPhase.gd:3779).
- The ranged auto-resolve path (RulesEngine.gd:5744-5757).
- The melee auto path, `_resolve_melee_assignment_saves` (RulesEngine.gd:15248-15266).
- The melee interactive `save_data` carries `has_devastating_wounds`, `devastating_wounds` and `has_precision` (RulesEngine.gd:13888-13898 and 16244-16257), so the fight scenario does reach the loop.

NOTHING ELSE HANDLES IT:
- `CasualtyPreference.gd` and the codex scripts have no precision-aware mortal-wound routing.
- Other `precision_wounds` consumers (ShootingPhase.gd:3600, RulesEngine.gd:15518+, WoundAllocationOverlay.gd) are 10e-only paths behind edition gates. `GameConstants.edition` defaults to 11 (40k/scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27).
- `devastating_wounds_choice=="normal"` (RulesEngine.gd:3184, 5338, 14474) is the attacker opting out, turning critical wounds into normal saves. It does not implement the commentary.
- No ruling in .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md, no 24.28.01 row in docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md, and no test covers the combination. test_iss047 sections E and E2 test precision with saves only; E3 tests the devastating-wounds choice without precision.
- No commit on any branch mentions it, and it is not on the list of fixes in progress elsewhere.

SCENARIO WEAPONS CONFIRMED to have both abilities:
- Adamantine 'Eadbutt, D3+3 (40k/armies/orks.json:2811-2829).
- Executioner Relic Blade (40k/armies/sm11e_codex_test.json:9956-9972).
- Moonfang (40k/armies/sm11e_codex_test.json:10243-10262).
- Space Wolves Headhunters also grants both (SWAbilities11e.gd:609).
The fight result is as the claim says: a critical wound kills one W2 Intercessor (the rest of its mortal wounds are lost) while the selected Captain is untouched.

SCOPE AND CITATIONS (not material):
- The wrong result only appears when a non-CHARACTER model is still alive next to the selected CHARACTER group, which in practice means attached units. A lone CHARACTER target, or one whose bodyguards are all dead, gets the right result by accident.
- Line numbers drift slightly: the call is at RulesEngine.gd:3845, not 3836. The 06.02 picker is at Allocation.gd:297-337; lines 376-399 are `apply_devastating_wounds_11e`, which calls it.
- A CHARACTER group always holds exactly one model, so the fix sketch's "wounded first within the group" makes no difference.
- Separate observation, not checked in depth: the melee auto path passes the unfolded bodyguard unit (`units.get(target_id)`, RulesEngine.gd:15215). In that path the Captain is not in any allocation group, so precision cannot promote him there either.

## EA-47 · FAQ under 11.03 (a 'can move up to X"' consolidation rule changes the maximum distance) — Consolidation validator ignores consolidation-distance bonuses such as TACTICAL FOCUS

- **Severity:** medium · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** FAQ: 'If I have a rule that says my unit can move up to 6" when it makes a pile-in move or consolidation move, does this also change the maximum distance of those moves to 6"? Yes.' TACTICAL FOCUS: 'When making that consolidation move, your unit can move up to D3+3".'
- **Engine:** _validate_consolidate_11e caps each moved model at _fight_effective_move_cap(...) with its default 3" base (FightPhase.gd:6610, via 6382-6402); the only adjustments it applies are the Custodes Unstoppable Destroyer and Dacatarai caps. effect_consolidate_max (set by TACTICAL FOCUS, SMCodexAMovement.gd:89/199, and by ALWAYS LOOKIN' FER A FIGHT, StratagemManager.gd:2581) is read only by _get_consolidation_distance (FightPhase.gd:2853-2880), which the UI uses as the drag limit (FightController.gd:2977-2978, 3000-3001).
- **Scenario:** An Assault Intercessor Squad (Tacticus Attack Force) spends 1 CP on TACTICAL FOCUS and rolls D3 = 2. The drag allows 5", but the CONSOLIDATE action is rejected with 'consolidation exceeds 3" limit', so the CP buys nothing. 11e allows the 5" move.
- **Fix sketch:** Compute the consolidation base once (the larger of 3", effect_consolidate_max and any other 'up to X"' grant) and pass it as base_cap to _fight_effective_move_cap in _validate_consolidate_11e. Apply the Custodes fight_move_cap adjustment exactly once so Dacatarai's -2" is not applied twice.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/FightController.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexAMovement.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** TACTICAL FOCUS's D3+3" consolidation, which the 11.03 FAQ confirms is the move's new maximum distance, is never usable. Nothing that limits the move reads effect_consolidate_max:
- the validator: _validate_consolidate_11e, FightPhase.gd:6610, base 3" plus the Custodes cap only;
- the UI drag clamp, ghost, range rings and drop verdict: FightController._fight_move_base_cap() at 4172-4183, used at 3291, 3567, 4048, 4156 and 4284;
- the AI consolidation planner: AIDecisionMaker._fight_move_cap_px(unit_id) at 22306 and 22617.

_get_consolidation_distance only feeds ConsolidateDialog's "Move up to N"" text. Example: an Assault Intercessor Squad spends 1 CP and rolls D3 = 2. The dialog says "Move up to 5.0"", but the range rings are 3" and every drag snaps back to 3", so the player gets nothing for the CP. Any payload over 3" sent directly is rejected with 'consolidation exceeds 3" limit'. The AI also spends the CP and moves at most 3".

Fix: compute the base once as max(3", effect_consolidate_max), then feed it to the validator, the controller's _fight_move_base_cap and the AI's _fight_move_cap_px. Apply the Custodes fight_move_cap adjustment only once.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the core of the claim. The details of how it shows up in play are wrong.

Rule: TACTICAL FOCUS (Stratagems.csv / space-marines RULES.md:635) says "your unit can move up to D3+3"" for that consolidation move. The 11.03 FAQ (errata_faq_commentary.md:497-498) says yes, an "up to X"" rule changes the maximum distance. The launch text (12.08) gives MAXIMUM DISTANCE: 3". RULINGS.md WP-M3a:403 only records that TACTICAL FOCUS "is written as the engine's effect_consolidate_max". No ruling limits the bonus.

Engine:
- The CONSOLIDATE validator is FightPhase.gd:583 -> _validate_consolidate -> _validate_consolidate_11e. At line 6610 it calls _fight_effective_move_cap(...) with the default base of 3.0. _fight_base_move_cap -> CustodesDetachments.fight_move_cap (lines 718-737) only applies Unstoppable Destroyer and Dacatarai. A 5" move gives dist 5 > 3 + 0.02, which raises "Model X consolidation exceeds 3" limit". _process_consolidate_step_11e adds no cap of its own, and SM fight_move_gate is not involved.
- effect_consolidate_max is read in one place only: _get_consolidation_distance (FightPhase.gd:2853-2880).
- SMCodexAMovement.gd:189-199 writes the flag at use time and clears it at phase end, so the flag is live when the move is validated. Even so, TACTICAL FOCUS never grants any extra distance.

What the claim gets wrong:
1. "The drag allows 5"" is false. FightController.gd:2977/3000 pass _get_consolidation_distance into ConsolidateDialog.setup, and there max_distance is only used in the "Move up to %.1f"" rules text (ConsolidateDialog.gd:486-494). The actual drag limits all use FightController._fight_move_base_cap() (4172-4183), which calls phase._fight_base_move_cap(unit_id) with a 3" base. That covers the release clamp (4048), the live ghost clamp (4156), the pivot clamp (4284), the range rings (3291) and the green/red verdict (3567). So in the UI the dialog promises "up to 5.0"" while the rings are 3" and models snap back to 3". The CONSOLIDATE action is then accepted, not rejected. The 'exceeds 3" limit' rejection only happens when a payload over 3" is sent directly (dispatch_action, network or a script).
2. The AI is affected too. AIDecisionMaker._fight_move_cap_px(unit_id) defaults to a 3" base (22306, 22617), so the AI also spends the CP and still moves at most 3".
3. ALWAYS LOOKIN' FER A FIGHT (StratagemManager.gd:2581) is a legacy 10e effect with no row in the 11e data. TACTICAL FOCUS is the reachable 11e case.
4. Minor: _cus11e_step_unit_ready (FightPhase.gd:4182) logs "ready to consolidate (up to 3")" even after TACTICAL FOCUS.

Tests miss it: test_sm11e_stratagems_a.gd:1071-1076 and tests/scenarios/sp/sm11e_tactical_focus.json only check _get_consolidation_distance and the flag. Neither performs a consolidation longer than 3".

## EA-48 · 01.02.02 Persisting Rules Effects — Damocles-class Uplink doctrine lasts until the next Command phase instead of ending with that Movement phase

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.02.02: an effect with a WHEN and no stated duration is active only for that period. Damocles-class Uplink: 'In your Movement phase ... select one combat doctrine. That combat doctrine is active for that INFANTRY unit.' (no duration).
- **Engine:** SMDetachmentsA._use_damocles writes the per-unit doctrine with sw11e_doctrine_expires_player (SMDetachmentsA.gd:953-975). SWArmyRules11e.on_command_phase_start removes it only at the start of the player's next Command phase (SWArmyRules11e.gd:302-321). This is recorded as a ruling in RULINGS.md:355 ('no printed duration — the Combat Doctrines / Adaptive Strategy duration').
- **Scenario:** Gauntlet Task Force: the embarked Captain's Uplink gives an Intercessor squad the devastator doctrine in the Movement phase, and the squad advances. In the Shooting phase the engine still treats the doctrine as active (ranged_attacks_have_assault, SWArmyRules11e.gd:214-215), so the squad assault-shoots with every ranged weapon. Under 01.02.02 the doctrine ended with the Movement phase, so the advanced squad could only shoot [ASSAULT] weapons (10.05). The Uplink doctrine also keeps the army doctrine off that unit for the rest of the round.
- **Fix sketch:** Expire the Uplink's per-unit doctrine (and marker sm11e_damocles_doctrine) at the end of that Movement phase and restore the army doctrine. For the assault and tactical riders, which belong to a move made while the doctrine was active, stamp the advance or fall-back move at move time so expiring the doctrine does not take them away.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMDetachmentsA.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWArmyRules11e.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Damocles-class Uplink prints no duration, so under 01.02.02 its doctrine is active only until the end of that Movement phase. The engine keeps it active until the start of the player's next Command phase:
- SMDetachmentsA._use_damocles (SMDetachmentsA.gd:953-974) writes sw11e_doctrine with sw11e_doctrine_expires_player set to the player.
- Only SWArmyRules11e.on_command_phase_start (SWArmyRules11e.gd:302-321) clears it.
- RULINGS.md:355 records this choice, but predates the commentary and contradicts it.

As a result, a squad given the devastator doctrine that advanced can assault-shoot every ranged weapon in the Shooting phase (AssaultShooting._sw11e_assault / ranged_attacks_have_assault, SWArmyRules11e.gd:214). Under 10.05 it could only shoot its [ASSAULT] weapons. Other rules that check for an active doctrine also keep reading it through the rest of the turn and the opponent's turn: Laurels of Triumph, Venator Omni-auspex, Spear of Chogoris, and the tactical reactions in SMCodexAMovement.

Fix:
- Expire the Uplink doctrine at the end of that Movement phase.
- Record the assault/tactical rider on any advance or fall-back made while the doctrine was active, so the charge/shoot eligibility it granted survives the expiry.
- Do NOT restore the army doctrine afterwards. Under the Combat Doctrines rule ("any combat doctrine previously active for that unit is no longer active for that unit"), the replaced army doctrine stays off that unit, so the unit has no doctrine until the next selection.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute the claim and could not; the core of it is confirmed.

RULE TEXT. The Damocles-class Uplink text prints no duration. It reads "In your Movement phase ... select one combat doctrine. That combat doctrine is active for that INFANTRY unit." I checked this in three places: the raw Wahapedia Enhancements.csv, 40k/data/40kdc/enhancements.json and SPACE_MARINES_GENERIC.md:66. The official commentary in errata_faq_commentary.md:28 (01.02.02) covers this case directly: "If you have a rule that specifies when it is triggered (e.g. 'In the Shooting phase') and it does not specify how long it is active for, it is only active for the duration of that specified period", and "If a rule or effect is given to a unit and it does not specify how long it is active for, it is only active for the phase in which it was given." So the doctrine ends with that Movement phase. The duration is not inherited from the army rule. Every other doctrine-granting rule prints its own duration, "until the start of your next Command phase": Adaptive Strategy, Strategic Acumen, Master Tactician, and Author of the Codex, which shares the Uplink's "In your Movement phase" timing. In the army rule, that duration belongs only to the Command-phase selection.

ENGINE.
- SMDetachmentsA._use_damocles (lines 953-974) writes sw11e_doctrine and sets sw11e_doctrine_expires_player to the player. Its log line says "until the start of your next Command phase".
- The only thing that clears it is SWArmyRules11e.on_command_phase_start (lines 302-321).
- SMAbilitiesA, B and C also clear doctrines, but only ones they marked themselves (F_ACUMEN or F_DOCTRINE_SRC), and only when the Command phase is entered.
- The Movement-phase end hooks (SMDetachmentsA:636, SMAbilitiesB:865, SMAbilitiesC:939) never touch doctrines. No effect_-prefix wipe applies to these keys.
- test_sm11e_detachments_a.gd:690-692 asserts "expires at the start of P1's next Command phase".

THE RULING. RULINGS.md:355 ("no printed duration — the Combat Doctrines / Adaptive Strategy duration") does not refute the claim:
- It comes from commit 79261d3 (2026-10-23). The commentary file was added later, in d97882c (2026-11-07).
- It never cites 01.02.02.
- It contradicts the project's own default for effects with no printed duration, which RULINGS applies elsewhere as "until the end of the phase" (lines 52, 103, 390, 417).
- No delta-audit document records a decision on 01.02.02.

SCENARIO. The scenario checks out. AssaultShooting.eligible and weapon_allowed call _sw11e_assault, which calls SWArmyRules11e.ranged_attacks_have_assault (line 214), which calls group_doctrine_active('devastator'). EffectPrimitives.has_effect_advance_and_shoot follows the same path. Together they let an advanced unit shoot every ranged weapon. Under 10.05 it could only shoot its [ASSAULT] weapons. The impact is also wider than the claim says. Other rules that check whether a doctrine is active read the leftover doctrine through the rest of the turn and the opponent's turn: Laurels of Triumph, Venator Omni-auspex (SMDetachmentsA:247), Spear of Chogoris and Press the Attack (SMAbilitiesC), and the tactical-doctrine reactions in SMCodexAMovement:152/275.

OVERSTATED PARTS.
- The claim calls it a defect that "the Uplink doctrine also keeps the army doctrine off that unit for the rest of the round", and the fix sketch says to "restore the army doctrine". Both conflict with the Combat Doctrines rule: "If a rule makes a combat doctrine active for a unit, any combat doctrine previously active for that unit is no longer active for that unit." Nothing reactivates the replaced doctrine. Keeping the army doctrine off that unit (SWCommon.active_doctrines) is consistent with the rules as written. When the Uplink doctrine lapses, the unit should have no doctrine, not the army doctrine back.
- "For the rest of the round" is inexact: the engine's expiry is the start of the player's next Command phase.
- The marker sm11e_damocles_doctrine is never read and never cleared, so it is inert.

The fix sketch's other point is correct: the assault and tactical riders should be recorded on the move itself. advance_allows_charge and fall_back_allows_* check the doctrine again when the charge or shot is declared, so simply expiring the doctrine would wrongly take those riders away.

## EA-49 · 01.04.02 Closest Or Nearest Model/Unit — Pile-in and consolidation don't fix each model's closest target; surge ties are picked automatically

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** medium
- **Rule:** 01.04.02: 'When a rule refers to the closest or nearest model or unit, this is the closest model or unit to the model or unit using that rule. If two or more are equally close, the controlling player of the model or unit using that rule selects which is the closest.'
- **Engine:** PileInMove.model_move_allowed and ConsolidationMove.model_move_allowed compare the minimum distance to ANY selected target before and after the move (PileInMove.gd:69-78, 152-162; ConsolidationMove.gd:131-142). So a model may end closer to a farther target while moving away from its actual closest one. SurgeMove.before_moving keeps the first strictly-closest enemy in dictionary order (SurgeMove.gd:58-67), so the player is never offered a choice when two units are equally close.
- **Scenario:** An Ork mob engaged with enemy units A and B piles in. One Boy is 2.0" from A and 2.4" from B. It moves 3" to end 0.5" from B and 3.5" from A. The engine accepts this (0.5 < 2.0), but A is its closest pile-in target and the Boy ended farther from A, so the move is illegal under 12.03 and 01.04.02.
- **Fix sketch:** Before the move, work out each model's closest target unit, letting the player choose when two are within a small epsilon, and require the end distance to THAT unit to shrink. In SurgeMove, collect every enemy unit within epsilon of the minimum distance and let the controlling player pick the surge target.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/PileInMove.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/ConsolidationMove.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/SurgeMove.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** PileInMove.model_move_allowed (PileInMove.gd:69-78) and ConsolidationMove.model_move_allowed in Ongoing and Engaging modes (ConsolidationMove.gd:131-142) test whether the model's minimum distance to ANY selected target is smaller after the move (_distance_to_closest_target, PileInMove.gd:152-161). They never fix each model's closest pile-in target or closest selected enemy unit (12.03/12.08 read with 01.04.02), and they do not enforce "engaged with it if possible".
- The test accepts every legal move plus some illegal ones. Exact ties already pass a move toward either unit, which is the same as offering the player the choice, so pile-in and consolidation need no tie prompt.
- The illegal case: a model can end closer to a farther target and farther from its closest one, as long as its new distance to the farther target is below its old distance to the closest. Clean example: a Boy outside engagement range, 2.5" from A and 2.9" from B, ends 0.5" from B and farther from A; the engine accepts it.
- The claim's own Boy at 2.0" is already engaged with A (11e engagement range is 2"). Its move also breaks 12.03 AFTER (each model must stay engaged with what it started engaged with), which the engine checks only unit-wide.
- Who can reach it: human pile-in drags are separately filtered by FightController.gd:4026 (closest enemy model of any unit, fixed at drag start, centre-to-centre). For pile-in the gap is reached through AI, dispatched or network PILE_IN actions. The consolidation drag and Confirm path use the template itself (FightPhase.check_consolidation_model_move_11e), so a human can make such a move.
- Surge: SurgeMove.before_moving (SurgeMove.gd:58-67) keeps the first strictly closest enemy unit in board.units order. Every surge path uses it with no picker (MovementPhase BEGIN_SURGE_MOVE, ReactiveMove11e.surge_target via ShootingPhase and the Ork reactive move). So the 01.04.02 player choice on equally close units is missing, though exact float ties are rare.
- Fix: before each model moves, record the set of selected units within epsilon of its minimum distance. Require the model to end closer to one of them, and engaged with it if it can. Add a player picker only for surge ties.
- Severity: low.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute it. The code facts check out, and nothing recorded in the repo overrides them.

RULES
- 12.03 WHILE MOVING (core_rules.txt:1757-1759): "Each model that is moved must end its move closer to the closest pile-in target, and engaged with it if possible." 12.08 Ongoing and Engaging use the same wording for the closest selected enemy unit (:1964-1972).
- 21.02 BEFORE MOVING (:3313): "Select the closest enemy unit to be the surge target."
- 01.04.02 commentary (errata:83-85): the closest unit is the one closest to the model or unit using the rule. If two or more are equally close, the controlling player selects which one is the closest.
- Nothing settles fixed vs dynamic timing or surge ties: the errata has no 12.03, 12.08 or 21.02 FAQ, RULINGS.md has no entry, and neither do the delta or doc-change audits.
- Read with 01.04.02, "closer to the closest pile-in target, and engaged with it" names one unit per model, fixed before the model moves. If "closest" were judged at the end of the move, "engaged with it if possible" would mean almost nothing and the tie choice would be pointless.

ENGINE
- These checks are authoritative. FightPhase._validate_pile_in_11e (FightPhase.gd:6451, per-model check at about 6521) calls PileInMove.model_move_allowed. _validate_consolidate_11e (:6538, about 6619) and the human consolidation drag check check_consolidation_model_move_11e (:6734) call ConsolidationMove.model_move_allowed.
- PileInMove.gd:69-78 and ConsolidationMove.gd:131-142 compare _distance_to_closest_target (PileInMove.gd:152-161) before and after the move. That function is the minimum over every target unit's models, so no target is ever fixed per model, and "engaged with it if possible" is not enforced at all.
- Every move legal under the fixed reading passes this test, so it never wrongly rejects. On an exact tie it passes a move that ends closer to either unit, which is the same as offering the player both choices. In the non-tie case it lets illegal moves through. In the claim's scenario the minimum distance goes from 2.0" to 0.5", so the move is accepted even though the Boy ends farther from A.
- Surge: SurgeMove.gd:58-67 keeps the first strictly closest unit in board.units order. Every surge path uses this result:
  - MovementPhase.gd:6916
  - ReactiveMove11e.surge_target:153-157, used by ShootingPhase.gd:5532
  - OrkCodexShootingReactive.gd:138
  Ork11eReactiveMovePanel only displays the target. There is no picker anywhere, so the 01.04.02 tie choice is missing.

OVERSTATEMENTS
- Pile-in and consolidation ties already behave as player choice. The tie choice is missing only for surge, and exact float ties are rare.
- The FightController human pile-in drag (FightController.gd:4026) runs its own 10e-style check: closest enemy model of any unit, centre-to-centre, fixed at drag start. That would revert the scenario's drag. So for pile-in the gap is reached through AI, dispatched or network PILE_IN actions. Consolidation drags call the template directly, so a human can make such a move. "player_visible: false" is only partly right.
- At 2.0" the scenario Boy is already within A's 11e engagement range (GameConstants: 2"). Its move therefore also breaks the 12.03 AFTER rule that each model must stay engaged with the units it started engaged with. The engine checks that only unit-wide (PileInMove.gd:88-93), so the scenario mixes two gaps.

## EA-50 · 01.05.02 — An Advance die can be re-rolled twice: automatic ability re-roll, then Command Re-roll

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.05.02: 'You can never re-roll a dice more than once.'
- **Engine:** For a unit with an ability Advance re-roll (Swift Onslaught or Seeker's Instincts via has_effect_reroll_advance, Space Wolves Bestial Fury), MovementPhase re-rolls a 1-3 automatically (MovementPhase.gd:1404-1408) and records nothing. It then checks is_command_reroll_available and pauses with a Command Re-roll offer on the same die (:1424-1466). _process_use_command_reroll re-rolls it a second time (:1625-1700). The Charge phase already blocks this (ChargePhase.gd:747-750).
- **Scenario:** A Custodian Guard unit led by a Blade Champion (Swift Onslaught: 'Re-roll advance rolls') advances and rolls a 2. The engine re-rolls it to a 1 and then offers COMMAND RE-ROLL (1CP). The human, or the AI via AIDecisionMaker.evaluate_command_reroll_advance, re-rolls the same die a second time.
- **Fix sketch:** Set a local ability_rerolled = true inside the MovementPhase.gd:1404-1408 branch. When it is set, skip the Command Re-roll availability check and the pause, as ChargePhase.gd:747-750 does.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute this and could not. The claim holds as stated.

Rule: in the errata/commentary file at 01.05.02 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:107-109) the text reads: "You can never re-roll a dice more than once, and re-rolls happen before modifiers." The launch text's Command Re-roll (15.02) only says "you re-roll that roll" and has no exemption.

Engine, checked line by line:
- 40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd:1404-1408: when tw_override == 0 and advance_roll <= 3, and has_effect_reroll_advance(unit) or unit_has_detachment_advance_reroll(unit) is true, the die is re-rolled automatically and advance_roll is overwritten. The only trace is a log_phase_message. No flag or local variable records that the die was re-rolled.
- has_effect_reroll_advance (40k/autoloads/EffectPrimitives.gd:901-905) is true for:
  - FLAG_REROLL_ADVANCE
  - CustodesAbilities.advance_reroll_source, which covers Swift Onslaught and Seeker's Instincts across the whole Attached group (CustodesAbilities.gd:627-639)
  - SWAbilities11e.reroll_source (Bestial Fury)
- The codex text confirms all three abilities re-roll advance rolls (Datasheets_abilities.csv rows 000002518, 000002520 and 000000314).
- Lines 1416-1423 skip the free Waaagh!, Wrath and Honour of Vigilance re-roll offers for exactly these units, so free_rr_source stays "". Line 1424 then calls StratagemManager.is_command_reroll_available.
- That check (StratagemManager.gd:3652-3664 -> can_use_stratagem; the command_re_roll_11e definition is at :5013) has no "die already re-rolled" condition. If CP is available, the phase pauses (:1433-1466) and emits command_reroll_opportunity with the already re-rolled value as original_rolls.
- MovementController._on_command_reroll_opportunity (:5618) shows "Command Re-roll available! (1 CP)" and opens CommandRerollDialog.
- AIPlayer._on_command_reroll_opportunity calls AIDecisionMaker.evaluate_command_reroll_advance (:28271). That function always re-rolls a 1, and re-rolls a 2 when 3+ CP are banked. It never checks for an earlier re-roll.
- _process_use_command_reroll (:1625-1700) spends the CP and rolls the same die again. That is a second re-roll.
- A test confirms Command Re-roll really is offered on an 11e advance: test_ork_codex_movement.gd:1117-1123.

Nothing elsewhere handles this:
- MovementPhase has no ability_rerolled-style state.
- Neither the StratagemManager guard nor the dialog checks for an earlier re-roll.
- No RULINGS.md or docs entry permits it.

The developers' own conventions show this is an oversight:
- ChargePhase.gd:747-750 skips Command Re-roll when ability_reroll_used is set.
- CommandPhase.gd:1021 and :1039 skip it after the Tempered in Battle free re-roll. RULINGS.md:382 says "after it no Command Re-roll is offered (a die is never re-rolled twice)".
- RULINGS.md:109 suppresses the Waaagh! free re-roll when another ability already grants an advance re-roll.
- ShootingPhase, FightPhase and RulesEngine refuse to re-roll an already re-rolled die.

The scenario plays out as described. A Blade Champion-led Custodian Guard unit rolls a 2, the engine auto re-rolls it, gets a 1, then offers Command Re-roll (1 CP), and the human or AI re-rolls the same die again.

The fix sketch is correct: set a local flag in the :1404-1408 branch and skip the Command Re-roll check and pause when it is set. A roll of 4-6 that the ability did not re-roll should still be offered Command Re-roll.

The claim is slightly narrower than the real scope. The same gap applies to FLAG_REROLL_ADVANCE sources (Bloodthirsty Belligerence, SUPERFUELLED BOILER) and to the 10e-only Blitz Brigade Eager for the Fight. That makes it broader, not overstated, so no correction is needed. Severity low and player-visible are accurate.

## EA-51 · 01.07.02 — Module 'battle-shock rolls' skip Tempered in Battle's leadership-roll re-roll, which forced 'Battle-shock tests' get

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 01.07.02: 'The term Battle-shock test is the same as battle-shock roll'. 01.07: a battle-shock roll is a leadership roll. Tempered in Battle: friendly ADEPTUS ASTARTES units within 6" 'can re-roll leadership rolls'.
- **Engine:** force_battle_shock_test applies the Tempered in Battle re-roll (FactionAbilityManager.gd:2396-2404, per RULINGS.md:382). The separate rollers roll 2D6 once with no re-roll check: Rokkit Barrage and Mobbed (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1103-1115, called at :2236 and :2375), Sanctified Flames (CustodesAbilities.gd:1211-1225, called at :1263) and Fearsome Assault (SMAbilitiesC.gd:1009-1019, called at :989).
- **Scenario:** Orks vs Space Marines. An ADEPTUS ASTARTES VEHICLE (e.g. a Brutalis Dreadnought) is within 6" of a Tempered in Battle bearer and engaged with a Mobbed Boyz unit. Its Mobbed battle-shock roll (-1 or -2) fails and cannot be re-rolled. A Squig Flingin' test on the same unit would be re-rolled (FactionAbilityManager.gd:2397).
- **Fix sketch:** Route the four module rollers through FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test(unit, mod, source), adding an option to return diffs for the whole group. Alternatively, have each roller call SMDetachmentsB.battle_shock_reroll_source() on a failed roll and re-roll both dice. Routing through the shared helper also unifies the Ld choice in the 01.06.01 gap.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/orks/OrkAbilities11e.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesAbilities.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd`, `40k/autoloads/FactionAbilityManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Tempered in Battle's re-roll is only applied in two places: CommandPhase.gd:1022 and FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2397). Four live module battle-shock rollers skip it and roll 2D6 once:
- OrkAbilities11e.battle_shock_roll: Rokkit Barrage (:2236) and Mobbed (:2375)
- CustodesAbilities.battle_shock_roll: Sanctified Flames (:1263)
- SMAbilitiesC._battle_shock_roll: Fearsome Assault (:989), which only matters in an ADEPTUS ASTARTES mirror match

An ADEPTUS ASTARTES unit within 6" of a Tempered bearer that fails one of these rolls is battle-shocked with no re-roll. On the same unit, the 11e IMPENDING KRUNCH (OrkCodexOther.gd:580) or Roar of Mork (OrkDetachments11e.gd:1114) roll would be re-rolled. The claim's Squig Flingin' example is a 10e stratagem not in the 11e Ork codex. Severity: low.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: The 01.07 launch text says that to make a battle-shock roll, the controlling player "makes a leadership roll for it". Tempered in Battle (Aura), the 11e SM codex version in Tacticus Firestorm Force (SMDetachmentsB.gd:130), says: "Friendly ADEPTUS ASTARTES units within 6" of this model can re-roll leadership rolls." So every battle-shock roll can be re-rolled. The engine's own ruling agrees: .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md:382 says Tempered covers "battle-shock tests (the leadership rolls the engine makes): the Command-phase battle-shock test and every forced battle-shock test".

ENGINE: SMDetachmentsB.battle_shock_reroll_source is called in only two places: CommandPhase.gd:1022 and FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test (:2397). All of the claim's line references are correct. The four module rollers each roll 2D6 once and never call it:
- OrkAbilities11e.battle_shock_roll (1103-1116), used by Rokkit Barrage (:2236) and Mobbed (:2375)
- CustodesAbilities.battle_shock_roll (1211-1225), used by Sanctified Flames (:1263)
- SMAbilitiesC._battle_shock_roll (1009-1019), used by Fearsome Assault (:989)

Nothing else re-rolls these:
- The dice helpers (Ork _d6; Custodes _roll -> Common.roll_dice) have no re-roll hook.
- GameState has no hook on the battle_shocked flag.
- All four are live: ChargePhase.gd:1515, FightPhase.gd:225, the Ork post-shot paths at :2164 and :2281, and the Custodes unit_has_shot card. The old 10e automatic Sanctified Flames test was removed (ShootingPhase.gd:6811).
- No RULINGS entry excludes these rolls from re-rolls. RULINGS:102, :191-192 and :450 only describe them as 2D6 + mod vs Ld.
- Tempered tests (test_sm11e_detachments_b.gd:870-887) only cover force_battle_shock_test.

Meanwhile these all go through force_battle_shock_test and do get the re-roll: the 11e IMPENDING KRUNCH (OrkCodexOther.gd:580, the same "after a charge, engaged enemies roll at -1" shape as Mobbed), Roar of Mork (OrkDetachments11e.gd:1114), Huntress' Eye, Kaptaris, Howlmaw, SMCodexBAction and Desperate Escape. So the inconsistency is real. SM vehicles carry the ADEPTUS ASTARTES keyword (sm11e_codex_test.json Ballistus Dreadnought), so the Mobbed scenario can happen. The psychic "on a 1" battle-shocks (SWAbilities11e, SMAbilitiesA/B, OrkDetachments11e _battle_shock_diffs) are D6 rolls, not leadership rolls, and are correctly outside Tempered.

MINOR INACCURACIES (they don't change the verdict):
1. The scenario compares against Squig Flingin'. That is a 10e Kult of Speed stratagem (StratagemManager.gd:234/2698) and is not in the 11e Ork codex (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/orks/RULES.md Kult of Speed has no such row). The valid 11e comparison is IMPENDING KRUNCH or Roar of Mork.
2. Fearsome Assault only matters in an ADEPTUS ASTARTES mirror match.
3. 01.07.02 is just a note that 'test' and 'roll' mean the same thing. The engine does not treat the two words differently. The real gap is that these four rollers never check the Tempered re-roll.

Severity is low and the effect is player-visible.

## EA-52 · 02.01.01 — A datasheet name only counts as a keyword when the data happens to list it

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'each datasheet name acts as an additional keyword for that unit.'
- **Engine:** Only CharacterAttachmentManager.can_attach (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:85-93) and enhancement candidate matching add meta.name. The shared predicates (RulesEngine.unit_has_keyword :9822-9828; codex has_keyword/group_has_keyword) read meta.keywords only. Builder exports copy dataset keywords (server/public/js/lib/gameformat.mjs:500-504), and armies/sw11e_gw_import_2000.json has Scout Squad x2, Outrider Squad, Terminator Assault Squad, Captain/Chaplain on Bike and Ancient in Terminator Armour without their name keyword.
- **Scenario:** A Phobos Shadow Force list imported through the builder has a Scout Squad without 'SCOUT SQUAD', as the shipped import does. Shadow Masters (SMDetachmentsA.gd:791), MORTIS SNARES (SMCodexACommand.gd:486) and TACTICAL WITHDRAWAL (SMCodexAMovement.gd:419) all refuse that Scout Squad.
- **Fix sketch:** Append meta.name.to_upper() inside the shared keyword helpers, or normalise keywords to include the datasheet name when an army is loaded (ArmyListManager).
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWCommon.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesCommon.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** The engine implements 02.01.01 only through data: wahapedia-import.mjs:570-574 adds the name to the datasheets it writes. The keyword checks read meta.keywords only. That covers RulesEngine.unit_has_keyword, SWCommon/CustodesCommon.has_keyword and group_has_keyword, and also the live Formations-phase leader matching:
- GameState.get_eligible_bodyguards_for_character (GameState.gd:427-465), which feeds FormationsDeclarationDialog.gd:302 and the AI through FormationsPhase.get_available_actions
- FormationsPhase._validate_declare_leader_attachment (:212-223)
- DeploymentPhase.gd:1242-1251

Only CharacterAttachmentManager.can_attach and EnhancementRules11e add meta.name. can_attach is used only by the deploy-time attach dialog, which is skipped once formations are declared.

The Phobos Shadow Force scenario does not reproduce. Current game and builder data give Scout Squad 'SCOUT SQUAD' (WP-M1, fe653cf). Only the stale, player-selectable sw11e_gw_import_2000.json lacks the name keyword on its 7 units: it was generated 13 minutes before WP-M1, and it is not a Phobos Shadow Force army.

The real player-visible effect: with that shipped army, the Formations dialog does not offer Logan Grimnar or the Ancient in Terminator Armour -> Terminator Assault Squad, or the Chaplain on Bike or Captain on Bike -> Outrider Squad. ffdcc79's fix and its test only cover can_attach.

Builder-made armies whose bodyguard records still lack or split the name lose those attachments the same way: 87 of 1010 leader pairings, e.g. Khorne Berzerkers, Skitarii Rangers/Vanguard, Kroot Carnivores, Pink Horrors, Canoptek Wraiths and Death Company Marines with Jump Packs.

Fix: add meta.name in the shared keyword and eligible-bodyguard checks (or normalise keywords when an army loads), and regenerate the stale import.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: Commentary 02.01.01 (errata_faq_commentary.md:152-154) says each datasheet name acts as an additional keyword. RULINGS.md and docs/ record no ruling on it. Everything below comes from static reading only; nothing was run.

The claim's engine facts check out (line numbers have drifted):
- RulesEngine.unit_has_keyword (now RulesEngine.gd:9975-9981), SWCommon.has_keyword/group_has_keyword (:108-113, :259, :579), CustodesCommon.has_keyword (:104-109) and OrkAbilities11e.group_has_keyword (:316-320) all read meta.keywords only.
- No loader adds the name. ArmyListManager only appends enhancement keywords (:1227-1236).
- Only two places add meta.name: CharacterAttachmentManager.can_attach (:85-92) and EnhancementRules11e._unit_terms (:135-148).
- The engine relies on the data carrying the name. wahapedia-import.mjs:570-574 appends it, but only for the datasheets it writes.
- Builder exports copy the dataset keywords (gameformat.mjs:500-504).
- sw11e_gw_import_2000.json does lack the name keyword on exactly the 7 units listed.

The Phobos Shadow Force scenario does not hold up:
- Since WP-M1 (fe653cf, 2026-10-23 01:46) the game's 40kdc units.json and the builder overlay carry the name keyword for Scout Squad, Outrider Squad, Terminator Assault Squad, Captain on Bike, Chaplain on Bike and Ancient in Terminator Armour. The overlay is codex-11e-datasheets.mjs, applied by store.js:22 through data-patches.mjs:113/224, and the parity test asserts the builder data equals the game data.
- The shipped import was generated in ffdcc79 at 01:33, 13 minutes before WP-M1, from the raw vendored bundle, which lacks these names. It was never regenerated.
- So a Phobos Shadow Force list imported today does get SCOUT SQUAD.
- No shipped army runs Phobos Shadow Force. The stale import uses Assault Brethren, Saga of the Beastslayer and Stormlance Task Force, and detachments come only from the army file; there is no in-game picker.
- Shadow Masters, MORTIS SNARES and TACTICAL WITHDRAWAL would only refuse a Scout Squad from an export made before WP-M1.

The real player-visible effect is one the claim does not mention:
- The live 11e attach path is the Formations phase. Games start in FORMATIONS (GameState.gd:50), and DeploymentController.gd:1007-1014 skips the deploy-time attach dialog once formations are declared. That dialog is the only caller of can_attach, so the name fallback never runs in a normal game.
