- GameState.get_eligible_bodyguards_for_character (GameState.gd:427-465) matches can_lead against meta.keywords only, with no name fallback and no LeaderPairingsLoader merge. FormationsDeclarationDialog.gd:302 and FormationsPhase.get_available_actions (:1324, which the AI also uses) both call it.
- FormationsPhase._validate_declare_leader_attachment (:212-223) and DeploymentPhase.gd:1242-1251 have the same gap.
- The stale import is player-selectable (it is in army_allowlist.json). In it, Logan Grimnar and the Ancient in Terminator Armour (can_lead TERMINATOR ASSAULT SQUAD...) and the Chaplain on Bike and Captain on Bike (can_lead OUTRIDER SQUAD) can only match their bodyguard by name. Those pairings are therefore not offered in the Formations dialog.
- ffdcc79 only fixed can_attach, and test_sw11e_codex_data.gd:288-293 only tests can_attach.
- The gap is wider than the shipped army. The builder writes can_lead as bodyguard datasheet names (gameformat.mjs:573-580). 87 of the 1010 leaderAttachments pairings have a bodyguard record that lacks or splits its name keyword, for example Khorne Berzerkers stored as 'Khorne' + 'Berzerkers', Skitarii Rangers/Vanguard, Kroot Carnivores, Pink Horrors, Canoptek Wraiths and Death Company Marines with Jump Packs. Builder-made armies with those units lose those attachments in the same way.

## EA-53 · 02.02.01 — Modified charge roll is not capped at 12in

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** missing · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Charge rolls cannot be greater than 12 ... if the result would be 13 or more, it is changed to 12.'
- **Engine:** ChargePhase._resolve_charge_roll adds every bonus with no cap (ChargePhase.gd:846-850, total_distance += charge_bonus). The charge-move budget uses that total (ChargePhase.gd:2235, 2272); only target selection is capped via min(12, roll) (ChargeMove11e.gd:106-107).
- **Scenario:** A Custodes unit under FURY OF THE EMPEROR (+2) rolls 11 and gets a 13in movement budget. It reaches a target 11.5in away whose only path around a wall is 12.8in long; under the rules the roll is 12in and the charge fails.
- **Fix sketch:** Set total_distance = mini(total_distance, 12) in _resolve_charge_roll after all modifiers. The re-roll paths at :1077 and :1147 recompute through the same function, so they are covered too.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule confirmed: the 02.02.01 commentary in docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:165 says 'Charge rolls cannot be greater than 12. After all the cumulative modifiers to a charge roll have been calculated, if the result would be 13 or more, it is changed to 12.' RULINGS.md and docs/rules/11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md record no decision to depart from it, no test pins either behaviour, and git history shows no fix on any branch.

Engine: ChargePhase._resolve_charge_roll (40k/phases/ChargePhase.gd:802-850) sums every charge-roll bonus, then does 'total_distance += charge_bonus; charge_data.distance = total_distance' (848-849) with no cap. The final hook, CustodesCodexOther.charge_roll_modifier (558-569: FURY +2, GRAVIMETRIC GRENADE -1, INEXORABLE), has no cap. EffectPrimitives.get_effect_plus_charge (911) returns the raw flag.

Every check reads that same uncapped value: the move validator _validate_charge_movement_constraints (2235, 2272); the feasibility checks _is_charge_roll_sufficient (3222, 3241) and _per_target_charge_requirements (3296); the client drag clamp (ChargeController.gd charge_distance at 4134/4248, _clamp_charge_to_budget at 2828); the AI's rolled_distance from the legal actions (ChargePhase.gd:2915-2919); and the dice/event log text ('= 13\"'). ChargeMove11e.max_distance_inches is never called. The only 12in limit is selectable_distance_ceiling = min(12, roll) (ChargeMove11e.gd:106-107), which is the separate 11.04 'target within 12\"' selection rule.

The scenario is reachable: FURY is offered at the unit_declares_charge window before the roll (CustodesCodexOther._candidates / W_DECLARES_CHARGE) and sets a +2 flag. A raw 11 becomes 13; an 11.5in target is selectable (min(12,13)=12); a 12.8in detour passes 12.8 <= 13+0.02 but should fail at 12.

The fix sketch is correct: both re-roll paths (1077, 1147) write the raw 2D6 back and call _resolve_charge_roll again, and every consumer reads charge_data.distance or the emitted total. Severity low is right: it only matters on a raw 11-12 with a bonus, and only when the path cost to a target within 12in exceeds 12in (detour, terrain penalty, or rear models).

Side note: the NO ESCAPE charge (CustodesCodexMovement.resolve_reactive_charge:531-533) uses the same uncapped pattern, but only FURY could push it past 12. FURY is used in your own Charge phase and NO ESCAPE in the opponent's Movement phase, so that site is effectively unreachable.

## EA-54 · 02.02.01 — AP improvement vs worsening is applied in a different order depending on the resolution path

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Apply addition modifiers, then subtraction modifiers. 'Improving AP: subtract the appropriate amount'; 'Worsening AP: add the appropriate amount ..., to a maximum of 0'.
- **Engine:** The ranged interactive, auto-resolve and overwatch paths and the melee auto path apply every improve_ap and then the generic worsen (RulesEngine.gd:15706-15741, 5588-5622, 1896-1921, 14235-14259). The human-defender melee save path worsens first and improves afterwards (RulesEngine.gd:15925-15955). Separately, -1D/-1AP effects take the best value instead of adding up, per a recorded ruling (EffectPrimitives.gd:746-749, 796-804; RULINGS.md:171), which contradicts 'All modifiers are cumulative', although no shipped same-attack combination was found.
- **Scenario:** An AP 0 melee weapon with +1 AP (e.g. Cunning Hunters) attacks Meganobz with Arrogant Invulnerability (attacks have -1 AP). The interactive melee save path resolves AP -1, while the same attack auto-resolved gives AP 0. Under the 02.02.01 order (worsen as an addition capped at 0, then improve as a subtraction) the answer is AP -1.
- **Fix sketch:** Write one shared compute_ap(base, improvements, worsenings) that applies worsening (capped at 0) before improvements, and call it from every path. Revisit the best-of ruling against 'All modifiers are cumulative'.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/autoloads/EffectPrimitives.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** AP improvements and the generic AP worsening (EffectPrimitives.get_effect_worsen_ap: Arrogant Invulnerability, Living Fortress, flag WORSEN_AP, SMCodexB ARMOUR OF CONTEMPT) are applied in a different order depending on the path.

- Improve first, then worsen (a net sum clamped at 0): ranged interactive (RulesEngine.gd ~15885-15926), ranged auto-resolve (~5637-5676), overwatch (~1891-1922), and melee auto saves (~14413-14445, used for AI defenders or auto-allocate).
- Worsen first, then improve: prepare_melee_save_resolution (~16110-16145). It is used whenever a human defender rolls their own melee saves, in the one-shot and staged fight flows (default setting; always when networked).
- The codex modify_ap hooks (Custodes PSY-CHAFF / IN AURAMITE CLAD, SMCodexACombat ARMOUR OF CONTEMPT) run after the improvements on every path, so they are consistent.

The results differ only when the worsen would clip at 0 (worsen > |base AP|), in practice an AP 0 weapon. Shipped example: an Assault Brethren unit with an AP 0 melee weapon (Intercessor Squad 'Knives and Fists' / 'Close combat weapon') uses GENE-WROUGHT MIGHT with the assault doctrine (+1 AP) and fights Meganobz (Arrogant Invulnerability). This resolves at AP -1 against a human Ork defender and AP 0 against an AI defender. The Cunning Hunters example does not diverge, because shipped Grey Hunters melee weapons are AP -1/-2.

The literal 02.02.01 order (worsen = 'add, to a maximum of 0', applied in the addition step; improve = 'subtract', applied in the subtraction step) gives AP -1. On that reading the five improve-first sites are wrong. Reading '+1 AP' as an addition modifier gives AP 0 instead. Either way the paths must be unified behind one shared helper and one recorded ruling.

The best-of -1 D / -1 AP handling is a recorded ruling (RULINGS.md:171, :471) with no shipped same-attack case, so it is not part of this defect.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule (errata doc, 02.02.01): "All modifiers are cumulative". The order is multiplication, then addition, then division, then subtraction. "Improving AP: subtract the appropriate amount". "Reducing or Worsening AP: add the appropriate amount..., to a maximum of 0". The final clamp is "AP cannot be worse than 0".

Engine helpers (RulesEngine.gd ~10836-10844):
- improve_ap(ap, by) = ap - by on signed AP.
- worsen_ap(ap, by) = mini(0, ap + by), so every worsen is clamped at 0.
- Because of that clamp, the order matters whenever the worsen exceeds |base AP|.

Order verified at each site in the current file:
- Ranged interactive prepare_save_resolution (~15885-15926), ranged auto-resolve (~5637-5676), overwatch (~1891-1922) and the melee one-shot/staged auto save (_resolve_melee_assignment_hits ~14413-14445, consumed via hit_context["ap"] in _resolve_melee_assignment_saves): apply all improvements, then the Custodes/SM codex modify_ap hooks, then EffectPrimitives.get_effect_worsen_ap. That equals a net sum clamped at 0.
- prepare_melee_save_resolution (~16110-16145): applies get_effect_worsen_ap FIRST, then Prey / Try Dat Button / scoped improve / ork11e_ab_mods AP, then the codex hooks.

The worsen-first path is live:
- It is used whenever a human defender rolls their own melee saves. That is FightPhase._process_roll_dice_interactive, or the staged fight via resolve_melee_wounds -> save_data_list. It is the default (auto-allocate default OFF) and always applies in networked play.
- Its AP is what is actually rolled: save_data.ap feeds the overlay, _apply_saves_via_allocation_11e (line 4157) and apply_save_damage.
- AI defenders, or local auto-allocate, use the improve-first value.

No recorded decision covers this:
- Nothing in RULINGS.md or docs fixes the AP order.
- 11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md marks "Multiple-modifier order" 🟡, with only the damage order verified, and notes "each site hand-ordered".
- The only order note is a 10e-era line (docs/history/ABILITIES_AUDIT.md:319, "improve_ap ... before defender's worsen_ap").

Where the claim is overstated:
1. Its named example does not diverge. Shipped Grey Hunters (the only Cunning Hunters unit) have melee weapons at AP -1/-2 (Chainsword, Power fist/weapon), and both orders give the same result (-1 -> -2 -> -1 vs -1 -> 0 -> -1). A real shipped divergence exists: GENE-WROUGHT MIGHT (Assault Brethren, any ADEPTUS ASTARTES unit, +1 AP to melee with the assault doctrine, via SMCodexB -> ork11e_ab_mods) on an AP 0 melee weapon (Intercessor Squad "Knives and Fists" / "Close combat weapon") against Meganobz (Arrogant Invulnerability). That gives AP -1 against a human defender and AP 0 against an AI defender.
2. "The answer is AP -1" is the literal reading: worsen counts as an addition and improve as a subtraction, so additions go first. That reading makes the five improve-first sites wrong. It is not beyond dispute: reading "+1 AP" as an addition modifier, or as a net sum, gives AP 0. Either way the paths disagree, and that disagreement is the defect.
3. Only the generic EffectPrimitives worsen is reordered. The codex hooks (PSY-CHAFF / IN AURAMITE CLAD, SMCodexACombat ARMOUR OF CONTEMPT) run after the improvements on every path.
4. The best-of -1 D / -1 AP point is a recorded developer ruling (RULINGS.md:171 and :471). 24.02 Duplicated Abilities covers only core and weapon abilities, and the claimant found no shipped same-attack combination, so it is not a defect here.
5. The cited line numbers are stale, though the code content matches.

## EA-55 · 02.02.03 — Random S characteristics are parsed to a flat S3

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Other Random Characteristics: ... roll to determine the value on an individual, per-model or per-weapon basis each time that characteristic is required.'
- **Engine:** get_weapon_profile keeps raw strings for A and D, but converts S with is_valid_int() and otherwise uses 3, with no strength_raw (RulesEngine.gd:8828-8829).
- **Scenario:** The tutorial Ork Battlewagon's Zzap gun (S 'D6+6', armies/tutorial_orks.json) wounds a T10 vehicle as S3, needing 6+, instead of rolling S7-12.
- **Fix sketch:** Keep strength_raw in the profile and roll it with roll_variable_characteristic per weapon, each time S is needed for a wound roll.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** RulesEngine.get_weapon_profile (RulesEngine.gd:8981-8982) turns any non-integer S into a flat 3 and keeps no strength_raw. No wound path rolls it, so 02.02.03's random S is not implemented. This is already open item #3 in docs/RULES_AUDIT_FOLLOWUPS_2026-10-17.md. The bug is latent with current 11e content: no shipped 11e weapon has a dice-valued S (40kdc weapons.json has 0 of 3471; the 11e Zzap gun is a flat S8). The tutorial Battlewagon never fires its menu's "D6+6" Zzap gun, because its roster took a Kannon and the loadout resolver drops the Zzap gun (pinned by tests/unit/test_unit_loadout_resolver.gd:73). The bug only triggers on legacy 10e-era data, e.g. 40k/saves/shooting_phase.w40ksave, where Battlewagons took "1x Zzap gun" at S "D6+6". There the gun wounds as S3. Even then its [ANTI-VEHICLE 4+] still wounds VEHICLE targets on 4+, so the error mainly matters against non-VEHICLE targets. Severity is low, and players don't see it in current 11e play.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: The errata/commentary entry 02.02.03 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:171-173) says that apart from M, A and D, any other random characteristic is rolled "each time that characteristic is required". That covers a dice-valued S. No ruling in .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md or docs/ says otherwise.

CODE DEFECT IS REAL:
- In 40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd, get_weapon_profile flattens S at lines 8981-8982 (the claim cites 8828-8829, which is out of date): `strength_value = int(strength_str) if is_valid_int() else 3`.
- The returned profile carries attacks_raw and damage_raw but no strength_raw (line 9039 is plain "strength": strength_value).
- Every wound path reads weapon_profile.strength: line 3138 (ranged), 5298 (auto-resolve), 1786 (overwatch), 14351 (melee), and scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd:434.
- roll_variable_characteristic is only ever called for attacks_raw and damage_raw. No codex script or helper rolls S.
- The developers already log this exact bug as open item #3 in docs/RULES_AUDIT_FOLLOWUPS_2026-10-17.md: "Dice-valued Strength is never rolled — Zzap gun S D6+6 wounds as S3". It is also a named cause in tests/test_ai_combat_math_property.gd:186.

OVERSTATED:
(a) The scenario cannot happen. The tutorial U_BATTLEWAGON_T's wargear is 1x Kannon, 1x Lobba plus melee items. It has no Zzap gun.
- _ensure_loadout_resolved and _resolve_single_model_loadout narrow its ranged loadout to Kannon – Frag, Kannon – Shell and Lobba.
- _get_model_weapon_ids honours ranged_loadout, so Shooting (via get_unit_weapons) never offers the Zzap gun.
- tests/unit/test_unit_loadout_resolver.gd:73 explicitly asserts "drops unequipped option 'Zzap gun'".
- All five tutorial fixtures (data/tutorials/fixtures/*.w40ksave) carry the same wargear. The T4 shooting lesson drives U_BOYZ_T, not the Battlewagon.

(b) The "needs 6+ vs T10" maths is wrong even when the bug fires. The Zzap gun has [ANTI-VEHICLE 4+] (abilities threshold 4). AttackSequence.evaluate_wound_roll counts an unmodified 4+ as a critical wound, which always succeeds. So against a VEHICLE it still wounds on 4+; the true S7-12 would give 4+ (S7-10) or 3+ (S11-12).

(c) No current 11e content has a dice-valued S:
- 40k/data/40kdc/weapons.json: 0 of 3471 profiles. The 11e Zzap gun is a flat S8.
- The web builder's vendored server/public/js/vendor/40kdc-data.mjs: all S values are integers.
- gameformat.mjs:379 writes String(prof.stats.S) from that data.
- The only "D6+6" in 40k/armies is the tutorial's menu entry, which is never equipped. The 10e lists that did equip it were deleted in commit 1d818bc (v1.52.1).

(d) The bug can only fire from legacy 10e-era data. Example: 40k/saves/shooting_phase.w40ksave, where U_BATTLEWAGON_A and U_BATTLEWAGON_B took "1x Zzap gun" at S "D6+6" (there are 74 such strings across the saves). Saves are not refreshed on load; StateSerializer only validates them. In those games the Zzap gun does wound as S3.

## EA-56 · 03.02.03 — Redeploy rules are not playable, and the redeploy validator skips normal deployment rules

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'remove that unit from the battlefield, then deploy it again using all the normal rules (e.g. ... Infiltrators ...)'.
- **Engine:** RedeploymentPhase accepts actions, but humans only get 'End Redeployment' (Main.gd:12950-12951) and the AI always skips (AIDecisionMaker.gd:5849-5870). SET_REDEPLOY_MODEL_POS checks only the model centre against the DZ and exempts Infiltrators from it with no >8in stand-off. CONFIRM_REDEPLOY has no coherency check (RedeploymentPhase.gd:197-233).
- **Scenario:** A Space Wolves list that paid for Hunter's Guile or Chariots of the Storm (redeploy up to three units) can never use it. A scripted or networked redeploy of an Infiltrators unit could be placed right next to enemy models.
- **Fix sketch:** Reuse DeploymentPhase's validators (wholly within the DZ, the Infiltrators 8in stand-off from GameConstants, _check_deployment_coherency) for redeploy positions, and add a redeploy placement UI and AI policy.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/RedeploymentPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/Main.gd`, `40k/scripts/AIDecisionMaker.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 03.02.03 is not implemented. Humans only get "End Redeployment" (Main.gd:12950-12951), and the AI always skips (AIDecisionMaker.gd:5939-5965). RULINGS.md (lines 158, 306, 381, 461) records this as a known "NOT CLICKABLE" gap. So Hunter's Guile, Chariots of the Storm, Castellan's Mark, Razgit's Magik Map, Kaptin's Hat, Cyber-familiar and Strategium Command never let anyone redeploy.

Even a scripted dispatch cannot do it. RedeploymentPhase._validate_begin_redeploy requires GameState.unit_has_redeploy(), which only matches native ability names containing "redeploy", "Phantasm" or "Red Corsairs". Every redeploy source that ships comes from the FactionAbilityManager enhancement table, so BEGIN_REDEPLOY, which get_available_actions still advertises, is rejected. Only SKIP_REDEPLOY, SEND_TO_STRATEGIC_RESERVES and END_REDEPLOYMENT_PHASE succeed.

For a unit with a native redeploy ability, the SET_REDEPLOY_MODEL_POS deployment-zone check never runs. _is_in_deployment_zone reads zone["polygon"], but zones store the polygon under "poly", and an empty polygon returns true. So any model, Infiltrators or not, can be placed anywhere on the board, as long as its centre is on the board and it doesn't overlap a model from another unit (circle test). CONFIRM_REDEPLOY (lines 212-234) only checks that every model has a position.

The redeploy path checks none of the following, all of which DeploymentPhase._validate_deploy_unit_action enforces except the unengaged rule (no explicit check there; a legal deployment-zone or Infiltrators position keeps units well apart):
- wholly within the deployment zone
- the Infiltrators 24.20 stand-off: more than 8" from the enemy deployment zone and all enemy units
- coherency (03.03)
- unengaged (03.02)
- models of the same unit overlapping each other
- walls and terrain
- bases hanging off the board edge
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: the 03.02.03 commentary says a redeploying unit is removed and then deployed again "using all the normal rules", including Infiltrators. The normal rules are 03.02 set-up (in coherency, unengaged), 03.03 coherency, "wholly within your deployment zone" (confirmed by the 03.02.02 commentary), and 24.20 Infiltrators (more than 8" from the enemy deployment zone and all enemy units).

What I checked in the engine:
1. Players cannot use it. For the Redeployment phase, Main.gd:12950-12951 only sends END_REDEPLOYMENT_PHASE. The only other place outside the phase that names BEGIN/SET/CONFIRM_REDEPLOY is the AI highlight-colour table (Main.gd:2304). No other UI or controller emits them, and no test or scenario sends SET_REDEPLOY_MODEL_POS.
2. The AI always skips. `_decide_redeployment` (now at AIDecisionMaker.gd:5939-5965, not 5849-5870) ends the phase or sends SKIP_REDEPLOY. AIPlayer and PlanManager have no redeploy code.
3. The developers record this as a known gap, not as a ruling that it works. RULINGS.md lines 158, 306, 381 and 461 say "NOT CLICKABLE: the Redeployment step has no player controls ... and the AI skips them". The SWEnhancements11e.gd header says the same. Version 1.50.0 in version_history says "there is still no redeploy step for players". Its "redeploy for the AI only" is wrong, since the AI skips. Nothing on another branch or in git history fixes this.
4. Placement validator (RedeploymentPhase.gd): Infiltrators skip the zone check (lines 199-204) and nothing replaces it with the 8" stand-off. The confirm validator (lines 212-234) only checks that every model has a staged position, so there is no coherency check. The unengaged rule is not checked either.

Two points are understated:
(a) The deployment-zone check never runs, so it is not even a centre-only check. `_is_in_deployment_zone` reads `zone["polygon"]`, but every zone stores its polygon under "poly" (the DeploymentZoneData fallbacks and all deployment_zones/*.json files have only the keys player and poly). An empty polygon returns true ("allow anywhere"). So any redeploying model can be placed anywhere on the board, whether or not it has Infiltrators.
(b) BEGIN_REDEPLOY is rejected for every redeploy source that ships. `_validate_begin_redeploy` requires `GameState.unit_has_redeploy()`, which matches only native ability names containing "redeploy", "Phantasm" or "Red Corsairs". Hunter's Guile, Chariots of the Storm, Castellan's Mark, Razgit's Magik Map, Kaptin's Hat, Cyber-familiar and Strategium Command all come from the FactionAbilityManager enhancement table. `get_available_actions` still lists BEGIN_REDEPLOY for those units, but validation rejects it, so even a scripted dispatch can only skip, send to Strategic Reserves, or end the phase. No shipped army has a native redeploy ability; the only scenario that uses one injects it by hand. The placement weaknesses can therefore only be reached through such an injected ability today.

## EA-57 · 04.03.01 — Identical attacks from different weapons are never gathered into one batch

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.03 Gather Attack Dice / 04.03.01: other weapons at that unit making identical attacks (same BS/WS, S, AP, D and applicable abilities) are gathered with the selected weapon. Per the 04.03 FAQ, [PRECISION] is not 'applicable' against a target with no CHARACTER.
- **Engine:** Only resolve_shoot merges, and only groups with a single weapon id (RulesEngine.gd:841-855). The live sequential/interactive path (resolve_shoot_until_wounds 927-931 + ShootingPhase weapon_order) and melee (resolve_melee_attacks 13376) resolve each weapon as its own save batch. attack_identity_key excludes 'pistol' but not 'close_quarters', and keeps [PRECISION] whatever the target (AttackSequence.gd:430-451).
- **Scenario:** Boltguns and a bolt pistol (both S4 AP0 D1, BS3+) wound an attached unit of one Boy (W1, Sv6+) plus a Warboss (Sv4+), with save dice 4 and 1. Gathered, the dice resolve lowest-first: the 1 kills the Boy and the Warboss saves the 4. As the engine's separate batches, the boltgun's 4 kills the Boy and the pistol's 1 wounds the Warboss.
- **Fix sketch:** Before rolling in the sequential/interactive and melee flows, coalesce confirmed assignments that share an attack_identity_key and target into one batch. Add 'close_quarters' to NON_RESOLUTION_ABILITIES. Drop 'precision' (and inapplicable ANTI) from the key when the target lacks the matching models.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/rules/AttackSequence.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 04.03 cross-weapon gathering is not implemented in any live path. The Shooting phase merges only assignments with the same weapon_id and target: at _process_confirm_targets, and in resolve_shoot, which splits any group with more than one weapon. resolve_shoot itself only runs for Overwatch, snap-shot follow-ups, codex forced shots and Mathhammer.

The interactive paths all resolve and save per weapon: the staged and single-player fast-roll paths (_resolve_next_weapon), the networked fast path and the AI path (resolve_shoot_until_wounds, one save_data per assignment), and melee (resolve_melee_attacks and the interactive version). The defender therefore gets one allocation batch, and one overlay, per weapon. Identical boltgun and bolt-pistol (or Shoota and Slugga) attacks are never sorted lowest-first together, and [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] and [PRECISION] scopes end at each weapon's batch. This is player-visible, low to medium severity.

Separately, AttackSequence.attack_identity_key would block correct gathering once it is wired in. It keeps close_quarters even though 24.27 says it is identical to the excluded pistol, and all shipped 11e pistols use close_quarters. It keeps PRECISION and ANTI whatever the target (04.03 FAQ). It also keeps gather-time dice-count abilities such as [RAPID FIRE] (24.30 applies when gathering dice), so the real 11e Boltgun [RAPID FIRE 1] and Bolt Pistol [CLOSE-QUARTERS] (both BS3 S5 AP-1 D1) would still not match. These key defects have no live effect today, because the key only feeds same-weapon merges.

The developers already list this gap as deferred in docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md:94. The ✅ in 11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:105 is inaccurate.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. The code, the rules and the developers' own audit all back it.

RULE: In the launch text (core_rules.txt:668-700), gathering is mandatory: if other weapons targeting that unit make identical attacks, "they do so now and you gather those weapons' attack dice as well". The pg-20 example gathers 2 boltguns and a bolt pistol into five dice. The 04.03.01 commentary defines identical attacks as same BS/WS, S, AP, D and the same applicable abilities. The 04.03 FAQ in the errata file says [PRECISION] is not applicable against a target with no CHARACTER. Under 24.27, [PISTOL] and [CLOSE-QUARTERS] are "identical for all rules purposes".

ENGINE:
(1) RulesEngine.gd:841-855 (resolve_shoot) calls gather_identical_attacks. It only merges groups where weapon_ids.size()==1, and splits any group with more than one weapon back into separate assignments. Its own comment says cross-weapon gathering "lands with the ISS-048 rework". ISS-048 is closed as DONE, but its status text never mentions this.
(2) resolve_shoot only runs for Overwatch (Charge/MovementPhase _resolve_overwatch_shooting), snap-shot follow-ups, codex forced shots and Mathhammer.
(3) The real Shooting-phase flows never gather across weapons:
- _process_confirm_targets (ShootingPhase:1607) merges only matching weapon_id+target.
- The staged single-player path and the SP fast roll (_resolve_next_weapon, ShootingPhase:4071) send "Only this weapon" per call.
- The networked fast path (:1879) and the AI path (_resolve_ai_shoot, :3074) call resolve_shoot_until_wounds, which loops per assignment (RulesEngine:927-929) and emits one save_data per weapon.
- Saves then resolve one save_data per batch (resolve_allocation_batch_11e; _auto_roll_saves_11e). Neither AllocationGroupOverlay nor ShootingController merges save_data.
(4) Melee: resolve_melee_attacks loops per assignment (:13376), and resolve_melee_attacks_interactive builds "Save data per assignment". FightPhase does no gathering.
(5) AttackSequence.gd:430 sets NON_RESOLUTION_ABILITIES = [pistol, assault, extra_attacks], so close_quarters stays in the key. attack_identity_key takes only a target id and keeps PRECISION/ANTI whatever the target.

DATA: The shipped 11e armies tag pistols "close quarters", never PISTOL (sm11e/sw11e Bolt Pistol, Ork Slugga). RULINGS.md:485 says the same.

LIVE: GameConstants.edition defaults to 11 (scripts/rules/GameConstants.gd:27), so players hit this.

NO RULING JUSTIFIES IT: docs/rules/11th_edition_delta_audit.md:94 lists 04.03 as partial (🟡) with "cross-weapon gather deferred; melee gather not implemented". 11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:105 marks batch resolution ✅ via the "live weapon-order/fast-roll UI", but the code shows that UI is per-weapon, so that tick is wrong. RULINGS.md has no 04.03 decision. No branch or commit addresses this.

SCENARIO: The Boy/Warboss walk-through is correct under 05.04 lowest-first allocation (CHARACTER last; an unmodified 1 always fails).

OVERSTATEMENTS (minor):
- Same-weapon merging also happens at confirm time, not only in resolve_shoot.
- The identity-key problems are latent today, because the key only feeds same-weapon merges.
- player_visible should be true: the defender gets one allocation overlay per weapon.

## EA-58 · 04.03.05 — A fight activation that makes no attacks still counts as 'has fought / has attacked'

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 04.03.05: 'If a unit was selected to fight and none of the models in it make any attacks, it has not fought.'
- **Engine:** When no enemy is in ER after the fight moves, _request_attack_assignment calls _finish_fight_activation_11e (FightPhase.gd:1205-1217). That runs FOD removal 'has fought' (2901) and Ork on_unit_has_fought (2902). It also runs the on_unit_has_attacked expiries (2945-2949) and SMAbilitiesC.on_unit_has_fought (RIGHTEOUS FURY heal, 2958). It then opens the enemy_has_fought window and offers COUNTER-OFFENSIVE (2958-3013).
- **Scenario:** Captain Titus's unit uses RIGHTEOUS FURY when selected to fight, but its only engaged enemy died to Deadly Demise before it swung. It makes no attacks, yet heals D3 'when it has fought'. The opponent is also offered COUNTER-OFFENSIVE 'after an enemy unit has resolved its attacks'.
- **Fix sketch:** Record whether the activation actually resolved any attack. On a zero-attack activation, stamp has_fought for sequencing but skip the has-fought/has-attacked hooks and the enemy_has_fought/Counter-Offensive windows, and leave FOD-kept models until the end of the phase.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesC.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, slightly overstated. A fight activation that ends with no attacks is treated as "has fought". Cause: the zero-target branch of _request_attack_assignment (FightPhase.gd:1205-1217) calls _finish_fight_activation_11e. A player reaches it when an overrun fight's optional pile-in is declined or cannot reach, or when the last engaged enemy dies mid-activation. Effects:
- RIGHTEOUS FURY heals Titus (SMAbilitiesC.gd:1024, no attack check; D3, or 2D3 if an enemy engaged when it was used later lost a model).
- The enemy_has_fought window offers INDOMITABLE GUARDIANS and TRANSHUMAN SWIFTNESS ("when an enemy unit has fought").
- Try Dat Button!'s two-dice hazard roll fires, contradicting the engine's own RULINGS.md:133 ("a unit that never attacks makes no hazard roll").
- Models kept by Fight On Death are removed at activation end instead of at end of phase (05.04.05).
- COUNTER-OFFENSIVE is offered. Its 15.12 trigger is "has resolved its attacks", so this point is a reasonable but interpretive reading.
SKIP_UNIT (FightPhase.gd:3333-3341) also does the Fight On Death removal and the Try Dat hazard roll on a zero-attack activation. Its Fight On Death removal is pinned by test_merge_916_917_interactions.gd section A as "it has fought". The on_unit_has_attacked expiries (2945-2949) do nothing on this path, because those effects need the enemy to have selected targets first. HAMMER BLOW is gated on hit targets, so it is unaffected. Severity: low.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: errata 04.03.05 commentary says a unit selected to fight that makes no attacks "has not fought". 05.04.05 keeps Fight On Death (FOD) models "until their unit is selected to attack and has attacked, or until the end of the phase". The 12.04 FAQ confirms a selected unit can make no attacks. RULINGS.md and docs/ hold no ruling on 04.03.05 or 04.03.06.

Engine: the zero-target branch of _request_attack_assignment (FightPhase.gd:1205-1217) calls _finish_fight_activation_11e. A player reaches it when an overrun fight's optional pile-in is declined or cannot reach (_validate_pile_in_11e accepts an empty move, around line 6487), or when the last engaged enemy dies mid-activation. _finish_fight_activation_11e never checks whether an attack was made. All cited lines match (2901, 2902, 2945-2949, 2958, 2962-2975, 2992-3013).

Verified effects on a zero-attack activation:
1. SMAbilitiesC.on_unit_has_fought (1024-1053) heals Titus D3, or 2D3 under the engine's kill approximation, with no attack check. The RIGHTEOUS FURY offer (1422-1429) has no engagement gate. The rule text is "when this unit has fought".
2. The enemy_has_fought window opens and offers INDOMITABLE GUARDIANS and TRANSHUMAN SWIFTNESS ("when an enemy unit has fought"). Neither offer builder checks for attacks (CustodesCodexFight.gd:401/481, SMCodexAMovement.gd:357).
3. Ork11eDetachments.on_unit_has_fought runs _finish_try_dat, which makes the Try Dat Button! two-dice hazard roll. This contradicts the developers' own RULINGS.md:133: "a unit that never attacks makes no hazard roll".
4. _ork11e_fod_remove(...,"has fought") removes FOD-kept models at activation end, not at end of phase.
5. COUNTER-OFFENSIVE is offered. Its WHEN is "just after an enemy unit has resolved its attacks", so this point is a reasonable reading, not literal 04.03.05.

Overstated or omitted: the on_unit_has_attacked expiries (2945-2949) are no-ops here, because those effects need the enemy to have selected targets first. HAMMER BLOW is gated on hit targets (SWAbilities11e.gd:1548-1561). The BasePhase enemy_unit_has_attacked event keys on destroyed units, so it is not raised. The claim misses SKIP_UNIT (FightPhase.gd:3333-3341), which also does the FOD removal and the Try Dat hazard roll. That FOD removal is pinned by test_merge_916_917_interactions.gd section A (FC-2) as "it has fought", a merge-fix choice rather than a RULINGS entry. None of this overlaps the Hidden items already being fixed elsewhere.

## EA-59 · 05.03 FAQ1 — Allocation validator rejects every order when two non-CHARACTER groups are wounded (latent soft-lock)

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 05.03 FAQ: several non-CHARACTER groups that contain a wounded model 'can be selected in an order of your choosing but must be in an order that makes them come before any non-CHARACTER groups that do not contain a model with a missing wound'.
- **Engine:** Allocation.validate_order errors unless EACH wounded non-CHARACTER group is seq[0] (Allocation.gd:88-92), which no order satisfies when there are two. AllocationGroupOverlay then disables Confirm (682-690), and _on_confirm_pressed returns early (727-730), in auto_mode too (162-167), so the batch never resolves. default_order (Allocation.gd:119-129) is FAQ-correct, and RulesEngine falls back to it (3652-3659).
- **Scenario:** Latent. It needs a mixed unit (e.g. Tankbustas W2 + Boss Nob W3, Warbikers W3 + Nob W4) with a wounded model in both groups. Current allocation, mortal-wound and heal rules keep wounds in one group, and I found no live path that creates this. Any rule that does would freeze the save overlay for human and AI defenders alike.
- **Fix sketch:** Replace the first check with 'every wounded non-CHARACTER group precedes every unwounded non-CHARACTER group'. Track 'seen unwounded NC' the same way the CHARACTER check does (Allocation.gd:103-112).
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/Allocation.gd`, `40k/scripts/AllocationGroupOverlay.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute it. Every part of the claim checks out against the code.

RULE: The launch text (core_rules.txt:791-801) only covers a single wounded non-CHARACTER (NC) group, which "must be first". The 05.03 FAQ (errata md:359-360) handles several wounded NC groups: any order among themselves, as long as they come before every unwounded NC group. RULINGS.md (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md) and docs/ record no decision that overrides this. test_iss041_allocation_groups.gd only tests one wounded group.

ENGINE:
- Allocation.validate_order (Allocation.gd:88-92) loops over every group and adds an error unless each wounded NC group is seq[0]. With two such groups, at most one can be seq[0], so every possible order fails.
- AllocationGroupOverlay._validate_order (682-690) disables Confirm when the order is invalid.
- _on_confirm_pressed (727-730) returns before rolling any saves.
- setup's auto_mode branch (162-167) calls _on_confirm_pressed and returns, so it hits the same block.
- The overlay has no timer or fallback. AIPlayer.gd:3400 treats an open AllocationGroupOverlay as a pending human interaction, so the AI waits indefinitely.
- The Shooting, Fight and Movement controllers all use this overlay.
- default_order (Allocation.gd:119-129) and CasualtyPreference.group_order_for both put every wounded NC group first, which matches the FAQ. RulesEngine._apply_saves_via_allocation_11e (3652-3659) falls back to default_order when validation fails.
- So paths that skip the overlay do not freeze. For example, an AI attacker's saves resolved through ShootingPhase._auto_roll_saves_11e only log a rejection and use the default order.
- Batches that go through the overlay would freeze, for human defenders and auto_mode/AI defenders alike. This matches what the claim says.

REACHABILITY: I agree it is latent. No path I traced can leave two NC groups wounded at once:
- Normal damage: Allocation.apply_save_rolls puts damage on the current group's wounded model and only moves to the next group once the current one is fully destroyed.
- Mortal wounds, hazard rolls and devastating wounds: 06.02 mortal wounds, 06.03 hazard rolls (which inflict mortal wounds) and 24.10 devastating wounds all pick a wounded NC model first. _materialize_allocation_11e updates the unit copy in place, so devastating wounds follow the model that normal damage just wounded.
- Heals: the heal helpers (OrkAbilities11e, OrkCodexMovement, SMAbilitiesC, SMDetachmentsB) heal a wounded model before reviving a destroyed one with 1 W.
- W bonuses: these come only from list-build effects (ArmyListManager, SM/SW/Custodes wargear and enhancements), which keep full-health models at full. I found no runtime diffs that change W.
- Attached units: every leader model is marked is_character, so leaders never form NC groups.

The fix sketch (every wounded NC group must come before every unwounded NC group, tracked the same way as the CHARACTER check at Allocation.gd:103-112) is correct. The low severity and player_visible=false ratings are accurate.

## EA-60 · 05.04.04 — Destroyed-model triggers (Deadly Demise, transport destruction) resolve mid-activation

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 05.04.04: if a rule is triggered when a model is destroyed and the model was destroyed by an attack, 'those rules are only resolved and that model is only removed after the attacking unit's attacks have been resolved'.
- **Engine:** Kill detection runs on every executed action (ShootingPhase.gd:279-288; FightPhase.gd:372-385). It resolves transport destruction and Deadly Demise immediately (ShootingPhase.gd:349-353, 431-468; FightPhase.gd:442-449), between one weapon's saves and the next weapon of the same attacking unit.
- **Scenario:** Boyz assign choppas at a Rhino and the Nob's power klaw at an adjacent squad. The choppas wreck the Rhino first and Deadly Demise rolls a 6, so D3 mortal wounds hit every unit within 6", including the Boyz, before the Nob swings. If the Nob dies he never attacks; in 11e the Boyz finish all their attacks before Deadly Demise resolves.
- **Fix sketch:** Queue units destroyed during an attacking unit's activation (shooting weapon sequence or fight activation). Run the destroyed-triggers when that activation completes (shooting completion paths / _finish_fight_activation_11e), keeping the models on the battlefield until then.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is real but narrower than stated. Kill detection (_check_kill_diffs on every executed action) resolves emergency disembark and Deadly Demise as soon as a unit is wiped. On the weapon-by-weapon paths that happens before the attacking unit has finished, against 05.04.04 and the 24.08 example. In shooting (sequential/staged human resolution, including single-player fast-roll auto-continue, and AI attacks where a human defender rolls saves batch by batch) the triggers fire after one weapon's saves and before the next weapon rolls. Shooters killed by the explosion are then dropped from later weapons, and later weapons whose target the explosion destroyed are skipped. In the staged fight the next weapon's hits are rolled in the same action as the previous weapon's saves, so Deadly Demise lands between that weapon's hit and wound rolls and before any later weapon's hits. The one-shot paths (AI atomic SHOOT with auto saves, resolve_melee_attacks, non-staged interactive melee) already resolve the triggers after the whole activation. In the scenario as written (choppas, then the klaw) the Nob has already rolled hits, so he still finishes his attack even if Deadly Demise kills him, though the explosion can hit the klaw's target before its wounds and saves. He loses his attack only if his weapon resolves after another one. Severity low.
- **Verifier reasoning:** RULE: the launch core rules ('Destroyed', 40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt ~4036-4043) and the official commentary for 05.04.04 (errata_faq_commentary.md:393-395) agree. When a model is destroyed by an attack, any rules it triggers are resolved, and the model is removed, only after the attacking unit's attacks have been resolved. The 24.08 Deadly Demise example (core ~3687-3700) states the order: the attacking unit's unresolved attacks first, then the emergency disembark, then the Deadly Demise roll, then removal. The 18.05 errata only changes where disembarking models are placed. RULINGS.md and docs/ contain no ruling that defers or waives this; delta-audit row 24.08 covers only the disembark-before-Deadly-Demise order.

ENGINE: ShootingPhase.execute_action (279-291) and FightPhase.execute_action (372-387) run _check_kill_diffs after every successful action. For a unit that is now fully destroyed, it immediately runs transport destruction / emergency disembark and Deadly Demise (Shooting 349-355, 431-468; Fight 442-449, 519-553) and applies the mortal wounds at once. The only hold, _window_hold_deadly_demise (BasePhase 1310), waits for a unit_destroyed stratagem window that has offers; it does not wait for the attacker to finish. Grepping for 05.04.04 or "attacking unit's attacks" finds no deferral queue, and _finish_fight_activation_11e only handles Ork Fight On Death.

WHERE IT HAPPENS MID-ACTIVATION:
(a) Shooting, sequential and sequential_staged modes (the default for a human attacker; single-player fast roll uses the same path via a deferred _auto_continue_next_weapon, 7610-7621). Each weapon's APPLY_SAVES returns its own diffs, so the triggers resolve before the next weapon is rolled. The next weapon's hits then go through _resolve_assignment_hits -> _filter_eligible_model_ids, which drops shooters marked 'dead' (RulesEngine 2205-2220, 11920-11924). _resolve_next_weapon also skips weapons whose target was destroyed in the meantime.
(b) AI atomic SHOOT against a human defender who rolls their own saves: each save batch returns its diffs separately (_process_apply_saves_ai_atomic).
(c) Staged fight (human attacker in single-player, or AI attacker vs a human defender's saves): after weapon N's saves, _staged_fight_assignment_complete -> _staged_fight_roll_hits rolls weapon N+1's hits and returns weapon N's diffs in the same result. Deadly Demise therefore lands after N+1's hit roll but before its wound roll and saves, and before any later weapon's hits. Melee hit resolution skips dead attackers.

ALREADY CORRECT: the one-shot paths already resolve the triggers after the whole activation. These are the AI atomic SHOOT with auto saves (one combined result), _process_roll_dice_auto / resolve_melee_attacks, and non-staged interactive melee.

OVERSTATED: in the auditor's exact two-weapon scenario (choppas, then the Nob's klaw), the klaw's hits are rolled before Deadly Demise fires. _resolve_melee_assignment_wounds does not re-check that the attacker is alive, so the Nob still finishes his attack even if the explosion kills him. The explosion can still damage or wipe the klaw's target before its wound roll and saves. The Nob loses his attack only when his weapon resolves after another weapon. In shooting, a shooter killed by Deadly Demise loses all of its later weapons.

## EA-61 · 05.04.06 — Measuring to a destroyed unit uses every model's stored position, not the last model destroyed

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** medium
- **Rule:** 05.04.06: 'When a player has to measure the distance to a destroyed unit, they measure to the last model destroyed in that unit.' A destroyed model is measured to any point its base occupied.
- **Engine:** Destroyed models keep their position (only alive=false is written, RulesEngine.gd:4081). _destroyed_enemy_near_objective_11e returns true if ANY model of a destroyed unit is within objective range, including models killed earlier elsewhere (MissionManager.gd:3178-3191). That check drives the 'destroyed_near_central' VP (2775-2779). Deadly Demise likewise measures from every model of the destroyed unit (RulesEngine.gd:17484-17497).
- **Scenario:** Secure Asset: a 20-Boy mob loses 12 Boyz on the central objective in round 2, and its last 8 Boyz are destroyed 20" away in round 4. The engine still awards the destroyed-on-central-objective VP from the dead Boyz' stored positions. Measured to the last model destroyed, the unit does not qualify.
- **Fix sketch:** When a unit's final alive=false diff is applied, record its last destroyed model (index + position). Use only that model for destroyed-unit distance checks (mission scoring, Deadly Demise of multi-model units).
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/MissionManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** This is not a 05.04.06 measurement defect; it is a defect in how mission scoring approximates "started the turn on the objective".

Secure Asset's 2VP kill row requires an enemy unit that STARTED the turn within range of a central objective and was destroyed (missionCards.json:1234-1237, victim_started_turn_on_objective). The engine approximates this from every dead model's stored position (MissionManager._destroyed_enemy_near_objective_11e, 3178-3191), including models killed in earlier rounds. This happens because on_turn_start_11e (1726-1737) snapshots only unit IDs, not positions.

Effects:
- A mob that lost models on the central objective in an earlier round and is finished off elsewhere wrongly scores 2VP. The claim's scenario is correct on this point.
- A unit that started on the objective but moved off before its models died can be missed.

The same proxy affects:
- Purge and Secure (destroyed_started_on_objective)
- Death Trap (destroyed_in_terrain_area)
- Overwhelming Force (SecondaryMissionManager._check_unit_near_any_objective, "alive or dead")

Fix: at on_turn_start_11e, record which enemy units are within range of each objective or inside each terrain area. Score destroyed units against that record, not against the last model destroyed.

Deadly Demise is a separate rule (24.08), not 05.04.06. It should roll for each destroyed model and measure from that model. The engine rolls once on whole-unit destruction and measures from all models, which only matters for multi-model units such as Killa Kans.

Where 05.04.06's destroyed-unit sentence really applies (FLYIN' HEADBUTT, VICTORY BEFORE DEATH), every eligible unit is single-model, so the engine already gives the same result as measuring to the last model destroyed.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The code facts and the Secure Asset scenario hold up, but the claim pins them on the wrong rule, and its fix would not give rules-correct results.

What I confirmed:
- The 05.04.06 commentary says what the claim quotes (errata md:401-403). The launch text has no such passage, and no ruling on it exists in RULINGS.md or docs/.
- RulesEngine.gd:4081 writes only alive=false, so dead models keep their positions.
- MissionManager._destroyed_enemy_units_this_turn_11e (3162) picks units that were alive at the start of the turn and are fully dead now.
- _destroyed_enemy_near_objective_11e (3178-3191) then checks every model of those units, including models killed in earlier rounds. _model_in_objective_range (511) has no alive check.
- That check drives Secure Asset's 2VP row (2775-2779; PrimaryMissionData11e.gd:300-303).
- So in the claim's scenario the engine does award 2VP from the round-2 Boyz' stored positions.

Why 05.04.06 is not the governing rule here:
- missionCards.json:1229-1240 defines Secure Asset's row as destroyed-while-on-objective with victim_started_turn_on_objective=true, objective_role=central. The card text says "enemy units that started the turn on central objectives."
- So the official test is where the unit was at the start of the turn. It is not a measurement to a destroyed unit.
- The engine labels the row a "dead-model position proxy" with approximate:true, and the UI shows "~" next to the card name (ScoringController.gd:372).
- The turn-start snapshot (on_turn_start_11e, 1726-1737) records only unit IDs, never positions.
- Using only the last model destroyed would still be wrong both ways. A unit that started on central and whose last model died after a pile-in or consolidation off it should score but would not. A unit that came onto central during the turn and died there should not score but would.
- The same proxy is used by Purge and Secure (destroyed_started_on_objective, 2841), Death Trap (_destroyed_enemy_in_terrain_11e, 3199) and the Overwhelming Force secondary (SecondaryMissionManager._check_unit_near_any_objective, 2066, "alive or dead").

Deadly Demise:
- Rule 24.08 (core rules txt:3676-3684) triggers per model: "each unit within 6\" of that model." That falls under 05.04.06's sentence about a destroyed model, not the one about a destroyed unit.
- The engine rolls once, only when the whole unit is dead (ShootingPhase.gd:313-353, BasePhase.gd:1278, FightPhase.gd:519, WoundAllocationOverlay.gd:1191). It then measures from every model (RulesEngine.gd:16929, 17465-17514).
- That is correct for single-model units. It is a separate 24.08 deviation for Killa Kans (3-6 models, Deadly Demise 1). The claim's "last model only" fix would be wrong there.

Where 05.04.06's destroyed-unit sentence really applies, there is no visible deviation:
- FLYIN' HEADBUTT (_wreck_distance, OrkCodexOther.gd:618) and VICTORY BEFORE DEATH (vbd_objectives, CustodesCodexOther.gd:413, include_dead=true) are the two sites.
- Every eligible datasheet is single-model: Dakkajet, Burna-bommer, Blitza-bommer and Wazbom Blastajet; Caladius, Caladius Annihilator, Pallas and Coronus. All are model_count 1/1, so "every model" and "last model destroyed" give the same answer.
- IN SACRIFICE, VICTORY (multi-model Deathwing/Ravenwing units) is not implemented.

This was static reading only. I did not run the game.

## EA-62 · 09.05.01 Commentary (one Normal Move per phase) — Reactive normal moves are not limited to one Normal Move per phase

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 09.05.01: 'A unit cannot make more than one Normal Move in a phase.'
- **Engine:** Only the Movement phase's BEGIN_NORMAL_MOVE validator checks _normal_moves_this_phase (MovementPhase.gd:633-637). Reactive normal moves (MovementPhase._ork11e_begin_reactive_normal_move at 6415; ShootingPhase._ork11e_start_reactive_move at 5523) and the stratagems that grant them (RESPONSIVE TACTICS / TRANSHUMAN REACTIONS, SMCodexAMovement.gd:387-397; MASTERFUL TACTICS, SMCodexBAction.gd:187-197, 406-407) never check it. has_unit_normal_moved_this_phase and mark_unit_normal_moved_this_phase (MovementPhase.gd:7009-7018) have no callers.
- **Scenario:** An army takes Phobos Shock Force and Tactical Brethren (1 DP each). In the opponent's Movement phase, an Infiltrator Squad uses TRANSHUMAN REACTIONS after one enemy move and MASTERFUL TACTICS after another, making two normal moves in the same phase. 09.05.01 forbids the second.
- **Fix sketch:** Stamp a phase-scoped flag (round:active player:phase) on every normal move, reactive ones included, on all Attached components. Refuse a reactive normal move, and hide its offer, when the stamp matches. Make BEGIN_NORMAL_MOVE read the same flag instead of the MovementPhase-local dictionary.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexAMovement.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexBAction.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** 09.05.01 is not enforced for reactive normal moves. MovementPhase._ork11e_begin_reactive_normal_move (6446) and ShootingPhase._ork11e_start_reactive_move (5532) never read `_normal_moves_this_phase`; only BEGIN_NORMAL_MOVE does (MovementPhase.gd:636-640). The has_/mark_unit_normal_moved_this_phase helpers (7040-7048) are dead code. However, the cited scenario (TRANSHUMAN REACTIONS then MASTERFUL TACTICS on one Infiltrator Squad) cannot happen. The engine already enforces 15.01's one stratagem per unit per phase (StratagemManager.gd:1042-1056, re-checked per target in get_window_offers at 1717-1722), so the second stratagem is never offered. The reachable violation needs a move from a source that isn't a stratagem. Example: in the opponent's Movement phase, a Lieutenant with Combi-weapon's unit uses RESPONSIVE TACTICS (Gladius) or MASTERFUL TACTICS (Tactical Brethren) after one enemy move. After another enemy move it uses its Evade and Survive datasheet ability (SMAbilitiesA.gd:1898-1908 / 2202-2207), which only checks its own once-per-phase use, unengaged, and 8". That is two normal moves in one phase. The same gap applies to the other free reactive normal-move abilities, detachment rules and enhancements (ON TO DA NEXT, Predatory Instinct, Repulsor Suspensor Technology, Mobile Hunter, the Ork ones). Severity low, player-visible for human players.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: the errata doc line 470 (09.05.01 Commentary) says "A unit cannot make more than one Normal Move in a phase." I found no RULINGS.md or docs ruling that exempts reactive moves. The movement-intents research even notes that a reactive move "is the unit's one normal move this phase".

The claim's engine facts hold (line numbers have drifted slightly):
- Only `_validate_begin_normal_move` reads `_normal_moves_this_phase` (MovementPhase.gd:636-640). BEGIN_ADVANCE reuses that check.
- `_process_confirm_unit_move` stamps the dictionary for mode NORMAL (3566-3569). Reactive moves set up as mode NORMAL get stamped too, but nothing ever reads the stamp for them.
- `_ork11e_begin_reactive_normal_move` (6446) and ShootingPhase `_ork11e_start_reactive_move` (5532) do no per-phase check. Neither does ReactiveMove11e.validate, `_validate_confirm_unit_move` (1003) or `_validate_stage_model_move`.
- `has_unit_normal_moved_this_phase` / `mark_unit_normal_moved_this_phase` (7040-7048) have no callers anywhere.
- The RESPONSIVE TACTICS / TRANSHUMAN REACTIONS `_check` (SMCodexAMovement.gd:386-397) and the MASTERFUL TACTICS `_check` (SMCodexBAction.gd:512-523) do not check for an earlier normal move. Krump and Run eligibility (MovementPhase.gd:5161+) doesn't either.

The scenario is wrong, though. Rule 15.01 (core rules lines 2498-2501) says "each player cannot target the same unit with more than one stratagem in the same phase". The engine enforces this in StratagemManager.can_use_stratagem (1042-1056; it compares target, turn, phase and active player, and counts the whole Attached unit via `_attached_unit_member_ids`). get_window_offers also re-runs can_use_stratagem for every candidate target (1717-1722), and use_stratagem validates the same way before recording the use (1352, 1437). So once TRANSHUMAN REACTIONS has been used on the Infiltrator Squad, MASTERFUL TACTICS is never offered on it that phase. That play is illegal under 15.01 anyway. Two stratagems can never give one unit two normal moves in a phase.

The gap is still reachable when at least one move comes from a source that isn't a stratagem. Example: Evade and Survive, the Lieutenant with Combi-weapon's datasheet ability (once per phase, in the enemy_ended_move window). Its offer (SMAbilitiesA.gd:1898-1908) only checks its own once-per-phase use, that the unit is unengaged, and the 8" range. It then hands a reactive_normal_move follow-up to MovementPhase (2202-2207). These offers are live in normal play; only ScenarioRunner sets `offers_suppressed`. Each enemy move opens a fresh enemy_ended_move window (MovementPhase.gd:3833). So the Lieutenant's unit can take RESPONSIVE TACTICS or MASTERFUL TACTICS after one enemy move and Evade and Survive after another, in either order: two normal moves in the opponent's Movement phase. Other free reactive normal moves have the same gap: ON TO DA NEXT, Predatory Instinct, Repulsor Suspensor Technology / Mobile Hunter, and the Ork abilities and enhancement.

In the Shooting phase, a human cannot chain SHADOW MASTERS and EXECUTE AND REDEPLOY in one unit_has_shot window. That is only because BasePhase.gd:897 does not re-queue the window while a follow-up is pending, not because the rule is enforced.

I did not run the game (static reading only, as instructed).

## EA-63 · 09.07.01 / 09.02.02 — fall-back moves made outside the Movement phase — Tactical Fluidity's fall-back ignores fall-back modes; WIND-SWIFT EVASION's Desperate Escape skips the battle-shock roll

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 09.07: 'Ordered Retreat: If your unit is not battle-shocked, you can select this mode. Desperate Escape: Otherwise, you must select this mode. Make a hazard roll for each model…' and AFTER MOVING 'Desperate Escape: If your unit is not battle-shocked, you must make a battle-shock roll'. 09.07.01: a unit that an enemy rule instructs to make a Desperate Escape test must use desperate escape when it falls back, and must make a battle-shock roll.
- **Engine:** SMAbilitiesB._auto_fall_back_diffs (SMAbilitiesB.gd:1169-1240, used at 1791-1797) moves the unit up to 6" without calling FallBackMove.select_mode or before_moving. It never makes DE hazard rolls, even when the unit is battle-shocked or engaged with a Corner the Quarry, Savage Frenzy or Wimp-kickaz unit. RULINGS.md:420 justifies this with 10e reasoning ('no model crosses an enemy model'), which conflicts with the 11e modes. WIND-SWIFT EVASION does roll DE hazards (SMCodexAMovement.gd:627-650) but never makes the post-move battle-shock roll that MovementPhase makes (MovementPhase.gd:3609-3618).
- **Scenario:** At the end of the opponent's Fight phase, a battle-shocked Captain in Phobos Armour's unit (or one engaged with Nobz carrying Wimp-kickaz) uses Tactical Fluidity. The engine moves it away with no hazard rolls; the rules require Desperate Escape with one hazard roll per model before moving. Separately, an unshocked MOUNTED unit forced into DE by Savage Frenzy uses WIND-SWIFT EVASION: it rolls the hazards but makes no battle-shock roll.
- **Fix sketch:** Route Tactical Fluidity through FallBackMove.select_mode and before_moving, as SMCodexAMovement._fall_back_before_moving already does, and allow crossing enemy models only in DE. After any DE fall-back outside MovementPhase, make the 09.07 battle-shock roll with FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test if the unit was not battle-shocked. Update the RULINGS.md:420 entry.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMAbilitiesB.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_marines/SMCodexAMovement.gd`, `.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md`
- **Verifier reasoning:** Could not refute; all three parts hold. RULE: launch 09.07 (core_rules.txt:1417-1441): BEFORE MOVING, Ordered Retreat only if not battle-shocked, otherwise Desperate Escape is mandatory with one hazard roll per model. AFTER MOVING (DE): if not battle-shocked, make a battle-shock roll. Commentary 09.07.01/09.02.02 (errata:460-474): a rule that forces DE also requires the hazard rolls AND a battle-shock roll. Tactical Fluidity's printed text (Datasheets_abilities.csv:557) is 'this unit can make a fall-back move of up to 6"', so it is a 09.07 fall-back with modes. TACTICAL FLUIDITY: SMAbilitiesB.use (1791-1798) calls _auto_fall_back_diffs (1169+). That only emits model position diffs plus the once-per-round marker. It never calls FallBackMove.select_mode / before_moving / after_moving_effects and makes no hazard or battle-shock roll. A battle-shocked unit CAN reach this: 01.07 only bars stratagems and actions; SMAbilitiesB ability cards are routed to SMAbilitiesB.can_use before StratagemManager's battle-shock gate (StratagemManager.gd:961 vs 1073-1079); _targets for fight_phase_end only checks used_this_round and _engaged (1578-1584); the only battle-shock filter in the file is for psychic powers (_psychic_ready, line 546). Corner the Quarry, Savage Frenzy and Wimp-kickaz are not phase-locked, in the printed text or in the engine helpers (CustodesAbilities.corner_the_quarry_source, SWAbilities11e.savage_frenzy_source, OrkEnhancements11e.forces_desperate_escape), so select_mode would force DE at the end of the Fight phase if it were called (FallBackMove.gd:61-72). RULINGS.md:420 explicitly records 'no desperate-escape tests: no model crosses an enemy model'. That crossing-based premise is the 10e trigger; under 11e the mode depends on battle-shock or forcing rules, not crossing. Even in 10e a battle-shocked unit tested every model. It also contradicts the project's own RULINGS.md:397 for WIND-SWIFT, which treats an out-of-phase fall-back as using the modes. So the ruling is an incorrect simplification, not a valid exemption. WIND-SWIFT EVASION: SMCodexAMovement._fall_back_before_moving (627-652) does call select_mode and before_moving, so the hazard rolls happen. After the move, _finish_fight_move -> BasePhase._finish_window_followup only applies positions. Nothing calls after_moving_effects (so pending_battleshock_roll is never set) or force_battle_shock_test. The only DE battle-shock roll in the engine is MovementPhase.gd:3609-3620; grep finds no other 'Desperate Escape' force_battle_shock_test caller and no other pending_battleshock_roll consumer. Because WIND-SWIFT is a stratagem and SMCodexAMovement.allows_battle_shocked_target returns false, its target is never battle-shocked. So its DE only happens when an enemy rule forces it, where the 09.07 battle-shock roll is always owed and never made. Neither path has a test for these cases (test_sm11e_abilities_b.gd section 13; no DE coverage in the WIND-SWIFT test or scenario). No commit on this branch has changed these paths since they were added. The cited line numbers match (function bodies run a few lines past the quoted ranges). The claim is not overstated. Minor context: the AI never uses Tactical Fluidity (score 0.0), so only a human player reaches that path. Side observation outside the claim: _auto_fall_back_diffs checks only end positions, not paths, so even the ruling's own 'no model crosses an enemy model' premise is not enforced.

## EA-64 · 12.08 Errata (Ongoing Consolidation forced fights) — Forced fights after consolidation key on 'was eligible' instead of 'selected to fight', and also fire after Objective consolidations

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Errata 12.08, Ongoing Consolidation (the launch Engaging text is the same): 'If one or more enemy units engaged with your unit have not been selected to fight this phase, your opponent must select each of those units, one at a time; when each is selected, it becomes eligible to fight and is selected to fight.' Objective Consolidation has no such clause.
- **Engine:** _process_consolidate_step_11e calls _scan_newly_eligible_units_after_consolidation after every consolidation whatever its mode (FightPhase.gd:3274-3301). The scan skips any unit stamped was_eligible_to_fight (FightPhase.gd:4797-4807) and any unit in units_that_fought, and _begin_consolidation_step_11e marks every passed (p.74) unit as fought (FightPhase.gd:4377-4392). So a unit that was eligible but never selected to fight can never be forced (already documented as FC-5 in docs/CORE_RULES_REMEDIATION_2026-10.md:279-280 and 334-335). Separately, an Objective consolidation that ends engaged triggers forced fights the rules don't give.
- **Scenario:** (a) An Ork unit charged but its target was destroyed; more than 5" from every enemy, it passed (12.04.01). A Custodes unit with Unstoppable Destroyer (4" consolidation) makes an ongoing consolidation into it. The errata makes the Ork player select it to fight; the engine does not. (b) A unit 4" from an unfought enemy (so in Objective mode) consolidates 3" onto an objective and ends within 2" of that enemy. The engine gives the enemy a forced fight; the rules give none.
- **Fix sketch:** Run the forced-fight scan only for the ongoing and engaging modes, and only over enemy units engaged with the consolidating unit. Exclude only units actually selected to fight: track the p.74 passed units separately instead of folding them into the fought set.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with two refinements. (1) The units the engine can never force are those still eligible when the Fight step ends, i.e. units whose player passed under p.74. They carry was_eligible_to_fight, so _scan_newly_eligible_units_after_consolidation skips them, and _begin_consolidation_step_11e marks them fought in the FightSequencer and sets fight_done_turn, so the sequencer never offers them either. An Engaging consolidation (launch text) or an Ongoing one (errata) that ends engaged with such a unit therefore never makes its owner select it to fight. This is the known, unfixed FC-5. A stamped unit whose eligibility lapsed before it was selected is not marked fought, so the sequencer's live-engagement check would still offer it once re-engaged, but without the forced-fight dialog or the switch of selecting player. (2) An Objective consolidation that legally ends within engagement range of an enemy unit that was never eligible this phase forces that unit to fight, which the rules do not allow. This happens both through the scan, which runs regardless of mode, and through FightSequencer.has_eligible/_forced_fights_pending_11e, which treats any live-engaged, un-fought unit as eligible and refuses further CONSOLIDATE until it fights. A fix therefore has to change the sequencer side as well as gate the scan by mode. For example: during the Consolidate step, treat as eligible only units explicitly forced by Engaging/Ongoing moves, and track units actually selected to fight separately from the p.74-passed units.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule text: errata 12.08 (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_errata_faq_commentary.md:492) adds the clause 'If one or more enemy units engaged with your unit have not been selected to fight this phase, your opponent must select each of those units... it becomes eligible to fight and is selected to fight' to Ongoing Consolidation. The launch Engaging text (core_rules.txt:1981-1986) has the same clause. Objective Consolidation has none, and the errata commentary on modes (line 462) says mode-labelled conditions 'only apply if you selected that mode'. p.74 (core_rules.txt:4059-4065) makes a pass an alternative to selecting a unit, so passed units were never 'selected to fight'.

(a) Confirmed for p.74-passed units. _scan_newly_eligible_units_after_consolidation (FightPhase.gd:4789-4805, 4836) skips every unit with the cumulative was_eligible_to_fight stamp and every unit in units_that_fought. _begin_consolidation_step_11e (4374-4390) handles units still eligible when the Fight step ends, which can only be p.74-passed units: it calls sequencer_11e.mark_fought and sets fight_done_turn on them. FightSequencer.eligible_to_fight returns false for units that are fought or fight_done, so neither the scan nor the sequencer (_forced_fights_pending_11e -> has_eligible) can ever force them. The developers recorded this as 'verified real, NOT fixed' (FC-5, CORE_RULES_REMEDIATION_2026-10.md:279-280 and 334-335). That is a known defect, not a ruling, and RULINGS.md and the tests contain no decision saying this behaviour is intended. The unit test header in test_consolidation_new_fights.gd assumes already-eligible units 'are picked up by the normal fight flow', but at Consolidate time passed units have been marked fought.

(b) Confirmed. _process_consolidate_step_11e (3222-3301) calls the scan after every consolidation with no mode check; chosen_objective is only used for logging. ConsolidationMove.model_move_allowed and after_moving_conditions in Objective mode check only objective range, coherency and overlaps, and the FightController drag uses the same verdict, so a legal Objective move can end within 2" of an enemy. An enemy that was never eligible this phase (no stamp) is then flagged and the forced-fight dialog opens. The FightSequencer also treats that live-engaged, un-fought unit as eligible, so CONSOLIDATE is refused until it fights. No test or ruling pins forced fights after Objective mode as intended; test_global_consolidation_11e only asserts the Engaging case. No stamp refresh runs between the move and the scan (stamp callers at lines 309, 985, 1172, 1189, 2941, 4238, 4373).

Minor overstatements: 'can never be forced' holds only for units still eligible at the end of the Fight step, i.e. p.74 passes. A unit stamped eligible whose eligibility lapsed before it was selected is not marked fought, so the sequencer would still offer it once re-engaged, though without the dialog. Also, the fix sketch's mode gate on the scan alone would not fix (b), because the sequencer's live-engagement eligibility still forces the fight.

## EA-65 · 12.08 Errata (Ongoing: each model that started engaged stays engaged) — 'Each model that started engaged must still be engaged' is checked per unit, not per model

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Errata 12.08, Ongoing Consolidation: 'Each model that started this move engaged with an enemy unit must still be engaged with that enemy unit.'
- **Engine:** ConsolidationMove.before_moving records a unit-level started_engaged_with list (ConsolidationMove.gd:97), and after_moving_conditions only checks check_units_in_engagement_range(unit, enemy) for each entry (ConsolidationMove.gd:169-175). _validate_consolidate_11e adds no per-model check (FightPhase.gd:6625-6630). Pile-in has the same unit-level check (PileInMove.gd:64, 88-93).
- **Scenario:** A unit is engaged with Boyz mob A and mob B. A model 1.9" from A (not in base contact), whose closest selected enemy is B, moves toward B and ends 2.5" from A, while another model stays engaged with A. The engine accepts the move; the errata forbids that model leaving engagement with A.
- **Fix sketch:** In before_moving, store for each model key the enemy unit ids it is engaged with. In after_moving_conditions, require each of those models to still be within engagement range of each recorded enemy unit. Mirror the change in PileInMove.
- **Files:** `40k/scripts/rules/movetypes/ConsolidationMove.gd`, `40k/phases/FightPhase.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, with two corrections. (1) The per-model clause is launch text for both 12.03 (pile-in) and 12.08 Ongoing Consolidation; the 12.08 errata repeats it unchanged and only adds the forced-fight sentence, so this is not an errata regression. (2) The gap only matters when the moving unit is engaged with two or more enemy units. With a single engaged enemy unit, the WHILE rule (each moved model ends strictly closer to its closest target model) already keeps each started-engaged model engaged. In the multi-enemy case, ConsolidationMove/PileInMove.after_moving_conditions check each started-engaged enemy at unit level only (any model pair in range, RulesEngine._check_units_in_engagement_range). So a model engaged with A can move toward a closer B and leave A's engagement range while another model keeps the unit engaged with A, and both FightPhase validators accept it. Severity low; it lets a player or the AI make a slightly too permissive move.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: the per-model sentence ("Each model that started this move engaged with an enemy unit must still be engaged with that enemy unit") is already in the LAUNCH text, in both 12.03 Pile-in AFTER MOVING and 12.08 Ongoing Consolidation AFTER MOVING (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt ~1764-1766 and ~1978-1980). Errata 12.08 (errata_faq_commentary.md:492) repeats it unchanged and only appends the forced-fight sentence. Under 03.04 a model is engaged with an enemy unit while it is within engagement range of one of that unit's models.

Engine: ConsolidationMove.before_moving (line 97) and PileInMove.before_moving (line 64) store only a unit-level list of enemy unit IDs (_engaged_enemy_ids). The AFTER checks (ConsolidationMove.gd:169-175, PileInMove.gd:88-93) call rules.check_units_in_engagement_range(unit, enemy, board), which (RulesEngine.gd:11373-11395) returns true as soon as ANY pair of models is in range. _validate_consolidate_11e (FightPhase.gd:6625-6631) and _validate_pile_in_11e (FightPhase.gd:6530-6535) add no per-model check, and neither does the UI drag check (check_consolidation_model_move_11e, which only calls model_move_allowed: base-contact lock plus 'end closer to the closest target model').

Search for something that already handles it: no per-model 'started engaged' tracking exists anywhere in 40k/ (grep for started_engaged / engaged_at_start / per-model engagement helpers). RULINGS.md, the delta audits and PRPs/fight_phase_11e_cleanup_plan.md record no decision to relax the rule. The tests (test_iss050_fight_phase_11e.gd:111-118, test_audit_already_done_pin.gd:221-228) only pin the unit-level case. Codex per-model helpers (OrkAbilities11e._model_engaged_with_unit, SMCodexBAction._models_engaged_with) exist but are not wired into these checks.

Scope: if the unit is engaged with only ONE enemy unit, the WHILE rule already covers it. A moved model must end strictly closer to its closest target model and started within 2", so it stays engaged; unmoved models stay engaged too. The gap only opens with two or more engaged enemy units, which is exactly the auditor's scenario: a model 1.9" from A whose closest target is B moves toward B and ends 2.5" from A while another model keeps the unit engaged with A. model_move_allowed accepts it (after < before), and the unit-level AFTER check passes. The claim's cited line numbers all match. Verified by static reading only; the game was not run, per the task.

## EA-66 · 13.06.01 Commentary (Solid terrain and movement) — INFANTRY can end a move inside a Solid wall and then see through it

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** If a terrain feature has the Solid rule, 'a model cannot end a move such that any part of it is through any enclosed part of that terrain feature that is 3″ or less from ground level – not even through small openings such as doors and windows'.
- **Engine:** End-of-move checks use Measurement.model_overlaps_any_wall (e.g. MovementPhase.gd:779-788). On 11e layouts, walls are polygon 'feature' pieces with empty wall lists, so only TerrainManager.solid_terrain_endpoint_blocker_11e applies. That function returns '' (no blocker) for INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE (TerrainManager.gd:1283-1288; Measurement.gd:301-316). A model whose centre is inside such a feature is treated as occupying it, and that wall then stops Solid-blocking its sight lines (visibility_exclusions_for :947-970; _line_blocked_11e :1187-1190). No model has elevation, so every model counts as being at ground level.
- **Scenario:** An INFANTRY model is moved so its base centre sits inside the 0.5″-thick dense wall of a corner ruin (e.g. corner-ruin-balanced-right-3 in take_and_hold_mirror_1). The move is accepted. The model can now see, and be seen by, enemy models on the far side of the wall at ground level. Rules: that end position is illegal, and the wall blocks line of sight (13.11).
- **Fix sketch:** For all keywords, reject end positions (normal move, charge, pile-in, consolidate, set-up) where the base overlaps a dense 'feature' piece the model cannot stand on, such as a wall thinner than its base. Limit the centre-occupancy Solid exemption in visibility_exclusions_for to features a model can stand on.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/TerrainManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/Measurement.gd`, `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/terrain_layouts/take_and_hold_mirror_1.json`
- **Verifier correction:** The claim is accurate and not overstated. Refinements for this branch:

1. Line refs: solid_terrain_endpoint_blocker_11e is TerrainManager.gd:1406-1418; visibility_exclusions_for is :988-1007; the _line_blocked_11e Solid skip is :1310-1313; the endpoint checks are MovementPhase.gd:782-791 and :921-931.

2. The rules-path visibility has the same centre-occupancy exemption: EnhancedLineOfSight.build_pair_context :107-123 and _check_single_line_of_sight_11e :516-519.

3. Scope is a little wider than claimed. EnhancedLineOfSight samples base-edge points for both shooter and target. So an INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE base whose centre sits just outside a 0.5" wall, but whose edge pokes past the far face, also sees and is seen past the wall. This is exactly the 'protruding elements' case 13.06.01 targets.

4. The exemption covers every endpoint the engine checks: normal/advance/fall-back moves, charge, pile-in/consolidate and deployment. It applies on all 45 gw-11e layouts, which author walls only as dense 'feature' polygons.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Refutation failed. Every part of the claim checks out on this branch (claude/affectionate-brown-rr3zen) and on main.
