RULE: The launch 13.06 'SOLID TERRAIN' sidebar (docs/rules/40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:2193-2213) and the 13.06.01 commentary (errata_faq_commentary.md:518-520) both say a model cannot END a move with any part of it through an enclosed part of a Solid (dense) feature that is 3" or less from ground level, not even through doors or windows. 13.06 lets INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE move THROUGH dense features. It does not let them end there.

ENDPOINT CHECK: Every endpoint caller uses Measurement.model_overlaps_any_wall(model, keywords) (Measurement.gd:301-316):
- MovementPhase.gd:790 (SET_MODEL_DEST) and :929 (STAGE_MODEL_MOVE)
- ChargePhase.gd:3061 (its comment says 'infantry may end among walls')
- FightPhase.gd:5032 (pile-in/consolidate)
- DeploymentPhase.gd:315/392
- MovementController drag

On 11e layouts this reaches only TerrainManager.solid_terrain_endpoint_blocker_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1406-1418), which returns "" through _passes_dense_11e for INFANTRY/BEASTS/SWARM/MOBILE. All 45 gw-11e layouts have zero wall segments, and the polygon loader (TerrainManager.gd:258-314) creates none. corner-ruin-balanced-right-3 is a 0.5"-thick, 5"-tall dense feature with walls: []. _position_intersects_terrain (MovementPhase.gd:7686) only rejects type 'impassable', and the wall's type is 'ruins'. _validate_confirm_unit_move (:1003) only re-checks fall-back engagement range and coherency. The engine's own test, test_solid_terrain_movement_11e.gd T5 (:136-140), asserts that INFANTRY may stage with its centre inside a wall stroke on take_and_hold_mirror_1.

VISIBILITY: visibility_exclusions_for (TerrainManager.gd:988-1007) puts any piece containing a model's centre into feature_ids. The Solid check then skips it, both in _line_blocked_11e (:1310-1313) and in EnhancedLineOfSight._check_single_line_of_sight_11e (:516-519). That second one is the rules path: RulesEngine._check_line_of_sight -> check_enhanced_visibility. The wall sits inside area-large-1 (3..10 x 35.5..47), so that area's obscuring is excluded too. elevation_inches is only ever read and never written outside tests, so every model is treated as ground level.

DEVELOPER RECORDS: ISSUES.md:675 lists 'the Solid 3"-enclosure end-of-move rule' as out of 2D scope, i.e. not implemented. The reason given there, that it needs an elevation model, does not apply when every model is at ground level. AIDecisionMaker.gd:2755-2757 and :30140-30150 say outright that '13.06.01 (commentary) forbids it, but the engine lets INFANTRY do it and then draws sight lines from the part that pokes through'. The AI avoids the exploit; the engine does not enforce the rule. RULINGS.md has no ruling on this. The issue is not on the 'already being fixed' list.

LIMITS: Hidden (13.09 detection range) and the obscuring of other terrain areas still apply. They do not change the finding. Line numbers in the claim match main and are shifted on this branch.

## EA-67 · 13.11.01 Commentary (Gone to Ground) — Dense terrain-area boundaries count as dense features for Gone to Ground

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** Gone to Ground needs the model to be 'not fully visible to the attacking model due to one or more intervening dense terrain features'. That means terrain features, not obscuring terrain areas.
- **Engine:** _obscured_by_dense_11e calls _line_blocked_11e with dense_only=true (TerrainManager.gd:1121-1133). In that mode, crossing a dense terrain-area boundary (piece_class 'area') blocks the line through the 13.10 obscuring branch (:1182-1186). The code itself says area boundaries are not physical structures (:1165-1168).
- **Scenario:** A hidden INFANTRY unit stands in a light-terrain area. An enemy unit 13″ away has a sight line that crosses the footprint of a different, dense ruin area through its open side, missing that ruin's walls. Rules: no dense feature is in the way, so detection range stays 15″ and the unit is visible. Engine: the dense area boundary triggers Gone to Ground, detection drops to 12″, and the unit cannot be seen.
- **Fix sketch:** When dense_only is set, count only Solid pieces (piece_class 'feature' or legacy pieces, the 13.11 branch) and ignore area boundaries.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/TerrainManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** TerrainManager._obscured_by_dense_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1244-1256) calls _line_blocked_11e(..., dense_only=true) (:1300-1314). In that mode, the 13.10 branch (:1306-1309) blocks a line when it crosses any piece_class "area" boundary of category "dense" that neither model is within, although the comment at :1287-1290 says boundaries are not physical structures. Every shipped 11e layout contains such pieces. As a result, a hidden model that is only partly obscured by a different dense area (some sight lines clip the area's open ground, none cross a dense feature) gets Gone to Ground's -3" against that observer. Through hidden_gate_allows (:1127-1135), which the RulesEngine and ShootingController targeting paths call, it becomes untargetable at 12-15" edge-to-edge. Under 13.11.01 it has not gone to ground and stays visible out to 15". If every line crosses the area, 13.10 already makes it not visible and Gone to Ground is moot. Fix: with dense_only, count only the 13.11 feature branch (piece_class "feature" or legacy pieces) and skip "area" boundaries.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. It holds on the current HEAD (b48b429). The auditor's line numbers come from an older base: the same code now sits about 123 lines lower.

Rule text: commentary 13.11.01 (errata_faq:539) says a model has gone to ground only while it is "not fully visible to the attacking model due to one or more intervening dense terrain features". It opens with "Hidden models that are concealed by dense terrain features". The same terrain chapter keeps features and areas apart. 13.08 cover says "intervening terrain features and/or one or more intervening obscuring terrain areas" (core_rules:2285-2287). 13.02 gives a category to each terrain feature, not to areas. Gone to Ground names only dense features, so a sight line that is blocked only because it crosses an obscuring terrain area does not qualify.

Engine:
- `_obscured_by_dense_11e` (TerrainManager.gd:1244-1256) calls `_line_blocked_11e(..., dense_only=true)` (:1300-1314) for each of the 9 sight points.
- In that call the 13.10 branch (:1306-1309, `if pclass != "feature"`) returns blocked for any piece_class "area" piece of category "dense" whose group neither model is within.
- The comment at :1287-1290 says area boundaries "are not physical structures, so they never Solid-block". Only the Solid branch (:1311-1313) represents the actual dense features.

This is not a corner case. Every shipped layout has these pieces: 544 dense "area" pieces in total. For example, area-large-1 in take_and_hold_mirror_1 is a 7"x11.5" rectangle whose only dense features are two 0.5"-thick L-shaped walls at opposite corners, so lines can cross its open corners without touching a wall.

The result feeds the live targeting gate. `hidden_gate_allows` (:1127-1135) runs this check for observers between 12" and 15" away, and is called from RulesEngine (:4044, 6747, 6886, 7556, 12158) and ShootingController (:2430). `detection_range_inches_for` (:1147-1151) uses it too.

Effect: if a hidden model is partly cut off by a different dense area's open ground (some lines clip the area, none hit a dense wall), the engine gives it -3" against that observer. At 12-15" it becomes untargetable, although by the rules it has not gone to ground and stays visible out to 15".

Nothing already handles this:
- RULINGS.md and docs/ record no ruling for 13.11.01 beyond the Shadow Masters note (RULINGS:356).
- The project's own docs describe Gone to Ground as "behind dense walls" (AI_TUNING.md:160) and "behind an intervening dense/Solid feature" (11th_edition_doc_change_audit.md:268).
- The tests only use legacy pieces with no piece_class (test_iss052_hidden_11e, iss052b scenario) or a dense feature wall (test_ai_fix_move_cover). None checks a bare dense area boundary.
- The Gone to Ground code dates from before the area/feature split, so the `dense_only` filter looks like a carry-over.

The claim is accurate and not overstated. The corrected text below only updates the line numbers and states the precondition: the dense area must block some sight lines but not all, because if every line crosses it, 13.10 already makes the model not visible.

## EA-68 · 17.03 Errata ([BLAST] vs engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE) — AI atomic SHOOT can fire [BLAST] at an M/V engaged only with the shooter

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 17.03 as amended: in your Shooting phase, enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE units that are engaged can be selected as targets of ranged attacks '(excluding attacks made with [BLAST] weapons)'.
- **Engine:** The player path enforces this (ShootingType.gd:47-51, CloseQuartersShooting target_allowed). The AI's atomic SHOOT goes through ShootingPhase._validate_shoot to RulesEngine.validate_shoot (ShootingPhase.gd:1137-1145), and _process_shoot never calls target_allowed. It relies on validate_blast_targeting, which skips the shooter's own engagement (RulesEngine.gd:11308-11309); the AI pre-filter uses the same check (AIDecisionMaker.gd:208-218).
- **Scenario:** An AI Leman Russ engaged with an enemy Dreadnought fires its battle cannon ([BLAST]) at the Dreadnought. 11e does not allow [BLAST] against the engaged vehicle.
- **Fix sketch:** At edition 11, run the selected ShootingType.target_allowed for each assignment in validate_shoot / _process_shoot, or stop skipping the shooter in validate_blast_targeting.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/phases/ShootingPhase.gd`, `40k/scripts/AIDecisionMaker.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** An atomic SHOOT can fire a [BLAST] weapon at a unit that is engaged only with the shooting MONSTER/VEHICLE. The atomic path is used by the AI and by the human 'Shoot All Remaining' button (ShootingController._build_auto_shoot_plan). The target can be an engaged enemy M/V (17.03 as amended) or, through the same gap, a non-M/V unit (10.06). RulesEngine.validate_shoot never runs ShootingType.target_allowed (only ASSIGN_TARGET and CONFIRM_TARGETS do, at ShootingPhase.gd:844/921). Its [BLAST] check, validate_blast_targeting, skips the shooter (RulesEngine.gd:11461-11462). _process_shoot adds no gate. The AI sanitizer (_ai_blast_target_allowed) uses the same actor-skipping check, and the auto-shoot plan has no [BLAST] filter at all. Step-by-step player targeting is correct. Severity low.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute it. This is a static trace only (no game run, as instructed).

RULE: The errata (errata_faq_commentary.md:606-608) adds '(excluding attacks made with [BLAST] weapons)' to 17.03. 04.02 needs targets to be 'Unengaged' unless otherwise stated. 10.06 says an M/V model's [BLAST] attack 'still cannot target a unit your unit is engaged with'. The launch FAQ (core_rules.txt ~4101-4110) answers 'No' in both directions. So a Leman Russ engaged with a Dreadnought may not fire its battle cannon at it.

PLAYER STEP PATH IS CORRECT: ASSIGN_TARGET (ShootingPhase.gd:833-846) and CONFIRM_TARGETS (:913-924) run the selected ShootingType's target_allowed. The base version (ShootingType.gd:45-53) refuses [BLAST] at an engaged M/V. CloseQuartersShooting.gd:43-61 refuses [BLAST] at any unit the M/V shooter is engaged with. target_allowed is called only at ShootingPhase.gd:844 and :921. test_iss048 tests these objects directly, not through SHOOT.

ATOMIC SHOOT HAS NO SUCH GATE:
- BasePhase.execute_action only calls validate_action. For SHOOT that is _validate_shoot (ShootingPhase.gd:1139-1146), which checks units_that_shot and then RulesEngine.validate_shoot.
- validate_shoot (RulesEngine.gd:6099-6311) has no shooting-type gate. Its friendly-engagement check covers only non-M/V targets and skips the actor (_is_target_in_friendly_engagement, :11399-11423).
- Its [BLAST] check is validate_blast_targeting (:11428-11471), which loops over friendly units with 'continue # Skip self' (:11461-11462).
- _process_shoot (:2933-3039) and _process_select_shooter set active_shooting_type but never check assignments. _resolve_ai_shoot then calls resolve_shoot_until_wounds (RulesEngine.gd:894-967), which has no targeting gate either.

THE AI REACHES THE CASE:
- An engaged M/V keeps all its ranged weapons (AIDecisionMaker.gd ~16926-16943).
- The fallback planner limits an engaged unit's targets to enemies within 1", i.e. the unit it is engaged with (:16964-16980).
- _sanitize_shoot_assignments takes eligibility from get_eligible_targets, where an engaged M/V's non-pistol weapons may target any visible enemy and nothing checks [BLAST] (RulesEngine.gd:7340-7353). It then calls _ai_blast_target_allowed (:214-218), which uses the same validate_blast_targeting that skips the actor.
- The LAST-1 test (test_ork_codex_shooting_fixes.gd:1343+) only covers the case where another friendly unit is engaged.
- The data supports the scenario: the Leman Russ battle cannon has keyword 'blast' (40k/data/40kdc/weapons.json).

NO RULING ACCEPTS THIS: No RULINGS.md or docs decision covers it. The delta audit marks 10.06 '[BLAST] never vs engaged' as done ('live target/weapon gates'), but those gates are only the ASSIGN/CONFIRM ones. main has the same actor-skip.

The claim is accurate but slightly UNDERSTATED:
- The human 'Shoot All Remaining' button (ShootingController._build_auto_shoot_plan, :4572-4638) also builds an atomic SHOOT from get_eligible_targets with no [BLAST] filter. An engaged M/V passes _can_unit_shoot.
- The same hole lets an engaged M/V fire [BLAST] at a non-M/V unit that only it is engaged with (10.06).
- Line refs have drifted: RulesEngine.gd 11308-11309 is now 11461-11462, and ShootingPhase.gd 1137-1145 is now 1139-1146.

## EA-69 · 18.02.01 Commentary (all embark conditions) — Embark range ignores the attached CHARACTER's models and is not re-validated server-side

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** A unit is able to embark only if it meets every 18.02 condition, including 'Each model in that unit is within 3" of that TRANSPORT.'
- **Engine:** The 3" check runs only when the embark prompt is offered (MovementPhase.gd:8349), and only over the bodyguard's own models (is_unit_within_embark_range, TransportManager.gd:784-794). _validate_embark_unit (MovementPhase.gd:9120-9151) has no range check, yet _process_embark_unit boards the whole attached group (9166-9197).
- **Scenario:** Every trooper in a squad ends within 3" of its Rhino while the attached Lieutenant stands 4.5" away. The engine embarks the whole attached unit; 18.02 forbids it. An EMBARK_UNIT action arriving over the network or bridge is not range-checked at all.
- **Fix sketch:** Check every model of TransportManager.get_attached_unit_group(unit_id) in is_unit_within_embark_range, and call that check from _validate_embark_unit.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/MovementPhase.gd`, `40k/autoloads/TransportManager.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, but the claim's line numbers are stale and its player_visible flag is wrong. The only 18.02 3" gate is MovementPhase._check_embark_opportunity (now line 8381). It calls TransportManager.is_unit_within_embark_range(unit, transport) (TransportManager.gd:784-794), which measures only the bodyguard's models. Neither _validate_embark_unit (9152-9183) nor TransportManager.can_embark checks distance at all. _process_embark_unit (9185-9244, loop at 9202) embarks every component of get_attached_unit_group. Result 1 (player-visible): a human is offered the Embark prompt, and the whole attached unit boards, while the leader is more than 3" from the TRANSPORT. That position is legal under 03.03 coherency, but 18.02 combined with 19.01 and the 19.04 FAQ forbids the embark. Result 2: any EMBARK_UNIT that reaches validate_action from a network client or the MCP bridge embarks a unit that has moved, at any distance. The AI never sends EMBARK_UNIT in the Movement phase, so it is unaffected. The project's own ruling (RULINGS.md:455, SMAbilitiesC._wholly_within) already reads 'every model within 3"' as every model of the attached unit, so the core path is inconsistent with it. Fix: add a group-level range check over get_attached_unit_group (the existing helper takes a unit dictionary, not an id) and call it from both the prompt gate and _validate_embark_unit.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Rule: 18.02 requires 'Each model in that unit is within 3" of that TRANSPORT'. The 18.02.01 commentary requires every 18.02 condition. 19.01 makes an attached unit 'a single unit for all rules purposes'. The 19.04 FAQ says rules about 'models in this/that unit' apply to every model in an attached unit, so the leader's models count. 03.03 coherency only needs 2" to one model and 9" to all, so a leader 4.5" out while every trooper is within 3" is a legal position. Engine, current HEAD: MovementPhase._check_embark_opportunity (8381) is the only 3" gate. It calls TransportManager.is_unit_within_embark_range(unit, transport) (784-794), which loops over unit.models only, and line 3828 always passes the bodyguard id (line 621 bars an attached character from moving alone). _validate_embark_unit (9152-9183) and TransportManager.can_embark (107-170) check owner, moved, disembarked, capacity, keywords and set-up-this-turn but no distance. BasePhase.execute_action adds no other check. _process_embark_unit (9185-9244, group loop at 9202) embarks every component of get_attached_unit_group. No other EMBARK_UNIT dispatcher exists (the AI never embarks in the Movement phase), and no RULINGS entry carves this out. The project's own reading runs the other way: RULINGS.md:455 and SMAbilitiesC._wholly_within (1517) treat 'every model within 3"' as every model of the attached unit. Inaccuracies: the line numbers match the tree before commit 9de9e77 and are about 32 lines stale; player_visible should be true for the prompt half, because a human is shown the Embark dialog and the out-of-range leader boards.

## EA-70 · 19.03 FAQ (keywords of destroyed models) — A destroyed attached CHARACTER still lends its keywords to the unit

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** no · **audit status:** wrong · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'These statements are only true so long as the models possessing those keywords remain part of the unit; destroyed models do not contribute their keywords to their prior unit.'
- **Engine:** attached_unit_keywords unions every listed attached character's keywords with no alive check (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:158-176). get_critical_wound_threshold uses that union for [ANTI-X] and [ANTI-non-X] (RulesEngine.gd:11006-11028). The codex group keyword helpers do the same (CustodesCommon.gd:255-260, SWDetachments11e.gd:106-110). Keywords are unit-level only, since the data has no per-model keywords.
- **Scenario:** A Librarian (PSYKER) leading Intercessors is killed. Later the Intercessors are shot with an [ANTI-PSYKER 4+] weapon and the engine still treats wound rolls of 4+ as critical; in 11e the unit is no longer PSYKER, so only 6s crit. [ANTI-CHARACTER] behaves the same after the leader dies.
- **Fix sketch:** Skip components with no alive models in attached_unit_keywords and in the codex group_has_keyword / group_kw helpers.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/CharacterAttachmentManager.gd`, `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/custodes/CustodesCommon.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/space_wolves/SWDetachments11e.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** A destroyed attached CHARACTER keeps lending its keywords to its former Attached unit. When a leader dies while its bodyguard survives, nothing detaches it or removes it from the bodyguard's attachment_data.attached_characters. check_bodyguard_destroyed, the only production caller of detach_character, runs only when the whole bodyguard is wiped out. Destroyed units also stay in GameState with alive=false models. As a result, CharacterAttachmentManager.attached_unit_keywords (lines 158-176), which has no alive check, still includes the dead leader's keywords. RulesEngine.get_critical_wound_threshold (lines 11154-11193) matches [ANTI-X] and [ANTI-non-X] against that union. Example: Intercessors whose PSYKER Librarian has died still take crits on 4+ from [ANTI-PSYKER 4+], and [ANTI-CHARACTER] behaves the same. The codex Attached-unit keyword helpers have the same problem because they loop over attached_group_ids without an alive check: CustodesCommon.group_has_keyword (255), SWCommon.group_has_keyword (259), SWDetachments11e.group_kw (106), CustodesDetachments.group_kw (227) and OrkDetachments11e.group_kw (222). The ISS-059 note (ISSUES.md:735) says this already works "via the existing detach-on-death flow", but no such flow exists. Its test (test_iss059_attached_units_11e.gd:93-94) calls detach_character by hand. Keywords exist only at unit level, so the fix is to skip components with no alive models. A PSYKER model inside a living bodyguard unit, as in the FAQ's Aspiring Sorcerer example, cannot be handled per model.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I tried to refute the claim and could not. The rule is clear in both texts. The launch text's pg-67 example says "While that model is part of that unit, that unit has the PSYKER keyword". The 19.03 FAQ (errata md line 649) adds that destroyed models do not contribute their keywords. No RULINGS.md entry or delta-audit note keeps a dead leader's keywords. RULINGS:65 and :119 only say that any component's keyword counts.

Engine findings:
1. CharacterAttachmentManager.attached_unit_keywords (lines 158-176) unions the keywords of every id in attachment_data.attached_characters. It never checks whether that id has alive models.
2. A leader that dies is never removed from that list. The only production caller of detach_character is check_bodyguard_destroyed (CAM:275-299), which runs only when every bodyguard model is dead. I grepped every detach_character( caller: the only other one is a test. I also checked every write that shrinks attached_characters: StateSerializer repairs ids that no longer exist, DeploymentPhase has a REPAIR_FORMATION_ATTACHMENT action, BasePhase sets up a new reserve copy, and a Custodes split rule clears the list. None of these removes a dead leader. PhaseManager has no pruning hook.
3. Destroyed units stay in GameState.state.units with their models set alive=false (GameState.is_unit_destroyed). So get_unit(dead_char_id) still returns the full keyword list.
4. RulesEngine.get_critical_wound_threshold (now lines 11154-11193; the claim cited 11006-11028) gets target_unit from units.get(target_unit_id). That is the real bodyguard dict, not a list of alive models only. It computes the union whenever attached_characters.size() > 0, which stays true after the leader dies. It then matches ANTI-X against the union, and for ANTI-non-X a dead leader's keyword can also wrongly block the crit. So Intercessors whose Librarian died still take crits on 4+ from [ANTI-PSYKER 4+], and the same happens with [ANTI-CHARACTER].
5. The rest of the engine expects dead components to stay listed and filters them out where they are read. UnitAbilityManager.gd:3514 and :3872 skip a character with `not _has_alive_models(char_unit)`. RulesEngine.attached_unit_target_id and attached_unit_models use _unit_has_alive_model. attached_unit_keywords is the one reader that does not filter.
6. ISSUES.md:735 (ISS-059) says the ANTI-PSYKER example "revert[s] to 6 once the leader dies and detaches ... through the existing detach-on-death flow". That flow does not exist for a leader who dies while the bodyguard is still alive. test_iss059_attached_units_11e.gd:93-94 sets alive=false and then calls cam.detach_character() by hand, so the test passes without ever running the real death path.
7. The codex helpers do the same. CustodesCommon.group_has_keyword (255-260) and SWDetachments11e.group_kw (106-110) both loop over attached_group_ids, which lists every component that exists, dead or alive. The claim also missed three more helpers: SWCommon.group_has_keyword (259-264), CustodesDetachments.group_kw (227-231) and OrkDetachments11e.group_kw (222-226). Their callers include the CustodesAbilities.gd:1262 PSYKER modifier, the CustodesEnhancements.gd:452 PSYKER target check, SW/Custodes stratagem CHARACTER and INFANTRY gating, and Ork detachment keyword gating.
8. The army JSONs contain no per-model keywords (0 entries across all 17 files), so the claim's "unit-level only" remark is correct. A fix can only drop a component once all of its models are dead. The FAQ's own Aspiring Sorcerer case, where the PSYKER model is inside the bodyguard unit, cannot be fixed at per-model level.

Assessment: the claim is accurate and not overstated. The RulesEngine line numbers are stale, and the list of affected codex helpers is incomplete.

## EA-71 · 23.04 FAQ (Scouts alternate) — Scout moves are resolved player by player instead of alternating

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'Yes, players alternate resolving any pre-battle rules units from their army may have, starting with the player who will take the first turn.'
- **Engine:** ScoutPhase starts with the first-turn player (FirstTurnRollOffPhase.gd:150-151, ScoutPhase.gd:38). _check_scout_progression only switches player once the current player has no pending Scout units left (ScoutPhase.gd:627-647).
- **Scenario:** Both players have two Scout units. The engine resolves P1 A, P1 B, P2 A, P2 B; the FAQ requires P1 A, P2 A, P1 B, P2 B, so each player can respond to the other's scout moves.
- **Fix sketch:** After each completed scout move or reserve set-up, pass the step to the other player if they still have pending Scout units. Stay with the same player when the other has none.
- **Files:** `40k/phases/ScoutPhase.gd`
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the claim. It holds up under static reading.

Rule: The 11e launch text for 24.31/24.32 (core_rules.txt:3859-3901) does not say what order players resolve Scouts in. The official FAQ (errata_faq_commentary.md:696-697) settles it: "players alternate resolving any pre-battle rules units from their army may have, starting with the player who will take the first turn." This FAQ overrides the 10e wording the engine was built from.

Engine:
- Who starts is correct. FirstTurnRollOffPhase.gd:150-151 sets meta.active_player to the first-turn player, and ScoutPhase.gd:38 reads it. If that player has no scouts, ScoutPhase.gd:77-81 switches to the other player.
- After each CONFIRM_SCOUT_MOVE (ScoutPhase.gd:557), SKIP_SCOUT_MOVE (:576) or SCOUT_RESERVES_DEPLOY (:961), _check_scout_progression (ScoutPhase.gd:628-650) runs. It hands over to the other player only when the current player's on-table and reserve pending lists are both empty (`if current_pending == 0`, :636). So the first-turn player resolves every Scout unit they have before the opponent resolves any.

Nothing elsewhere adds alternation:
- BasePhase.execute_action does no per-action hand-off.
- GameManager and TurnManager only re-seat the first-turn player after the phase ends (TurnManager.gd:35).
- NetworkManager only lists scout action names.
- The Main.gd unit list shows only the active player's pending scouts (:8685-8735).
- AIDecisionMaker._decide_scout resolves one unit per action and follows whatever the phase does.
- ScoutPhase is the only scout phase registered (PhaseManager.gd:89). The old ScoutMovesPhase is gone and SCOUT_MOVES is remapped (:100-105).
- Scouts are the only rules resolved in this pre-battle step (no other pre-battle hooks in engine or codex code), so the claim targets the right place.

No RULINGS.md entry or other doc records a decision to deviate. The surrounding comments and docs all repeat the 10e rule ("player going first moves their Scout units first"): ScoutPhase.gd:10, the test_scout_moves.gd header, MOVEMENT_PHASE_AUDIT.md:171 and DEPLOYMENT_AUDIT.md:69. No test or scenario checks the alternation the FAQ requires. The scenario iss067_scout_reserve_skippable_handoff only covers the case where the first player has nothing left.

The scenario, severity (low) and player-visibility in the claim are accurate. The only nit is the line range: the function is at 628-650, not 627-647.

Related issue, separate from this claim but relevant to any fix: the "End Scout Moves" button is always enabled (Main.gd:13275) and dispatches END_SCOUT_PHASE for the active player (Main.gd:12959). _validate_end_scout_phase (ScoutPhase.gd:442) only checks staged moves. _process_end_scout_phase (:580) marks every pending unit of BOTH players complete and emits phase_completed. So if the first-turn player presses it, the opponent's Scout moves are skipped entirely. An alternation fix should make that button pass or skip only the current player's remaining units.

## EA-72 · 24.24.01 (cross-check requested under 02.02.01) — Lone Operative reactivates per character after the bodyguard dies, even beside a non-LO leader or support

- **Severity:** low · **player-visible:** yes · **audit status:** partial · **verifier confidence:** high
- **Rule:** 'If all models in an attached unit have Lone Operative, that unit will benefit ... the highest value amongst that unit's models ... is applied to the whole unit. If an attached unit below starting strength only contains models with Lone Operative, Lone Operative becomes active again.'
- **Engine:** LO is never applied while attachment_data.attached_characters is non-empty (RulesEngine.gd:7203-7213, 1636-1638, 6188-6195, 7358-7367), so the all-LO / highest-value case is missing. When the bodyguard is wiped, every character is detached into its own unit (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299). Dead characters are never pruned from attached_characters.
- **Scenario:** Ghazghkull (Da Grand Warlord's Ladz: LO while within 3in of another friendly ORKS INFANTRY unit) and a Bannernob (Support) lead Boyz (armies/orks.json). The Boyz are wiped. The engine splits them, the Bannernob now counts as 'another' Orks Infantry unit within 3in (OrkAbilities11e.gd:993-1003), and Ghazghkull can only be shot from within 12in. Under the rules the two remain one unit containing a non-LO model, so there is no LO.
- **Fix sketch:** Evaluate LO on the attached group: active only if every alive component has LO, with the range taken as the max. Keep characters left after a bodyguard wipe as one group (19.01.01), or at least evaluate LO over the former group. Prune dead components before the check.
- **Files:** `40k/autoloads/RulesEngine.gd`, `40k/autoloads/CharacterAttachmentManager.gd`, `40k/scripts/codex/orks/OrkAbilities11e.gd`
- **Verifier correction:** Real, but the root cause is broader than Lone Operative and two of the sub-points have no effect yet. When a bodyguard is wiped, the engine breaks the attached unit apart (CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed :275-299, not edition-gated; RulesEngine.attached_unit_target_id :4285, which cites a '19.05' that does not exist). This contradicts 19.01, 19.02 and 19.01.01, so Lone Operative is then judged separately for each surviving character. A leader with conditional LO left beside a non-LO leader or support therefore gets LO, and its own former teammate counts as the 'friendly Orks Infantry unit within 3"' that triggers it. Under the rules the survivors are one unit containing a non-LO model, so there is no LO. Codex-legal reproduction: Big Mek with Shokk Attack Gun (Leader, Support Shokka) + Bannernob or Painboy (Support) leading Boyz. After the Boyz die, the engine lets the Big Mek be shot only from within 12". The Ghazghkull example reproduces only because orks.json and the 40kdc launch data give him can_lead; his 11e Codex: Orks datasheet has no Leader ability. The missing all-LO / highest-X evaluation is accurate: the gates at RulesEngine.gd:1636, 6241, 7296, 7453 and AIDecisionMaker.gd:30193 all require attached_characters to be empty, and get_lone_operative_range reads only the head unit. The unpruned dead characters are also accurate. Both have no effect yet, because no bodyguard-eligible 11e datasheet in the data has Lone Operative. Severity low; player-visible in the target list.
- **Verifier reasoning:** I could not refute the core of the claim. Static reading only.

Rules: Commentary 24.24.01 is as quoted. The 11e launch text says an attached unit is "a single unit for all rules purposes" (19.01). 19.02 explicitly covers "If that unit only contains leader/support models", and the 19.04 footnote has leaders keep their abilities after the bodyguard dies. Commentary 19.01.01 adds that even under rules that split units, the leader and support units "remain a single unit for all rules purposes". So a leader + support left after a bodyguard wipe is still one unit. Under 24.24.01 it has Lone Operative only if every model in it has LO.

Engine:
(1) There are five LO gates: RulesEngine.gd:1636-1638 (snap/overwatch), 6241-6248 (validate), 7296-7304 (get_eligible_targets), 7453-7463 (ineligibility reason) and AIDecisionMaker.gd:30193. Every one requires attached_to==null AND attached_characters empty. has_lone_operative (:9987) and get_lone_operative_range (:10111) read only the one unit dict and never take a max across components. The claim's line numbers have drifted, but the content matches.
(2) CharacterAttachmentManager.check_bodyguard_destroyed (:275-299) is not edition-gated and is called from the Shooting, Fight and Movement (snap) phases and both wound-allocation overlays. It detaches every character into its own unit. attached_unit_target_id (:4285) does the same structurally and cites a '19.05' that does not exist in the 11e text or the errata doc. ISS-059 recorded this as a design choice ("an all-characters remnant detaches to its own profile"). Neither RULINGS.md nor the delta audit covers 24.24.01 or 19.01.01.
(3) OrkAbilities11e.conditional_lone_operative (:1002-1031) only skips units with the same head_id (:1012). Once the units split, the ex-teammate is a separate friendly ORKS INFANTRY unit, so it satisfies the 3" condition and the character gets 12" LO. Under the rules, no LO applies.
(4) Nothing prunes dead characters from attached_characters. The only callers of detach_character are the bodyguard wipe and save repair.

Overstatements:
(a) The Ghazghkull example works in-engine, because orks.json and the 40kdc 11th-launch leaderAttachments give him can_lead BOYZ. But in the 11e Codex: Orks data (.llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/orks) he has no Leader ability: no core abilities and no Datasheets_leader rows. A codex-legal reproduction is Big Mek with Shokk Attack Gun (Leader; Support Shokka) + Bannernob or Painboy (Support) leading Boyz or Nobz.
(b) The all-LO / highest-X branch and the dead-character pruning point are true but have no effect today. No bodyguard-eligible 11e datasheet in the shipped data has Lone Operative: the only non-CHARACTER LO units are Ghostkeel, Lictor, Neurolictor and Ravenwing Darkshroud, and none of them can be led.

## Appendix — every item checked

| bucket | ref | kind | status | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01a | 01.01.01 You | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Wording only ('you' = the controlling player). The engine already keys ownership and limits to the owning player, e.g. stratagem use history per player (StratagemManager.gd:1250). |
| 01a | 01.02 Units and Models | FAQ | implemented | End-of-phase objective control runs on PhaseManager.phase_completed (MissionManager.gd:128-134, emitted at PhaseManager.gd:452). That is before transition_to_phase calls exit_phase (PhaseManager.gd:127), which is where end_of_phase effects are cleared (StratagemManager.gd:1778-1780, e.g. MovementPhase.gd:369). end_of_turn effects outlast end-of-turn scoring and clear at the next Command phase (StratagemManager.gd:1782-1788 via CommandPhase.gd:207-210). |
| 01a | 01.02.01 Starting Strength and Half-Strength | Commentary | partial | The table and the odd-number 'cannot be at half' rule are right, including attached characters (GameState.gd:1308-1430). But once the bodyguard dies, CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299 detaches the leader, and from then on it is judged as a 1-model unit on its own wounds (GameState.gd:1323-1331). |
| 01a | 01.02.02 Persisting Rules Effects | Commentary | partial | Effects survive embarking and reserves: flags are kept (TransportManager.gd:203-235, OrkAbilities11e.gd:1260-1272) and expiry loops cover all units. The default for an effect with no duration is end of phase (RULINGS.md:390/417/492). But MORTIS SNARES (SMCodexACommand.gd:217-220), Da Jump's Deep Strike (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1335) and the Damocles-class Uplink doctrine (SMDetachmentsA.gd:966-967) last past their trigger period. Murderous Hurricane and Auspex Triangulation Shrines also run to end of turn, but that changes no outcome in the engine. |
| 01a | 01.02.03 Revived and Adding Models to a Unit | Commentary | partial | DELICIOUS EATING SQUIGS follows the set-up rules (OrkCodexMovement.gd:953-1131). The generic heal revives only flip alive=true and leave the model where it died, with no coherency, overlap or engagement check (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1226, SWAbilities11e.gd:1729, SMAbilitiesA.gd:2082, SMAbilitiesC.gd:1670, SMDetachmentsB.gd:712). |
| 01a | 01.02.04 Not On The Battlefield | Commentary | implemented | Embarked and reserve units have null positions and are excluded by is_on_battlefield (SWCommon.gd:144-155, TransportManager.gd:220-227). A unit's own aura still applies to it (SMDetachmentsB.gd:480). The battle-shock step skips only UNDEPLOYED units, so embarked (DEPLOYED) and IN_RESERVES units are tested (CommandPhase.gd:393-397). |
| 01a | 01.02.05 Other Model / Unit | Commentary | implemented | 'Other' exclusions compare unit ids and attached groups, not datasheets. Example: Hop Splat's 'one other enemy unit' skips only the target's own group (OrkDetachments11e.gd:821-834). |
| 01a | 01.02.06 Splitting Units | Errata | partial | Old Greymanes splits as evenly as possible (SWAbilities11e.gd:1081) and the SW splits are refused while a leader is attached (SWAbilities11e.gd:1057). Taktikal Know-wotz needs exactly 10 alive bodyguard models (GameState.gd:1154) and deep-copies attachment_data into the second half (GameState.gd:1164), so an attached Boss Snikrot ends up in both halves. |
| 01a | 01.02.06 Splitting Units | Commentary | implemented | A unit can split only once (split_from_combat_squads / F_SPLIT gates, DeploymentController.gd:518, SWAbilities11e.gd:1016-1019). Each half of a pre-battle split gets its own model array, so its starting strength is its own model count (GameState.gd:1164-1178, SWAbilities11e.gd:1076-1108). The attached-leader case is covered under the errata item. |
| 01a | 01.02.07 Describing Units | Commentary | implemented | Keyword and battle-shocked descriptors count if any component qualifies (SWCommon.gd:259-264, AttackSequence.gd:302-312). The codex hidden-unit helpers are excluded here because another branch is already fixing them. |
| 01a | 01.02.08 A (Unit/Model/Object) | Commentary | implemented | 'Within range of an objective' helpers return true for any objective, not exactly one (CustodesDetachments.gd:295-314, OrkAbilities11e.gd:554-565). |
| 01a | 01.02.09 Tokens | Commentary | implemented | Tokens are stored state, not models (Teleport Homer token = flag sm11e_aba_homer {x,y}, SMAbilitiesA.gd:186; 'not drawn on the board', RULINGS.md:422). They never block a move or count as a model. |
| 01a | 01.02.10 Adding a new unit to your army | Commentary | implemented | New units get their own model array when they are added (INFESTED WAR ZONE, BasePhase.gd:1007-1043; UNLEASH THE LIONS one-model units, CustodesCodexOther.gd:516-534), so starting strength equals the models at that moment. |
| 01a | 01.02.11 All Types of Model | Commentary | implemented | 'Move through all types of model' grants (Calistus, Shokk-boosta, Grapnel Launchers) skip both the enemy-model check and the friendly/enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE crossing check (MovementPhase.gd:759-767, 7431-7433, 7482-7484). |
| 01a | 01.02.12 Replacing One Model with Another | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No implemented 11e rule replaces one model with another (searched the codex texts and the engine). The 'set back up as close as possible to where it was destroyed' enhancements use their own placement (CustodesEnhancements.gd:720-723). |
| 01a | 01.03.01 Player's Rules | Commentary | implemented | Once-per-battle/turn/phase limits are tracked per player (_usage_history[player], StratagemManager.gd:1250-1281). The engine has no always-on mission-rule hooks, and end-of-turn mission rules run after other rules (PhaseManager.gd:48-57, per 07.03). |
| 01a | 01.03.02 Rules Sequencing | Commentary | implemented | After a unit shoots, the active player's unit_has_shot window opens before the opponent's enemy_has_shot window (ShootingPhase.gd:5284-5293, 5452-5458). Movement-phase windows follow the same order (MovementPhase.gd:3839-3850). |
| 01a | 01.04.01 Within / Wholly Within | Commentary | implemented | 'Within' measures to the closest base edges with inclusive <= checks (Measurement.gd:185-192, 362-370; e.g. SMDetachmentsA.gd:940). 'Wholly within' uses the far edge or the full base shape (TransportManager.gd:796-816, MovementPhase.gd:4442-4461). There are no FRAME units in the data. The table-quarter test is the exception (see 01.04.05). |
| 01a | 01.04.02 Closest Or Nearest Model/Unit | Commentary | partial | Closest is measured from the model or unit using the rule (PileInMove.gd:152-162, SurgeMove.gd:39-73). But pile-in and consolidation compare the minimum distance to any target before and after the move (PileInMove.gd:69-78, ConsolidationMove.gd:131-142), and surge ties are settled by iteration order instead of by the player (SurgeMove.gd:64-67). |
| 01a | 01.04.03 As Close As Possible | Commentary | implemented | Surge 'as close as possible' is checked against the solver's reachable positions with a 0.5" tolerance (ReactiveMove11e.gd:266-286). Emergency disembark sets up nearest-first (RulesEngine.gd:17233-17238). No implemented rule moves 'as close as possible to an objective' (Purge and Sanctify and Enraged Rush are not implemented). |
| 01a | 01.04.04 Base Contact or Base to Base Contact | Commentary | implemented | Base contact = edge distance within 0.05-0.1" (PileInMove.gd:137-149, RulesEngine.gd:13268). The overhang clause has nothing to apply to: models are base footprints only. |
| 01a | 01.04.05 Table Quarters | Commentary | partial | The board is split into quarters through the centre (SecondaryMissionManager.gd:1083-1088). But _is_unit_wholly_in_rect tests only model centre points against rectangles that share the centre lines inclusively (SecondaryMissionManager.gd:2937-2949). It is used by Engage on All Fronts and Reconnaissance Sweep (MissionManager.gd:2719-2724). |
| 01a | 01.04.06 Drawing a Line | Commentary | no_engine_surface | The only line the rules have players draw is line of sight, which sets its own 1 mm width (40k_11th_edition_core_rules.txt:1025-1029, 06.01). No implemented rule tells players to draw a line. |
| 01b | 01.05.01 | Commentary | implemented | Torrent rolls no hit dice and sets critical_hits=0, so Lethal, Sustained and Precision cannot fire (RulesEngine.gd:2509-2518; the branch ignores the overwatch BS7 override). Lethal Hits auto-wounds count as regular, never critical, wounds (RulesEngine.gd:3370-3396, melee :14862-14891, staged re-roll :1188-1189). Insane Bravery and Mob Mentality roll no dice (CommandPhase.gd:1384-1431, :1433-1477; Mob Mentality also applies the 08.03 recovery). At 11e, Insane Bravery cannot target a shocked unit: StratagemManager.gd:965 resolves insane_bravery_11e before the :1073 battle-shock gate, so the no-recovery case cannot arise. |
| 01b | 01.05.02 | Commentary | partial | Done right: 2D6 rolls are re-rolled in full (charge ChargePhase.gd:1073/1143; battle-shock CommandPhase.gd:1025-1026/1272-1273); damage re-rolls re-roll the whole expression (CustodesCodex11e.gd:351, SMAbilitiesC.gd:871); the re-roll comes before modifiers (RulesEngine.gd:689-721, 769-801); a staged die that was already re-rolled is refused (RulesEngine.gd:1066-1086, :1163); charge and battle-shock ability re-rolls block Command Re-roll (ChargePhase.gd:748, CommandPhase.gd:1038). Gaps: the Advance die can be re-rolled twice, and wound 'failed' dice are picked without modifiers or Anti crits. Context only: Command Re-roll is offered on battle-shock rolls (CommandPhase.gd:1035-1086), but 15.02 |
| 01b | 01.05.03 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Pure cross-reference to 02.02.01, which should be audited there. The dice clauses it points to (modify after re-rolls, ±1 hit/wound cap) are at RulesEngine.gd:689-721 and 769-801. Lead for the 02.02.01 audit: ChargePhase.gd:847-849 adds charge-roll bonuses with no cap at 12. |
| 01b | 01.05.04 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Pure cross-reference to 02.02.02, which should be audited there. Example of compliance: Salvus drops only the negative wound modifier (RulesEngine.gd:3333-3338), the selective ignore that 02.02.02 permits. |
| 01b | 01.05.05 | Commentary | implemented | Both pre-battle roll-offs roll one D6 per player and re-roll ties (RollOffPhase.gd:143-166, FirstTurnRollOffPhase.gd:115-136). The plan-editor auto roll-off also re-rolls ties (Main.gd:11169). The engine has no other roll-offs. |
| 01b | 01.05.06 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No engine code or implemented 11e codex rule checks dice for doubles or triples. Grep of autoloads/, phases/, scripts/ and the 11e codex snapshots found none. |
| 01b | 01.05.07 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No rule picks one highest or lowest die out of several. The only lowest-to-highest ordering is the 05.04 sort of save rolls for identical attacks (Allocation.gd:180-181), where equal values are interchangeable. |
| 01b | 01.05.08 | Commentary | implemented | Every 'change the advance roll to a 6' rule (Shokk-boosta, Wind Walker, Sudden Onslaught, Veil of Time, Unstoppable Momentum) passes a rolled 6 to _resolve_advance_roll with is_roll=true. Later +N modifiers still apply and can push it past 6 (MovementPhase.gd:1341-1365, 1391-1398). No engine rule reacts to a particular advance value. The only hit/wound/save or deadly-demise 'set to unmodified 6' rules are Hero of Lion's Gate (Legends Valerian, not imported) and Vengeful Animus (Medusa's Wrath detachment, not implemented). |
| 01b | 01.06.01 | Commentary | partial | Every leadership test and battle-shock path is the same 2D6 >= Ld roll (CommandPhase.gd:984-1012, FactionAbilityManager.gd:2377-2395, Taktik :1265-1271). But for an Attached unit that roll uses the HIGHEST (worst) Ld (CommandPhase.gd:963; SMAbilitiesC.gd:1006), not 'one or more of the Ld characteristics' (01.06). The Ork and Custodes module rolls use the lowest (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1093-1099, CustodesAbilities.gd:1097-1103). .llm/codex_11e_wahapedia/RULINGS.md:191 flags this inconsistency as not fixed. |
| 01b | 01.07 | FAQ | wrong | ActionsManager.complete_actions fails every battle-shocked unit performing an action (ActionsManager.gd:272-276). There is no exception for CONSERVAI or Tactical Mainstay units, whose rules let them start an action while battle-shocked (:138-146). RULINGS.md:16 rules the opposite of this FAQ. The same FAQ is repeated under 16.01. |
| 01b | 01.07.01 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Flavour text. It adds no mechanics beyond 01.06 and 01.07. |
| 01b | 01.07.02 | Commentary | partial | Codex 'Battle-shock test' rules (Squig Flingin', Impending Krunch, Roar of Mork, Kaptaris, Huntress' Eye, Howlmaw, SMCodexBAction, Desperate Escape) go through FactionAbilityManager.force_battle_shock_test. That is the Command-phase roll: same Ld helper, Tempered in Battle re-roll, Mob Mentality. But the 'battle-shock rolls' for Rokkit Barrage, Mobbed, Sanctified Flames and Fearsome Assault use their own rollers, which skip the Tempered in Battle re-roll (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1103-1115, CustodesAbilities.gd:1211-1225, SMAbilitiesC.gd:1009-1019). |
| 02-03 | 02.01.01 | Commentary | partial | Only CharacterAttachmentManager.can_attach (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:85-93) and enhancement matching add meta.name as a keyword. The shared predicates (RulesEngine.unit_has_keyword :9822-9828, codex has_keyword/group_has_keyword) read meta.keywords only, and armies/sw11e_gw_import_2000.json has 7 units without their name keyword. |
| 02-03 | 02.02 FAQ (Damage changed to 0: after saves) | FAQ | no_engine_surface | Grepping codex and engine finds no shipped rule that sets D to 0. Allocation.apply_save_rolls would record a 0-damage failed save as 'prevented' after the save roll (Allocation.gd:249-255), which matches the FAQ. |
| 02-03 | 02.02 FAQ (charge-roll / max-distance modifiers vs charge-target selection) | FAQ | partial | Charge-roll bonuses are summed before the post-roll target filter (ChargePhase.gd:795-850, 873; ChargeMove11e.gd:106-107), which is correct. But a charge cannot take to the skies (MoveType.take_to_skies_modifier is only used in MovementPhase), so the -2in never applies, and FLY chargers get the terrain benefit for free (ChargePhase.gd:2238-2266, 2311-2319). |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (order of application) | Commentary | partial | Damage order is correct: roll D, +melta/+D, halve (round up), then -D floored at 1 (RulesEngine.gd:3714-3732); a 'set' Ld (Seeker of the Unfound) is applied before +/-Ld (CommandPhase.gd:955-972). AP improve vs generic worsen runs in opposite orders on ranged/auto paths (RulesEngine.gd:15706-15741, 14235-14259) and the human-defender melee path (15925-15955). -1D/-1AP sources take the best rather than stacking, by a recorded ruling (EffectPrimitives.gd:746-749, 796-804; RULINGS.md:171); no shipped same-attack combination was found. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (characteristic limits after all modifiers) | Commentary | partial | Present: AP never worse than 0 (worsen_ap RulesEngine.gd:10688-10691), D>=1 (:3731-3732), OC>=0 and battle-shocked OC unmodifiable (MissionManager.gd:765-769), WS improvement floored at 2+ (:14015). Missing: BS is not clamped to 2+..6+ (:2761, :2790, :4948, :4977 into AttackSequence.gd:54) and Ld is not clamped to 5+..8+ (CommandPhase.gd:946-972). M floors at 0, not 1 (MovementPhase.gd:264); no shipped case reaches it. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (dice-roll modifiers; +/-1 hit/wound cap) | Commentary | wrong | Re-rolls come before modifiers and 'unmodified' means the post-re-roll value (RulesEngine.gd:684-718; AttackSequence.gd:39-58). But the +/-1 cap is applied to PLUS_ONE/MINUS_ONE presence bits, not the cumulative total (RulesEngine.gd:676-718, 790-798, 2796-2806, 10520-10523). Save rolls are also clamped +/-1 (Allocation.gd:175, RulesEngine.gd:3673) although 11e caps only hit and wound rolls; this is latent because only one shipped save-roll modifier exists. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (charge roll cannot exceed 12) | Commentary | missing | _resolve_charge_roll adds every bonus without a 12 cap (ChargePhase.gd:846-850), and the charge-move budget uses that total (ChargePhase.gd:2235, 2272). Only target selection is capped, via min(12, roll) (ChargeMove11e.gd:106-107). |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (detection range 9in-30in) | Commentary | partial | detection_range_inches_for sums the base (15in or 'Detection Range X'), Gone to Ground -3 and codex bonuses, then applies only the 9in floor (TerrainManager.gd:1069-1088; GameConstants.gd:94-95). There is no 30in ceiling, but the highest value shipped bonuses can reach is 15+6+3+3+3 = 30in (SM Optics, Omni-scramblers, Priority Target, Auspex shrines), so nothing differs today. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (Lone Operative 9in-30in) | Commentary | partial | get_lone_operative_range returns the first number in a 'Lone Operative X' ability, or 12, with no clamp (RulesEngine.gd:9958-9973). No shipped data has an LO-range modifier or an LO X value, so the missing clamp cannot change anything yet. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.01 (stratagem CP: at most +1, never below 0) | Commentary | implemented | Every discount is floored with maxi(0, ...) (StratagemManager.gd:108-116, 1001-1027, 1355-1408). The only increase source is Lord of Deceit +1 (StratagemManager.gd:1015, 1382), so the +1 limit cannot be exceeded. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.02 | Commentary | implemented | Ignore-modifier rules drop only the harmful entries and keep bonuses: [PSYCHIC] and Walking Bastion (RulesEngine.gd:2749-2759), the Salvus/Inevitable Annihilation/Machine Spirit stack filters (:2738-2746), ignore_hit_penalties (:10529-10531; SWAbilities11e.gd:573-575), and the Vexilla, which ignores only the Ld penalty (CommandPhase.gd:970-971). |
| 02-03 | 02.02.03 | Commentary | partial | Correct: random A is rolled per model per weapon at resolution (RulesEngine.gd:2271-2273; melee :14020-14021), and random D is rolled per inflicting attack after the model is selected (:3714-3716). No unit has a random M. Random S is not supported: get_weapon_profile turns a non-integer S into 3 (RulesEngine.gd:8828-8829). |
| 02-03 | 02.02.04 | Commentary | wrong | All live heals (OrkAbilities11e.gd:1196-1240, SMAbilitiesA.gd:2052-2095, SWAbilities11e.gd:1699-1740, SMAbilitiesC.gd:1638-1680, SMDetachmentsB.gd:682-720) heal a wounded model first, but revive any destroyed model in the attached group, CHARACTER included (dead leaders stay in attached_characters; CustodesCommon.gd:163-173). They also pick the wounded model bodyguard-first, and the model-only Gift of the Iron Wolf (SWAbilities11e.gd:1799) uses the unit heal. RulesEngine.apply_heal_11e (:9907) has no callers. |
| 02-03 | 02.02.05 | Commentary | implemented | 'Full wounds remaining' is consistently current_wounds == W (Allocation.gd:197-201 wounded-first selection; heal_diffs st.cw < wounds). |
| 02-03 | 02.02.06 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Only points to 02.02.01; covered by the 02.02.01 rows. |
| 02-03 | 02.03.01 | Commentary | unclear | There is no generic mechanism: tiered codex rules are hand-coded per ability (for example ARMED TO DA TEEF's riled-up upgrade, whose first tier is unconditional), so they cannot all be checked statically here. One legacy 10e rule, SPESHUL SHELLS, knowingly drops its 'closest eligible target' condition (RulesEngine.gd:10146-10154). |
| 02-03 | 02.04 FAQ (+1 A) | FAQ | implemented | +A grants add the bonus once per bearer of each weapon (bonus x model_ids, RulesEngine.gd:2404-2408; melee :14046-14060; Ork per-model +A :2276). That is +1 to each weapon's A, not an extra die per attack. |
| 02-03 | 02.04.01 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No shipped weapon has S '-'. If one did, get_weapon_profile would default it to S3 rather than S1 (RulesEngine.gd:8828-8829). |
| 02-03 | 02.05 FAQ (multi-word keywords) | FAQ | implemented | Every keyword check is an exact, case-insensitive whole-string match (RulesEngine.gd:9822-9828; CustodesCommon.gd:104-109; OrkAbilities11e.gd:316-320). No substring matching was found. |
| 02-03 | 02.05.01 | Commentary | implemented | Attached units use keyword unions (CharacterAttachmentManager.attached_unit_keywords :158-176; [ANTI] including ANTI-non-X/Y, RulesEngine.gd:11008-11040; codex group_has_keyword over components). Per-model keywords within one datasheet are not modelled, but no shipped datasheet mixes them. Core MONSTER/VEHICLE checks read only the bodyguard dict (ModifierStack.gd:146-157); no shipped leader is a MONSTER or VEHICLE. |
| 02-03 | 02.06 FAQ (Munitorum Field Manual attach lists win) | FAQ | unclear | Leader pairings are roster leader_data.can_lead merged additively with Wahapedia Datasheets_leader.csv (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:47-72; LeaderPairingsLoader.gd). The shipped MFM PDF (v3.9) lists points only, so MFM differences cannot be checked, and a pairing the MFM removed would still be allowed. |
| 02-03 | 02.06.01 | Commentary | implemented | Bearer-only effects check the allocated model (Praesidium Shield and Refuse to Yield, RulesEngine.gd:3734-3743) or the bearer's own unit dict (enhancements sit on single-model CHARACTER units). |
| 02-03 | 02.06.02 | Commentary | implemented | get_unit_weapons collects the wargear of every model, including attached characters via composite '<char>:<model>' bearers (RulesEngine.gd:7230-7245). |
| 02-03 | 03.01.01 | Commentary | wrong | Each move gets one cap from the bodyguard's unit-level M (get_unit_movement MovementPhase.gd:203-266; move_cap_inches :1183-1214). That cap is checked against every staged model, attached characters included (:744-749, :870-879), and unstaged characters are moved rigidly by the bodyguard's delta (:9253-9330). |
| 02-03 | 03.01.02 | Commentary | partial | Codex move-through rules (Shokk-boosta, Grapnel Launchers, Calistus; MovementPhase.gd:7105-7114) still enforce the overlap, wall, board-edge and ER checks (:904-935), which is correct. But every FLY unit skips the enemy-base and MONSTER/VEHICLE-base crossing checks even when it has not taken to the skies (:759-769, :893-903, :8145-8152, :8232-8239). |
| 02-03 | 03.01.03 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Only points to 02.02.03; every shipped stats.move is an integer, so no unit has a random M. |
| 02-03 | 03.01.04 | Commentary | implemented | Stated distances are caps, not requirements: surge max distance is X (SurgeMove.gd:35-36), and reactive 'up to X' moves set move_cap_inches = X (MovementPhase.gd:6374-6381). |
| 02-03 | 03.02.01 | Commentary | implemented | Set-up moves validate before applying. A rejected PLACE_REINFORCEMENT or disembark leaves the unit in reserves or embarked, with no moved flag, so it can try again later in the phase (MovementPhase.gd:4209-4316). |
| 02-03 | 03.02.02 | Commentary | missing | There is no large-model fallback. Disembark requires the farthest base point within 3in or 6in (TransportManager.gd:806-815, used at MovementPhase.gd:8546); ingress requires bases wholly within 6in of an edge (IngressMove.gd:62-70, 113-126); deployment requires bases wholly within the DZ. |
| 02-03 | 03.02.03 | Commentary | partial | A Redeployment phase exists, but humans only get 'End Redeployment' (Main.gd:12950-12951) and the AI always skips (AIDecisionMaker.gd:5849-5870). Its validator checks only the model centre against the DZ, lets Infiltrators go anywhere, and has no coherency or stand-off check (RedeploymentPhase.gd:197-233). |
| 02-03 | 03.03 FAQ (models destroyed for coherency count for missions) | FAQ | implemented | Removal sets alive=false, i.e. destroyed (PhaseManager.gd:576-611; ScoringPhase.gd:744-760). A removal can never empty a unit or component (AttackSequence.gd:229-235, 347-349), and kill missions score only fully destroyed units (SecondaryMissionManager.gd:1983-2003), so no result differs, even though the removal paths do not call check_and_report_unit_destroyed. |
| 02-03 | 03.03 FAQ (single contiguous group) | FAQ | missing | check_unit_coherency only requires one mate within 2in and every model within 9in (AttackSequence.gd:229-262). It has no connectivity test, and every validator delegates to it. |
| 02-03 | 03.03 FAQ (move requirements cannot force incoherency) | FAQ | implemented | Requirements apply only to the models the player moves (PileInMove.gd:68-78), and every move template still requires coherency at the end (MoveType.gd:64-70), so no requirement can force an incoherent end position. |
| 02-03 | 03.03.01 | Commentary | partial | Coherency is enforced for deployment, charge, fight, disembark and scout moves. However, the normal/advance/fall-back confirm uses only the bodyguard's models and keys staged moves by bare model_id (MovementPhase.gd:1041-1072, 1061). End of Turn never removes a lone attached character (AttackSequence.gd:347-349). The 11e ingress branch returns before its coherency block (MovementPhase.gd:4269-4316 vs 4418-4435). |
| 02-03 | 03.04 FAQ (passing through engagement range) | FAQ | implemented | Engagement is judged only at end positions: normal, advance and fall-back moves check ER at the destination (MovementPhase.gd:7080-7094), and charge and pile-in check engagement at the end (ChargeMove11e.gd:114-131; PileInMove.gd:81-93). |
| 02-03 | 03.04.01 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Explanatory text only; engagement range itself is 2in horizontal / 5in vertical (GameConstants.gd:31-37). |
| 02-03 | 24.24.01 (requested cross-check: Lone Operative on attached units) | Commentary | partial | LO is skipped whenever a unit has attached characters (RulesEngine.gd:7203-7213, 1636-1638, 6188-6195, 7358-7367), so the all-LO / highest-value case is missing (no shipped combination). After a bodyguard is wiped, each character detaches as its own unit (CharacterAttachmentManager.gd:275-299), so LO reactivates per character even next to a surviving non-LO support. Dead leaders are never pruned from attached_characters. |
| 04-05 | 04.01.01 | Commentary | implemented | Ranged: get_eligible_shooter_models only counts models that carry the weapon (RulesEngine.gd:11910). Melee: get_unit_melee_weapons drops models with no melee weapon (RulesEngine.gd:15609). Only data fallback: _melee_weapon_swingers keeps every eligible model when no carrier resolves (RulesEngine.gd:13866-13879). |
| 04-05 | 04.01.02 | Commentary | wrong | The 24.07 per-model either/or is not enforced for human-declared shooting at 11e. MA-25 is gated edition<11 (ShootingPhase.gd:853). The 11e confirm only runs shooting-type gates (ShootingPhase.gd:898-922). validate_shoot's check reads only the PISTOL keyword (RulesEngine.gd:6229-6256, 8900-8909), but 11e data carries 'close quarters'. ShootingController.gd:7041-7049 says exclusivity does not apply at 11e. Only the AI applies 24.07 (AIDecisionMaker.gd:289-310). |
| 04-05 | 04.01.03 | Commentary | partial | Hunter profiles are enforced (RulesEngine.gd:7253, 6162-6166). Choosing ONE profile per weapon per model is not enforced for human shooting: every profile row is auto/quick-assigned (ShootingController.gd:1578, 1735, 7029-7100), and _process_assign_target only dedups the same weapon_id (ShootingPhase.gd:1502-1516). Only the AI, snap shots and forced-shoot builders pick one profile. |
| 04-05 | 04.01.04 | Commentary | implemented | Abilities/modifiers apply per weapon assignment and persist through that weapon's Hazardous check, run after the assignment resolves (RulesEngine.gd:857-872, incl. effect_grant_hazardous). Roll modifiers are applied per weapon in _resolve_assignment_hits. |
| 04-05 | 04.01.05 | Commentary | implemented | Definitional. 'Selected to attack' rules hook the unit_selected_to_shoot / selected-to-fight windows (e.g. ARMED TO DA TEEF, OrkCodexFight.gd:496-513; Magna Imperator, CustodesDetachments.gd:579-590). |
| 04-05 | 04.02 FAQ (visible vs in-range model) | FAQ | wrong | _check_target_visibility only accepts the target when the SAME enemy model is both within range and visible (RulesEngine.gd:6779-6790). get_eligible_targets (7277) and validate_shoot (6216) gate on it. The per-model filter get_eligible_shooter_models already decouples them (nearest model for range, any model for LoS: 11926-11985). |
| 04-05 | 04.02.01 | Commentary | implemented | Targets are chosen per weapon, with per-model split-fire (ShootingPhase.gd:1496-1548). CONFIRM only needs one assignment, so unassigned weapons simply do not attack (ShootingPhase.gd:899-900). Melee assignments carry a target per weapon/section (FightPhase.gd:1286-1291). |
| 04-05 | 04.02.02 | Commentary | implemented | 'Against' reactions open after targets are confirmed (enemy_targets_unit_shooting, ShootingPhase.gd:5049-5090; enemy_targets_unit_fight in FightPhase). 'Allocated' effects such as Praesidium Shield / Refuse to Yield -1 D run per allocated model in the damage provider (RulesEngine.gd:3734-3742). |
| 04-05 | 04.02.03 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Definition only. No implemented rule keys on 'shooting at / targeting a single target'. |
| 04-05 | 04.03 FAQ1 (+1 A) | FAQ | implemented | +A effects add the bonus per firing model per weapon (bonus x model_ids.size(), RulesEngine.gd:2403-2416, 4608-4621; melee 14046-14079), not an extra die per attack. |
| 04-05 | 04.03 FAQ2 (cover per attacking model) | FAQ | implemented | Benefit of cover is judged from each attack's own firing model and worsens that attack's BS (RulesEngine.gd:2766-2794, 4953-4981). Wound/save dice of one weapon stay in one pool; see the 04.03.01 batching gap. |
| 04-05 | 04.03 FAQ3 ([PRECISION] identical vs non-CHARACTER) | FAQ | partial | attack_identity_key always keys on [PRECISION], even against a target with no CHARACTER (AttackSequence.gd:430-451). No live path gathers attacks across weapon ids anyway (RulesEngine.gd:841-855), so [PRECISION] and non-[PRECISION] weapons are never rolled together. |
| 04-05 | 04.03.01 | Commentary | partial | Identity is defined (AttackSequence.gd:432-451), but only resolve_shoot merges, and only same-weapon groups (RulesEngine.gd:846). The live sequential/interactive path (resolve_shoot_until_wounds 927-931) and melee (13376) never gather identical attacks across weapons. The key excludes 'pistol' but not 'close_quarters'. |
| 04-05 | 04.03.02 | Commentary | partial | Splitting is by MODEL only. The dialog's Split divides model counts and needs more than one model (AttackAssignmentDialog.gd:135-139, 753). Assignments carry no per-target attack count (FightPhase.gd:1286-1291). The one-weapon rule rejects the same model+weapon on a second target (FightPhase.gd:1235-1257). |
| 04-05 | 04.03.03 | Commentary | partial | Charges re-filter targets after the roll (ChargePhase.gd:862-895). For attacks, weapons whose target is destroyed are skipped (ShootingPhase.gd:3988-4013; FightPhase.gd:1731-1741), and models that lose range/LoS are dropped at resolve time (RulesEngine.gd:4424-4436). No new target can be selected. |
| 04-05 | 04.03.04 | Commentary | implemented | The Hidden 'has shot' stamp (last_shot_idx) is written only when real ranged attacks complete. Give-up/skip paths do not stamp it (ShootingPhase.gd:3240-3251, 6079-6084). |
| 04-05 | 04.03.05 | Commentary | partial | A fight activation with no enemy in ER (zero attacks) still runs _finish_fight_activation_11e (FightPhase.gd:1205-1217 -> 2891-2979). That fires the 'has fought'/'has attacked' hooks (2901-2902, 2945-2958) plus the enemy_has_fought and Counter-Offensive windows (2958-3013). |
| 04-05 | 04.03.06 | Commentary | implemented | on_unit_has_attacked expiries run after the whole activation: shooting post-shot windows (ShootingPhase.gd:5438-5445) and fight (FightPhase.gd:2945-2949). The zero-attack edge case is covered under 04.03.05. |
| 04-05 | 05.01 FAQ1 (crit 4+ while snap shooting) | FAQ | implemented | Snap/overwatch forces BS 7 and clamps a lowered crit threshold back to 6 (RulesEngine.gd:2468-2474, 4677, 6549-6553). The legacy overwatch path counts only unmodified 6s (1754). The 'unless otherwise stated' Sentinel Protocols exception is kept (2476-2480). |
| 04-05 | 05.01 FAQ2 (crit 4+ with 5+ to hit) | FAQ | implemented | evaluate_hit_roll checks the unmodified-1 auto-fail first, then scores a hit if unmodified >= crit threshold OR modified >= BS (AttackSequence.gd:52-56). Ranged and melee share it. |
| 04-05 | 05.01.01 | Commentary | implemented | A crit sets is_hit/is_wound true alongside is_crit (AttackSequence.gd:54-56, 82-84). Lethal/Sustained/Devastating key off is_crit. |
| 04-05 | 05.02.01 | Commentary | implemented | Uses the highest T among alive bodyguard models, else among the unit's own models (RulesEngine.gd:6272-6304). Used by every wound path (1792, 3159, 5263, 14222). |
| 04-05 | 05.03 FAQ1 (order of several wounded non-CHARACTER groups) | FAQ | partial | default_order already puts wounded non-CHARACTER groups first in any order (Allocation.gd:119-129). But validate_order demands EACH wounded non-CHARACTER group be first (Allocation.gd:88-92), so with two such groups every order is rejected. |
| 04-05 | 05.03 FAQ2 (roll saves even if some are worse than 6) | FAQ | implemented | Every wounds_to_save die is always rolled (RulesEngine.gd:3711) and checked against the current group's Sv/InSv in turn (Allocation.gd:226-254). |
| 04-05 | 05.03 FAQ3 (no grouping/allocation to destroyed models) | FAQ | implemented | build_groups skips dead and 0-wound Fight-On-Death models (Allocation.gd:29-37). apply_save_rolls only targets models with remaining > 0 (Allocation.gd:206-224), as does model_can_be_allocated (RulesEngine.gd:16657-16660). |
| 04-05 | 05.03.01 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Terminology only ('saving throw' = save roll). |
| 04-05 | 05.03.02 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No implemented rule changes an attack's characteristics from its hit/wound roll value. AP/D modifiers are all applied before rolling (RulesEngine.gd:5588-5622, 14235-14259). [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] crits already resolve as a separate post-damage pool (RulesEngine.gd:3807-3861). |
| 04-05 | 05.04 FAQ1 (Fight On Death models still present) | FAQ | implemented | Kept FOD models stay alive=true with 0 wounds, so they count for measuring, visibility and engagement. They are excluded only from allocation (FightPhase.gd:3039-3054; Allocation.gd:33-37). |
| 04-05 | 05.04 FAQ2 (FNP in Resolve Damage step) | FAQ | implemented | FNP is rolled per inflicting attack inside the damage provider, after the model is selected (RulesEngine.gd:3743-3760; Allocation.gd:256-259). |
| 04-05 | 05.04.01 | Commentary | partial | apply_save_rolls advances to the next group once the current one is destroyed (Allocation.gd:188-204). But the engine-side melee path (AI defender / auto-allocate, RulesEngine.gd:15030, 15067) and ranged resolve_shoot (4412, 5687) group only the bodyguard dict, so an attached CHARACTER group never becomes current. |
| 04-05 | 05.04.02 | Commentary | implemented | The damage provider applies additions (melta, Bigger Shells, +D), then halving (ceil), then subtractions with min 1 (RulesEngine.gd:3715-3742). That matches the 02.02.01 order and the D>=1 floor. |
| 04-05 | 05.04.03 | Commentary | no_engine_surface | No implemented rule triggers on 'suffered damage'. FNP-prevented attacks are logged as 'prevented' events (Allocation.gd:260-263). |
| 04-05 | 05.04.04 | Commentary | partial | Three gaps: (1) an attached unit counts as destroyed once its bodyguard models die (ShootingPhase.gd:3991, 4812-4823, 3766; FightPhase.gd:1718-1741). (2) Destroyed-triggers resolve per action, mid-activation (ShootingPhase.gd:279-288, 349-353; FightPhase.gd:442-449). (3) Every wipe is credited to the opponent whatever the cause (ShootingPhase.gd:341-342; FightPhase.gd:433-434; ScoringPhase.gd:1012-1013). |
| 04-05 | 05.04.05 | Commentary | implemented | The FightPhase FOD intercept keeps the model, with no roll once its unit was selected to fight. It stays until its unit has fought or the phase ends, and its destroyed-triggers resolve on removal (FightPhase.gd:2895-2901, 3039-3130, 3210). |
| 04-05 | 05.04.06 | Commentary | partial | Dead models keep their stored positions (only alive=false is written, RulesEngine.gd:4081). Distance to a destroyed unit then scans every model, not the last one destroyed: MissionManager.gd:3178-3191 and Deadly Demise RulesEngine.gd:17484-17497. |
| 06-12 | 06.01 FAQ — LoS drawn from any part of the observing model to any part of the observed model | FAQ | partial | Targeting LoS samples points on both bases (EnhancedLineOfSight.gd:42-80 via RulesEngine._check_line_of_sight:6849). But TerrainManager._visibility_lines_11e (TerrainManager.gd:1193-1206) draws lines only from the observer's base centre (1194). That check decides 13.08 'not fully visible' cover (TerrainManager.gd:1421; live per attack at RulesEngine.gd:2788/4975) and the Explosives 'visible' gate (StratagemManager.gd:3257). |
| 06-12 | 06.01 FAQ — model overhanging the battlefield edge | FAQ | no_engine_surface | No base may extend past the board edge (MovementPhase._position_outside_board_bounds, MovementPhase.gd:7683; the reserves frame keeps bases on the table, StrategicReservesZoneVisual.gd:121-124). The 2D engine models nothing beyond the base, so no part of a model can overhang. |
| 06-12 | 06.01.01 Commentary — an unqualified 'visible' means visible to the unit using the rule | Commentary | implemented | Codex helpers measure from the unit using the rule: OrkAbilities11e._visible(from_id=ability unit) (OrkAbilities11e.gd:479-483, e.g. :1528); Custodes GRAVIMETRIC GRENADE from the targeted unit's group (CustodesCodexOther.gd:382-398); same pattern in SMAbilitiesA.gd:367 and SWAbilities11e.gd:300. |
| 06-12 | 06.02 FAQ — 'when a mortal wound is allocated' triggers at the Select Model step | FAQ | no_engine_surface | No implemented rule triggers on a mortal wound being allocated (Watcher in the Dark is not implemented). The Select Model step is Allocation.select_mortal_wound_target (Allocation.gd:297-345). WOLF TOTEMS and LAYERED CERAMITE follow the recorded pre-emptive S-panel ruling (RULINGS.md:271, :385). |
| 06-12 | 06.02.01 Commentary — definition of Normal Damage; extra MW are part of the same attack | Commentary | unclear | Normal damage is applied before mortal wounds (RulesEngine._apply_saves_via_allocation_11e, RulesEngine.gd:3807-3812; Allocation.gd:348). The 'part of that same attack' clause has no single code site to verify. Note RulesEngine.gd:3826-3830 applies half-damage and -X D to [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] mortal wounds; whether that is correct is a 24.10 question, not settled here. |
| 06-12 | 06.03 FAQ — failed [HAZARDOUS] rolls: allocate the MW to the unit per 06.02, not to the HAZARDOUS-weapon models | FAQ | wrong | resolve_hazardous_check sends every MW to one model holding the Hazardous weapon, using the Balance Dataslate priority (RulesEngine.gd:11545, 11672-11717). It applies min(MW, that model's wounds) and drops the rest (11573). The code is not edition-gated. |
| 06-12 | 06.03 FAQ — 'make a hazard roll' means one roll for the unit, not one per model | FAQ | implemented | Single-roll rules roll one die (TRY DAT BUTTON! calls AttackSequence.hazard_rolls(u, 1), OrkDetachments11e.gd:793). Per-model rolls only where the rule prints them (Shokk-boosta OrkAbilities11e.gd:2438-2458, Desperate Escape FallBackMove.gd:95, combat disembark DisembarkMove.gd:108). |
| 06-12 | 06.03.01 Commentary — 'Hazardous test' is the same as a hazard roll | Commentary | no_engine_surface | Terminology only. No engine rule text says 'Hazardous test', and every hazard source uses the 1-2 fail band (AttackSequence.hazard_rolls, AttackSequence.gd:143; [HAZARDOUS] threshold RulesEngine.gd:11484). |
| 06-12 | 07.01 Errata — no Warlord CP at the start of the battle round while the WARLORD is destroyed | Errata | implemented | Da Boss (OrkArmyRules11e.gd:299-310), Aquila Commander (CustodesKatah.gd:295-299) and Transhuman Strategist (SWArmyRules11e.gd:453-458) all require Common.is_alive(warlord). They are called once per round (CommandPhase.gd:93-109). |
| 06-12 | 07.02.01 Commentary — out-of-phase rules cannot use other phase-locked rules | Commentary | implemented | Fire Overwatch (opponent's Movement phase) opens no unit_has_shot window; only ShootingPhase opens it. 'In your Shooting phase, when this unit has shot' offers check own turn and SHOOTING phase (SMAbilitiesB.gd:1569-1572, SMDetachmentsA.gd:785, :844). |
| 06-12 | 07.02.02 Commentary — 'start of'/'the phase' triggers fire in both players' turns | Commentary | implemented | Sweeping Advance is offered at every Fight phase end with no turn check (FightPhase.gd:5512-5520). Fearsome Assault fires at every Fight phase start (SMAbilitiesC.gd:969-998). Start-of-round CP fires once per round at the first-turn player's Command phase (CommandPhase.gd:93-109). |
| 06-12 | 08.02.01 Commentary — Core CP: both players gain 1CP each turn | Commentary | implemented | CommandPhase._generate_command_points gives both players +1 CP in every Command phase (CommandPhase.gd:293-307). |
| 06-12 | 08.03.01 Commentary — at most one battle-shock roll per unit in the Battle-shock step | Commentary | implemented | Each unit gets one queue entry (CommandPhase.gd:421-436). Creeping Dread's extra test merges into the unit's single test (already-queued units are skipped) and its -1 is folded into that roll (CommandPhase.gd:2474-2477). |
| 06-12 | 09.02.01 Commentary — every unit is selected to move; unselected units remain stationary | Commentary | implemented | get_units_requiring_selection plus get_end_movement_remain_stationary_changes resolve every unmoved unit as Remain Stationary at END_MOVEMENT (MovementPhase.gd:4041-4077). |
| 06-12 | 09.02.02 Commentary — modes are exclusive; conditions labelled with a mode apply only if that mode was selected; ordered retreat is optional | Commentary | partial | Mode selection is right: FallBackMove.select_mode keeps ordered retreat optional (FallBackMove.gd:52-72) and consolidation modes are mandatory in order (ConsolidationMove.gd:62-93). But every FALL_BACK skips the 'cannot move through enemy models' check (MovementPhase.gd:759, 893, 8145, 8232), so the Desperate-Escape-only permission applies to Ordered Retreat too; DE's can_move_through_enemies flag (FallBackMove.gd:112) is never read. The UI never lets an unshocked unit pick DE (MovementController.gd:1498-1509). |
| 06-12 | 09.02.03 Commentary — Reinforcements-step rules happen during the Move Units step | Commentary | no_engine_surface | There is no separate Reinforcements step: PLACE_REINFORCEMENT (ingress) is accepted at any point of the Movement phase (MovementPhase.gd:4209-4250), and no implemented rule keys off a 'Reinforcements step'. |
| 06-12 | 09.05.01 Commentary — a unit cannot make more than one Normal Move in a phase | Commentary | partial | Only BEGIN_NORMAL_MOVE checks _normal_moves_this_phase (MovementPhase.gd:633-637). Reactive normal moves (MovementPhase.gd:6415, ShootingPhase.gd:5523) and the stratagems/abilities that grant them (SMCodexAMovement.gd:387-397, SMCodexBAction.gd:406-407) never check it. has_/mark_unit_normal_moved_this_phase (MovementPhase.gd:7009-7018) have no callers. |
| 06-12 | 09.07.01 Commentary — forced Desperate Escape: DE mode, one hazard roll per model, battle-shock roll, penalties apply to the hazard rolls | Commentary | partial | Implemented: forced DE for CUT 'EM DOWN, WHERE D'YA FINK, Wimp-kickaz, Corner the Quarry and Savage Frenzy (FallBackMove.gd:63-71); penalties on every DE hazard roll including the WHERE D'YA FINK extras (FallBackMove.gd:85-110); battle-shock roll after DE (MovementPhase.gd:3609-3618). Missing: the hazard count and MW allocation use only the bodyguard's models (FallBackMove.gd:79-95, MovementPhase.gd:2948). Fight-phase fall-backs skip parts: Tactical Fluidity has no modes at all (SMAbilitiesB.gd:1169), and WIND-SWIFT EVASION's DE makes no battle-shock roll (SMCodexAMovement.gd:627-650). |
| 06-12 | 10.02 FAQ — a unit with no ranged weapons can be selected to shoot (makes no attacks) | FAQ | partial | SELECT_SHOOTER refuses any unit with no eligible target (an ungated 10e carry-over labelled 'P3-96', ShootingPhase.gd:805-809). No outcome changes in implemented content: a unit that makes no attacks 'has not shot' (04.03.04), and the SM selection-triggered abilities with non-attack effects are also offered at phase_start (SMAbilitiesB.gd:1254-1260). No gap filed. |
